# Open questions (build log)

| ID | Question | Default until answered | Source | Raised |

| BQ-27 | PRD 8.5 and 10.3 say side effects go through the outbox and gates name the exact action, but not how the outbox entry names the content it sends or where it lands (a reply's thread, 11.4), nor what happens when an agent redrafts after approval or during the L2 undo window. | The entry carries a pinned `item` (artifact id and version) and a `destination` (a connector id for the thread or folder); the gate card shows the same pair, `approval.requested` and `policy.applied` record the item, and an outgoing `data.transfer` names it. A redraft is a new version and so a new proposal with a new gate. A policy send has no card, so `policy.applied` also records the connector, destination and district; the entry equals them, the policy's scope covers them (recipients and domains match exactly) and an L2 undo window is at least 1 s; ADR-0002 decision 11, outbox rules OB-01 and OB-05, card rule CR-12 | PRD 8.5, 10.3, 10.4, 11.4, FR-CHN-13 | 2026-09-25 |
| BQ-28 | PRD 9.3 and 10.1: `vis` is the server-side redaction scope ("district ids or all") and a Team Lead sees their districts plus summaries, but nothing ties an event's `vis` to the district it renders in. | A `vis` list includes the event's own `district` and names only districts of the world's layout (event rule EV-13); `all` stays allowed and the harness chooses it from the world's viewer scopes. ADR-0012 (Proposed, issue #29) would require a list whenever `district` is set. | PRD 9.3, 10.1, FR-HIL-09 | 2026-09-25 |
| BQ-29 | PRD 10.4 L2 is "instruction is approval" and FR-HIL-05 says the policy sends "when the user instructs a reply", but no contract links a policy send to that instruction, says who may give it, or how Tilo knows which thread it is about. Nor does the PRD say whose policies apply in a world. | `policy.applied` carries `instruction_id`: at L2 it names a `world.requested` or `channel.message_in` of the same world, earlier than the send, by the policy's owner, whose `data.destination` equals the send's destination (never null at L2, so an L2 policy never starts a new email); at L3 it is null. `destination` on those two events is set from where the message was sent (a forwarded thread, the thread a request was started from, the card it answers), never read out of the text by a model; with none, the send waits at a gate. Only a channel with a known sender carries an instruction: a `world.requested` from `web` or `mobile_web` (signed in) or a `channel.message_in` from `whatsapp` (the owner's bound number, FR-CHN-03); never email, whose sender is not authenticated (10.3: email answers questions only). Each instruction opens at most one outbox entry; a cancelled entry uses it up. A policy applies only in worlds its owner started (PRD 10.1: only an Owner starts runs and creates policies) and only after its `created_by.at`. Outbox rule OB-01, ADR-0005 decision 4. | PRD 10.1, 10.3, 10.4, 11.1, FR-HIL-03, FR-HIL-05, HR-7 | 2026-09-25 |
| BQ-30 | PRD 10.4 lists policy limits "per day, credits" and FR-HIL-06 tests the daily limit for L3 only; may an L2 policy have no daily cap? | No: L2 and L3 policies both need an integer `limits.per_day` (`policy.schema.json`), because both act without a person at the gate. L0 and L1 policies may leave it null. | PRD 10.4, FR-HIL-05, FR-HIL-06 | 2026-09-25 |
| BQ-31 | PRD 10.4 says policies are "revocable in one tap" and FR-HIL-03 that after a policy is removed "the next send waits at a gate", but neither says what a revoked policy's record holds, nor how a later check tells a send made before the revocation from one made after. | Revoking sets the policy to L1, like Stop all autopilot (FR-HIL-07): L1 is the default with no policy, so a revoked policy authorises nothing. `policy.changed` with `revoked` or `stop_all` must carry `level` L1 (schema). Each change appends a `history` entry at its new level; history is oldest first and never empty, and the record's `level` equals its last entry's. Outbox OB-01 checks a policy send against the level in force at `authz.at` (the last history entry at or before it), so a send after a revocation or Stop all is never covered, even if the level was raised again later, and a send before a revocation stays covered. Only the level is kept in history; an edit of scope, limits or undo window is checked against the current record (the harness validator runs before each write, when current and in force agree). ADR-0005 decision 4. | PRD 10.4, FR-HIL-03, FR-HIL-07 | 2026-09-25 |
| BQ-32 | PRD 10.1 lets the owner open any gate, a Team Lead "in their domain, if the policy allows" and an Approver "assigned gates", and 16.2 says the owner assigns Team Lead domains and T3 approvals over WhatsApp need a PIN (SEC-06), but no contract said who was assigned to a gate, what a Team Lead's domain is, how a policy allows a Team Lead, or whether a PIN was checked. | `approval.requested` names the gate's `approver` as an owner-made record, `{user, assigned_by: {by, at}}` (null when none), which counts only when `assigned_by.by` is the world's owner and `assigned_by.at` is no later than the answer, so a user id the harness names alone, or a Team Lead naming themself, opens nothing and a `team_lead` allowance (`domain` and the `policy_id` that allows it, null when no Team Lead may), both copied by the harness from the owner's records, never by a model; an Approver assigned after the gate is raised (FR-HIL-12 share link) needs a new proposal. A Team Lead's domain is a `team_lead.schema.json` record (the `config/teams.yaml` entry shape) with `assigned_by` the owner; one domain per Team Lead; district scoping (8.3) is left to the P2 routing ticket. A policy allows Team Leads through `team_lead_domains` (expert domains, empty by default), read from the current record, for every gate of its action type in the owner's worlds, whatever its level or scope. `approval.resolved` records `pin_verified`; a T3 gate answered on WhatsApp opens only with it. Outbox rule OB-01, ADR-0006. | PRD 8.3, 10.1, 16.2, SEC-06, FR-HIL-12 | 2026-09-25 |

The schema cannot restrict which variable is named: config changes go through a pull request (PRD 6.4), and the adapter reads only that variable and sends it only to that `base_url`; ADR-0002 decision 10 | PRD 6.4, 16.1 | 2026-09-25 |

PRD 10.4 says only "the user's own instruction"; which card answers are instructions? | Only the owner's own typed words: `channel.message_in` records `card_kind` (copied from the card) and `option` (from the button payload), and counts at L2 only with `option` null and `card_kind` null or `needs_you` (the typed answer to "What should I say?", PRD 11.2). | BQ-58 | Issue #3 (SH) makes `handoff_at` tunable with the rule 0 < `compact_at` < `handoff_at` < 1, which a schema cannot check, and FR-AGT-13 ("retries up to 3") does not say whether the first attempt counts. | `handoff_at` is fixed at 0.70 (D-08, FR-AGT-09), so `compact_at` is in (0, 0.70). `max_attempts` counts the first attempt and is 1..3, the 8.3 must-ask "a unit fails 3 times". The harness loader also requires `silent_after_s` > `heartbeat_s`. `contracts/agents.schema.json`, ADR-0002 decision 12 | PRD 8.3, 8.5, D-08, FR-AGT-09, FR-AGT-13 | 2026-09-25 |
| BQ-59 | PRD 13.3 does not say what a manifest's `produced_by` is for a version no agent wrote (a restore, FR-MEM-06), how a source names a table cell, or what a source `ref` may hold, given that exports leave Tilo (issue #5, SH). | A restore keeps the restored version's `produced_by`, names that version as its only parent, and lists `artifact.rolled_back` in `events`. A `cell` source's `ref` is `<artifact_id>@<version>#<cell>` and an `artifact` source's is `<artifact_id>@<version>`, naming a version in `inputs`. A web source's `ref` is the fetched URL without user info, query, fragment or path parameters (`;`, `=`), and a cell id holds only letters, digits and `. _ ~ ! : $ -`, so no token rides in their syntax (PRD 16.1 rule 4, 16.2); the fingerprint still identifies the content. Every `ref` is one of four closed shapes, and since a bare token can still look like a file name or an item id, the secret redactor checks each manifest before it is written or exported and a manifest it would change is refused (MF-02, MF-03, MF-06 in `contracts/manifest.rules.md`); ADR-0002 decision 12 | PRD 13.3, 13.4, FR-MEM-06, FR-MEM-07 | 2026-09-25 |
| BQ-60 | PRD 8.3 and 13.1 give a Decision a scope but not its unit, district ids are only unique within a world, and nothing says whose Decision may answer which question, where, or after the answerer loses the role (issue #4, SH; security review CC-SH). | Only the source world's owner or a Team Lead gives a Decision (PRD 13.1); an Approver's answer settles its card only. Scope is worlds (`all` means every world of the org where the answerer has that authority: worlds the person owns, or worlds of the owner who assigned the Team Lead; or ids among them) and districts (`all`, or ids applied in each world in scope). A Team Lead's Decision names its domain and answers only questions routed with that domain; an owner's answers any domain in scope; each reuse checks the answerer again in that world at that time (the person is its owner; the Team Lead's record still exists, same domain, same person as the Decision's `answered_by.user`, assigned by its owner no later than the answer); a gate is never answered from a Decision (DC-01, DC-02, DC-04 in `contracts/decision.rules.md`). Team Lead district scoping (8.3) stays with the P2 routing ticket (BQ-32); ADR-0002 decision 12 | PRD 8.3, 13.1, 15.1, FR-AGT-07 | 2026-09-25 |
| BQ-61 | PL-03/PRD 6.4: the router picks "by role, capability and remaining budget", but no exact cost-estimate formula is given for what a call would cost against that budget. | Estimate from `config.price(profile_id)` (PL-06) and the request's counted input tokens plus its resolved `max_output_tokens`, converted to credits via `config.credits()` (PRD 14.1); a candidate the budget cannot cover is recorded as a `BudgetExceededError` attempt, never sent, and the next candidate is tried; `BudgetExceededError` is raised only when no candidate left fits, carrying every attempt; acceptance tests (`test_PL_03_router_budget.py`) assert only the two directions every formula must get right — an unaffordable budget is always refused before any call, an amply large one always proceeds | PRD 6.4 PL-03, 14.1 | 2026-09-25 |
| BQ-62 | EFF-03/PRD 6.6 "Small contexts" says "tool outputs compacted before they reach a model", but `GenerateRequest` (frozen in ticket P0-05, `extra="forbid"`) has no field for prior tool call outputs. | `Router.generate(role, request, tool_outputs=[...])` (oldest first) folds surviving tool outputs into the final prompt sent to the provider, after the deterministic compactor runs; `GenerateRequest` itself is unchanged | PRD 6.4 PL-03, 6.6 EFF-03 | 2026-09-25 |
| BQ-63 | EFF-03: ticket P0-06 says "always pass max_output_tokens" but not which value wins when both the caller's request and the role's config set one. | The caller's explicit `GenerateRequest.max_output_tokens` is respected as given; the role's config value only fills in when the caller left it unset | PRD 6.6 EFF-03 | 2026-09-25 |
| BQ-64 | PL-05: the conformance gate ("a role whose primary or fallback profile has a failing result is refused") has no specified result-file shape (not a frozen `contracts/` schema) and no specified default for `load_providers_config` callers that predate this ticket. | Result shape: `{profile_id, date, build, checks: {schema_adherence, tool_calling, repair_rate, latency_budget, safety_handling}, result: "pass"\|"fail"}`, one file per profile at `<conformance_dir>/<profile_id>.json`; `load_providers_config` gains an optional `conformance_dir` keyword that defaults to `None`, meaning "skip the gate" (every existing P0-04 call site keeps loading unchanged); passing it activates the gate, and `TILO_REQUIRE_CONFORMANCE=1` additionally refuses a *missing* result | PRD 6.4 PL-05 | 2026-09-25 |
| BQ-65 | PRD 14.2 subtracts measured steering and review minutes but does not say what a measured span points at, and "take-over" appears nowhere else in the PRD (issue #10, SE). | An attention span's `ref` is a card ULID (question and card spans), an artifact id (review spans) or null (intake, or a take-over with no card); the schema allows exactly these three shapes and `tilo-api` checks which goes with which kind (MT-07). Overlapping spans of one person count once (MT-08). `contracts/attention.schema.json`, ADR-0002 decision 13 | PRD 14.2, FR-MTR-05 | 2026-09-26 |
| BQ-66 | PRD 2.4 and 16.5 require written, revocable pilot consent and deletion 30 days after the study, but say nothing about what withdrawal does or whether a survey quote may be stored without consent to quote it (issue #11, SE). | Withdrawing consent deletes every record with that tester id at once; otherwise records go on `delete_after` (the study's last day plus 30 days, 2026-11-11) unless `keep_data`. A survey `quote` is stored only with `quote_consent` (schema: null otherwise). Tag, diary and survey records need an earlier consent (MT-12 to MT-14). `contracts/pilot.schema.json`, ADR-0002 decision 13 | PRD 2.4, 14.4, 16.5, FR-MTR-09 | 2026-09-26 |
| BQ-67 | **Needs the user.** PRD 19.2's heading says "Eval set (170 cases)", but its eight rows sum to 160 (10+10+6+50+30+20+20+14). Which is right? | The rows: `config/evals.yaml` `groups` lists them, and the harness checks the case set against them (MT-10). If 170 is meant, the user names the group that gets the extra 10 cases | PRD 19.2, EFF-07 | 2026-09-26 |
| BQ-68 | PRD 16.3 item 2 names "the safety provider (Model Armor by default)" but not how config selects it, what a fake flags, or which native media types a screen accepts (issue #47, CC-SP-47). | `build_safety_provider` reads a `safety` role in `config/models.yaml` (`provider: fake` or `model_armor`; any other provider or a missing role fails start-up). No safety role is added to the example config yet: a `safety` role would also enter the PL-05 conformance and router role sets, which assume a `ModelProvider`; SP adds it when wiring `get_safety()`. Native screening accepts `application/pdf`, `image/png` and `image/jpeg` only; anything else raises `RequestError`. The fake flags a fixed list of prompt-injection phrases, case-insensitively; a native file is clean in the fake. The live Model Armor adapter refuses until billing (ADR-0003) **Update 6 Oct: Model Armor adapter built (FR-INT-02, PR #450):** `tilo_providers.model_armor_adapter` is the live adapter, the `safety` role in `config/models.yaml` is on `armor-default`, it replays without `LIVE=1` and fails closed on a miss; the refusal above no longer applies (#450 is merged). | PRD 7.1 FR-INT-02, 16.3, 6.4 | 2026-09-26 |
| BQ-69 | FR-HIL-01 (PRD 10.1): the card layer needs a card's district and its assigned Approver, but card.schema.json has neither. | `CardStore.submit(card, outbox_id, district, assignee)` takes them as companion data from the harness (as with `outbox_id`); no district means owner-only; district and assignee join the merge key. | BQ-70 | FR-HIL-01 (PRD 10.1): a Team Lead "opens gates in their domain, if the policy allows", but the card layer has no lead record or policy. | The card layer refuses a Team Lead on gate and consent cards (403) and lets them answer needs_you and meeting cards of their districts; Team Lead gate answers go through `open_gate` once a route carries the lead record and policy. A Viewer gets 403 on both card routes. | PRD 10.1; PI-02 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-71 | FR-HIL-02 / PI-03: a timed-out gate or consent card must post a Nudge and stay open, but `question.answered` closes the card on every channel (registry hud_effect). | A held card posts one `alert.raised` (severity nudge, its own derived `alert_id`, `card_id` = the card id); only needs_you and meeting cards post `question.answered` (default, nudge). | BQ-04; PRD 10.3, 9.5 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-72 | FR-INT-09 plan validator: PG-06 asks for "raised to T3 by any context modifier that applies", but a plan carries no per-action facts (recipients, attachments, deletion), and nothing says whose policies PG-10 may use when the caller gives no world owner. | PG-06 checks "never lower" against the higher of the risk-table tier and the connectors' declared tier; the policy engine applies the modifiers at run time. `validate_plan(owner=None)` binds each policy to its own recorded owner (every stamp by that owner); the harness passes the world owner | PRD 7.1, 10.4; plan.rules.md PG-06, PG-10 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-73 | SEC-07 / FR-HIL-09: event `vis` is `"all"` or 1+ districts (`district_list` minItems 1), so an owner-only event (a card with no known district) cannot be written. | Every part carries `world_id`, `card_id`, `district` and `vis` [district]; a card with no district carries `vis` None and `owner_only` True, and the writer must not default it to `"all"` (it withholds it from non-owner views). **Needs contract change:** `event.schema.json` vis cannot express owner-only, and `question.answered` data has no `card_id` or chosen/default option (the card id travels as a part-level companion). ADR-0009 (Proposed, issue #125) proposes `vis: []` for owner-only and `card_id` plus `option` on `question.answered`. | EV-13; PRD 10.1 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-74 | FR-HIL-02: when same-type cards merge, does one answer or one timeout close the whole group? | Yes: the group is one card to the person; the first answer (any member's own options) or the first member timeout closes every open member and writes one part (`question.answered`, nudge on timeout). A card submitted after the group closed starts a new group. | PRD 10.3 "Same-type cards ... merge"; code review of PR #121 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-75 | FR-INT-08 (issue #64, CC-SP-64): a budget grant goes through no connector. But a policy's `scope.connectors` needs at least one id, and the engine's proposal, `policy.applied` and an outbox entry each name a connector. Which id does `grant_budget`, or any other connector-less action, carry? And what limits the T1 `grant_budget` to grants under the run budget, when the risk-tier table holds no amounts? | `grant_budget: T1` in `config/autonomy.yaml` (PRD 10.4). The connector id is the reserved `internal`, which SP proposed. It is a valid `connector_id` that `config/connectors.yaml` never declares, and a budget policy's scope is `connectors: [internal]`. A policy grant is one `budget.granted` with `authz` by the policy (BQ-23). A grant has no gate or outbox entry, so no `policy.applied` is written. The bound is the Treasury's ceiling `caps.run_credits` (MT-03; `Treasury.grant` does not enforce it yet, issue #51) plus the policy's own limits. `limits.credits` stays a daily total (BQ-14), which also bounds each grant (issue #106). The user may override this, for example with another id, before the P5 connector registry lands. ADR-0002 decision 14 | PRD 7.1 FR-INT-08, 10.4, 14.1, 17.3 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-76 | FR-CHN-07 D1: PRD 10.3 says email links are "valid 24 h" but not whether the boundary instant counts. | A link expires 24 h after issue (issue time truncated to whole seconds); at or after that instant it is `expired`. | PRD 10.3, 16.2; FR-CHN-07 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-77 | FR-CHN-07 D2: PRD 10.3 says email carries "questions only; gates need sign-in" but not how a malformed card is handled. | An `answer` link is issued and redeemed only for a `needs_you` card whose `answer_channels` include email; gate and consent cards are `not_allowed` even if they list email; meeting follows its channels (the schema excludes email). Re-checked at redeem time. | PRD 10.3; PI-04 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-78 | FR-CHN-07 D3: what does holding a link prove? | Holding the link answers a question card (`authn` "link"); a different signed-in viewer is refused (`wrong_user`); opening a gate, consent or meeting card with no signed-in viewer returns `requires_sign_in` and carries no option. | PRD 16.2 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-79 | FR-CHN-07 D4: does a refused request burn the link? | No: every check runs before the single atomic nonce consume, so a forwarded link presented wrongly cannot burn it for its owner. | PRD 16.4 SEC-08 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-80 | FR-CHN-07 D5: should an unknown and a retired key id be told apart? | Both are `unknown_key`; the key id is checked before the signature, and no claim is parsed before the MAC matches. | PRD 16.4 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-81 | FR-CHN-07 D6: an answered card (on any channel, or closed by timeout) and its sibling option links. | An `answer` link for an answered card is `card_answered`; `open` links still open it (read-only view). | PRD 10.3 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-82 | FR-CHN-07: mail scanners and clients prefetch links with GET, which would burn or answer a single-use link; PR #138 review also found the HTTP layer needs the points below. | For the HTTP-layer ticket: (1) `answer` needs a POST confirm; a GET only shows the card and never calls `redeem` for `answer`. (2) `open` redeems on GET, so a scanner prefetch can burn an open link: decide whether `open` also needs a POST confirm (flagged, not decided). (3) Put the token in a URL fragment the page POSTs, not the path: Cloud Run logs record the path and a Referer can leak it (else `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` and a log exclusion). (4) Add a public non-consuming verify (MAC, expiry, card checks) so a GET can show the card; never decode claims without the MAC. (5) `redeem` consumes the nonce even when `requires_sign_in` is True: do not consume then, or redirect after sign-in without redeeming again. (6) `card_answered` is only a read: the answer consumer applies the first answer with a compare-and-set on the card (BQ-74) so two sibling option links cannot both win. (7) The durable `NonceStore` is atomic create-if-absent and purges on `expires_at` by its own clock; link keys are dedicated, in Secret Manager, readable by `tilo-channels` only. (8) `requires_sign_in` also applies to an `open` link on a question card that does not list email (fail closed). | PRD 11.6; SEC-08; PR #138 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-83 | CL-INT-1 (CP2 API flow, PRD 7.1, 16.3): the PRD is silent on which approach is recommended, what happens to flagged task text, how a free-text answer fills a structured chip, and which connector and district a world-level budget grant names. | Recommended: the cheapest approach by its credit maximum, ties in the Director's order. Task text or a file name the safety screen flags is refused (422) before any file is stored or a model reads it; so is any typed answer or brief edit (every string, nested ones included), leaving the task unchanged, and a screen error is 503. A brief edit may add url and web sources only; file, connector and memory sources must already be the task's own. An answer equal to the Director's guess keeps the chip; the suggested answers map to chip values (formats, no deadline, coming Friday, web source); other text is set as typed, and a value the chip cannot hold is 422 so the person edits the chip. The grant names connector `internal` and district `world`, so only a policy scoped to all districts covers it (#106). The safety role's fallback is its own fake profile: screens never fall back to a weaker one (#103). Memory lookups use an in-memory store per API store until P7 | PRD 7.1 FR-INT-04, FR-INT-06, FR-INT-08, 16.3; services/api/tests/API_CL_INT_1.md | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-89 | FR-CHN-03 / FR-CHN-05 (PRD 10.3, 11.1, 16.2, SEC-06): the PRD gives no code length or lifetime for number binding, no rule for a number already bound to someone else, no boundary for "re-confirmed every 30 days", no rate for the opt-in reply, no way to enter the T3 PIN on WhatsApp, and no way to close a WhatsApp card answered elsewhere (a sent interactive message cannot be edited). | Code: 6 digits, valid while now < sent + 10 min, 5 attempts (the 5th wrong one voids it), single use, stored only hashed. A number bound to another user is refused at `start` with `number_taken` and no code is sent (this tells the second user the number is in use; flagged for the security review); two pending users race, first confirm wins. Bound while now < verified_at + 30 d; re-confirm is start + confirm again; the old number stays bound until a new one is confirmed. One opt-in reply per inbound message from an unbound number, pointing to the web app, never echoing the message. PIN: tapping Approve on a T3 gate asks for it; the next text from the same number within 300 s is checked; one try per tap; Hold and Edit first need no PIN (only approve opens, CR-07). Typed text never answers a card option (BQ-56). Closing: one short text to the bound number, later taps get "already answered". `services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_03_05.md`. Hardening (#156), binding: SEC-10 code-send caps of 3 per number per hour, 10 per number per day, 5 per user per hour and 20 per user per day, in fixed clock windows counted in the store; over a cap the result is `rate_limited`, nothing is sent and the pending code keeps its attempts. The caps apply when `send_caps=SEND_CAPS` is passed, because the FR-CHN-03 acceptance tests start uncapped (20 starts in a row, the property test), so the HTTP route must pass it. Numbers with a leading 0 after "+", fewer than 8 digits or all one digit are refused. A replaced code keeps its attempt slots until they expire (`expires_at` for the store's TTL purge). The pending code is written before the send; a store failure is `unavailable` and nothing is sent. After its write, a confirm re-checks the owner; the losing racer gets `number_taken` and keeps its old number. The opt-in reply goes at most once per number hash per 10 min when `opt_in_allowed` is passed (the acceptance tests pin one reply per message). A keyword or card handler that raises gives route `failed` and one short reply. Hardening, cards: for 300 s after a PIN prompt's window closes, or after its one try, a digits-only text from that number is consumed as `pin_late` ("That PIN came too late. Tap Approve again.") and is never verified, archived or routed. An API error replies "Could not confirm your answer. Check the card in Tilo before tapping again." `send_card` returns `failed` for a send error or a malformed card (bad or offset-less `created_at`, bad `timeout`, missing options) and `closed` for a card already answered; a retry of an open card returns the first wamid without sending again; a record failure after the send still returns `sent`. Hold and Edit first on a gate leave the WhatsApp copy open; the replies are "Held. You can still approve here or in Tilo." and "To edit, open it in Tilo: <card link>. Nothing was approved." (tilo-api records nothing for Edit first). Either one spends a pending PIN prompt for that card, so a PIN typed afterwards is `pin_late`; a new Approve tap asks again. A re-send of a card to another user or number retires the earlier buttons. Sent cards and their buttons are kept until deadline + 1 h, so late taps get "already answered" or "timed out"; after that they are evicted. Follow-ups (#156 part 2): `start` claims the user's send window before the owner lookup, so every start (refused or not, not `invalid_number`) counts against the user cap and a capped account learns nothing about who owns a number; starts by the user who holds the number (its `num:<hash>` record names them, including a binding that lapsed but was not unbound or replaced; no extra record is kept) are not counted against or blocked by the per-number cap, so another account cannot lock the real owner out, and unbinding or moving to another number ends the exemption (the user cap still applies to them); unknown `send_caps` keys, a window under 1 s or a limit under 1 raise ValueError and the default stays off (`send_caps={}` is explicit no caps). Two concurrent sends of one card send once; a repeated Hold or Edit first tap from the card's number writes one `channel.message_in`, makes one API call and gets the same reply again. | PRD 10.3, 11.1, 11.6, 16.2, SEC-06, SEC-10, FR-HIL-11 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-90 | CL-INT-2 (CP2 run after approve, PRD 8.5, 9.4, 10.3, 10.4): the PRD is silent on a gate card's timeout, what Pause all means for a gate answered later, the policy-run path in the thin slice, and the production defaults of the run seams. | A gate card times out after 24 h to `hold` (never to go). Pause all is final for the run: it writes nothing more, and an answer to its gate card is 409 with nothing written. Every send asks a person in this slice, even one a policy would run (the `policy.applied` path is FR-HIL-05/06); the card suggests `edit`. The send step is retried for about 6 s, then the unit fails and the run writes `world.failed`. Production defaults until their tickets land: the plan compiler refuses (no Director plan prompt yet, FR-INT-09), the action resolver has no address book, no live connector adapter is wired, the outbox store is in memory, and `time_saved` is null (FR-MTR-02). Review r1 (#151): an owner's L0 policy (`decide` says `draft_only`) keeps the draft in Tilo: no gate card, no outbox id, no send; the draft is delivered `in_app`. PRD 10.4 wins over API_CL_INT_2.md's "any result other than run/run_after_undo gates". A send with no recipients or no destination fails closed before the gate (`world.failed`). A run that fails, gives up or is paused marks its unsent outbox entry `failed`, and dispatches and records only its own world's entries. The `data.transfer` of a send already under way when Pause lands is still written, since the email went (HR-1) | PRD 8.5, 10.3, 10.4, FR-WLD-07; services/api/tests/API_CL_INT_2.md | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-91 | FR-CON-04 / FR-CON-05 (PRD 11.4, 12.6): no number for the attachment or download size, nor for blocked attachment types. | 25 MiB of attachments per message in total and Gmail's own blocked extensions (`BLOCKED_ATTACHMENT_EXTENSIONS`; last extension, case-insensitive, trailing dots and spaces ignored) refuse the entry (`failed`); one Drive download is at most 25 MiB, checked from metadata before the content is fetched | PRD 11.4, 12.6 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-92 | FR-CON-05 (PRD 12.2, 16.1): which Drive scope reads "the files the user picks", and where writes go. | `drive.file` for every Drive capability (Picker grants per file), never `drive` or `drive.readonly`; writes go only to the `Tilo` folder in the Drive root that the adapter created (an app-property marks it, so a user folder named `Tilo` is never written); if racing first saves made two, the smallest id is used, and a retry looks for its save in every folder | PRD 12.2, 16.1 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-93 | FR-CON-04 / FR-CON-05 (PRD 6.6, 12.1): connector `limits` give no retry bound. | Reads: 3 requests per call and at most 60 s of waiting (Retry-After, else 1 s then 2 s); a Retry-After over 60 s is `RateLimited` at once. A side effect never waits or retries in the call (a wait could outlast the 30 s outbox lease and let another instance act): a 429, 5xx or lost answer leaves the entry due, and the next dispatch starts again with the earlier-attempt lookup. In a side effect a 400 or 404 answer is final (`SideEffectRefused`, entry `failed`) | PRD 6.6, 12.1 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-94 | FR-CON-04 (PRD 12.2): Gmail label changes have no action id or risk tier. | Not built: no label-changing method or action (an unlisted action is T3, BQ-44) | PRD 12.2, 10.4 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-95 | FR-CON-04 / FR-CON-05 (PRD 9.5, PI-16): should a read emit `data.transfer` with direction `in`? | Reads emit no `data.transfer`: the registry marks it a side effect that needs `authz`, and a read has none; only outbox side effects emit it (direction `out`) | PRD 9.5, 16.3 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-99 | CL-INT-1 follow-ups (#139; FR-INT-02/04/08, PRD 7.1, 10.1, 14.1): what does the PRD leave open in the answers and approve routes? | (a) A chip edit and an answer on one field: the last action wins. An answer drops that field's earlier edit; an edit after an answer already stands over it; in one request the edit wins (a JSON body has no order), and a skip beside it records no assumption. (b) The goal question's "Something else, which I will describe" is a prompt, not a goal: `apply_answer` refuses it (422) and the person describes the goal in the goal chip. (c) "The web as well" adds `{kind: web, ref: "web", label: "The web"}`; the goal is never a search query, and the specialist composes queries when it runs. (d) A file the safety screen flagged is quarantined: it stays in the intake response (`safety_flagged`) and the artifact store but never enters `brief.sources`. (e) Intake extracts the Director's brief before storing any file, so a failed extraction (502) stores nothing. (f) The most one approval may grant is `caps.run_credits` in `config/metering.yaml` (300, PRD 14.1, 17.3); the named constant `MAX_BUDGET_CAP_CREDITS` (300) applies when the file or value is absent. A larger or non-finite `budget_cap` is 422. | PRD 7.1, 10.1, 14.1, 17.3; issue #139; `services/api/tests/test_FR_INT_04_clint1_followups_139.py` | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-100 | FR-INT-08 (issue #106): `budget.granted` carries `granted` and `cap`, and `budget.py` always sets `granted = cap`, so a top-up would set the cap to the increment. PRD 14.1 says an autonomy policy may pre-approve small top-ups, but the Treasury ceiling (MT-03, issue #51) that must bound a raised cap is not enforced yet, and no running world asks for one (a task's grant is once only, 409). Also: what does an L3 `grant_budget` policy with `limits.credits: null` cover? | Top-ups are refused explicitly until the Treasury ticket lands: `request_budget_grant` and `grant_budget_by_person` take `existing_cap` (default 0) and raise `TopUpNotSupported` for anything else, NaN included; `granted == cap` stays true for a world's first grant. When the Treasury ticket lands, a top-up adds to the cap (`granted` is the increment, `cap` the new total) under the MT-03 ceiling. A `grant_budget` policy with no credit bound covers no cap: `tilo_policy.decide` gates it as `l3_credits_cap_reached` (and `budget._over_limit` mirrors that), so the person confirms. A null-credit `grant_budget` policy whose id sorts first gates even when a bounded one also covers the grant (fails safe). `config/autonomy.yaml` changes only in its comment. The action type `grant_budget` and the `internal` connector are logged in BQ-75 and BQ-83. | PRD 7.1 FR-INT-08, 10.4, 14.1, 17.3; issues #51, #106 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-101 | FR-CON-10 (PRD 13.2, 13.3): which raw-archive record is a source read? The archive payload shape is not in `contracts/`. | A `tool_call` record whose payload is `{tool, source: {kind, ref}, content}`: `kind` one of web, file, connector, cell, artifact; `ref` as read; `content` the text read, after the archive's redactor. The manifest takes `kind`, `ref` (cleaned, BQ-104), the record's `ts` and a SHA-256 of `content`, never the content. Overridable default (pinned in `services/connectors/tests/API_FR_CON_10.md`). | PRD 12.4, 12.6, 13.2, 13.3; FR-CON-10 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-102 | FR-CON-10 (MF-04): which events does a manifest list? | The causing events the caller passes, all of the producing run (an event of another run is refused, MF-04), listed by `seq` oldest first with duplicate ids removed. `produced_by.skill` is the latest `skill.loaded` of the producing agent; `authz` is the first `data.transfer` out that carried this exact version. Overridable default. | PRD 12.4, 12.6, 13.2, 13.3; FR-CON-10; MF-04, MF-05, HR-7 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-103 | FR-CON-10: what order do a manifest's lists take, so the same run always gives the same bytes? | `sources` and `claims` sorted by `id`, each claim's `source_ids` sorted; `parents` and `inputs` keep the caller's order. Overridable default. | PRD 12.4, 12.6, 13.2, 13.3; FR-CON-10 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-104 | FR-CON-10 (MF-03, BQ-59): a web source fetched from a URL with user info, a query, a fragment or `;` path parameters. Refuse it, or record it cleaned? | Record it cleaned: scheme and host lower-cased, port kept, user info, query, fragment and every `;` path parameter dropped; the fingerprint still identifies the content. A ref that is not an http or https URL is refused (MF-03). Overridable default. | PRD 12.4, 12.6, 13.2, 13.3; FR-CON-10; MF-03, BQ-59 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-105 | FR-CON-10 (PRD 13.2): the run's archive records fail `tilo_archive.verify_chain`, or hold another run's records. | `build_manifest` refuses (`rule` "archive"): no fingerprint from a broken chain is trusted. Given `archive_head` (the chain head from an anchor outside the archive bucket; optional in the API, production callers must pass it, #187), a rewritten, forged-tail or truncated archive is refused too. Overridable default. | PRD 12.4, 12.6, 13.2, 13.3; FR-CON-10; FR-MEM-04 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-106 | FR-CON-10 (MF-06): does the schema check or the secret redactor run first? | The schema check runs first, so the redactor only sees well-formed strings; a schema refusal names paths, never values. Then every source `ref`, claim `location` and `text` and any artifact `label` goes through the redactor; a change refuses (MF-06) without echoing the string. Since #187 the redactor gets every string of the manifest except the hex digests (ids, agent_id, model_role, profile, skill, authz.ref, event ids). Before the schema check, in linear time: at most 1000 `parents` and 5000 `inputs`, no `(artifact_id, version)` twice (type-strict), caller types; each refused as "schema", so jsonschema never runs its O(n^2) uniqueItems on those lists (the validator drops that keyword for them). Overridable default. | PRD 12.4, 12.6, 13.2, 13.3; FR-CON-10; MF-06, SEC-14 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-107 | FR-INT-10 (issue #173): the PRD and contracts give no size or depth bound for a plan; a plan at its array caps (about 250 KiB) took 4.4 s to validate, and lists nested hundreds deep made measuring it slow. | `validate_plan` rejects as `schema`, before any walk, a plan nested deeper than 16 containers (`MAX_PLAN_DEPTH`; the schema needs 4) or over 64 KiB of compact UTF-8 JSON (`MAX_PLAN_BYTES`), and reads at most 50 schema errors (`MAX_ERRORS`) | PRD 7.1, contracts/plan.schema.json | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-108 | Issue #72 (PRD 19.3, EV-09): `flagged_or_blocked` accepts any `alert.raised` event, and the contract's `alert.raised` data (`alert_id`, `card_id`) has no safety marker, so a red-team case can pass EV-09 on an unrelated needs_you alert. What marks a safety alert? | Needs contract change (architect, ADR first): a safety marker on `alert.raised` data, or a dedicated event. Until then the check keeps accepting `alert.raised`, `outputs.safety_flag` or a refusal, and red-team recordings should set `safety_flag`. | evals/src/tilo_evals/checks.py; issue #72 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-109 | FR-MEM-05 / SEC-14 (PRD 13.2, 16.2) stay open after the #37 split: the archive redactor is not built, and MF-06 (`contracts/manifest.rules.md`) and ADR-0002 still name "the secret redactor that guards the raw archive". Issue #57. | `RunArchive` takes an injected `redactor` and nothing in production constructs one; it must not be wired into harness or channels until the shared redactor lands (identity or lambda is an automatic FAIL). The MF-06 and ADR-0002 wording stays until the contract/ADR owner updates it ("needs contract change": say the redactor is pending FR-MEM-05). Redo carry-overs are in #57 item 5 | PRD 13.2, 16.2, SEC-14, ADR-0008 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-110 | FR-CHN-04 (PRD 11.1, 11.6, 13.2): how is an off-topic WhatsApp message decided, answered and recorded? | Applied on the PRD defaults (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_04.md`). (1) Off-topic is whatever no in-scope handler takes (keywords, cards); no model classifies, so no message or PIN reaches a provider. Email-reply instructions (FR-CHN-10) and task start or status are not built; when they are, they take their messages first. (2) One fixed line for every message, which says Tilo is automated (answers "are you a bot?" truthfully) and points to the web app; it never echoes the message. (3) No event and no archive record: the message belongs to no world or run, event parts and `archive_ref` are per run, and a `channel.message_in` with null `option` is the shape of an owner's L2 instruction (OB-01). PRD 13.2 says the archive holds every inbound channel message; a run-less record needs a contract change. (4) One reply per message; a per-number limiter, if added, must be optional like `opt_in_allowed`. Reactions and audio are not pinned (FR-CHN-16). | PRD 11.1, 11.6, 13.2; FR-CHN-04 tests | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-115 | CL-INT-5b (FR-INT-09, PRD 7.1, 10.4): the PRD does not say who writes a plan's gates, which units a plan may hold, how many drafts the Director gets, or how the compile seam learns the world owner and the approaches the person saw. | The Director drafts `units` only (at most 30, roles specialist, writer or verifier: the roles the harness runs; the schema allows 200). Code assembles the plan: the approaches the person saw, the chosen id, the confirmed cap, and one gate per side-effect action guarding every unit that performs it, at the higher of the risk table's tier and the connectors' declared tier, with `policy_ref` null (a model never names a policy; the policy engine decides at run time). `validate_plan` runs with the world owner (BQ-72). Two drafts in all (`MAX_ATTEMPTS`): the first and one repair, which sends only rule ids, pointers and the validator's own id-only messages, inside delimited blocks; then `PlanCompileError` and `world.failed` with the fixed reason "the Director could not plan this task". The compile seam takes a `context` (`PlanInputs`: approaches, owner, skill catalogue, connectors, risk table, policies), which `Run._prepare` builds. The seam `deps.get_plan_compiler` refuses only when the provider config has no `director` role. The dev `fake` profile (CL-INT-5a) needs a plan template before a dev run can start | PRD 7.1, 10.4; plan.rules.md PG-04, PG-06, PG-10; BQ-72, BQ-90 | 2026-10-03 |
| BQ-111 | #59 security follow-up (BQ-97 item 4), left open by #178: P7's memory store is keyed by org and user, with scope and expiry (PRD 13.1). No store exists yet, so there is nothing to scope. | Until P7, nothing writes to the API's in-memory store (BQ-97). The P7 memory ticket builds the key (org, user), scope and expiry first, with a test that one user's entry never reaches another's lookup. | PRD 13.1, BQ-97 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-121 | FR-CHN-08 (PRD 11.6): is mail already in the inbox when Email Autopilot starts "new mail"? | No. The cursor starts at the history id `watch()` returns, so only later mail is triaged. A repeated start keeps the stored cursor. `services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_08.md`. | PRD 11.6 FR-CHN-08 ("New mail") | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-122 | FR-CHN-08: which labels are watched and triaged? | INBOX only (`labelIds=["INBOX"]`, `historyTypes=messageAdded`). The user's own sent mail, drafts and archived mail are not triaged. | PRD 11.2, 11.3; FR-CHN-08 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-123 | FR-CHN-08 (NFR-08): what is the watch renewal window, and how often do the sweeper's poll and renewal run? | `RENEW_BEFORE_S` = 1 day (Google recommends a daily re-watch). `POLL_INTERVAL_S` = 30 s through Cloud Tasks, because Cloud Scheduler's 1-minute floor would leave no margin under 60 s p95. `poll_due` polls a user whose history was last read at least `POLL_INTERVAL_S / 2` ago. | PRD 6.3, NFR-08; FR-CHN-08 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-124 | FR-CHN-08: how far does a resync after an expired history id (Gmail 404) go? | One page of the newest INBOX mail, at most `RESYNC_MAX_MESSAGES` (50 = gmail `batch_size`), oldest first, skipping mail already triaged (dedupe claims kept 365 days). Older unseen mail is not triaged. | PRD 6.6 (bounded work), config/connectors.yaml; FR-CHN-08 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-125 | FR-CHN-08 (SEC-08): how does the Pub/Sub push endpoint authenticate, and what does it answer? | An OIDC token from the push subscription's service account: signature and expiry by the verifier, then aud = the endpoint URL, iss = Google, email = the service account and `email_verified is True`, checked by the endpoint. 401/403 when unauthenticated, 400 when malformed, 2xx for an unknown address, 5xx when processing fails. | PRD 16.2, SEC-08; FR-CHN-08 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-126 | FR-CHN-08 (SEC-03): are `users.watch` and `users.stop` side effects that need the outbox? | No. They are read-scope calls on the user's own mailbox that send nothing outside Tilo: no outbox entry, no grant, no event. `GmailAdapter` gains `watch`, `stop_watch`, `list_history` and `recent_inbox` as reads (the FR-CON-04 public-surface test lists them). | PRD 10.4, SEC-03; FR-CHN-08 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-127 | FR-CHN-08 (NFR-22): what happens when triage itself fails for a message? | At-most-once, as the WhatsApp handler does: the message's dedupe claim is kept, the failure is logged by exception type, the rest of the batch is still triaged and the cursor moves on. A retry would need a claim release the `DedupeStore` does not have; FR-CHN-09 (tilo-api) owns retrying its own work once it has accepted a message. | PRD 11.3, NFR-22; FR-CHN-08 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-128 | FR-CHN-08: which event, if any, marks "mail triaged" or a watch failure? | None is emitted by FR-CHN-08. `mail_triaged` units are counted through `building.completed` (BQ-03) by FR-CHN-09. Anything emitted later must be a registered type. | PRD 9.5, 14.2, BQ-03; FR-CHN-08 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-129 | FR-CHN-08 (PRD 16.1): are Gmail addresses kept out of logs? | Yes. They are personal data, treated like full phone numbers in the channels logging rule: the watcher and endpoint log exception types only, never an address, token, body or exception text. | PRD 16.1, services/channels/CLAUDE.md; FR-CHN-08 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-130 | FR-CHN-09 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_09.md`): How are the five triage categories and the priority spelled? | `needs_reply`, `needs_action`, `fyi`, `newsletter_promo`, `suspicious`; priority `high`, `normal`, `low` (the E-MAIL eval ground truth). PRD 11.3's "score" is read as these three levels. | PRD 11.3, 19.2 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-131 | FR-CHN-09 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_09.md`): How is "≥ 85% agreement with the user's own labels after day 1" computed? | Over messages the user labelled (a correction, including one that confirms the category) and first triaged at least 24 h after the user's Autopilot start; agreement = the category first given equals the user's label. Category only. Unlabelled mail is not counted, so silence is never agreement. | PRD 11.6, 19.2, EV-13 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-132 | FR-CHN-09 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_09.md`): What does a triage correction change? | It becomes a per-user sender rule in P7 preferences, keyed on the bare From address (ASCII case-insensitive; an address with a control, zero-width or NFKC-unstable character gets no rule), that takes precedence over the model for later mail from that address; the latest correction wins. A rule, mute or VIP never downgrades a model `suspicious` (the From header is not authenticated), so the model is always asked; a VIP may still raise its priority. No domain-wide rules. | PRD 11.3, 11.4, 13.1, 16.3 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-133 | FR-CHN-09 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_09.md`): What does "don't ask me about these" do? | Later mail from that address is never `needs_reply`; it goes to the digest as `fyi`. A later relabel of that sender ends the mute. | PRD 11.3 (Learning, Delivery) | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-134 | FR-CHN-09 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_09.md`): How does the VIP list change priority? | A VIP address always gets `high`. Category is unchanged. | PRD 11.3 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-135 | FR-CHN-09 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_09.md`): What does triage do with mail the safety provider flags? | `suspicious` with `flagged` true and priority `high`, ahead of any model answer, user rule or label, so it is never auto-answered; the model is not called. | PRD 11.4, 16.2, 16.3 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-136 | FR-CHN-09 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_09.md`): What is the safe fallback for an unusable model answer? | `fyi` / `normal`, marked `source: fallback`: never `needs_reply` and never `newsletter_promo`. A model or safety provider error raises with nothing stored or emitted, so a retry can triage the mail. | PRD 16.3, 11.3 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-137 | FR-CHN-09 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_09.md`): Is a triage fallback a `mail_triaged` unit? | No: PRD 14.2 counts failed or unverified units as zero, so a fallback emits no `building.completed`. | PRD 14.2, BQ-03 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-138 | FR-CHN-09 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_09.md`): Which mail headers are screened by the safety provider? | From (with its display name), To, Cc, subject and the whole body, in one check before any model call. | PRD 11.4, 16.3 item 2 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-120 | #30 item 5 and 6, #163 (PRD 8.5, 10.4, FR-AGT-13, PI-12): how long does the outbox keep retrying a side effect, how far apart, and how does it end? `outbox.schema.json` has no field for a not-before time or an attempt count, and the registry has no event for an outbox entry that fails. | `RetryPolicy()`: at most 8 failed attempts, or a failure 24 h after the entry's first failure, then `failed`; between attempts 30 s doubling, capped at 1 h; a `RateLimited` waits its `retry_after_s` (capped at 1 h); an ambiguous `ProviderUnavailable` (lost answer, 5xx, timeout) waits at least 60 s so Gmail's search index can show an earlier send; missing consent or a missing or expired connection uses no attempt (the 24 h bound still applies); a claim waits 2 s past `scheduled_at` for a fast dispatcher clock, re-checked inside the store, which uses its own clock when it has one (Firestore: server time). Not-before, attempts and first failure are store-side metadata outside the contract entry. An entry that reaches a cap raises one `alert.raised` per entry, ever (`alert_id` = the outbox id, `card_id` null, part key `world_id`) through `Outbox(alert=...)`, delivered at least once; nothing new is registered. Only an unambiguous streak ends `failed`: once any attempt was ambiguous (`ProviderUnavailable`) the email may have gone, so the caps never fail it; after the alert it is retried every `max_delay_s`, each try re-running the earlier-attempt lookup, until it settles or a person abandons it (the alert cannot say "may have been sent": `alert.raised` has no such field). A `retry_after_s` that is not a number >= 0 falls back to the backoff; an error while recording a failed attempt releases the lease, is logged by type and never stops the batch. `RetryPolicy` refuses non-finite or negative values, `max_attempts` < 1 and `max_age_s` <= 0 (`ValueError`). `Outbox` without `retry=` keeps the immediate retries tests/API.md pins (no delays, no skew margin), still bounded; production wiring passes `RetryPolicy()` | PRD 8.5, 10.4, FR-AGT-13, PI-12; issues #30, #163 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-139 | FR-CHN-10 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_10.md`): FR-CHN-10 names "50 drafting cases" but the E-MAIL set has 20 drafting cases (31 to 50) and 30 triage cases. Which 50? | The 20 E-MAIL drafting cases plus 30 fixture cases in `services/channels/tests/fixtures/FR_CHN_10_drafting_cases.yaml`, run offline on scripted answers. Growing the eval set's drafting cases to 50 (or moving the fixture cases into `evals/`) is a separate issue. | PRD 11.6, 19.2 | 2026-10-03 |
| BQ-140 | FR-CHN-10 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_10.md`): How is "invented commitment" decided in CI, where the E-MAIL check `no_invented_commitments` is an autorater? | A deterministic checker (`find_invented`): numbers, dates and links must come from the instruction, the thread or the user's own signature; commitment words (free, discount, waive, refund, guarantee, promise, warranty, pay, no charge, ...) only from the instruction. Every text is first read the same way (NFKD, controls, invisible characters and combining marks dropped, Cyrillic and Greek look-alikes mapped to Latin, hyphen-split words joined); stroke letters are read as their base ("refunđ" reads "refund"); in model text a direction control, or a word holding a letter of any script but Latin, Cyrillic and Greek, mixing scripts, or holding another non-ASCII Latin letter (small capitals, IPA), is an `obfuscation` finding. This fails closed on non-English text ("Hawaiʻi", "Tanaka様" are findings), which is accepted for an English-only checker. Spelled numbers and ordinals are numbers, except "one", "first" and "second" (too common; a known gap: "at one percent", "on the first"), and a bare `name.tld/path` is a link. A commitment word is allowed only when the instruction mentions it and holds no negator anywhere (no, not, n't, never, without, decline, refuse, reject, skip, ignore, forgo, nope, "turn down", "rule out", "hold off", ...; "no extra charge" is a commitment, not a negator); a draft sentence with exactly one negator just before the word stays allowed when the instruction mentions it. Up to 5 model questions of at most 300 characters get the same number, date and link check and are never shown when they fail it. Paraphrased commitments with none of these markers stay with the E-MAIL autorater (PRD 19.3 "deterministic checks first"). | PRD 11.4 Content, 19.3 | 2026-10-03 |
| BQ-141 | FR-CHN-10 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_10.md`): What happens to a draft that invents something? | PRD 11.4 "unclear points are asked, not guessed": the draft is not offered; the user is asked (`needs_you` with the findings). At most one regeneration first (at most 2 model calls), and only a clean answer ships. | PRD 11.4, 6.6 | 2026-10-03 |
| BQ-142 | FR-CHN-10 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_10.md`): Does a commitment the other party asks for in the thread count as supported? | No. Only the instruction can add a commitment, so a thread asking for a discount does not let the draft offer one. Restating the other side's request with such a word (e.g. "you asked for a refund") is asked back to the user: a safe false positive. | PRD 11.4 Content | 2026-10-03 |
| BQ-143 | FR-CHN-10 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_10.md`): Does drafting save a Gmail draft (`create_draft`, T1)? | No. Drafting makes a Tilo artifact (`artifact.created`, PRD 11.6 Events) and nothing else: no outbox entry, no Gmail draft, no send, no preview message. The preview is FR-CHN-11 and the send FR-CHN-13; FR-CON-04 only says the Gmail adapter can make drafts. | PRD 11.2, 11.6, FR-CON-04, FR-CHN-11, FR-CHN-13 | 2026-10-03 |
| BQ-144 | FR-CHN-10 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_10.md`): Does a `Reply-To` header pick the recipient of "reply-to-sender"? | No. The reply goes to the From address of the latest message not sent by the user. A Reply-To outside the thread would be a new recipient, which is T3 (FR-CHN-12) and never added by drafting. | PRD 11.4 Threading and Formats, 16.2 | 2026-10-03 |
| BQ-145 | FR-CHN-10 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_10.md`): Which formats does FR-CHN-10 draft? | Reply (default) and reply-all. Forward and new email always add a recipient, so they belong to FR-CHN-12's T3 path. | PRD 11.4 Formats, FR-CHN-12 | 2026-10-03 |
| BQ-146 | FR-CHN-10 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_10.md`): How does the model return a draft? | JSON `{"body", "questions"}`; recipients, subject and headers are never read from it. Untrusted text is cut to fixed raw caps before any NFKC or regex (body 8000, subject and From 500 characters per message, last 50 messages; an over-long address or reply header is dropped); an answer over 20000 characters is unusable. The whole thread shares 30000 raw characters, newest message first; raw text past the caps is screened up to 100000 characters, and more marks the draft flagged. Reply-all keeps at most 20 recipients. | PRD 11.4, 16.3 items 1 and 3, EFF-03 | 2026-10-03 |
| BQ-147 | FR-CHN-10 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_10.md`): What does drafting do with a thread the safety provider flags? | It still drafts if asked (the user gave the instruction), but marks the result `flagged` and the draft `safety_flag`, so a send of it is T3 and it can never be auto-sent. | PRD 11.4 Injection, 16.2, `config/autonomy.yaml` | 2026-10-03 |
| BQ-148 | FR-CHN-10 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_10.md`): Is the user's signature a source of facts? | Yes, for facts (its phone number is not invented); never for commitments. | PRD 11.4 Inputs | 2026-10-03 |
| BQ-149 | FR-CHN-10 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_10.md`): Is a drafted reply a `reply_handled` unit? | No; a reply is handled when it is sent (FR-CHN-13). PRD 14.2 counts unverified units as zero. | PRD 14.2, BQ-03 | 2026-10-03 |
| BQ-150 | FR-CHN-10 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_10.md`): What is the idempotency key? | `(user_id, instruction_id)`, the id of the user's instruction (the `channel.message_in` it came in on WhatsApp). Redrafting after Edit is a new instruction. `artifact.created` is at-least-once (an emit that fails after the store is retried after a 60 s lease); consumers dedupe on `artifact_id`. | PRD 11.4 Preview, `contracts/outbox.rules.md` OB-01 `instruction_id` | 2026-10-03 |
| BQ-151 | FR-CHN-10 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_10.md`): How are user-added presets stored? | Not pinned; only the five PRD presets are. | PRD 11.4 Presets, FR-CHN-15 | 2026-10-03 |
| BQ-152 | FR-CHN-11 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_11.md`): Who decides L1 or L2 for an Email Autopilot send? | An injected autonomy-policy port (`level(user_id, "send_email")`), wired in production to the deterministic engine (`tilo_policy`, which gates every L2 send until ADR-0013, BQ-57). The autopilot itself never schedules a T3 send, whatever the port answers. | PRD 10.4, FR-HIL-04, FR-HIL-05 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-153 | FR-CHN-11 (issue #243): How is "money, contract or legal terms" detected before any L2 auto-send? | A deterministic `t3_content_signal(text)` on the draft body, independent of the drafting checker's `ready`: money (amounts with a currency, refund, invoice, payment, wire, bank, IBAN), legal (contract, liability, lawsuit, NDA, terms, waiver) and commitment (we will, guarantee, promise, commit, deadline) word families, after NFKC and removing format characters. It fails closed (signal set) on format characters or non-ASCII letters after NFKC, so an accented name also makes the draft T3: a safe false positive. Any signal adds the `money_or_legal` modifier. Only the body is checked; the subject comes from the thread. | PRD 10.4, 11.4, issue #243 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-154 | FR-CHN-11: Which recipients count as known (not a new recipient)? | The user's known contacts, the From of every message in the thread, and the To and Cc of the user's own messages in it. To and Cc of inbound mail and any Reply-To are set by the sender, so an address found only there is new (`new_recipients`, T3): a reply-all to an inbound Cc needs a person unless that address is a contact. A message is the user's own only when it is From the mailbox and carries Gmail's `SENT` label (`labelIds`), since a From header alone is set by the sender; without that label the own-message rule adds nothing (fail closed). | PRD 10.4 T2/T3, 11.4 Formats, FR-CHN-12 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-155 | FR-CHN-11: What does Edit do during the undo window, and after the send? | Edit cancels the pending entry first, then redrafts through the FR-CHN-10 drafter with the original instruction plus the change (so the checker runs again). The redraft is a new proposal and waits for a person's Send (L1), also under an L2 policy (OB-01: a typed answer on a card is never an instruction; OB-05: a redraft is a new proposal with a new gate). If the entry can no longer be cancelled, Edit returns nothing and changes nothing; a correction is a new gated action. | PRD 11.4 Preview and Undo, 13 (compensating action), `contracts/outbox.rules.md` OB-01, OB-05 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-156 | FR-CHN-11: Does an L1 Send (a person tapped it) get an undo window? | No: it is queued with `undo_window_s` 0 (the PRD's undo window is the L2 rule). Cancel after a person's Send reports `too_late`, never `cancelled`. | PRD 10.4, 11.4 Undo, OB-02 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-157 | FR-CHN-11: How are control characters in a draft and in findings handled? | Format (Cf) characters and control (Cc) characters other than newline and tab are removed from the body that is previewed and sent and from every finding line (`kind: text`), which is plain text with no link or button. If cleaning changes the body, the stored artifact version that is sent must match the cleaned body (the tests pin `item.artifact_id` only). | PRD 11.4 Preview and Injection, 16.3 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-158 | FR-CHN-11: How does tilo-channels reach the outbox without importing `tilo_connectors`? | Through an injected port with the outbox's own method names (`enqueue(entry, evidence)`, `cancel(outbox_id)`); entries must be valid against `contracts/outbox.schema.json` and OB-02. The tests use a fake; the production wiring and the `evidence` that `tilo_policy.check_entry` reads are not pinned here. | SEC-03, `services/connectors/CLAUDE.md`, `contracts/outbox.rules.md` | 2026-10-04 |

Provenance lines with CJK or emoji will show boxes or missing glyphs. Which font do we embed? | Keep the built-in Helvetica, no embedding, until a font story is agreed; the PRD names no font. Missing glyphs are a known limit, not a defect. | FR-CON-10, PRD 12.4, issue #202 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-181 | Issue #202 (FR-CON-10; PRD 13.3): what does the appendix print for a manifest with no claims? PRD 13.3 says "unverified" and gives no wording for this case (checked against 13.3 on 2026-10-04). | `Verifier: unverified (no claims)`, so a manifest with no claims is told apart from one with some claims unverified (`Verifier: unverified (0/2 claims verified)`). | FR-CON-10, PRD 13.3, issue #202 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-182 | FR-CHN-13 (`services/channels/tests/API_FR_CHN_13.md`): Where does the Gmail adapter get the text of an Autopilot reply? Its `ArtifactSource` (P7's store) does not exist for drafts, and the drafted artifact version holds the raw body, not the cleaned one the person saw (BQ-157). | tilo-channels serves it: `tilo_channels.autopilot_send.DraftArtifacts(cards=...)`, over the same card store the `Autopilot` writes, `get(artifact_id, version)` returns `{"kind": "email", "subject", "body_text"}` equal to the card's `Preview.subject` and `Preview.body` (cleaned, CR and CRLF as line breaks), from any instance. An unknown artifact or version raises `KeyError`, which the adapter turns into a refused, failed entry. tilo-channels still never imports tilo_connectors. | PRD 11.4 Preview, OB-05, BQ-157 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-183 | FR-CHN-13: Which safety flag counts when the `DraftResult` passed to `Autopilot.preview` and the stored draft disagree? | Either one. The draft FR-CHN-10 stored for the instruction (`draft.safety_flag` or `flagged`) is read, so a result that looks clean for a stored, flagged draft still gives a T3 card that waits for a person. A result with no stored draft keeps FR-CHN-11's behaviour (its own flags). | PRD 10.4 T3, 11.4 Injection, BQ-147 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-184 | FR-CHN-13: The send path runs the real tilo_connectors `Outbox`, but the OB-01 evidence the autopilot passes to `enqueue` (BQ-158) is not built. How do the FR-CHN-13 tests get past `tilo_policy.check_entry`? | They replace `check_entry` with an accept-all while they run (`_chn13_lib.outbox_skips_ob01_evidence`). Schema (OB-04), fresh-state (OB-02) and idempotency-key (OB-03) checks still run. Wiring real OB-01 evidence (approval or `policy.applied` events) for Autopilot sends stays with ADR-0013 and the BQ-158 follow-up, not FR-CHN-13. | SEC-03, OB-01, ADR-0013, BQ-158 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-185 | FR-CHN-13: Which message does the sent reply answer (In-Reply-To, References)? The drafter replies to the last message not from the user; the Gmail adapter (FR-CON-04) builds the headers from the thread's last non-draft message. | The message the card answers, never the thread at send time (revised 2026-10-04 after the PR #265 security review). The card stores the draft's `in_reply_to` and `references` (the drafter's, from the message it replied to); `DraftArtifacts` serves them with the card subject, and for an item that carries `in_reply_to` the Gmail adapter sends exactly that Subject, In-Reply-To and References (References bounded to the last 20 ids) with the thread id. A message that arrives after the preview, a thread whose last message is the user's own, or format characters in the thread subject change nothing; an `in_reply_to` that is not exactly one `<id>` (the replied message had no usable Message-ID) refuses the send (entry failed). Items without `in_reply_to` keep FR-CON-04's headers from the thread's last message. | PRD 11.4 Threading, FR-CON-04 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-224 | CHN-GATE-C (issue #256, "T3 signal coverage"; `services/channels/tests/test_FR_CHN_11_CHN_GATE_C_t3_signal_coverage.py`): the money / legal / commitment word lists miss plain-ASCII paraphrases ("We agree.", "Consider it done."), Latin-script languages written in plain ASCII, newer money words and visibly split or leetspeak words, so such a draft rides the L2 undo window. What decides L2 auto-send? (The user delegated the decision.) | (a) L2 auto-send is an allowlist: a short body (at most 2,000 characters) made only of ordinary scheduling talk (greeting, sign-off, names, weekdays, thanks, "works for us", "see you then"), no digits other than a time of day (2pm, 3:30pm, 10am), no commitment, money or legal word, no link. Anything else with a reply is T3, modifier `money_or_legal` (the only content condition in `config/autonomy.yaml`), never scheduled. Spanish, German without umlauts, Indonesian / Malay, Tagalog, Swahili and romanised Hindi in plain ASCII are T3 by this rule, not by word lists. (b) Word lists add transfer, account number, sort code, paypal / venmo / zelle / btc, owe, loan, penalty, agree / accept / authorise / sign, solicitor, gdpr, bucks / grand / quid / Rs / RM, "5k" and bare amounts; plain-ASCII paraphrases of a promise are T3. (c) Visible split or leetspeak words (re-fund, r.e.f.u.n.d, r3fund, c0ntract, re CR fund) are read after dropping `- . _ *` separators and mapping digit look-alikes (0 o, 1 i, 3 e, 4 a, 5 s, 7 t, @ a) and are T3. Conflicts with existing tests, resolved in their favour: the issue says "no digits", but the existing benign bodies carry "2pm", so a time of day is allowed; the existing preview test pins a 1,560-character benign body at tier T2, so "short" is 2,000 characters, not less. Names (David, Sarah) stay allowed as in every existing benign body. Security rounds 1 and 2 (PR #277): a name is allowed only as the greeting's run (at most two Title-case words after the opening "Hi" / "Thanks" / "Good morning"), the signature's run (the last line, right after the last sign-off-only line) or a repeat of their words; a time of day ends a run; "do", "have", "back" and similar are off the list and "on it", "on us", "for free", "will do", "yours" are commitments. Accepted safe false positives (a person approves): "Yours sincerely" (commitment), "10 a.m." and "e.g." (read as links), accented names and any name outside the two runs. Accepted open gaps: a one- or two-word run can still hold non-names ("Hi Fifty Thousand," with "Best,\nUpfront Approved"), closable by accepting only thread participants' names; and a body cannot see what it agrees to ("Yes, confirmed." answering a money question rides L2), which needs an inbound-aware rule (separate ticket). | PRD 10.4 T3, 11.4, FR-HIL-04, issue #256, BQ-153 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-186 | CHN-GATE-A (issue #256 gate 1; `services/channels/tests/API_CHN_GATE_A.md`): with a read port a stop no longer creates an entry, but what then stops an enqueue that is already in flight (it passed its `#stop` check before the stop was written) from storing a live entry after the stop read "not found" and answered, if that enqueuing instance dies before its own re-check? A read port alone cannot, and FR-CHN-11's round-3 test already requires "cancelled" in exactly that race. | The outbox port gets a revocation besides the read port (`get`, `get_by_idempotency_key`): `revoke(world_id, idempotency_key)` cancels a scheduled entry under the key, or records the key so the outbox refuses to create it from then on (the issue's "check_entry must refuse creation after a revocation", kept in the outbox, not the policy engine). It never creates an entry, and keys and revocations are never pruned. A Cancel or Edit (and the sweeper) never calls `enqueue`; it answers "cancelled" only after the outbox revoked the key or showed the entry cancelled, "too_late" only when the outbox showed it sending, sent or failed, else `CancelPending`. With a healthy outbox a Cancel of a never-enqueued card is "cancelled" with 0 entries. A revocation recorded through the policy engine instead (an event `check_entry` reads) would need a contract change and is not taken. | PRD 11.4 Undo, 11.6, OB-01, OB-03, BQ-170, issue #256 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-187 | CHN-GATE-A (issue #256 gate 2): which stops does the sweeper settle, how does it find them, and how often does it run? | Every recorded stop not yet confirmed. An injected `StopQueue` port (`add`, `pending`, `remove`; Firestore in production) holds them: a stop is added before it is recorded and removed once settled, so an instance dying anywhere leaves either no recorded stop or a queued one. `Autopilot.sweep()` settles each through the outbox port ("cancelled", "too_late", or "pending" for the next run; one card's failure is never raised and never stops the others), never enqueues and never touches a card without a stop. `SWEEP_INTERVAL_S` = 10 (at most a quarter of the 60 s window, so a stop gets several tries before `scheduled_at`). `stops` is optional on `Autopilot` only so the FR-CHN-11 benches keep working; production wiring passes one. | PRD 10.4 L2, 11.4 Undo, 11.6, BQ-170, issue #256 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-188 | CHN-GATE-A (issue #256 gate 2): how do surfaces show "not confirmed, retry" for `CancelPending`? | `Autopilot.stop_state(user_id, card_id)` returns None (no stop), "pending" (recorded, not confirmed: shown as "Not confirmed, retry", and Cancel is the retry), "cancelled" or "too_late". The tapping caller still gets `CancelPending` (BQ-170 unchanged); the `Preview` and `Outcome` shapes are unchanged. | PRD 11.4 Undo, BQ-170, issue #256 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-WIRE1-1 | WIRE-1: Park pages with no tilo-api endpoint. Which Park `Api` methods have no tilo-api route today? Reads: `getAgenda` (calendar events are not served; `/schedules` is the recurring world schedules, a different thing), `getWorlds` (no list route, only `GET /worlds/{id}`), `getDecisions` (cards are per world: `GET /worlds/{id}/cards`), `getPlans` (`/tasks/{id}/approaches` is per task), `getSkills` (`/worlds/{id}/skill-proposals` is per world), `getUser`, `getToday`, `getLearn`, `getTeam`, `getConnectors`, `getNotifications`, `getTeamPolicy`, `getConnectorTerms`, `getProfileSettings` (no route at all). Writes: `decide`, `undo`, `startWorld`, `saveSkill`, `setToolLevel`, `bookSlot`, `addBackup`, `setPref` (the card, intake and skill-proposal routes are per world or per task and do not take the Park keys). Only `getLedger` has a route (`GET /costs`, `cost_report`). | PRD default: they stay `NotBuilt` and the page shows "Not connected yet"; no endpoint is invented and `contracts/` is not touched. A page gets live data only when a ticket adds its route. `getLedger` maps only what `/costs` carries (`spent` from `totals.usd`; `by.tool` from `by_profile`; `by.role` from `by_task_type`; `by.world` stays empty, since the report has no per-world group); budget, forecast, daily, tx, runs, waits, memory, hours and activity are empty or zero, never fixture values. Amounts are US dollars as served, so the live Ledger carries `currency: "USD"` and shows dollars; the fixture (no currency) keeps pounds. | PRD 9.4 HR-1..HR-7, `services/api/tests/API_FR_MTR_10.md`, `apps/web/src/park/api/index.ts` | 2026-10-07 |

Which route serves it? tilo-api has `/schedules` (recurring world schedules, a different thing) and `/v1/plate` (work items, no Park method reads it), but no calendar route. | Not wired: `getAgenda` stays `NotBuilt` and the live page shows "Not connected yet". | BQ-WIRE1-3 | Park wiring: the Worlds page and Home (`getWorlds`) need a list of the signed-in person's worlds with their state. tilo-api has only `GET /worlds/{id}` (and per-world layout, events, costs). What lists them? | Not wired: `getWorlds` stays `NotBuilt`. The Worlds list is S-14 (Must, P3, "Worlds list and replays"), so a list route (`GET /worlds`, scoped to the caller, with each world's status and last `seq`) needs a ticket on the API lane. It is not invented here, and `contracts/` is untouched. Replays (FR-WLD-08) come from the per-world events route once the list exists. | S-14, FR-WLD-08, PRD 9.4 HR-1..HR-7 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-WIRE1-4 | Park wiring: the Decisions page and the shell's needs-you count (`getDecisions`, plus `decide` and `undo`) need the caller's open cards across all worlds. tilo-api serves cards per world only (`GET /worlds/{id}/cards`, `POST .../cards/{card_id}/answer`). What feeds the cross-world queue? | Not wired: they stay `NotBuilt`. The needs-you queue is S-06 (Must, P4), with FR-HIL-01 (card structure) and FR-HIL-11 (an answer on one channel closes the card elsewhere in 2 s or less).
