| CON-D1 | PRD silent: how long before expiry is an access token refreshed, and how long does an OAuth state live? | Refresh when 60 s or less is left; the OAuth state lives 600 s and is single-use, spent by any use (another user, a denied consent, an error). | PRD 12.1, 16.1, 16.5; FR-CON-02, FR-CON-03 (PR #123) | 2026-10-02 |
| CON-D2 | PRD silent: a denied consent. | The connection waits (PRD 10.2); nothing is stored and the state is spent. | PRD 12.1, 16.1, 16.5; FR-CON-02, FR-CON-03 (PR #123) | 2026-10-02 |
| CON-D3 | PRD silent: what a failed refresh means. | Only `invalid_grant` expires the connection (and emits `connection.expired` once). A 5xx or network error raises `TokenRefreshFailed`, keeps the state `connected` and emits nothing. A connection with no refresh token whose access token ran out also expires. An expired record keeps no tokens. | PRD 12.1, 16.1, 16.5; FR-CON-02, FR-CON-03 (PR #123) | 2026-10-02 |
| CON-D4 | PRD 12.1 and 16.5: `delete_user` must leave no token in any store, but a revoke can fail. What happens then? | Decided by the orchestrator (PRD wins): `delete_user` tries each revoke; one that still fails drops its sealed token anyway and raises `alert.raised`. A plain `disconnect` that fails keeps the token sealed in a retry store keyed by alert id (never overwritten), retried by `retry_pending_revokes`, dropped after 7 days (`retry_retention_s`) with a warning. Trade-off: a live Google grant can outlive its user; the person can still remove it in their Google account, and the alert tells Tilo's operators. | PRD 12.1, 16.1, 16.5; FR-CON-02, FR-CON-03 (PR #123) | 2026-10-02 |
| CON-D5 | Connector declarations give a description per action, not per scope. What is a consent line? | The description of the declared action(s) of the requested capability that need the scope, one distinct line per text (scopes sharing a line fold into it). A needed scope that no requested action describes is refused (`ConsentRefused`) before an event is emitted or a flow minted; a scope is never shown without words. | PRD 12.1, 16.1, 16.5; FR-CON-02, FR-CON-03 (PR #123) | 2026-10-02 |
| CON-D6 | A callback response grants none of the requested scopes, or its tokens cannot be stored. What happens to the tokens? | Nothing is stored and no `connection.consented` is emitted (it is emitted only after the record is stored, just before `connection.established`). The issued tokens are revoked at Google unless the user has any connected connector (CON-D10: the grant is shared, so revoking would end it). | PRD 12.1, 16.1, 16.5; FR-CON-02, FR-CON-03 (PR #123) | 2026-10-02 |
| CON-D7 | Where does the redirect base URL come from, and what may it be? | `GOOGLE_OAUTH_REDIRECT_BASE_URL`; default `http://localhost:8080`. https is required unless the host is localhost, 127.0.0.1 or ::1. | PRD 12.1, 16.1, 16.5; FR-CON-02, FR-CON-03 (PR #123) | 2026-10-02 |
| CON-D8 | `alert` is meant to be required, but the pinned FR-CON-02 tests build the broker without it. | Optional for now: without a sink a failed revoke is logged at ERROR. The HTTP wiring ticket must pass it; make it required when the pinned tests allow. | PRD 12.1, 16.1, 16.5; FR-CON-02, FR-CON-03 (PR #123) | 2026-10-02 |
| CON-D9 | The consent state table and the revoke-retry store are in memory; the broker trusts the caller's `user_id` and does not bind the connection to a Google account. | Single `tilo-connectors` instance until the Firestore-backed stores land (CAS writes, shared state). Follow-ups, not built: bind `user_id` to the signed-in principal (HTTP ticket), bind the refresh token to the account subject, a factory guard for `FakeKms` outside dev and CRC32C checks in `CloudKms`. | PRD 12.1, 16.1, 16.5; FR-CON-02, FR-CON-03 (PR #123) | 2026-10-02 |
| CON-D10 | Every Google connector uses one OAuth client and `include_granted_scopes=true`, so Google keeps one grant per account and client. What may Tilo revoke? | Tilo never revokes while the user has another connector in state "connected", whether or not the KMS can read its record now (a KMS outage must not make a connected Gmail look absent). A discarded callback token (no scope granted, or the record could not be stored) is not revoked then; `disconnect` of one connector deletes it locally WITHOUT a revoke at Google and the last connector's disconnect revokes the grant (`DisconnectResult.revoked_at_provider` is False for the skipped ones). `delete_user` still revokes every token (the pinned FR-CON-02 test), so a repeat revoke at Google can answer 400 and raise an alert. Trade-off: the dropped connector's tokens stay valid at Google until the last one is disconnected; Tilo cannot use them. `retry_pending_revokes` follows the same rule: while the user has a connected connector it revokes nothing and KEEPS the pending entry until its retention (7 days) ends, then drops it unrevoked as before; the last disconnect ends the grant anyway and a failure of that one makes its own entry. `delete_user` revokes regardless (it runs after the records are gone). | PRD 12.1, 16.1, 16.5; FR-CON-02, FR-CON-03 (PR #123) | 2026-10-02 |
| CON-D11 | A failure after the record is stored (the `connection.consented` hook or the `connection.established` emit raises). | `store_tokens` puts the previous record back (or deletes it if there was none) and re-raises, so nothing is connected without its events and `authorization_url` offers consent again; the consent service then discards the tokens under CON-D10. A `connection.consented` already emitted is not withdrawn (events are append-only). | PRD 12.1, 16.1, 16.5; FR-CON-02, FR-CON-03 (PR #123) | 2026-10-02 |
| CON-D12 | A KMS decrypt failure in `disconnect` used to lose the token to revoke; the redirect base accepted odd URLs. | The unreadable record's ciphertext moves unchanged into the retry store (`sealed_as: token`), is retried when the KMS answers again, kept until the 7 day retention (measured from the retry id's own time, since the copied record's date is unbound) and then dropped with a warning. The redirect base needs a host and refuses userinfo, query, fragment and whitespace. | PRD 12.1, 16.1, 16.5; FR-CON-02, FR-CON-03 (PR #123) | 2026-10-02 |
| CON-D13 | The check-then-revoke in `disconnect`, the window before `connection.consented` is recorded, a failed rollback and a future-dated retry key. | A per-user in-memory lock covers the live-grant check plus the delete or revoke in `disconnect` and the whole of `retry_pending_revokes` (two concurrent disconnects cannot both skip the revoke; CAS replaces it on Firestore, CON-D9). Until the `connection.established` emit returns, that connector's adapters and disconnects wait (at most 10 s), so no adapter gets the token before `connection.consented` is recorded (CON-D14). If the rollback write fails, the original error is logged (its type only) and the record is overwritten with an `expired` marker so it is not usable; the original error surfaces. A retry key more than 300 s in the future is malformed and dropped; a smaller skew counts as age 0. | PRD 12.1, 16.1, 16.5; FR-CON-02, FR-CON-03 (PR #123) | 2026-10-02 |
| CON-D14 | Locks around callouts, and revokes deferred past a sibling (issue #149, #163). | `store_tokens` takes the user lock, then the connector lock (as `disconnect` does), and a discarded consent token is checked and revoked under the user lock (`TokenBroker.discard_tokens`), so a retry, discard or disconnect cannot revoke the grant between a store's check and its put. No lock is held while the consented hook, an `emit` or the `alert` sink runs: the record is put under the locks, then marked as saving; a call back in from that thread is refused with `TokenError` rather than hanging, other threads wait up to 10 s. A rollback whose `expired` marker lands emits `connection.expired`. A disconnect that skips the revoke only because every live sibling record is unreadable keeps its own readable token as a pending revoke (with an alert). The last connector's disconnect retries the user's other pending revokes (or drops expired ones). A KMS or store failure while saving a refreshed token raises `TokenRefreshFailed`. Open: multi-account sub binding (CON-D9), a scheduled retention sweep, bounded lock maps. | PRD 12.1, 16.1, 16.5; FR-CON-02, FR-CON-03 | 2026-10-02 |
| FR-HIL-07 | Decided by CL-TRUST on the PRD defaults (overridable): the kill-switch dispatcher keeps one append in flight per world; an attempt unsettled after `dispatch.attempt_abandon_after_s` (config/dispatch.yaml, 3 s) is presumed lost and the same event id is resent (idempotent, EV-10), with at most two presumed-lost attempts per world before no new thread starts; attempts run on daemon threads, one per attempt, so a hung writer never blocks exit or another world. After a failed append the dispatcher calls the writer's optional `reopen()` (only from the in-flight attempt, only after `WriterBusy`) before the resend (issue #55 item 2); the concrete harness writer adapter is a later ticket. FR-HIL-07 itself needs no more than 5 s, so the level change never waits on I/O. | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-84 | FR-CON-07 (PRD 12.3, 16.2, SEC-09): the PRD says "allowlist" but not how a host matches. | Exact host, or a `*.parent` rule for any depth below the parent but not the parent itself; ports are not part of the allowlist; IP literals (incl. odd encodings) are never allowed and are a config error as an entry; any userinfo, backslash, whitespace or control character blocks; hosts compare in IDNA A-label form; https only unless `allow_schemes` says otherwise; a query carrying a secret or PII (what `mask_text` masks) is blocked as `sensitive_query` (PRD 16.2 "no data in query strings"). | PRD 12.3, 16.2; `services/browser/tests/test_FR_CON_07_egress_policy.py` | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-85 | FR-CON-07 (PRD 12.3, 16.1 rule 4): what the worker masks, and what a blocked request does. | Mask sk-style keys, GitHub tokens, AWS key ids, JWTs, Google OAuth tokens, `Bearer` tokens, the value of password/secret/token/api_key/otp pairs, e-mail, `+` phone numbers, and Luhn-valid 13 to 19 digit card numbers (a failing digit run stays). A blocked top-level navigation or redirect hop raises `EgressBlocked` and a blocked subresource is dropped; both are logged in `EgressPolicy.blocked`. There is no event type for a blocked egress request, so the Browser Station cannot animate one yet (contract gap, not added here). | PRD 12.3, 16.1; `test_FR_CON_07_masking.py` | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-86 | FR-CON-08 (PRD 12.3, 16.1 rule 1, EV-04): how a login wall raises a card and what is refused. | At most one needs-you card per host per run, options `connect_api` / `upload_export` / `skip`, safe default `skip`, `add_context` false, host name only on the card (no path, query or page text). Typing is refused on every field of a login wall and on secure fields (password, one-time code, payment) on any page. A blocked SSO hop returns `login_required` instead of raising. | PRD 12.3, 16.1; `test_FR_CON_08_login_card_and_worker.py` | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-87 | FR-CON-07 / FR-CON-08 security round 1 (PRD 16.1 rules 1 and 4, 16.2): how strict are typing, secure-field handling and URL logging? | Secure fields (password, one-time code, `cc-*`) are keyed on the accessibility node, not its ref; a secure node with no ref makes `screenshot()` raise `ScreenshotRefused` and its value is dropped from the tree. `type()` re-reads the tree and page text, refuses (and raises the once-per-host card) if the page is now a login wall, and refuses any ref that is not an editable input node in that fresh tree. A tree nested deeper than 256 levels raises `TreeTooDeep` (fail closed). Every URL that enters a Decision, an error or a return value has userinfo removed in every form before truncation and its host and path masked, raw and percent-decoded (a percent-encoded secret replaces the whole path with `[masked:secret]`). `MAX_URL_LENGTH = 8192`: a longer URL is `invalid_url`, so the gate fails closed for subresources with long query strings. | PRD 12.3, 16.1, 16.2; security-reviewer round 1 on PR #140 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-88 | FR-CON-07 security round 1: what bounds keep the masker linear on hostile page content, and how are hosts compared? | A card window is made of digit groups of 2+ digits (a lone digit is a separator) and never grows past 19 digits; the key-value pattern matches the keyword directly (no prefix scan), the e-mail local part is at most 64 characters, domain labels at most 63; JWTs must start at a token boundary. Hosts normalise with UTS 46 non-transitional processing (`idna` package, as browsers do), so `glaßes.com` is not `glasses.com`; an invalid joiner context is `invalid_url`. | PRD 12.3, 16.2; security-reviewer round 1 on PR #140 | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-57 | Issue #28 asked how an L2 instruction records the action asked for (#21 follow-up, PI-21). Decided by the architect on the PRD default (user delegation 2026-10-02). PRD 10.4 says L2 acts only when the owner's instruction "explicitly asks for the action", and code does not read the words. So an instruction that records no action type opens no send. Until ADR-0013 lands, every L2-covered send waits at a gate, as at L1, the PRD's default level. This replaces PI-21's interim history rule in the engine (P0-03b, `packages/policy` `coverage.instruction_counts`, builder follow-up). Phase A loses nothing, because the CP2 flow approves through cards (ADR-0008). FR-HIL-05 cannot pass until ADR-0013 lands. ADR-0013 (Proposed, awaiting user approval) records the action without a model: a `needs_you` card names the `action_type` its question asks about (PRD 11.2 "What should I say?"), a typed WhatsApp answer copies it from the card, and a web request takes it from a fixed control. OB-01 then requires it to equal the policy's action type. | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-96 | Issue #68 (FR-INT-06 to FR-INT-08 defaults). Decided by the architect on the PRD defaults. (1) Before planning, the Director's approach ranges are shown, checked for order only (FR-INT-06, PG-09). Once the chosen approach compiles to a PlanGraph, every estimate shown or re-estimated is `estimate_units` over its units (FR-INT-07, FR-INT-10). The model's numbers are never shown after planning. A unit type with no history withholds the estimate instead of falling back to them (the PRD is silent here; default chosen by the architect; revisit once the seed is filled). (2) The seed stands: `unit_cost_seed.json`, `seed: true`, empty at launch, unknown unit types refused, filled from the first eval pass (PRD 7.3), which has not run yet. (3) At plan approval `granted` equals the cap (FR-INT-08). A top-up (FR-MTR-03, PRD 14.1) records the top-up as `granted` and the new cap as `cap`, built with FR-MTR-03. (4) Superseded by BQ-75 and ADR-0002 decision 14. A grant writes no `policy.applied`; it is one `budget.granted` with the policy's `authz`, the policy id as actor, and connector `internal`. Only L3 runs a grant; L2 waits, because a grant carries no instruction (PRD 10.4). (5) Stands: grants count against the policy's `per_day` and `credits` like sends (BQ-14, BQ-52). (6) Done: `tilo-api` passes `tilo_policy.load_risk_table()` (`services/api/src/tilo_api/tasks.py`), and `config/autonomy.yaml` lists `grant_budget: T1`. | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-97 | Issue #59 (FR-INT-03 to FR-INT-05 defaults and review follow-ups). Decided by the architect on the PRD defaults. (1) The fixed per-field weights stand as the deterministic reading of "ranked by expected change to the plan" (FR-INT-04); the PRD gives no method, so this is a default chosen by the architect; revisit with eval data. (2) Stands, with one rule added: the Director flags every field the task text does not state, so a guess is always flagged and FR-INT-05's similar task reaches memory. A preference never overrides the person's own words. Injected text that suppresses a flag costs only a convenience: preferences authorise nothing, and every chip stays editable. (3) Stands: over the cap of 3, the guess is kept as an assumption with `question: null` (FR-INT-04). (4) Stands: fixed English wording; the first suggestion is the guess and is what is assumed on skip. (5) Stands, including asking instead of applying a value that would make the brief invalid. (6) Stands: every applied preference or past answer emits `memory.hit` (PRD 13.1). `origin_cost` is the recorded cost, never an estimate (14.3); a lookup dearer than its origin is refused, so `saved` is never negative and EV-09 holds. (7) Stands: one repair on the same profile, then the fallback (PL-03; 6.6 cascades), within the run budget. (8) Done: `tilo-api` adds each readable intake file as a `file` source by artifact id (`create_task`). Code-review follow-ups (the PRD is silent; default chosen by the architect; revisit): skipping writes the assumed value into the field, because the assumption must be the value used; assumptions use "Label: value" in both cases; the chip test covers all seven chips; the TASK delimiter is neutralised whatever its case and spacing. Security follow-ups: items 3 (screening, BQ-83) and 6 (the Director agent as actor) are done. (1) The repair prompt puts the quoted error in its own delimited block (PRD 16.3 item 1). (2) The `director` role gets `max_input_tokens` (EFF-03). (4) P7's store is keyed by org and user, with scope and expiry (PRD 13.1); until P7, nothing writes to the API's in-memory store. (5) Stands: P4 never lowers a tier from the brief (BQ-72), and every source ref is checked for ownership and against the egress allowlist before use. | 2026-10-02 |
| BQ-98 | Issue #48 (FR-INT-01 and FR-INT-02 intake defaults). Decided by the architect on the PRD defaults. (1) The PRD's "50 MB" is read as written, in SI units: 50,000,000 bytes, the stricter reading, a one-constant change to `MAX_TOTAL_BYTES` (builder follow-up). Text is 1 to 4,000 characters and required even with files attached (FR-INT-01). (2) Stands as the interim text extraction. Hidden runs are included so that hidden instructions are screened (FR-INT-02). Office-to-PDF in `tilo-render` stays PRD 7.3's plan. (3) Yes, and done: the typed task text and the file names are screened before anything is stored (PRD 16.3 item 2, SEC-04; `screen_task_text`, BQ-83). (4) Stands: a flag is an `fyi` Trace row from `safety_provider`, and `archive_ref` is filled once P7's raw archive exists. (5) Stands (FR-INT-01: each file shows its extraction status). (6) Superseded: the API passes the signed-in principal, and `u_local` is the package default for local callers only. Events stay in the API store until the harness hand-off (CL-INT-2). (7) Stands. | 2026-10-02 |
| CL-INT-3 | Web HTTP client (`apps/web/src/api/http.ts`, PR #214): the API gaps found while wiring it, decided on the defaults with nothing invented in the web. (1) No route reads the world's layout, so `getLayout` rejects (non-retryable) and live world rendering (FR-WLD-05) waits for one (needs API route). (2) `GET /worlds/{id}/events` has no cursor parameter; the client polls the whole list, keeps a seq cursor and dedupes by id. The owner view must return `v`, `world_id`, `run_id` and `item` (CL-INT-4); the client fills only `v` and `world_id` (the request's own world), never `run_id` or `item`. A row without `run_id` or the `item` key is withheld, polling stops and an out-of-date-server error is reported through the optional `onError` of `subscribeEvents` (not on the `TiloClient` interface yet, needs the orchestrator's OK, nor shown by any screen yet). (3) `TiloClient` has no `pauseWorld` and `answerCard` has no world id (`HttpClient` needs the card to come from `listCards`); the cards route takes `{option}` only, so `context` is not sent. (4) Production wiring of the principal, store and event writer, and CORS, are missing, so a browser cannot yet reach `just dev-api` (CL-INT-4). | 2026-10-03 |
| CL-INT-4 | Scoped layout view (`GET /worlds/{id}/layout`, security round 1 on #221). The layout carries no tie from a worker agent to a unit or district, and no hidden-count-free way to list workers, so what may a non-owner see of the agents? Also: which count hints remain? | A non-owner gets an allowlisted view: only their districts, buildings, gates and islands, and the Director, Narrator and the Leads of their districts; no worker and no `team_lead` is listed; geometry is null; nothing visible, or a malformed stored layout, is a 404. Events for a visible district still name worker actors that the scoped layout does not list (EV-12): follow-up, needs a contract change that ties an agent to a district or unit (needs contract change: `layout.schema.json` agent gets an optional `unit`). **Follow-up, same class as #29 / PR #54 item (d):** `tier` (an agent-count bucket of the whole world) and the numbered ids (`A_leadN`, `BN`, `IN`, `GN`, `UN`, `LN`, "Connector N") still give a scoped viewer lower bounds on hidden counts; fix by renumbering per view or opaque ids. | PRD 9.2, 10.1, SEC-07, FR-WLD-05 | 2026-10-03 |
| BQ-159 | FR-CHN-14 (`services/channels/tests/test_FR_CHN_14_draft_latency.py`): EV-15 measures "WhatsApp instruction received -> draft shown" (p95 <= 15 s), but this ticket covers only the drafting leg, and a real model cannot run in CI (EFF-01). What is measured and with what provider times? | The whole 15 s is given to the drafting leg (read, safety checks, model calls, checker); the webhook and preview legs (FR-CHN-02, FR-CHN-11) get their own tests. Fake clock with seeded provider delays: thread read 0.3-0.8 s, safety check 0.2-0.5 s, model call 2.0-4.5 s, plus the drafter's own CPU (`process_time`). Cases: the 50 FR-CHN-10 drafting cases, a third with an invented first answer (2 model calls). The real-model p95 is measured in the pilot (EV-15). | PRD 11.6 FR-CHN-14, 3 EV-15, 17.2 NFR-09, 19.2 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-160 | FR-CHN-14: what happens when a provider times out inside drafting? | `API_FR_CHN_10.md` already allows "raises with nothing stored and nothing emitted, or a not-ready result". The tests accept either, and pin the cost: one timeout of 10 s is one wait (no retry into a second timeout), so the user hears back inside 15 s and the channel layer can say "I could not draft that, please try again". A deadline inside the drafter is not pinned (it needs no new contract). | PRD 11.4, 17.2 NFR-09, `API_FR_CHN_10.md` | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-161 | FR-CHN-14: is there a CPU budget for the checker? PRD 19.3 asks for "deterministic checks first" but gives no number. | The drafter plus checker may use at most 10% of the 15 s budget (1.5 s CPU) per draft: over all fixture answers together, over a maximum-size adversarial body (20,000 characters of dense numbers, dates and links against a 5 x 20,000 character thread), and at p95 per draft. | PRD 19.3, 11.6 FR-CHN-14 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-162 | PRD 19.2 (#201): which 5 of the 20 E-HAND cases are `deploy_gate` cases? | E-HAND-01..05, chosen to span 5 distinct multi-step domains so the deploy gate is exercised across domains and not on one scenario. The other 15 stay non-gating. Their cited sources are synthetic placeholders until harness context-handoff recordings land (CL-EVID). | PRD 19.2, #201, AGE-07 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-163 | FR-CON-04 live smoke (PRD 5.4 CP1): the `--draft` run goes through the outbox, but a command line is not a gate card. How is the person's authorisation recorded? | The person who runs the command with `--draft ADDRESS` is the authority: the entry is a T1 `create_draft` with `authz {by: person, ref: the smoke user}` and gate evidence (`approval.requested` and `approval.resolved`, channel `web`, the nearest registered channel) built in memory and never emitted, so `check_entry` passes unchanged and no event type is added. Revisit when a CLI channel exists. | PRD 10.4, 12.6 FR-CON-04; `services/connectors/tests/API_FR_CON_04_live_smoke.md` | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-170 | CancelPending (FR-CHN-11, #249): pinned Outcome statuses have no retryable value, so what do Cancel and Edit return when the outbox cannot confirm a cancel? | They raise `CancelPending` (a RuntimeError exported from `tilo_channels.autopilot`) and the owner retries; default: raise, not a new status. Revisit with #256 (read port / sweeper). | PRD 10.4 Undo, OB-01 to OB-05 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-171 | FR-CHN-12 (`services/channels/tests/test_FR_CHN_12_recipient_attachment_forward_t3.py`): Is a forward or a new email to a known address (the thread's sender or a contact) T3, and which format counts? | Yes: a forward or new email always adds a recipient outside the thread (BQ-145), so it is T3 (`new_recipients`) whoever it goes to. The format is read from both the instruction's `DraftContext.mode` and `Draft.mode`; a mismatch or any format other than `reply` / `reply_all` never rides the L2 undo window and, if sendable, is T3. New email is named `new`. | PRD 11.4 Formats, 10.4 T3, BQ-145 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-172 | FR-CHN-12: What happens to a ready draft with attachments? | It is T3 with modifier `attachments` (`config/autonomy.yaml`), shown on the card's tier and modifiers, and never scheduled. Attachment drafts are never sent until the preview lists attachments (the tests accept refusal); once a person can send one, the outbox entry carries each attachment (by artifact id, as an `artifact_ref`), `attachments` in its modifiers and `authz` person (OB-04, outbox schema). | PRD 10.4 T3, 11.4 Formats, `contracts/outbox.schema.json` | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-173 | FR-CHN-12: Is a Bcc to a known contact T3? | Yes. Drafting never sets a Bcc (FR-CHN-10), and the other recipients cannot see one, so any Bcc is a new recipient (`new_recipients`, T3). A Bcc address is never sent unless the card shows it; `Preview` has no `bcc` field today, so such a draft is never sent. | PRD 11.4 Formats, card rule CR-12 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-174 | FR-CHN-12: When does a recipient address count as the same as a known one? | Only when equal after lower-casing. No NFKC, trimming, format-character removal, trailing-dot, plus-tag, quoted-local-part, display-name, percent or source-route, or IDN folding: any variant is a new recipient (T3). Fail closed: a variant that really reaches the same mailbox costs one tap. | PRD 10.4 T3, 16.2, BQ-154 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-190 | FR-MEM-01 (`packages/memory/tests/test_FR_MEM_01_freshness_rules.py`): PRD 13.1 says web results are "fresh for 7 days". Is a result exactly 7 days old fresh? How are "normalised request" and "files until they change" read? | Fresh while age is at most 7 days (exactly 7 days still reused; 7 days and 1 second is refetched). Age counts from the fetch, so a refetch restarts it. A file result has no age limit and is reused while the source fingerprint is unchanged; a changed fingerprint is a different key, so it is fetched again. Normalised request: strip and collapse runs of whitespace, no case folding (URL paths are case-sensitive). `email_body` results are never stored. | PRD 13.1, 13.5 FR-MEM-01 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-191 | FR-MEM-01/02: the PRD fixes behaviour, not an API, and no result cache exists yet. Where does it live and what is its interface? | New uv workspace package `packages/memory` (`tilo_memory`, module `tilo_memory.cache`): `ResultCache(clock, fit_check, verifier, lookup_cost)`, `get_or_fetch(CacheRequest(kind, text, fingerprint), fetch, base, seq)` returning a result with `value`, `outcome`, `reason`, `saved`, `events`; plus `cache.log` and `cache.credits_saved`. The fit check and Verifier are injected callables returning a bool; production wraps them through `packages/providers` (PL-01, role `verifier` for the Verifier). Full shape in `packages/memory/tests/_memory_testlib.py`. Tests use fakes only. A builder may propose another shape but must update the tests to match, not weaken them. Only the result cache has a freshness rule in 13.1; the other layers (decisions FR-AGT-07, preferences FR-INT-05, artifact memory, skills) are other tickets. | PRD 13.1, 6.4 PL-01 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-192 | FR-MEM-02: "logged as a miss" but `contracts/events/registry.yaml` has no `memory.miss` type (only `memory.hit` and `memory.saved`). | A miss is an entry in the cache's own append-only lookup log (`outcome` "miss", a `reason`, `saved` 0) and emits no event; a rejected reuse emits no `memory.hit`, so nothing is credited. If the Trace lens must show misses, that needs a new event type: needs contract change (architect, ADR first). | PRD 13.1, 13.5 FR-MEM-02, 14.3 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-193 | FR-MEM-02: what happens after the fit check or Verifier rejects a fresh cached result, and in what order are they run? | Never served: the source is fetched and the fresh result replaces the entry (so a later lookup can hit it). Reasons are distinct: `fit_rejected` and `verifier_rejected`. Both must accept; the tests do not fix their order or require the Verifier to be skipped after a fit rejection. A stale or changed-source entry is not shown to either check. | PRD 13.1, 14.3, 6.6 cascades | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-198 | FR-AGT-05 (`services/harness/tests/test_FR_AGT_05_*.py`): PRD 8.3 lists four must-ask rules but not how code decides "touches money, personal data, legal terms or outside communication not covered by an autonomy policy", nor how the model's judgement of an ambiguous brief or a trade-off enters. | Two layers. Deterministic floor: a unit whose `actions` include a type above T1 in `config/autonomy.yaml` (or not listed there, which is T3, BQ-44) always asks (`sensitive_step`) unless the type is T2 and in `covered_actions` (the types an autonomy policy covers); a T3 type is never waved through by coverage. A unit that has failed `retries.max_attempts` (3) times asks (`repeated_failure`) with no model call. Model layer: the unit's role provider replies `{"ask": false}` or `{"ask": true, reason, question, options, suggested, why}`; it can add a question (`ambiguous_brief`, `trade_off`, or `sensitive_step` for money, personal data or legal terms that code cannot see) and can never remove one a rule requires. The interface (`MustAsk`, `assess`, `raise_question`, `run_plan(must_ask=)`) is pinned in `agt_must_ask_support.py`. | PRD 8.3, 8.6 FR-AGT-05, 10.4 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-199 | FR-AGT-05: what if the model's reply to the ambiguity check cannot be read (not JSON, wrong shape, missing `ask`)? | The harness asks (`ambiguous_brief`) instead of proceeding: when it cannot tell, a person decides. It costs one extra question, never a silent guess. | PRD 8.3, 16.3 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-200 | FR-AGT-12 (`services/harness/tests/test_FR_AGT_12_*.py`): PRD 8.1 says the Verifier "checks claims, numbers and formats independently of the author" and 8.6 asks for >= 90% of planted false claims caught or marked unverified, but fix no method. How is a claim judged, and what counts as independent? | Judged in order, first match wins: no `source_ids` -> unverified; no usable cited source (text missing, or its SHA-256 differs from the manifest fingerprint) -> unverified; a number in the claim that no usable cited source contains -> contradicted (no model call); otherwise the `verifier`-role model decides, one call per claim, `{"verdict": supported or contradicted or cannot_tell}`, and anything unreadable or a raised error -> unverified. Result: all supported -> passed, any contradicted -> failed, else unverified; a manifest with no claims is unverified (BQ-181). The author's `verified` flag is recomputed, never trusted. Independent means: only `providers["verifier"]` is used (no fallback to another role), no producer id, skill or profile in any prompt, and a verifier-rank agent signs the review and refuses its own work (`VerifierNotIndependent`). Formats (file validity) are an E-DOC check, not tested here. The interface is pinned in `agt_verifier_support.py`. | PRD 8.1, 8.6 FR-AGT-12, 13.3, 14.2, 19.3 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-201 | FR-AGT-05 and FR-AGT-12: how do the new checks meet `run_plan`, and what is the eval set? | `run_plan(..., must_ask=None, verifier=None)`: with `must_ask`, a unit that must ask is parked (its work does not start, the run returns; `question.routed` and a `needs_you` card are the record; answering is FR-AGT-06/07). With `verifier`, every artifact a successful unit hands over gets exactly one `artifact.reviewed`, caused by that handoff and signed by the verifier-rank agent; an artifact the loader cannot load is reviewed as `unverified`, never skipped. The eval sets are test fixtures: 12 planted and 12 clear tasks with recorded model replies (`fixtures/must_ask/tasks.json`), 44 planted false claims (2 are number-free claims the scripted model wrongly approves, so the honest rate is 42/44) and 20 true ones (so a Verifier that marks everything unverified fails). PRD 19.2's 170-case set has no must-ask or verifier group, so `evals/` is not changed. | PRD 8.6, 19.2 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-213 | FR-AGT-12: how big a source can a claim check send to the model, and what does `run_plan` do with a review that fails or is unverified? | A source over 30,000 characters (`MAX_SOURCE_CHARS` in `verifier.py`), or sources of one claim that together exceed it, is refused, never cut: the source is not hashed or scanned and the claim is `unverified`. `run_plan(verifier=)` records the review and does not act on it (no rework, no skipped dependents); acting on `failed` and `unverified` results belongs to the planned-rework tickets. The Verifier agent must be a verifier-rank agent in the layout that works no unit, else `PlanRefused` before anything is written; a deliverable it cannot load, or that it produced itself, is recorded as `unverified`. A manifest that breaks `manifest.schema.json` is `unverified` with no claim status. | PRD 8.1, 8.6, 13.3, 14.2 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-212 | FR-AGT-05: PRD 8.3 and 10.2 give a question card a timeout and a safe default but no numbers, and a model-written question has no "safe" option of its own. | The card times out after 3600 s (`QUESTION_TIMEOUT_S` in `must_ask.py`); its safe default is the suggested option (a question card can only hold reversible options, so applying it never authorises anything). A question never authorises a side effect: sending and sharing stay with the outbox and the policy engine, whose T3 gate still needs a person. Rule questions use canned options in code (sensitive step: "Hold this step" suggested; repeated failure: "Stop here" suggested). A model reply that is not a valid ask object (wrong reason, option count or length, bad `suggested`) is unreadable and asks (BQ-199). | PRD 8.3, 10.2, 10.3 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-194 | FR-MEM-03 (`packages/memory/tests/test_FR_MEM_03_ledger_dedupe.py`, PRD 13.2, 13.5): what does the ledger do with a second message that has the same event `id` but other content, and with an unreadable or incomplete message? Where does dedup live, given the Pub/Sub BigQuery subscription in `infra/modules/events` writes straight to the table? | Key is the event `id`, never the Pub/Sub message id. First write wins and the row is never rewritten (append-only); the second message returns `duplicate` or `conflict` (builder's choice, both accepted). A message that is not a JSON object or lacks one of `v, id, seq, ts, world_id, type, severity` is `rejected` (no row, no exception, content not echoed) so a poison message cannot loop. The tests pin "no duplicate rows" on the physical table of the Python ingester; whether production dedups in an ingester or a deduplicating view is the builder's call and needs an infra change (not made here). | PRD 13.2, 13.5, 22.2 D-11 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-195 | FR-MEM-09 (`test_FR_MEM_09_user_erasure.py`, `test_FR_MEM_09_ledger_erasure_and_retention.py`, PRD 13.2, 16.5): the ledger is append-only but account deletion must reach it; which stores count as "every store", and how is a user's data found in each? | Every store sits behind a `RetentionStore` (`records()`, `delete()`), and a record is the user's when its `user_id` equals the request's (a ledger row belongs to the owner of its world). `REQUIRED_STORES` names Firestore, the BigQuery ledger, Cloud Storage archive, artifacts and cache blobs, and Memory Bank; erasing or verifying with fewer stores is refused (`IncompleteRegistry`). Records with no owner are never deleted by a user erasure and a blank or wildcard user id is refused. `LedgerRetentionStore` is the only code allowed to delete ledger rows. Done means a fresh re-read finds nothing, not that `delete()` returned. Late (over 30 days) is reported but never skips the work. | PRD 13.2, 15.1, 16.5 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-196 | FR-MEM-09 (`test_FR_MEM_09_retention_windows.py`, PRD 15.1): entity names, the edge of a window, and the "7-30 days by source" cache range. | Entity keys are PRD 15.1's snake_case (`event_live` 30, `event_ledger` 365, `policy_history` 365, `decision` 365, `task`/`world`/`run`/`card`/`artifact` 90, `archive_record` 30 demo, `cache_entry` 7 to 30; others none). A record is purged only when strictly older than its window (30 days exactly is kept). An unknown entity is an error, never kept forever by default. | PRD 15.1, 16.5 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-197 | FR-MTR-06 (`packages/metering/tests/test_FR_MTR_06_credits_saved.py`, PRD 14.3, EV-09): what does the credits-saved sum do with a `memory.hit` whose `saved` is not `origin_cost` minus `lookup_cost`, with a negative saving, or with a replayed event? | `tilo_metering.savings.credits_saved` computes origin minus lookup itself and raises `InconsistentMemoryHit` (a `ValueError`) naming the event id instead of summing a wrong figure; a negative saving or a non-finite, boolean or missing cost is a `ValueError` too. Only `memory.hit` counts (a `saved` on any other type is ignored), and each event id counts once so a replay cannot inflate it. | PRD 14.3, 14.4, MT-05, EV-09 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-206 | FR-MEM-03: the Pub/Sub to BigQuery subscription in `infra/modules/events` appends every delivery, so a redelivered message lands as a second physical row. Where is dedup in production? | `tilo_memory.ledger` pins no duplicate rows on the table its Python ingester writes (`EventLedger` over `BigQueryLedgerTable`: one `INSERT ... WHERE NOT EXISTS` on the event `id`). Production still needs either that ingester in front of the table (a push or pull subscriber replacing the direct BigQuery subscription) or a deduplicating view over it (`id`, first `ts` row). Not changed here: infra needs the user's approval. Follow-up ticket for reach-builder. | PRD 13.2, 22.2 D-11 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-207 | FR-HIL-10 harness half (`services/harness/tests/test_FR_HIL_10_*.py`, interface in `hil_wait_support.py`): `question.answered` data holds only `answered_by` (no card id, no chosen option), and the card has no `answer` field, so how does a waiting agent learn which question was answered and what the answer was? | The harness-owned writer already receives the CardStore's part with `card_id` as scope (`tilo_api.cards`, "question.answered data has no card field"), so a wait is keyed by that `card_id`. The chosen option travels in the part as a new optional top-level `option` (scope, not event data, like `card_id`); a timeout resolves to the card's `safe_default`. The builder adds `option` to the question part in `CardStore.answer` (additive; check the API tests' exact-part checks). No contract change: event `data` stays as is. New module `tilo_harness.question_wait` (`QuestionWait`, `Answer`) and `run_plan(..., question_wait=)`; `UnitContext.answer` carries the `Answer`. | PRD 10.2, 10.6 FR-HIL-10, 9.5 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-208 | FR-HIL-10: who applies the timeout, the API (`CardStore.tick()`, FR-HIL-02) or the harness, and how is it tested without a real timer? | Both may fire; whichever resolves the card first wins and the other changes nothing (one `question.answered` per card, the Director's default as a Nudge, EV-08). The harness waits for created_at + timeout with an injected clock (`now()`, `async sleep()`); tests move a fake clock and never poll. Real timing is asserted with `perf_counter` against 2.0 s with a 0.5 s writer on the path, in-process, no browser or server (as BQ-205). "A routed question appears in <= 2 s" is the API and console half (BQ-204/205) and is not re-tested here. | PRD 10.3, 10.6 FR-HIL-02, FR-HIL-10 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-209 | FR-HIL-10: what happens to a waiting unit's slot and to answers the harness does not expect? | A unit waiting on a person holds no `max_specialists` slot ("that branch waits", PRD 10.2; otherwise one question could deadlock a run); its dependants wait for it, other units go on. The first resolution per card wins: a later part for a resolved card writes nothing (it may raise, the tests allow either). A part for a card nobody waits on (harness restarted, or a stale card) is still committed as the person's recorded answer and resumes nobody. With `must_ask` but no `question_wait`, BQ-201's park-and-return is unchanged. Resuming after a harness restart (rebuilding waits from `question.routed` events) is not in this ticket. | PRD 10.2, 8.3, 10.6 FR-HIL-10 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-232 | FR-AGT-12 (security review of #278): how many claims does one review judge, and where is a cited source that fails its fingerprint recorded? | At most `MAX_CLAIMS` = 200 (the `max_claims` argument of `Verifier`; `manifest.schema.json` sets no limit and each claim is one model call); claims past it are `unverified` with no call. A cited source whose text is missing, too long, or fails its fingerprint is recorded in `Review.notes[claim id]` and the claim is judged on its other usable sources (BQ-200 rule 2); the note is not in the event, whose data is fixed by contract. Metering the verifier calls against the run budget is left to the metering tickets. Untrusted text in prompts goes through `tilo_harness.untrusted.neutralise` (NFKC, format and control characters removed, `<` and `>` replaced), also for the must-ask unit text. A manifest for another artifact or version than the ref reviewed, or one that breaks its schema, is `unverified` and comes back with every `verified` flag false. Each cited source is checked, hashed, scanned and neutralised once per review, off the event loop; runs of combining characters are counted on the decomposed (NFKD) form, as in the UAX #15 stream-safe format (U+FF9E and U+FF9F are not combining but decompose into non-starters; NFKC is quadratic on long runs): a source with a run over 30 is refused as unusable and noted, and every NFKC (`untrusted.nfkc`, also for numbers) drops the characters that would make such a run and stops at `limit` decomposed characters, so U+FDFA's 18x expansion is capped before the second pass. Every source and claim is length-capped before any normalisation or scan; the raw lengths of a claim's cited sources are summed (over 30,000 together: unverified and noted) before any is prepared; at most `MAX_SOURCES` = 20 distinct sources are prepared per review (the rest are refused and noted); and a review normalises at most `MAX_REVIEW_CHARS` = 3,000,000 decomposed characters (beyond: unverified and noted). Digits of any script count as the number they are. Residual: look-alikes of the angle quotes that `neutralise` itself emits (and of `<` and `>` such as U+02C2, U+276E, U+3008) are not removed, so ‹‹‹CLAIM reads like neutralised output; the template and `claim_system.md` treat only the ASCII markers as delimiters. | PRD 8.1, 8.6, 13.3, 16.3 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-202 | FR-INT-11: PRD 7.1 names the parts of a scenario config but not its keys, its file name, where a scenario's policies get created, or how skills resolve. | One file `config/scenarios/<id>.yaml` (`SC-01.yaml`) with `id`, `title`, `skills`, `connectors`, `seed_data` (repo-relative), `theme`, `autonomy` (policy entries within the `config/autonomy.yaml` max level, T3 at L1 only). `tilo_planning.scenarios.load_scenario` checks connectors, seed paths and shapes, and skills against a catalog when given one; SC-01's skills are not checked against `skills/` until FR-AGT-04 merges. A scenario's policies are created as the owner's own at approve (never at intake), with a `policy.changed` event. `contracts/scenario.schema.json` now exists (ADR-0014); the loader validates against it. `config/themes/` does not exist, so a theme is only checked as a slug. | PRD 4.3, 7.1 FR-INT-11, PR-7, 10.1, 10.4 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-203 | FR-INT-12: PRD 7.1 and 16 do not list which tasks are out of policy, who decides, or the shape of a refusal; PRD 19.2 has no refusal eval group. | Categories live in `config/out_of_policy.yaml` (`id`, `reason`, `alternative`, from PRD 10.4, 16.1, 16.3). The Director only names a category (`out_of_policy`; null or absent means in policy); code writes the reason and the nearest task from config, so no model-written or task-quoting text reaches a person, and an unknown category fails closed. HTTP: 422 `{error, alternative, category}`, nothing stored. The refusal set is a planning test fixture (24 refuse, 12 control), not one of the 170 eval cases; whether a real model picks the right category is a live eval for the owner to add as a PRD 19.2 group. | PRD 7.1 FR-INT-12, 16, 19.2 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-204 | FR-HIL-10: PRD 10.5 describes the console (queue by domain, history, answer-time stats) but gives no routes or stats definition, and says nothing of how it meets card merging or districts. | `GET /worlds/{id}/console` and `POST .../console/cards/{id}/answer` in `tilo_api.console`; the Team Lead and domain come from `config/teams.yaml` records (ADR-0006), not from `Principal.districts`; the queue is the open question cards routed to that domain only (the owner fallback and gates never appear); the domain is part of the merge key; history is that Team Lead's own answers; stats are the count and the median of answered_at minus created_at. "Promote to skill" (Should) is left out. | PRD 10.5, 10.6 FR-HIL-10, ADR-0006 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-205 | FR-HIL-10: how is "appears in <= 2 s" and "answering resumes the agent in <= 2 s" tested without a browser or a harness question wait? | At the API and event level, in-process: real `perf_counter` against 2.0 s with a 0.5 s event writer on the path, plus a fake clock that is never advanced (so no tick or poll can hide a delay). "Resumes" is the `question.answered` part reaching the writer before the answer returns. The harness resuming its agent from that event, and the console screen, are not tested here (no question wait exists in `services/harness`; the screen is an AG-WEB ticket). | PRD 10.6 FR-HIL-10, 17.1 | 2026-10-04 |
| BQ-218 | Must every completed run deliver at least one artifact? The PRD is silent: the vision is that agents finish real deliverables, and EV-01, FR-INT-03 and FR-CON-10 are deliverable-centric, but `artifact.delivered` is only written on the gated path, so research-and-report plans end "Done: 0 deliverables" (issue #283, GEN bank #282). | Yes: every completed run delivers its brief deliverables `in_app` (no outbound side effect, so no gate; send and save stay gated). Implementation (the plan compiler emits a deliver unit, an ungated `in_app` delivery path, S-12 empty-state wording) is a separate ticket after T4; not built here. | PRD 7.1, 14, vision; EV-01, FR-INT-03, FR-CON-10; issues #282, #283 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-214 | FR-HIL-10: `config/teams.yaml` may hold two records for one user (two domains). Which domain does the console show? | The first record in file order; the others are not reachable from the console until the owner splits them. Less access, never more (fail closed). No contract rule forbids it; if one user must lead several domains, that needs a console domain choice and an ADR. | PRD 8.3, 10.5, ADR-0006 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-215 | FR-HIL-10: how does the answer's option reach the harness (BQ-207 of `tests/FR-HIL-10-harness-resume`)? | `CardStore.answer` adds an optional top-level `option` to the `question.answered` part it hands the writer (scope, like `card_id`; event `data` is unchanged, so no contract change). Only a person's or Team Lead's answer carries it; a timeout part has none (the harness uses the card's `safe_default`). The harness half is a later ticket. Known gaps, needing a contract change (architect, ADR first): `question.answered` carries authz null by contract and the writer records actor `system` for a Team Lead's answer, and the option is not in the committed event, so a commit-then-raise retry with a different option dedupes to the first event while the console history records the second. | PRD 10.6, 9.5 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-216 | FR-INT-11: what does SC-01's file hold? PRD 4.3 says only "Sandbox Web seed, research skills, deck template". | `config/scenarios/SC-01.yaml`: the PRD 12.4 v0 skills it needs (`web-research`, `table-extraction`, `price-comparison`, `deck-writing`, `executive-summary`), connector `google_drive`, the three Sandbox Web pricing seeds, theme `metropolis` (a slug until `config/themes/` exists), and one policy: `save_file` at L2, 10 a day, 60 s undo. The deck template is not a scenario key; it stays with the render service. Skill ids are not checked against `skills/` until FR-AGT-04 merges. A seed file must sit under a directory named `seed`, resolve to exactly its entry (no link elsewhere, no trailing-dot alias) and have no hidden segment (SN-04, bounded to a seed root). | PRD 4.3, 7.1, 12.4 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-217 | FR-INT-11: when is the scenario read, and what if it stops loading between intake and approve? | At intake (an unknown or hostile id is 422, nothing stored) and again at approve, before anything changes. If it no longer loads, approve is 503 and changes nothing, so a run never starts half-configured. Its policies are created after the owner's own budget grant is recorded (never by it), once per task (a second approve is the existing 409). | PRD 7.1, 10.1 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-210 | FR-MEM-09 (`tilo_memory.erasure`, PRD 16.5): who may trigger an erasure, and what does an unreadable store mean for "done"? | `erase_user` takes an `ErasureRequest` that is valid by construction (user id `u_<...>` per the contract, aware time) and the module has no endpoint or trigger: authenticating the caller and authorising "delete this account" belong to the API ticket. A store that cannot be read is `remaining == -1` (unknown), the report is not complete and `verify` is not ok; failures carry the exception type name only. Registry names are fixed in `REQUIRED_STORES` (`firestore`, `bigquery_ledger`, `memory_bank`, `gcs_archive`, `gcs_artifacts`, `gcs_cache`); real Firestore/GCS/Memory Bank adapters and the production registry are follow-up tickets (the CLI with no registry configured exits 3). CLI exit codes: 0 clean, 1 data remains, 2 bad input, 3 registry incomplete. | PRD 13.5, 16.5 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-211 | FR-MEM-09 (`LedgerRetentionStore`): who owns a ledger row once the World entity is gone, how is a row aged when its `ts` cannot be parsed, and how does a replay after erasure get caught? | The owner is written to an owner index (`world id -> user id`, first write wins) at ingest (`EventLedger(table, world_owner=..., owner_index=...)`), so ownership outlives the World entity, which is erased with the user and purged at 90 days while ledger rows live 365. The index is kept at least as long as the rows and, for an erased user, until `release(user_id)` after the verifying script passes; it holds ids only. `LedgerRetentionStore` reads the index first and falls back to the live `world_owner` only for rows ingested without an index, so production must wire the index (a durable Firestore or BigQuery implementation is a follow-up; the in-memory one is for tests and dev). A late replay of an erased row is re-attributed by the index and `verify_erasure` fails until the erasure is re-run. `erase_user` reads the owned ids of every store before deleting from any, so registry order cannot hide rows. An unparseable or out-of-range `ts` is aged as never expiring (retention never deletes early) but stays erasable. Ledger deletes are scoped by (`id`, `world_id`). Stopping the replay at its source (tombstone or dead-letter) needs the ingester and infra ticket (BQ-206). | PRD 13.2, 16.5 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-219 | FR-MEM-09 (retention): ledger rows are aged by the `ts` the emitter set, so a wrong old `ts` purges an audit row early. | Logged, not fixed: aging uses the event `ts` (the acceptance tests pin it). Consider aging by ingest time in a follow-up. | PRD 15.1 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-220 | FR-MEM-09 (erasure scope): events that name the user (actor, `authz.by` ref) in a world the user does not own are not erased, because a ledger row belongs to its world's owner. | Logged, not fixed: PRD 16.5 does not say whether third-party worlds' references to an erased user are removed or pseudonymised; needs a decision (the owner of that world may need the audit trail). | PRD 16.5 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-221 | CHN-GATE-B (issue #256; `services/channels/tests/test_CHN_GATE_B_FR_CHN_11_thread_instruction_known.py`): what does `Autopilot.preview` do with a draft whose `thread_id` is not the context's? | It raises `ValueError` before anything is recorded (as it already does for a draft of another instruction): no card, no entry, nothing to Send, and the replay of an existing card with such a draft raises too and changes nothing. A T3 card without Send was rejected: it would still store the other thread's reply headers and show them. Equality is exact (no trimming or case folding). | PRD 11.4 Preview and Undo, FR-CHN-13 reply target, OB-01 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-222 | CHN-GATE-B (issue #256): may an empty instruction ride L2? | No. An instruction that is empty, whitespace-only (Unicode whitespace too) or only invisible format characters is not a person's instruction: the card is L1 with no schedule and no policy-port L2, and waits for its owner's Send (its tier is whatever the content gives). The PRD gives no default for an empty instruction; this is the fail-closed reading of "L2 acts on the person's own instruction". Invisible-only is included because it is empty to the person. | PRD 10.4, 11.4, OB-01, BQ-152 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-223 | CHN-GATE-B (issue #256): does every From in a thread make that address a known recipient (BQ-154)? | No longer. Known is: contacts (lower-case ASCII equality, BQ-174), To and Cc of the user's own messages (From the mailbox with Gmail's `SENT` label), and the From of the message the reply answers (the last message not from the mailbox), which the existing FR-CHN-11 and FR-CHN-12 tests pin as known with no contact. An earlier message's From adds nothing, so an inbound Cc of that address is a new recipient (T3). Not fixed (needs a user decision): a spoofed From on the answered message itself is still the reply's To and rides L2; fixing it needs sender authentication (Authentication-Results) or T3 for any non-contact reply at L2. A To that is an earlier sender but not the answered one is left to the builder (the drafter never produces it). | PRD 10.4 T3, 11.4 Formats, FR-CHN-12, BQ-154 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-225 | CHN-WIRE-1 (issue #307 ticket 1; `services/channels/tests/API_CHN_WIRE_1.md`): the autopilot card id is `"apc_" + sha256[:32]`, but `approval.requested` needs a ULID `card_id` and a `G<n>` (1 to 4 digits) `gate_id`. How are they derived without a contract change? | Deterministic functions in services (`tilo_channels.autopilot_gate`: `gate_card_id`, `gate_id_for`) of the autopilot card id alone: the ULID encodes 128 bits of a sha256 of it (so its first character is 0 to 7), `gate_id` is `G` plus a number below 10000 from other digest bytes. The ULID's time bits are hash bits (no contract reads them). `gate_id` may repeat across cards (about 1 in 9999): OB-01 always matches gate, card and outbox ids together. No contract change. The tests pin shape, determinism and distinct `card_id`s, not the digits. | PRD 9.5, OB-01, `approval.requested.schema.json`, issue #307 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-226 | CHN-WIRE-1: who is the world owner and who else may answer an autopilot gate? | The card's own `user_id` is the world owner (`world_owner` in the evidence) and the only answerer: `approval.requested` carries `approver: null` and `team_lead: null`, so another person, a Team Lead or a policy stamp on the approval opens nothing (OB-01, `opens_gate`). Approver and Team Lead gates for autopilot cards need the owner's records and are not built. | PRD 10.1, OB-01, `coverage.opens_gate` | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-227 | CHN-WIRE-1: what does `Autopilot.send` do with a missing or bad approval, and does that use up the card? | It returns `Outcome("refused", None)`, never raises for a bad approval, creates no entry and does not claim the card's `#decision` slot, so the owner's proper approval afterwards still queues one entry. The caller (`tilo-api`, not built) supplies the `approval.resolved` part (authz stamped with the tap time); the entry's `authz` is copied from it, never re-stamped, and the `approval.requested` in the evidence is the part stored at preview, never rebuilt. A later second approval for a card that already has its entry changes nothing. | OB-01, OB-03, SEC-03, issue #307 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-228 | CHN-WIRE-1: the FR-CHN-11, -12, -13 and CHN-GATE-A benches send with no approval over a fake outbox or a `check_entry` accept-all (BQ-184). How do they keep running? | `Autopilot(..., fake_evidence=False)`, a keyword-only constructor flag; `True` keeps the old behaviour (no `approval.requested`, no approval needed, `authz` stamped now, evidence `{instruction_id, level}`). Benches opt in through `_chn11_lib.fake_evidence_kwargs()`. Production never passes it. It is not a bypass: the real outbox refuses the old evidence. For the security reviewer: keyword-only, default False, tests only; consider refusing construction with a real `Outbox`. | BQ-184, issue #307, SEC-03 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-229 | CHN-WIRE-1: which cards get an `approval.requested`, how often, and with what fields? | Only a card created at L1 with a ready, sendable T2 or T3 draft; not an L2 policy card (policy path, tickets 4 and 5), not a card with no Send option. It reserves the entry id as the card's `outbox_id` at preview (it was `None` at L1), `irreversible` is `true` (a sent email cannot be recalled, as in the OB-01 fixtures), `item` is the card's pinned draft. Emitted at most once, by the instance that won the card's atomic create (a crash between the create and the emit loses the event but not the stored part, which is what OB-01 reads). A redraft after Edit is a new card with a new part. | `approval.requested.schema.json`, OB-01, OB-05, BQ-155 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-230 | CHN-WIRE-1: can `tilo-api` find the autopilot card from the gate's `card_id` (the hash cannot be reversed)? | Not decided here: this ticket pins only the forward mapping (BQ-225), and `Autopilot.send` is given the autopilot card id. Whether `tilo-api` (ticket 2) keeps a reverse index, or holds the pair it got from the emitted `approval.requested` when it routes the tap, is that ticket's choice; no contract change either way. | issue #307 ticket 2 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-231 | #227 E2 (S-11 Trace, PRD 18.3): "Open record" should link to the event's archive record, but there is no archive-record route in the web app or API, and the client path always sets `trace.archive_ref` to null (`services/api/src/tilo_api/redaction.py`, PRD 13.2). Where does a person open a raw record? | Applied: the control stays `aria-disabled` with the title "Coming soon", sized 28 px (44 px on a phone), and the page line "Opening a raw record is coming soon." stays; no route is invented. The archive-route part of #227 E2 stays open until an archive-record route and a redaction rule for what a person may read exist (a contract and API ticket). | PRD 13.2, 18.3 S-11 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-235 | FR-AGT-05 through the API (`services/api/tests/test_FR_AGT_05_api_must_ask_wiring.py`, `_agt05_lib.py`; issue #292 items 2 and 5): how does `tilo_api.runs.Run` meet `run_plan(must_ask=)`? | A new run seam `deps.get_must_ask_providers() -> Mapping[str, ModelProvider]` keyed by role; its default builds `_RoleModel(get_router(), role)` per worker role like `get_unit_runner` (PL-01: no model id in `services/api/src`). `Run._execute` builds `MustAsk(providers, config=<agents config>, card_sink=<the run's CardStore>, owner=<world owner>, covered_actions=<action types the owner's autonomy policies cover>)` and passes it to `run_plan`; `Run` gives the harness `layout`, `world_id`, `run_id` and `now()`. The sink submits the needs_you card WITH the unit's district (a Team Lead sees only their district's cards, and `question.answered` needs a `vis`; an event's vis is never widened). The card is stored BEFORE `agent.help_requested`, `agent.escalated` and `question.routed` are emitted (so `question.routed` never names an unstored card); a store that raises or refuses means no chain, no unit start, no `world.completed`. Which answer runs the unit is the harness's call from the `Ask` it raised (`Ask.runs`), never the runner or a model: a rule's card (sensitive_step, repeated_failure, unreadable reply) runs it only on its proceed option ("Prepare it", "Go ahead", "Try once more"); Hold, Stop, Skip and the deadline's safe default leave it unrun (no agent.working, no artifact, no gate card; the agent retires, `RunResult.held`, its dependents are skipped, the run ends with `world.failed` "a step was held"). A model-written ambiguity or trade-off card has no proceed option: any answer runs the unit with it in `UnitContext.answer`, and so does the Director's deadline default (PRD 10.3, FR-HIL-02). A rule card's safe default is never its proceed option, so it holds. A model-raised sensitive_step is still a model-written card (its labels and order are pinned by the FR-AGT-05 card test), so a person's "Hold" on it runs the unit: open, needs a decision (see PR #336). An ask that is not MustAsk's `Ask` (the FR-HIL-10 test doubles) runs on any answer; production asks all come from `MustAsk.assess`. `_store_card` strips Cc, Cf and Cs characters from the card text before storing it. A parked unit (`RunResult.parked`) is neither a failure nor a completion: no `world.completed` and no `world.failed` while any unit waits for a person. A policy covers an action when it is the world owner's and names that action type; the tests pin an L3 policy. No run-status route exists, so the waiting state is read from the stream (no terminal event) and `GET /worlds/{id}/delivery` (409). | PRD 8.3, 8.6 FR-AGT-05, 10.2, 10.3, PL-01, #292 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-236 | FR-AGT-05 through the API: the CP2 plan's send unit has `actions: [send_email]` (T2) and no autonomy policy covers it, so the PRD 8.3 rule ("outside communication not covered by an autonomy policy") makes it ask a question BEFORE its gate card. Does a plan gate count as coverage? | No: PRD 8.3 says an autonomy policy, and a plan gate is not one (BQ-198: a T2 type asks unless a policy covers it, T3 always asks). The deterministic rule fires with no model call (`sensitive_step`), the unit does not start, and no gate card or `approval.requested` is written until a person answers. Consequence for the builder: every existing run test whose plan has the send unit (the `run_flow` fixtures of `_clint2_lib.py`, the CL-INT-5c/5d dev run, the FR-HIL-03/04, FR-AGT-11, FR-WLD-03/07 and ST-3b tests) now meets a question first; they need a covering owner policy or the question answered in their helper, and a scripted `get_must_ask_providers` override in `build_run_flow` (without one the default Router judges every unit, and an unreadable reply asks, BQ-199). With the scratch wiring the tests were written against, about 45 existing API tests fail on exactly this. If the orchestrator would rather let the gate stand in for the question, that is a PRD 8.3 change, not a test change. | PRD 8.3, 10.4, BQ-198 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-237 | FR-HIL-10 through the API (`test_FR_HIL_10_api_answer_resumes_the_run.py`): how does the answer reach the waiting unit, and what does a waiting run look like? | Needs the harness `QuestionWait` (BQ-207 to BQ-209, branch tests/FR-HIL-10-harness-resume) and the API passing `question_wait=` to `run_plan`. The cards route stays as it is; `CardStore.answer` adds the chosen option to the question part as an optional top-level `option` (BQ-207), `Run.write` hands the part to the wait after the event is committed. Pinned: the other units go on while one waits; answering writes one `question.answered` (`answered_by` person, `vis` the district); the unit's work starts within 2.0 s of the answer, once, with `UnitContext.answer` holding `card_id`, `option` (the chosen id) and `answered_by`; its dependants and `world.completed` follow; a second answer is refused and resumes nobody. Not pinned at the API level: the timeout default (the harness tests fix it with a fake clock) and a restart (BQ-209). | PRD 10.2, 10.6 FR-HIL-10, 8.3 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-233 | FR-INT-11 (ADR-0014): approving a task that names a scenario creates the scenario's policies as the owner's own (BQ-202). A scenario may set L2 and L3 (up to its tier's max level), so one approve can switch on autopilot and standing rules. The approve step shows only the approach and the budget. Must the person see each policy (action, level, connectors, daily limit) before approving, or confirm them separately? (Policy preview.) **Resolved 2026-10-05: the user accepted the default.** | BQ-202 stands. Policies are created at approve, each with a `policy.changed` (`created`, `authz` by person), so they appear in the Trace and the policy list, and the owner can revoke any of them (L1) at any time. T3 stays at L1 at most (SN-06), and modifiers still raise a single action to T3 (PI-07). No screen lists them before approval, and none is built: a pre-approval policy list would be a new UI requirement (its own ticket), not part of FR-INT-11. | PRD 4.3, 10.1, 10.4, FR-INT-11, FR-HIL-06 | 2026-10-05 |

Text and structured outputs are at most 2,000,000 bytes (2 MB), files at most 50,000,000, a unit has at most 10 outputs and a world at most 200,000,000 bytes. These are constants in the store (config later if needed), checked before hashing. The alternative is to refuse the output as MF-06 does, which fails the unit. | ADR-0018 AR-07; PRD 16.1 rule 4, SEC-01, MF-06 | 2026-10-05 |

| BQ-245 | CHN-WIRE-4: how old may a stored decision be at its first enqueue (issue #307 "a small pinned bound, e.g. 60 s")? | `tilo_channels.autopilot.DECISION_MAX_AGE_S`, an int with `0 < value <= 60` (builder picks; the tests read the constant and use bound - 1 and bound + 1). At most the bound old: the stored authz is used. Older, or the policy state unknown: the engine is asked again with the stored proposal and the stored decision is replaced by the new one only if it is a clean `run_after_undo`; otherwise L1. The check applies only before the card's entry exists; once the key is stored a replay never asks again (OB-03). A decision stamped in the future (beyond a small clock skew the builder picks) is invalid. | issue #307, OB-01, OB-02 | 2026-10-05 |

It then needs the owner's `approval.resolved`; `send()` without it is refused. Once ADR-0013 lands the engine counts the card's own earlier `policy.applied` as the instruction used up (`instruction_counts`), so in production a late first enqueue always ends here (fail closed, accepted). | issue #307, PRD 10.4, HR-7 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-247 | CHN-WIRE-4: a `Decision` carries no recipients, destination or item, so what ties it to the entry it authorises? | The Autopilot: it stores, with the decision, the facts it proposed (recipients as `dict.fromkeys(to + cc)`, destination, item, tier, modifiers) and compares them with the card as it reads at enqueue time; any difference (a Cc added, another thread, a new item version, a changed tier) means no entry and L1. A redraft after Edit is a new card and a new question, never an old decision. | OB-01, OB-05, issue #307 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-248 | CHN-WIRE-4: what makes a `Decision` usable for an L2 entry? | Only a `tilo_policy.Decision` with outcome `run_after_undo`, tier equal to the card's (T2), level `L2`, no modifiers, a valid `pol_` policy id, `authz` exactly `{by: "policy", ref: <policy id>, at: <valid UTC ms timestamp, not in the future>}` and an integer `undo_window_s` of at least 1 (not a bool). Anything else, a raise or a non-Decision is L1. T3 cards are never put to the policy as L2. | PRD 10.4, OB-01, OB-04, BQ-152 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-249 | CHN-WIRE-4: the numbers and the storage the tests leave to the builder (freshness bound, skew, window cap, how a re-decision is serialised). | `DECISION_MAX_AGE_S` = 30 (inclusive), clock skew 5 s, an `undo_window_s` above 3600 (the policy contract's cap) is unusable (L1), at most 3 re-decisions per card (then a stale card drops without asking), the entry is refused and the card decided again when `created_at - authz.at` leaves the bound on the clock read that stamps it, a re-decision or drop is created at the index of the head read that judged the old one and never once the key is stored, a drop that loses the race to an enqueue stops the key like a Cancel, and Cancel and Edit of a dropped card go through the stop path. The first decision is slot 0 in the card doc; a re-decision is create-if-absent slot `<card_id>#pol<n>` holding the new part or `drop`, never rewritten, so racing instances agree on one and the newest slot is the card's decision (a `drop` is terminal and is read as L1 without rewriting the card). The drop's `approval.requested` and a re-decision's `policy.applied` are emitted by the slot's creator. No production adapter reads the owner's policy records yet (ticket 5 / wiring); with the real engine and `L2_ACTION_RECORDED` False every L2 candidate gates, and with the real `check_entry` the L2 entry is refused (`preview` raises out of the refused enqueue: no entry, no mail). | issue #307, #325, OB-01, OB-03 | 2026-10-05 |
