| BQ-LE2E-1 | PL-LIVE-E2E (#340): where does the command live, and what are its exit codes and outcome classes? | `tilo_evals.live_e2e` (the evals lane owns the bank runner; all model calls still go through `packages/providers`), thin script `evals/scripts/live_e2e.py`, recipe `just live-e2e`. Exit 0 ended as the case expects with every hard check holding, 1 a hard check failed or `failed`/`replay_miss`, 2 refused, 3 `stopped_at_cap` (mirrors the eval CLI's exit 3). Outcome classes: completed, held_at_gate, needs_answers, refused, failed, stopped_at_cap, replay_miss. Pinned in `evals/tests/API_PL_LIVE_E2E.md`. | issue #340, PRD 6.6 EFF-01, PL-03 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-LE2E-2 | PL-LIVE-E2E: what counts as "CI" and "Cloud Run" for the live refusal? | Live mode only: `CI` or `GITHUB_ACTIONS` non-empty is CI; the presence (even empty) of `K_SERVICE` or `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` is Cloud Run (the dev API's and the bank runner's rule). `--replay` and `--dry-run` run in CI. `--replay` with `LIVE=1` is refused (`replay_with_live`) so a replay can never go live. Gate order: live switch first, before any other key, file or import. | issue #340, EFF-01, vertex-smoke gate | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-LE2E-3 | PL-LIVE-E2E: where are recordings saved ("beside the case") and how are they keyed? | `evals/bank/live/<case id>/model-recordings/<role>/<key>.json`, the provider layer's own layout and key (`recording_key(role, profile, request)`, so a changed prompt or brief is a `ReplayMissError`, reported as `replay_miss`). Not under a folder named `recordings/` (gitignored, `.gitignore` line 56); `evals/bank/*.yaml` and `MANIFEST.sha256` are untouched (the digest globs `*.yaml` only). | issue #340, EFF-01 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-LE2E-4 | PL-LIVE-E2E: what is redacted from a recording, and when? | Secrets (the adapter's `redact()` plus the Authorization/Bearer and Google token shapes), email addresses and phone numbers (PRD 16.1 rule 4 and the ticket). The reply is redacted before the run uses it, in live mode too, so what is recorded is what the run saw and a replay builds the same later prompts; otherwise an echoed address would change the next prompt's key and the replay would miss. | issue #340, PRD 16.1 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-LE2E-5 | PL-LIVE-E2E: the `safety` role has no live adapter (`config/models.yaml` gives `safety-fake`, refused in live mode, FR-INT-02), so intake fails closed in a live run. | **Resolved (user decision 2026-10-05):** a live run screens intake with an explicit, named TRUSTED-BANK-INPUT screen (`tilo_evals.live_e2e.TrustedBankInputScreen`). It allows a text only if it is byte-identical (CRLF to LF, the bank digest's own rule) to the brief of the committed bank case being run, and re-hashes that case file on every call; it refuses everything else, and any native file, with a clear error that never repeats the text, so nothing untrusted can reach a live model. Within the same committed case it also allows the case's own attached files, file names and scripted answers (the other texts intake and the answers route screen), and a trusted text still gets the fake screen's injection-marker scan. It is NOT a general safety provider, is never registered in the factory, is used only by `just live-e2e` and does not touch the refusal of the fake safety provider in live mode elsewhere. The report states `safety: trusted_bank_input (Model Armor not wired)`. Model Armor stays unwired (ADR-0003). **Update 6 Oct: Model Armor adapter built (FR-INT-02, PR #450):** it is now the `safety` role's live adapter (`tilo_providers.model_armor_adapter`, role on `armor-default`). The live e2e still uses the trusted-bank-input screen by design (its input is the committed bank case, nothing untrusted); only the report wording changed, "(Model Armor not wired)" became "(by design; Model Armor is the safety role's live adapter, FR-INT-02, #450)". Behaviour is unchanged. | issue #340, FR-INT-02, PRD 16.3 item 2 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-LE2E-6 | PL-LIVE-E2E: `prompts/` has no `writer`, `lead` or `narrator` templates, so the run stage and the Story narration cannot make real calls yet. | **Resolved:** added `prompts/writer/unit.md`, `prompts/verifier/unit.md` (the executor's default runner loads `<role>/unit.md`, which the Verifier role lacked), `prompts/lead/review.md` and `prompts/narrator/story.md`, all through `load_prompt` (PL-04), with no timestamp or id so replay keys stay stable. The Narrator is one call per world; the Lead reviews each district's last unit (a milestone), so fewer calls than units. The committed `evals/bank/live/B-007` fixture is regenerated with `uv run python evals/tests/regen_live_e2e_fixture.py` (synthetic replies through the real code); the user's first live run with `TILO_RECORD=1` replaces it with real recordings. | issue #340, PRD 8.1, PL-04 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-LE2E-7 | PL-LIVE-E2E: the live run's delivery and gated send. | `side_effects` is `mock_only`: the gates are answered from the case's `gates` (approve_all or hold) and the connectors are the recording mocks; no real mail, chat or Drive write (CLAUDE.md: real sends need the user). | issue #340, CLAUDE.md | 2026-10-05 |

| BQ-LE2E-8 | PL-LIVE-E2E: choices the tests leave open. | (1) A unit's model reply is kept in memory and fed to the units that wait for it; no artifact store is wired, so each unit's item is a stand-in artifact id and the model's text is not saved beside the run. (2) A Verifier verdict is recorded in the unit's handoff summary and not acted on (the executor's own `review` rule). (3) A Story line is a Narrator line for one beat (at most 8 consecutive slices of the event list) and cites that beat's event ids (HR-1); a line for a beat that does not exist is dropped. (4) Any stop wins the outcome class: the first of a cap or a replay miss, even from the Narrator. (5) The Router's default output cap (1024 tokens, BQ-63) applies to the Director's own calls, which the planner makes without a cap of its own; if a first live run shows truncated Director JSON, raise `max_output_tokens` for that role in `config/models.yaml`. (6) Log lines are muted during a run so a provider's error text cannot reach a terminal (PRD 16.1 rule 4). | issue #340, PRD 8.1, 9.4, 16.1 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-LE2E-9 | PL-LIVE-E2E fix pass (PR #358): the Director's output cap, a new outcome class for a rejected request, how a replay says its recordings are synthetic, and which failed attempts the meter counts. | Director requests without a cap are sent the role's `max_output_tokens` from `config/models.yaml`, else a 4096 floor (`_DIRECTOR_OUT`; the Router's 1024 includes thinking tokens), and the estimate uses the same cap; `config/models.yaml` is left unchanged (a role cap there is a follow-up for the architect). `rejected` joins the outcome classes (a provider HTTP 400 or `RequestError`; exit 1; reason scrubbed and capped at 200 characters; every other status prints a code only). A `SYNTHETIC` file beside `model-recordings/` marks a fixture written by an injected fake provider and is removed by a real recorded run; replay prints which it found. An attempt that raised for any reason but a refused request (`RequestError`, `QuotaError`) or a replay miss is counted at the Router's own quote (input words and the full output cap), because the provider reported no usage. |
| BQ-239 | FR-HIL-10 (#338, Opus reviews of #336 and #351): do the question cards a unit waits on merge (BQ-74, PRD 10.3 "Same-type cards from one district within 60 s merge")? | No, a deliberate deviation from BQ-74 for run-owned question cards: `Run._store_card` submits them with `CardStore.submit(merge=False)`, so each is its own group, listed on its own, and its answer resumes its own unit only. Reason: a merged card means the person sees one card and one answer must then decide every unit that asked; mapping the answer onto a sibling card (by option label, or any other rule) let a card nobody saw run a step (a model-chosen label such as "Go ahead" selected the proceed option of a hidden rule card, BQ-235), and a refused answer or a commit-then-raise could still decide the siblings. With no sibling mapping there is nothing to hide. Default taken: cost is more cards for the person in one Workshop (each one a distinct question), the unanswered one gets its own deadline default (EV-08). Other card kinds (gate, approval, notice, needs_you outside a run wait) still merge as BQ-74 says. This departs from the explicit PRD 10.3 sentence "Same-type cards from one district within 60 s merge" for run-owned question cards: user to confirm. A group that is resolving or closed accepts no new member either way, so the code-written confirmation card raised the moment an answer commits is never hidden. | PRD 10.3, 10.6 FR-HIL-02, FR-HIL-10, BQ-74, BQ-235 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-263 | ADR-0018 T1: the ADR says `artifact.created` carries the stored hash "so the Trace shows the hash the store holds", but a registry `trace_row` may name only required data fields (contracts-check), and the three new fields are optional so that 1.0.0 events, intake and channels stay valid. How does the Trace show the hash? | The registry entry stays `artifact={data.artifact}` (no registry change in T1). The Trace shows `data.content_sha256`, `media_type` and `size_bytes` from the event data when present (the S-11 detail view, a web ticket), and `GET .../manifest` (T6) serves the manifest with the same hash. If a Trace column is wanted, the registry check may later allow optional placeholders that render empty when absent (a minor registry change). | ADR-0018 4 and 6; contracts/event.rules.md registry checks; PRD 9.4, 9.5 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-264 | ADR-0018 AR-07 (security review r1 of #366): only `clean` versions may leave Tilo without a person, but no outbox rule reads the version record. OB-05 relies on the policy engine's `safety_flag` modifier, which `tilo_policy` takes as a caller-supplied bool (default false). Should the outbox contract also say it? | AR-07 makes the policy engine the check point: for an action whose `item` is an artifact version, `safety_flag` is on unless that version's record, read from the store by `artifact_id` and `version`, says `clean`, so a flagged or unscreened version is T3 and needs a person. Needs contract change (outbox, not made here): add to OB-05 "An entry whose `item` names an artifact version lists `safety_flag` in `modifiers` unless that version's record (`artifact.schema.json`) has `screening` `clean`", with an invalid crossref case. Until then, the P4 and T6 callers of `tilo_policy` set `SideEffectFacts.safety_flags` from the record, never from a default. | ADR-0018 AR-07; outbox.rules.md OB-05; PRD 10.4, 16.3; packages/policy/src/tilo_policy/tiers.py | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-274 | FR-CHN-11, ADR-0013, issue #298 (CHN-GATE-C, first bullet; the tests cite it as BQ-223): a clean draft ("Yes, confirmed.") answering a risky inbound message ("please confirm the transfer of $5,000") has a clean own body, so it rode L2 and was sent after the undo window with no person. Which message counts, which words, and what when it cannot be found? | Default taken (the caller's spec; the PRD names no inbound families): the replied-to message is the thread message whose `Message-ID` header equals the draft's `in_reply_to` (compared as the Drafter wrote it, `_reply_header`); only that message counts, not earlier ones. It carries a signal when `autopilot_t3.inbound_signal(body)` is not empty: the same families as `t3_content_signal` (money, legal, commitment, a currency sign, hidden or look-alike characters, over `SIGNAL_CAP` as obfuscated, fail closed), without "unlisted" (an inbound message is free text, not held to the L2 allowlist). Inbound only (fix pass after security review round 1 of PR #389), "money" also on an amount shape with no money word (a digit group with a decimal part or thousands separator, a run of 3+ digits that is not a 2020-2039 year, "%", a number with an optional space and a magnitude suffix k, m, mn, b, bn, cr, l, lac or lacs such as "5m", "50 K", "10L") and on a number word (hundred, thousand, million, billion, lakh, lac, crore, cr, mil, mn, bn), and splits are rejoined before the families run: punctuation is a class (fix pass 2 after security review round 2; any run of non-letter, non-digit characters, a tab or line break included, between two letters or digits is dropped, not a list of separators), single letters or digits spread apart by spaces and/or such punctuation are joined, and both orders run (split then spread, spread then split), with "@" also read as "a", so mixed forms like "w i r-e" or "s-i g n" signal; a joined spread run is also searched without word edges, so "a w i r e" is not glued into "awire". All linear: a 20k-character plain field takes under 150 ms per call (7-70 ms measured, best of 3, the slowest shapes being "i'm\n" and "5pm\n" repeated; BQ-278 (i)-(m) built 6 Oct in `_join_hits`: a space next to a line break and a time of day are piece cuts, a phrase family cut inside a word is matched by piece, the joined text is capped at 2,000 characters and over it fails closed as "obfuscated", the first hit stops the scan); the slowest shape measured, 20k single letters (not plain), takes about 0.1 s. Redesign after security review round 3 (decided 6 Oct under the user's standing delegation; stricter than the 5 Oct decision): a denylist cannot list every obfuscation, so the replied-to body and Subject must also be structurally plain, `autopilot_t3.inbound_plain` (pure, linear): after clean_text (no NFKC: a raw non-ASCII character is never plain) every character is an ASCII letter, a space, a newline, one of `. , ? ! :` (only before a space, a newline or the end) or `'` (only inside a contraction: s, t, re, ve, ll, d, m), or a digit that is part of a time of day; two or more single-letter tokens in a row are not plain (a lone "a" or "I" is), and text over `SIGNAL_CAP` is not plain. Digits are allowed only as times, so the punctuation, underscore, glue and digit-suffix classes ("the_transfer", "the.trans.fer", "5million", "10/L") are closed by construction. L2 needs both `not inbound_signal` and `inbound_plain` for the body and the Subject; real inbound mail with a link, a quoted line, a hyphen, a digit or a non-ASCII character now waits at L1 for a person. The test fixture's default Subject is now "Engine tuneup scheduling" (the old "Engine tune-up scheduling" has a hyphen). Accepted gaps (not pinned by tests): words split by plain spaces into chunks ("trans fer"), compounds and glued prefixes ("wiretransfer", "etransfer"), vocabulary gaps (neft, upi, gpay, remittance, funds, bill, purchase, offer), commitment phrases spread or split by plain words, extra magnitude words ("5MM", "fifty K", "O-for-zero" style), and, confirmed by the reviewer in round 4: letters spread with plain spaces in chunks ("w ir e", "tr an sf er"), a number word glued to its neighbour ("fivemillion") and a spelled number with a bare suffix ("fifty K"). Fix pass after security round 4: the inbound forms also check every join of up to 11 consecutive pieces cut by line breaks or marks as a whole word (so "the", line break, "trans", line break, "fer" signals; ordinary multi-line talk stays clean); `inbound_plain` runs on `clean_text(text)` with no NFKC, so a raw non-ASCII character is not plain (U+0085, U+2028, U+2029 and other format characters are removed or turned into line breaks by clean_text first) (20k costly characters are rejected in milliseconds); a time of day between single letters does not end a single-letter run ("w 5pm i 5pm r 5pm e" is not plain). Numbering: this row was BQ-265 until the ADR-0020 row took that number in the phase0-stack merge; the tests of this ticket still cite BQ-265 in their docstrings and mean this row. A draft's own `t3_content_signal` is unchanged. The replied-to Subject is read the same way as the body (a signal, or a Subject that is not text, fails closed). Text quoted inside the replied-to body counts. User decision of 5 Oct for #298 (spoofed sender): L2 only if the replied-to message's `Authentication-Results` header (exact key) is text whose every DMARC verdict is `pass` (case-insensitive) and there is at least one; fail, softfail, none, temperror, permerror, no verdict, a pass beside a failure, a missing or non-text header all fail closed. A signal, an empty `in_reply_to`, an `in_reply_to` that matches no message, an unreadable or empty thread, or a body that is not text all fail closed: the card stays L1 (window 0, no `policy.applied`, no outbox entry, no policy-port call), still tiered by its own body (the inbound signal is not a tier modifier), and the owner's Send still works. More than one message with the same Message-ID fails closed (the id names no one message; this also bounds the work). A replied-to message older than the last MAX_MESSAGES (50) messages is outside the window, is not found and fails closed. No model call. Code: `tilo_channels/autopilot_inbound.py`, `autopilot_t3.inbound_signal`. Open follow-ups: (a) a risky message earlier in the thread than the replied-to one is not read (needs a user decision: all messages, or only the last N); (b) nothing re-checks the inbound message inside the 60 s undo window; (c) the From display name is not checked, only the DMARC verdict; (d) the card for a held reply shows T2 with no reason, so the owner is not told why it waits; (e) the Gmail adapter's `_message` in `services/connectors` does not yet surface `headers` (so no `Authentication-Results` and no `Message-ID`), so in production every reply fails closed to L1 until it does: add it to the #298 / #256 mapper follow-up (built, see BQ-278 n); (f) pin the DMARC authserv-id before the adapter surfaces headers: a header "attacker.example; dmarc=pass" is trusted today, and the header key is matched case-sensitively while the Gmail adapter lower-cases header names (both closed, see BQ-278 n); (g) two-digit amounts and the years 2020-2039 as amounts ("pay 50", "2026") and the vocabulary gaps (bill, purchase, offer) need a BQ decision; (h) a replied-to message older than the MAX_MESSAGES window plus a clean duplicate of its Message-ID inside the window still passes (the id then names one message in the window). Built CHN-GATE-F (7 Oct, issue #298), closing the glued-number-word gap above: `inbound_signal` also reads "money" for a number word glued to a unit with the outbound name rule's word edges (`autopilot_t3._GLUED`): counting words (not the abbreviations `_NUMBER_WORD` matches whole, so "Milan", "Craig", "milk" stay words) then zero or more abbreviation or magnitude words `_NUMBER_WORD` knows (mil, mn, bn, cr, lac, lacs: "Fivelacrupees", "Tenmildollars"; this middle group is disjoint from the counting group, because a group of all number words is quadratic), then a unit: a magnitude suffix (k, m, b, l, repeated, ks), an abbreviation or magnitude `_NUMBER_WORD` knows, or any word `_MONEY` matches (taken from those patterns, no copied list); or a magnitude (hundred, thousand, million, billion, trillion, lakh, crore) inside any word ("Halfmillion", "Fourtythousand"). "s" is no unit ("ones"), a bare number word ("Tenant", "Fifty-fifty") is no amount, spaces still split ("Ten rm" stays an accepted gap). Linear. Outbound `t3_content_signal` is unchanged. | PRD 10.4 T3 and L2, OB-01, FR-CHN-11 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-PPL40a-1 | PPL-40a: S-26 shows evidence quotes (FR-PA-03), but `minutes.schema.json` cites utterance ids only (a `transcript_text` field is an invalid fixture) and PE-05 says the API cuts one quote from the transcript. No contract names that read model. | The fixtures keep `apps/web/src/people/fixtures/quotes.json`, a plain map from meeting and utterance id to speaker and text, typed by `EvidenceQuote` in `apps/web/src/people/data.ts`, and the screen reads quotes only through it. It is the one fixture with no schema. Needs a contract (architect, not made here): an evidence-quote read model (meeting id, utterance id, speaker, text), owner-only for the meeting Owner and one quote for an assignee. | PRD addendum FR-PA-03, FR-MTG-04; people.rules.md PE-05; PPL-40a | 2026-10-05 |

It shows no count or row for the other sources and says nothing about them on the page. Snooze is "until tomorrow" (24 hours); "Not mine" appends a `reassign_requested` history entry and is offered only on an item someone else assigned (PE-03). Which one? **Resolved 2026-10-05: the user accepted the default ("accept all of #381").** | The lowest utterance id cited by both the work item (`source.evidence`) and the minutes action item its `source.ref` names, that exists in the transcript version the minutes pin. The row says "From a meeting, assigned by <name>", as S-27 does today. The user may amend PE-05 (a minor rules change) to allow the title. | addendum 6.3; PE-05 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-267 | ADR-0020: does a viewer who is not the meeting owner get a quote from a flagged (`minutes.flagged`) or not-`clean` (`screening`) transcript? **Resolved 2026-10-05: the user accepted the default ("accept all of #381").** | No: the quote's state is `withheld` and it carries no text; the work item is flagged anyway (PE-21). | BQ-268 | ADR-0020: how long is a quote, who is named as its speaker, and what is masked? **Resolved 2026-10-05: the user accepted the default ("accept all of #381").** | Cut by code at 240 characters on a word boundary (`truncated: true`); control and format characters removed for everyone. For a non-owner: the speaker is the directory display name (PE-20) of the attendee the label resolved to, only if the viewer shares a team with that person, else null ("A participant"); email addresses, phone numbers and URLs become `[removed]`, after the secret redactor. | PE-20; SEC-01; minutes text cap 240 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-269 | ADR-0021: mail triage stores no subject, body or display name, and `common.record_ref.kind` has no `mail`, so a plate mail row has no title and the panel cannot cite it. **Resolved 2026-10-05: the user accepted the default ("accept all of #381").** | The row's title is null and reads "Mail that needs your reply" with its received time; the plate never reads Gmail to build itself. `record_ref.kind` gains `mail` (common 1.2.0 to 1.3.0, additive), the id being the triage message id. A screened subject on the row is a later decision. | FR-PA-02, FR-PA-13, FR-CHN-09; triage.py | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-270 | ADR-0021: which cards and runs are on a person's plate, and in which group? **Resolved 2026-10-05: the user accepted the default ("accept all of #381").** | Unanswered, unexpired cards the person may answer under PRD 10.1 and ADR-0006, in worlds they own or gates naming them as Approver; runs of worlds they own that are running or blocked. Gate and consent cards, blocked cards and blocked runs lead (`attention`); other open cards are `soon`; running runs are `rest`. Nothing from a world where they are only a Viewer or Team Lead. | FR-PA-02; 10.1; ADR-0006 | 2026-10-05 |

Where do they go? **Resolved 2026-10-05: the user accepted the default ("accept all of #381").** | In `parked`, apart from `rows`: snoozed items until `snoozed_until`, reassign-asked items until the assigner acts, done items for 24 hours. | BQ-CHRM-1 | CH-RM, village-basic fixtures without WhatsApp (SD-01): the contracts stream's seq 35 to 37 (card out, answer in, approval) went by WhatsApp, and `channel.message_in` / `channel.message_out` only allow `whatsapp` and `email` (no `web`). What stands in for them, and what happens to the island and the 5 card fixtures that list `whatsapp`? | Text and enum values only, no schema change: seq 35 `channel.message_out` is `email` ("Tilo sent the approval card to your inbox.", tool `channel_send`); seq 36 `channel.message_in` is `email` with `option` null instead of `approve` (an email has no reply button, only a tapped button sets `option`, and an email never approves a gate, PRD 10.3, 11.1: the person signs in); seq 37 `approval.resolved` is `web` (a signed-in session, `pin_verified` false as before). Island I2 is named "Email", not "Inbox" (a Level in the same run is already named Inbox). The five `contracts/fixtures/card/valid/*.json` lists that include `whatsapp` are left alone: `services/api` and `services/channels` tests assert it there (SEC-06 PIN path, `send_card`) and the web copy `apps/web/src/api/fixtures/cards.ts` is drift-tested against them, so they change with the WhatsApp service tests in CH-RM. The 41 outbox crossref invalid cases and the 7 invalid event fixtures still say `whatsapp` where they copy the old approval. **Update:** CH-RM PR 1 (ADR-0019) superseded the "left alone" parts: the card fixtures, the web copy and the crossref and event fixtures no longer use WhatsApp as a valid path. | PRD supplement 2026-10-05 SD-01, section 3; contracts/fixtures/streams/village-basic.jsonl; CH-RM | 2026-10-05 |

Pure code in `apps/web/src/people/calendar.ts` (`prepFor`), no model. The real read model belongs to `tilo-api`. | PRD addendum FR-CAL-05; PPL-40b | 2026-10-05 |

| BQ-BUILD-1 | FR-CON-16: what is a "person-accepted requirement"? `contracts/` has no per-requirement acceptance record; the person accepts the brief (editable chips) and picks an approach at `POST /tasks/{id}/approve`. | Accepted = the approved brief's `goal`, the one deliverable's `title`, `audience`, `constraints`, `assumptions[].assumed`, and the chosen approach's `label`, `summary` and `skips`, with the owner's approval stamp (`tilo_connectors.handoff.accepted_requirements`). Not accepted (`declined_texts`) = draft values the owner removed or changed, and the label and summary of every approach not picked. Never read: the thread, `sources`, `deadline`, `sensitivity`, `applied_preferences` and the approach's risks and estimates. A spec a unit wrote reaches the prompt only as an excerpt of an item the owner granted (FR-CON-20), which the owner then sees on the gate. A per-item "accept these requirements" step needs a contract and is not built. | Supplement s6 FR-CON-16; PRD 7.1 FR-INT-03..06 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-BUILD-2 | FR-CON-16: the `writer` role writes the partner's prompt, but a model can add a requirement nobody accepted. | Code renders the prompt (`render_prompt`, pinned in `services/connectors/tests/API_FR_CON_16_17_20.md`): the accepted texts verbatim (whitespace collapsed), granted excerpts, and one framing paragraph from the writer. The writer sees only the accepted requirements. Its framing is used only if it is short, unchanged by the secret redactor, screened clean, holds no declined text and uses only words of the accepted texts or `framing_words` (`config/build_partners.yaml`); otherwise a fixed framing is used and the reason is recorded, never a refusal. | Supplement s6 FR-CON-16, E-BUILD; PRD 16.3 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-BUILD-3 | FR-CON-17: the Lovable link format and its length limit. The task asked for the limit in `config/connectors.yaml`, but each entry there is a closed `connector.schema.json` document. | Checked on docs.lovable.dev (Build with URL, 2026-10-05): `https://lovable.dev/#prompt=<percent-encoded prompt>`, prompt at most 50,000 characters, `autosubmit` ignored (so not sent). Lovable warns that very long URLs can exceed browser limits and gives no number, so Tilo caps the whole link at 32,000 characters. Both limits live in `config/build_partners.yaml` (loader-checked, no contract). Moving them into the connector entry is a `connector.schema.json` 1.1.0 change (an optional route block). | Supplement s6 FR-CON-17; PRD 12.1, 16.2 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-BUILD-4 | FR-CON-17: how does "brief too long, offer the MCP route" read while FR-CON-18 is not built? | `BriefTooLong` before any card: `truncated` false, one offer `mcp` with `available: false` ("unavailable until FR-CON-18"), and a fixed message that gives both lengths and limits and says nothing was cut or sent. Nothing is stored, gated or enqueued. The dispatcher checks again against the config in force and fails the entry rather than cut it. | Supplement s6 FR-CON-17, E-BUILD | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-BUILD-5 | FR-CON-20: the shape of a data grant, where it is checked, and what "outside the grant" means on a route that carries only text. | `tilo_connectors.grants.DataGrant`: one task (world and run), one partner, 1 to `max_items` (6) named items (`artifact` pinned to a version, `folder`, `record`), made and revoked only by the world's owner, expired by `end_run` when the run ends. Deterministic checks happen at three points: when excerpts are read (`granted_excerpts`), in `prepare_handoff`, and in the adapter at dispatch. On the link route only clean text artifacts are read. An excerpt is a contiguous part of a granted version, passed through the redactor and then the safety screen, and listed by `art_id@version` in the gate card's `exact`. The cap of 6 keeps `exact` within 500 characters. Grants are kept in memory (Firestore later) and emit no event, so the Trace does not show them yet. Showing them needs new event types (a contract change). | Supplement s6 FR-CON-20; PRD 10.1, 16.1 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-BUILD-6 | FR-CON-16: who stores the result (link, project id, status), as what, and with which provenance? | The adapter stores it during the outbox dispatch, so no link exists without an approved entry. The link record is canonical JSON pinned in the API doc: `{v, partner, route, url, prompt, outbox_id, project_id: null, status: "link_ready"}`. It is stored through `LinkStore` as `application/json`, origin `unit` (the handoff unit and its writer agent) and AR-05 key `<run>:<unit>:<attempt>:1`; the prompt is the same unit's output 0. Later, the harness emits `artifact.created` and `artifact.delivered` (`in_app`, outbox_id null) caused by the `data.transfer`, and adds the manifests (link `inputs` = the prompt version; the prompt's `sent` revision carries the authz). The JSON shape becomes a contract when the web renders it (FR-CON-22). The #752 fixture card uses its own Lovable approval, shows the prompt payload (including any granted excerpts), uploads no source files, enforces the fixed 50,000/32,000 character caps, and cannot open on a sample world. Live getBuildHandoff remains NotBuilt until the approved stored link is bound to its handoff id and prompt hash. | Supplement s6 FR-CON-16, FR-CON-22; PRD 13.3; ADR-0018 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-BUILD-7 | FR-CON-16: the gate card must show the exact prompt and the data it carries, but a card has no prompt or data-list field (`exact` is at most 500 characters). | The prompt is the gate's `item`: the card, `approval.requested`, the outbox entry and `data.transfer` all pin the same version (CR-12, OB-05), and the web shows it as plain text, not rendered Markdown. `exact` gives the prompt length and lists every carried item by id. `build_handoff` uses capability `send` and is T3, irreversible and `external_sharing`, so Approve is never the suggested option (CR-05, CR-08). | Supplement s6 FR-CON-16, FR-CON-20; PRD 10.3 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-BUILD-8 | FR-CON-17: an `auth: none` connector never gets a `connection.*` event, so `ConnectorRegistry.list_for_world` keeps Lovable's island `locked` (cloud cover). | Left as is. How a build partner is drawn (a pier or building labelled with the partner, changed only by recorded events) is FR-CON-22 (Should). | Supplement s6 FR-CON-22; PRD 9.4 HR-1 | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-BUILD-10 | FR-CON-16, FR-CON-20: security review of the build handoff found hidden code points, a cut inside a secret and a revoke by a foreign owner getting through. | Tag characters, bidi controls and similar invisible code points refuse a prompt (BH-05) and a stray code point sends a writer framing back to the fixed one (BH-03). An excerpt is accepted only if its redacted form is a part of the redacted whole text (DG-05). `revoke` needs the grant's own `granted_by` and, when given, its `world_id`. The adapter re-checks the grant right before storing the link. Item labels are checked against declined texts (BH-04). Zero-width characters, symbols and emoji stay allowed. A revoke after the last check cannot be undone for a link already stored; a stored link's withdrawal is FR-CON-22. | Supplement s6 FR-CON-16, FR-CON-20; PRD 16.3 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-BUILD-9 | FR-CON-20 DG-04: is a requested excerpt matched against the stored text or the line-ending-normalised text? The first API draft said normalised; a CRLF file then matches a request written with LF. | Raw match, the stricter rule: the request text must be a contiguous part of the decoded text as stored (a request that spans a CRLF contains the CRLF); the excerpt is then normalised to LF and the cap `max_excerpt_chars` applies to the normalised excerpt. The API doc DG-04 row says so. | Supplement s6 FR-CON-20; PRD 16.1 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-CHRM-2 | CH-RM PR 1 (ADR-0019): with WhatsApp gone, an L2 instruction is only a signed-in `world.requested`. PRD 11.2's typed answer to "What should I say?" on the web would be a `question.answered`, which carries no words or destination, and FR-CHN-14 (amended) has the instruction typed in the mail card or assistant panel. How is that typed instruction recorded, and what replaces BQ-56's card checks now that a `world.requested` has no `card_id`, `card_kind` or `option`? | **Resolved 2026-10-05: the user chose option A (defer).** No typed mid-run instruction path is built for now: a `world.requested` is written only when a person starts a run on the web, and every L2 send keeps waiting at a gate (PI-21). A dedicated mid-run instruction event gets its own ADR later (thread, where the words land, HR-1 to HR-7 effects). The earlier default (mail card or panel writes a mid-run `world.requested`) is not applied. | ADR-0019; PRD supplement 2.1 (FR-CHN-14, SEC-06); PRD 9.4, 10.4, 11.2; contracts/outbox.rules.md OB-01; contracts/events/registry.yaml `world.requested` | 2026-10-05 |
| BQ-AGT02-T4-1 | ADR-0018 T4 names only dev mode's store ("memory backend in dev mode"). What do tilo-api's `get_artifact_store`, `get_redactor` and `get_run_archive` do in production, where no Firestore/GCS backend (T8) and no shared secret redactor (FR-MEM-05, BQ-109) exist? Which org is a run's Scope in, before sign-in? | Fail closed: each production seam raises, so `run_seams` is None and no run starts until T8 and FR-MEM-05 wire real ones (never an identity redactor). Dev mode wires one in-memory store, a stand-in redactor that masks a few well-known key shapes, and an empty raw archive that refuses appends. Every Scope's org is the constant `deps.ORG_ID` (`org_tilo`), never taken from a request. Default chosen for the user (delegated). | ADR-0018 s5 T4; PRD 15.1; FR-MEM-05; BQ-109 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-AGT02-T4-2 | #393: a refused unit must not leave a stored-but-unannounced version (it puts the store's next version ahead of EV-15's). Check before the first put, or purge on refusal? | Check first: kind and size, a `parent` the world recorded, and a dry run of the `created` manifest (claims MF-01, MF-06, sources) for every output before any put. A corrupt blob gives only that output a new key (the others keep theirs, so no second copy). No purge: `ArtifactStore` has no owner-scoped delete (only the FR-MEM-09 retention adapter, by private record path). Refusals only the store sees after an earlier output was stored (redactor failure, AR-03 archive lookup, UTF-8 or blank text, the 200 MB world cap, a backend error, a corrupt read at manifest time) can still leave one; follow-up: a store `discard(scope, ref)` for unannounced versions. | ADR-0018 s6; EV-15; #393 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-CCP3-1 | CC-PEOPLE-3: ADR-0021 lists a plate row field `evidence` ("a meeting-sourced work item with a cited utterance") without a type. A boolean, or the utterance id? | A boolean, true only on a work item from a meeting that cites an utterance; S-27 then asks `GET /v1/work-items/{id}/quote`, which picks the utterance (PE-23). The row never carries an utterance id or text. | ADR-0021 5; PE-29 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-CCP3-2 | CC-PEOPLE-3: ADR-0020 2 says a quote is `expired` for a discarded or deleted meeting, but those meetings hold null `transcript`, `minutes` and `minutes_confirmed_by` and the PE-19 tombstone keeps no transcript reference. **Resolved 2026-10-06 (supervisor): ADR-0020 decisions 2 and 3 both stand as accepted; the reviewer's closer option was taken, so the user has nothing to confirm.** | Owner route: only the meeting owner, known from the record or the PE-19 tombstone (which keeps `owner`, no transcript reference needed), gets an answer, with every id `expired` once the meeting is discarded, deleted or past `delete_transcript_after`; every non-owner gets a 404 (decision 3) whether the meeting is live, missing, discarded, deleted or tombstoned, and a meeting that never existed answers a 404 to everyone, since nobody can be its owner. Item route: the item's owner gets `expired` for a swept transcript or a missing, discarded or deleted meeting, naming the lowest utterance the item cites on every path (the swept and deleted paths no longer differ); everyone else gets the item's 404. An `expired` quote has `transcript` null. The existence-oracle analysis holds: a non-owner's answer never depends on whether or in what state the meeting exists, and the owner can tell "missing" from "live" only from their own data. PPL-11a's acceptance tests must cover a missing, a discarded and a deleted meeting on both routes. | ADR-0020 2, 3; PE-19, PE-22, PE-23 | 2026-10-06 |

(4) The fingerprint hashes `rows` as JSON with sorted keys, no whitespace, UTF-8. | BQ-276 | NFR-27: the PRD gives the owner cost alerts (80% and 100% of a uid's cap, a global daily threshold, alert-only, redacted structured log to an email alert) but no units, windows or log shape. `caps.uid_lifetime` (the SEC-16 per-uid lifetime cap, 1,000 INR, and its INR-to-credits conversion) is in neither `contracts/metering.schema.json` nor `config/metering.yaml`. | Interim defaults, in `tilo_metering.CostAlerts` (constructor arguments, no contract edit): the uid cap is in credits (`uid_cap_credits`), the global threshold in USD (`daily_usd_threshold`, via `credit_usd`); `>=` counts as crossed; the uid window is lifetime (one 80% and one 100% line per uid, ever) and the global window is the UTC reservation day (one line per day); one commit jumping both thresholds writes both lines (80% first); only priced `commit` entries count; WARNING for `uid_cap_80` and `global_daily`, ERROR for `uid_cap_100`; the line holds allowlisted keys only and `uid_ref`, a one-way digest of the uid; a failing sink is swallowed and retried on the next check; the default sink prints one JSON line to stdout. **needs contract change: `caps.uid_lifetime` (SEC-16 per-uid lifetime cap 1,000 INR and its INR-to-credits conversion) in `contracts/metering.schema.json` and `config/metering.yaml`**, then the wiring in `tilo-api` reads it instead of constructor arguments. | NFR-27; SEC-16; NFR-24; PRD 14.1 | 2026-10-06 |
