Revisit if the owner finds repeats noisy. | NFR-27 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-278 | FR-CHN-11, ADR-0013 (PR #389 security round 5, follow-ups (i)-(p), none blocking): which of these to build before the 17 Oct submission? (i) a line break between a space and a word piece ("the\ntrans\nfer") is not rejoined; (j) a time of day as the cut between two multi-letter pieces ("trans 5pm fer"); (k) a phrase family cut inside a word by a line break ("account\nnum\nber"); (l) `_joins` costs 0.24-0.51 s per 20k-character plain field; (m) test gaps (a 3-piece cut and a late cut in a 9-10 letter stem; surviving mutants joins_pairs_only and joins_stem_8); (n) authserv-id pin and Gmail adapter headers (BQ-274 e, f); (o) earlier-message scan and undo-window re-check (BQ-274 a, b); (p) From display name and the T2-with-no-reason card (BQ-274 c, d). | Defaults, one per item: (i) close it: treat a space next to a line break as a piece cut when joining (small, linear). (j) close it with (i): a time token between two letter pieces counts as a cut. (k) close it with (i): join by piece, then run the families on the joined word. (l) cap the joined length at 2,000 characters and stop at the first hit; add a timing test under 50 ms; do this with (i)-(k) in one `autopilot_inbound` follow-up. (m) add the missing 3-piece and late-cut tests with (i)-(k). (n) do before the Gmail adapter surfaces headers: pin the authserv-id to the configured Gmail value and match header names case-insensitively; until then every reply fails closed to L1, which is safe. (o) accept for now: a risky earlier message is rare and the undo window is 60 s; revisit if the user wants all messages scanned (default would be the last 5). (p) show a reason on the held card ("waiting: inbound message needs a person") as a UI ticket; leave the display name unchecked (DMARC covers the domain). Order: (i)-(m) one small PR, then (n), then (p); (o) parked. Decided 6 Oct by the supervisor: (i)-(m) built in this PR, (n) next, (o) parked, (p) display name unchecked, reason-on-held-card UI waits for Claude Design screens. Built as decided in `autopilot_t3._join_hits`: (i) a space next to a line break and (j) a time of day between two pieces are cuts (`_prepare_cuts`); (k) a phrase family cut inside a word is matched piece by piece (`_PHRASES`, joined across cuts, a word break at any whitespace-only gap or cut); (l) the joined text is capped at 2,000 characters (`JOIN_CAP`; over it the text is not read in full and fails closed as "obfuscated", so a long plain message with many line breaks waits for a person, a safe false positive), the scan stops at the first hit, and plain-ASCII text skips the per-character Unicode checks (a 20k-character plain field takes 7-70 ms, test bound 150 ms best of 3); (m) tests for a 3-piece and 4-piece cut and for a late cut in a 9-10 letter stem now kill joins_pairs_only and joins_stem_8. Built for (n) (6 Oct, branch feat/BQ-278n-gmail-headers-plate-spoof): the Gmail adapter's `_message` surfaces `headers`, a map of lower-cased names with only message-id, in-reply-to, references and authentication-results (from, to, subject, date are already fields; nothing else leaves the adapter), the FIRST occurrence of a repeated name winning because Gmail writes its own Authentication-Results above the sender's; `tilo_channels.mail_reader.ConnectorMailReader` maps the connectors' names (`id`, `body_text`, thread `id`) to the reader contract (`message_id` is the Gmail id, `body`, `headers`) and holds no connector (SEC-02: production gives it an RPC client). Consumers read headers through `drafting.headers_of` (names lower-cased; two keys that differ only by case are ambiguous and read as absent, so they fail closed). The DMARC check believes an Authentication-Results header only when its authserv-id (the text before the first ";", trimmed, case-insensitive, whole value, no version suffix) equals `gmail_authserv_id` in `config/mail_trust.yaml` (default `mx.google.com`; a missing, blank, non-text, malformed or unreadable value means no trusted id and every reply waits at L1); "attacker.example; dmarc=pass" and an id with the trusted name only as a prefix, suffix, comment or look-alike are not trusted, and the verdicts are read from the text after the id only. The older FR-CHN-11 test headers keep their `mx.example` id through a conftest fixture that points `CONFIG_PATH` at `tests/fixtures/mail_trust_mx_example.yaml`; `test_FR_CHN_11_BQ278n_authserv_pin.py` runs against the real file. Residual risk: the trust rests on Gmail writing its header first and on the id; a connector that returned a later (sender-written) Authentication-Results first would defeat it, which the adapter test pins. Fix pass after security review of PR #427: the pin proves Gmail wrote the header, not that the text inside is Gmail's (it copies the envelope sender's local part into the spf comment and `smtp.mailfrom=`, and an arc comment), so `_dmarc_pass` parses the header per RFC 8601 (`_clauses`: nested comments dropped, quoted strings kept whole, split on ";" only outside both, an unbalanced comment or quote fails closed) and counts only a top-level clause whose method is exactly `dmarc`; at least one is needed and every one must be `pass` (the older tests keep two passes at L2, so "exactly one" was not adopted); the authserv-id and the configured id are compared as ASCII (U+212A folds to k); a header over 8,000 characters is not read. The reader also carries the body shape (`has_text_part`, `has_other_parts`, `attachments`, from the Gmail adapter's `_message`) and `inbound_is_clean` needs a lone plain-text body (HTML-only, a multipart/alternative decoy, a second text part or any attachment waits at L1; a message without these fields fails closed). See BQ-286. | FR-CHN-11, PRD 10.4 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-HIL12-1 | FR-HIL-12: the share-link record (token hash, world, card, approver, assigned_at, expiry, revoked) and its create and revoke events have no contract, so the record is API-internal and in-memory (a restart or a second Cloud Run instance loses links), and revoking a link leaves no audit event (SEC-11). The token is also in the request path, so the platform's own request log (Cloud Run) can hold it, which the app cannot mask; the app masks only its uvicorn access log. | Needs a contract change (ADR): a `share_link` schema and `share.created` / `share.revoked` events carrying the `authz`, with the store behind Firestore and the token sent in a header or fragment-fetched body instead of the path. Until then the link is short-lived (BQ-HIL12-2), needs the named Approver's sign-in, and opens one gate; nothing in an event, card or the app's log holds the token. | FR-HIL-12; PRD 10.1, 16.2; SEC-06, SEC-11; BQ-32 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-HIL12-2 | FR-HIL-12: how long does a share link live? PRD 10.3's 24 h is for email question links only. | 24 h by default, from `config/sharing.yaml` (`sharing.link_ttl_hours`, read on every request; a value outside (0, 168] falls back to 24). The link and the Approver's view of the card end together at expiry, or earlier when the owner revokes; the owner keeps the gate either way. | FR-HIL-12; PRD 10.3 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-HIL12-3 | FR-HIL-12: the legacy `CardStore.submit(assignee=...)` seam still lets a harness-named approver see and answer a gate with no owner-made `assigned_by` record, against BQ-32's stricter reading (`open_gate` still refuses it, but the card is shown and answerable). Close it? | Follow-up, not this ticket: only `share_gate` should set an assignee, and `submit` should drop the parameter once no caller needs it. | FR-HIL-12; BQ-32; OB-01 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-HIL12-4 | FR-HIL-12: a share re-raises the gate as a new card with its own `outbox_id`; what should the run do with a re-raised gate, and what stops the owner approving both cards? | RESOLVED in the fix pass of PR #428: the new card joins the original's group, so a gate and its re-raises are ONE wait (the first answer on any card closes them all in the one CardStore step, so a later answer on either gets 400 `already_resolved` and writes nothing). The run waits for an answer on any card of the gate, then sends once under that card's `outbox_id`, with that card's `approval.requested` as `reserved_by` and `open_gate` run against it. A gate has at most one live share (a new share ends the earlier one's assignment); a shared card is never shared again; the shared proposal keeps the original's actor and names it as its cause. A revoked or replaced share's card stays answerable by id for the owner (the acceptance tests pin it) but the owner's list shows the gate once. | FR-HIL-12; BQ-32; OB-01 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-279 | FR-PA-07: the tap "runs FR-CHN-11 unchanged (tiers, undo)"; PE-10 says `draft_reply` "makes a draft (T1), sends nothing and never takes the Autopilot path". Can a tap ride the L2 undo window? | No. A tap carries no instruction text (the `ui_action` has only a thread) and FR-CHN-11 already keeps a blank instruction off L2 (BQ-222), so the same `Autopilot.preview` gives an L1 card and a send only after the owner's approval. Tiers (T2, T3 modifiers) and the undo window of an L2 send for a typed instruction are untouched. | PE-10, FR-PA-07, BQ-222, BQ-CHRM-2 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-280 | FR-PA-07: which module owns the tap? | `tilo_channels.plate_reply.PlateReply`, a thin wrapper over the Drafter and the Autopilot (no send path of its own, SEC-03); tilo-api's plate action (PPL-11a) and the panel's `draft_reply` call it. The plate builder is not built and `plate.schema.json` is not merged, so it starts from the mail id of a row (`ref.kind` "mail"). | ADR-0021, FR-PA-16 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-281 | FR-PA-07: what is "on the plate" for the tap? | A triage result stored for exactly (user, message id) whose effective category is `needs_reply` (the person's label beats the model; a safety flag beats a label), the message still readable, and a connected mailbox of her own. "Already answered or dismissed" stays with the plate builder (ADR-0021 decision 2). | ADR-0021, FR-CHN-09 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-282 | FR-PA-07: what is the idempotency key of a tap? | `request_id`, the id of the tap (tilo-api mints it); the FR-CHN-10 `instruction_id` (and so the card id) is a hash of it with the mail id, so a replay, restart or second instance gives one card and one model call, while a new tap on the same mail is a new draft. A blank `request_id` is a caller bug and raises `ValueError`. | FR-CHN-10, OB-01 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-283 | FR-PA-07: which reply mode does the tap use? | `reply` (to the sender, no Cc). Reply-all, forward and new mail stay with typed instructions (they add recipients, FR-CHN-12). | PRD 11.4 Formats | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-284 | FR-PA-07: what does `draft_reply` return for a mail that is not on the plate (another person's, unknown, unreadable, not needs_reply, flagged, no mailbox, or not the world's owner), and what if the thread moved on? | None for every refusal, with nothing drafted, stored, emitted or queued and no reason given, so a caller cannot probe another person's mail; tilo-api maps None to 404 (PE-01). The owner is checked first (`Autopilot.owns`), before any read. The reply must answer the plate mail itself: `Drafter.reply_target` (read only, before drafting) returns the reader's `message_id` (the Gmail message id, which no sender writes) of the message a reply would answer, and it must equal the tapped mail id, so a later message from a stranger or a flagged sender in the same thread gets None, and so does a later message that copies the plate mail's Message-ID header or writes it with look-alike characters (the header text is never compared for this; a mail with no Message-ID header still gets None, fail closed, because nothing proves what the draft answers). The thread is read again by the drafter: `Drafter.draft(answers=<mail id>)` raises `ReplyTargetMoved` from the second read, before the model call and before anything is stored or emitted, when the reply target is no longer that message, and `draft_reply` answers None (a retry once the thread is stable drafts normally); a draft already stored for the same tap is returned as stored, and its `in_reply_to` is still compared with the mail's own header. A read that finds nothing (`LookupError` or a Gmail 404 `status`) is a refusal; any other read failure propagates. Built: the production reader (`ConnectorMailReader` over the connectors' Gmail read, whose messages now carry a `headers` map) gives the plate reply what it needs, proved end to end over the real `GmailAdapter` and `FakeGmailApi` (a tap on an on-plate mail yields the L1 card). | PE-01, PE-10, FR-PA-17, FR-CHN-10 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-INT15-1 | FR-INT-15: what does "outside what Tilo can fully do" mean in code, and how is a gap told apart from a refusal? | A closed list in `config/capability_gaps.yaml` (7 categories, each a release limit the PRD states: 3.4, 7, 12, 12.2, 12.4, 12.5, 23.1), separate from `out_of_policy.yaml`, ids never shared (checked before any Director call). The Director names `{category, part}` in its existing brief call; code shows only configured reason and alternative beside the label. A refusal id anywhere in the reply refuses the whole task; an unknown or near-miss id fails closed. What a plan may use stays the registry and skills (PG-11); the brief prompt lists the connected services from `config/connectors.yaml`, so a new connector shrinks `unconnected_service` with no change, and a connector that fills a whole category removes it in the same change. Spec: `packages/planning/tests/API_FR_INT_15.md`. | Supplement 4 (FR-INT-15), FR-INT-12, PRD 16.3 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-INT15-2 | FR-INT-15: where do the gaps live without a frozen-contract change? | Not in `brief.schema.json`: `assumptions` means "Assumed: ..." and `constraints` are chips the person may edit or delete (FR-INT-03), which would let a gap vanish. Gaps go `ExtractionResult.gaps` -> the task record -> the `POST /intake` body (`gaps`), and into the PlanGraph as the first lines of every approach's `skips` ("What this approach leaves out"), code-written, with the model's room cut to 5 minus the gap count. No contract changes. A brief 1.1.0 optional `not_done` field would read better on S-02 and at delivery; it needs the user's approval and is not needed for the acceptance. | plan.schema.json approach.skips, FR-INT-03, FR-INT-06 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-INT15-3 | FR-INT-15: a task with a safe part and an unsafe part: refuse all of it, or do the safe part? | Refuse all of it with FR-INT-12's configured reason and alternative. A refusal id in either `out_of_policy` or `out_of_scope` wins over any brief or gap. Doing the safe part of a task whose instruction asks for a policy breach could serve the unsafe aim, a model never decides authorisation (PRD 16), and FR-INT-12's "100% refused" holds only if no refused task partly runs. The alternative names the nearest task Tilo can do, so the person can resubmit the safe part. | PRD 16, 16.3, FR-INT-12 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-INT15-4 | FR-INT-15: what happens when Tilo can do no part of a safe task? | `NothingPossible`: the Director returns no deliverable and at least one gap; `POST /intake` answers 422 `{error: <configured lead>, gaps: [...]}` with no `category` (it is not a refusal), stores nothing and makes no further call. Not chosen: always delivering a substitute document, which would change the person's task without asking. No deliverable and no gap stays a fail-closed `BriefExtractionError`. | Supplement 4 (FR-INT-15) | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-INT15-5 | FR-INT-15: how many gaps per task, and what if the Director names more, an unknown id or a malformed item? | At most 3, after identical gaps collapse. More, an unknown or near-miss id (exact match, no normalisation), a malformed item or an unusable label is a fail-closed `BriefExtractionError` (502, nothing stored) on the one Director call. Never truncated: a cut list would drop a part without saying so. Amended after the security review (6 Oct): `out_of_scope` is unconstrained in the response schema (`{}`), because the router rejects a schema violation before code sees it, repairs once per candidate and escalates (up to 4 calls for the director's two profiles), and a repair is a fresh generation that can drop a refusal id. Every shape and length check is code, and the refusal scan runs first over whatever the reply holds: the value itself if a string, each string element, and each mapping element's string values (`category` first), even in an element that is otherwise invalid; a non-string `category` is simply invalid. Revisit with a dedicated "too many parts" message if live runs hit the cap. Follow-up (existing FR-INT-12 behaviour, not this ticket): `out_of_policy` and the brief's own fields still meet the router first, so a refusal beside a malformed brief field is repaired before code reads it. | PRD 9.4 (honesty), FR-INT-12 fail-closed precedent, security review of FR-INT-15 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-INT15-6 | FR-INT-15: who writes the words the person reads about a gap? | `reason`, `alternative` and the no-part lead come from config. The part label is the Director's, at most 60 characters after removing control, format, private-use and unassigned characters and the blank-looking U+115F, U+1160, U+3164, U+FFA0 and U+2800, and collapsing whitespace (no NFKC); it must keep at least one letter or digit, so a blank-looking or marks-only label is a `BriefExtractionError`, never a gap. It is shown beside configured text, like a brief chip. The skips line template `Not done: {part}. {reason}` is a code constant (at most 192 characters). | PRD 16.3, BQ-203 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-INT15-7 | FR-INT-15: where does the "20 partly-out-of-scope briefs" eval live? | With the planning tests, as FR-INT-12's refusal set does (BQ-203): `packages/planning/tests/fixtures/best_effort_set.yaml` (20 cases plus refusal and no-part controls) and `test_FR_INT_15_best_effort_eval_set.py`, a scripted Director behind the real Router, no paid call. No `config/evals.yaml` group: `contracts/evals.schema.json` allows exactly the eight PRD 19.2 groups, so a ninth is a contract change, and the frozen bank (`evals/bank/`) needs the user's approval. How often a real Director names the right gap is a live-eval question for the user (`LIVE=1`). | PRD 19.2, EFF-01, BQ-67 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-INT15-8 | FR-INT-15: is a payment request a gap or a refusal, and is non-English output a gap? | Moving money with the person's approval is a gap (`money_movement`): PRD 3.4 and 23.5 rule out autonomous movement of money and no payment connector exists, so the rest of the task proceeds. A payment "without asking" stays the `unapproved_send` refusal. `unapproved_send`'s alternative no longer suggests payments Tilo would make ("or a payment list you pay yourself"). Non-English plans and deliverables are a gap (`non_english`), per PRD 7 and 23.1. | PRD 3.4, 7, 23.1, 23.5 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-INT15-9 | FR-INT-15: where does the person see the gaps? | In the `POST /intake` body and on S-03 (each approach already shows "Skips: ..."), and in the PlanGraph. An S-02 "Not done" list and a reminder on the delivery screen (reading the chosen approach's skips) are UI follow-ups for the web lane; no event type is added (gaps do not animate, HR rules). | PRD 9.4, 18.3 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-CAL8-1 | FR-CAL-08 says "push or poll only inside the working window" but gives no window, and `config/personal.yaml` (CC-PEOPLE-2, with a "reminder window") does not exist yet; `person` has a `time_zone` but no working hours. Where is the window and is it per person? | A new `config/personal.yaml` with `working_window: {start: "09:00", end: "18:00"}` (FR-PA-05's reminder window) and a `calendar_sync` block, each read by its own loader, which ignores other top-level keys. One window for everyone, evaluated in each person's own `time_zone`; start before end (no overnight window). CC-PEOPLE-2 adds the file's contract schema and keeps both keys; FR-PA-05 and FR-PA-15 read the same `working_window`. Per-person hours need a `person` field (a contract change) and are not built. | Addendum 3.2 FR-PA-05, 3.4 FR-CAL-08, 7 CC-PEOPLE-2; `services/connectors/tests/API_FR_CAL_08.md` | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-CAL8-2 | Is the first sync right after a person connects Calendar outside their working window allowed, or does it wait for the window? | It waits: no Google Calendar request goes out outside the person's window, ever, so the acceptance clause holds without exceptions. S-29 (FR-CAL-01) says the calendar fills during the person's working hours. The user may instead allow one person-triggered sync at connect time. | Addendum 3.4 FR-CAL-02, FR-CAL-08 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-CAL8-3 | Push or poll? FR-CAL-08 allows either inside the window. | Poll only: one incremental sync per person every 15 minutes inside the window (`poll_interval_s: 900`). Push (`events.watch`) needs a public webhook, channel renewal and verification, and saves nothing on a free API. A later push would only mark the person due; the same window gate applies. | Addendum 3.4 FR-CAL-08, 4 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-CAL8-4 | What does the cache hold of a Google calendar? PE-13 fixes the fields, not the scope or the edge cases. | The person's `primary` calendar only, one entry per instance (`singleEvents`). A full sync lists now - 7 days to now + 366 days; a changed instance outside that horizon is not cached, and entries that ended before it are evicted. Known gap: an instance more than 366 days out at the last full sync appears only after it changes or a later full sync (the fix, a daily horizon-extension listing, is not built). Cancelled events, events the person declined and `workingLocation` events (a place, PE-13) are not cached. `attendee_count` counts other attendees, not the person or rooms, capped at 500. All-day dates become local midnights in the person's zone, end exclusive, never null. "Show as free" events are cached like any other and so count as busy for FR-CAL-06 (no contract field for transparency). An empty title is "(No title)"; links and email addresses in a title become "[removed]" (no conference link or attendee address is stored) and control and format characters are dropped (the PE-24 set). | PE-13, PE-19, PE-24 (CC-PEOPLE-3); addendum 6.7 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-CAL8-5 | PE-21 needs `flagged` on every calendar entry, and the addendum (6.6, E-RED) says calendar text is untrusted and screened, but nothing says when a synced Google title is screened. | The sync screens each new or changed title once through the SafetyProvider, reuses the verdict while the title is unchanged, and fails closed (an error or any answer but an explicit `flagged: false` stores `flagged: true`). These are SafetyProvider calls during a sync inside the window, never a model call and never on render; recurring instances of one title cost one call. | PE-21; addendum 6.6, 5 (E-RED); FR-CAL-08 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-CAL8-6 | `calendar.synced` has a world and HUD effect ("the Clock Tower bell rings once"). Is it written for every poll? | Only after a committed sync that is full or changed something; a no-change poll (most of the 36 a day) records nothing. At most once per commit; a failed emit is not retried (the cache is the truth). Written in the person's Desk world from the stored connection (PE-12), with `actor: system` and a count, never a title. | Registry `calendar.synced`; PE-12; HR-1 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-CAL8-7 | The addendum names `calendar.readonly`; Google's narrower `calendar.events.readonly` covers `events.list`, which is all this release needs (free/busy for teammates comes from Tilo's own cache, PE-04). Which scope? | `calendar.readonly`, as FR-CAL-02 and the user's consent-screen step (addendum 9.3) say. **The user may choose `calendar.events.readonly`**: then `config/connectors.yaml`, the loader's check and the OAuth consent screen change together. | Addendum 3.4 FR-CAL-02, 9.3; PRD 16.1 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-CAL8-8 | When is a title screened? API_FR_CAL_08.md step 6 reuses the stored `flagged` only for an entry whose own stored title is unchanged, but also says recurring instances of one title cost one call. Does a NEW instance of a series whose title was already screened in an earlier run (a daily series gains one instance a day inside the 366-day horizon) cost a call? | Yes, by the literal rule: an entry with no stored predecessor is screened once per distinct title per run, so a new instance of a known title costs one SafetyProvider call (never a model call, never on render); instances that arrive together in one run share one call. The acceptance test pins only: one call per distinct new or changed title in a run, none for an unchanged entry, none on render. A per-person title-to-verdict reuse would save those calls and is a later optimisation. | FR-CAL-08 (c); PE-21; BQ-CAL8-5; `services/connectors/tests/API_FR_CAL_08.md` step 6 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-SEC15-1 | SEC-15: how does a judge get live models "never forced to replay" without a request turning on the process-wide `LIVE=1` gate (EFF-01, a hard rule), which `RecordReplayProvider._is_live()` and every adapter read from the environment? | **Default (user delegated, may override):** the gate is not loosened. `Router`, `build_provider` and the three live classes take an explicit, injected `live_permitted` (default False); `deps.router_for_mode("live")` is the only caller that passes True, and it is reached only when `access.provider_mode` returns "live": the user's own `LIVE=1`, or a deployed process (`K_SERVICE`/`CLOUD_RUN_JOB`) with a stored judge declaration and `TILO_JUDGE_LIVE=1`. A permitted call never records (`TILO_RECORD` is read only under `LIVE=1`). No request reads or writes `LIVE`; dev, CI and every test stay replay/fake. Not covered: the `safety` role still follows the global switch (its live adapter is not built, ADR-0003), so a judge's live intake needs that wired first. User confirmed 2026-10-06: judges signed in with Google may use live models (user confirmed 2026-10-06: judges signed in with Google may use live models, within the SEC-16 caps and only after the live safety role and real sign-in exist; TILO_JUDGE_LIVE stays unset until the user flips it). | EFF-01; PL-01; SEC-15; ADR-0003 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-SEC15-2 | SEC-15: which variable lets a deployment give judges live models? | `TILO_JUDGE_LIVE=1` (exactly "1"), user-owned like `LIVE=1`: off by default, never set in code, infra defaults, scripts or CI, and only meaningful when deployed. The PRD names no variable; this is the smallest option that keeps "only the user sets the live switch". | SEC-15; EFF-01 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-SEC15-3 | SEC-15: "never forced to replay" versus the NFR-24 kill switch (US$25/day). | The kill switch wins: `provider_mode(..., replay_only=True)` is always replay, and `GET /access` then reports `models: "replay"` (PRD FR-MTR-04 and 17.3; the caps are the real protection, SEC-16). A judge is never made replay by the deployment default, only by the cost switch. | SEC-15; NFR-24; SEC-16 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-SEC15-4 | SEC-15: what happens with a missing declaration, a second answer, or no store? | Missing: 403 `declaration_required` (fail closed, so the web asks the question again). Set once in both directions: a different second answer is 409 `declaration_locked` and the stored value stays (no PRD text allows a change; an owner-side admin change is out of scope). No store wired (`deps.get_access_store` default) or a store error: 503 `not_configured`, so a deployed process with no store gives nobody access. Signed out stays 401 and the store is not read. | SEC-15; SEC-16 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-SEC15-5 | SEC-15: where is access enforced, and what stays public? | Only when deployed (`K_SERVICE` or `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` present, read per request); dev mode (`just dev-api`) and CI are unchanged. A pure ASGI middleware (`tilo_api.access.AccessGate`) runs before routing, so before any body is read or world looked up, and refuses every `(method, path)` outside `PUBLIC_ROUTES` (`GET /health`, `GET /healthz`, `GET /access`, `POST /access/declaration`), including unknown paths (no oracle). It resolves the principal and store through `app.dependency_overrides` with no arguments, so a future real sign-in dependency that needs the request must be adapted here. The web shows the sign-up question and the under-construction page (`apps/web/src/access/`); wiring `AccessGate` into `App` waits for a real sign-in, which does not exist yet. | SEC-15; PRD 16.1 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-SEC15-6 | SEC-15 security review: may anyone set `TILO_JUDGE_LIVE=1` on the deployed service? | **Ask the user first; nobody sets it.** Anyone who signs in can declare themselves a judge (the declaration is a claim, not a check), the SEC-16 per-uid rate limit and lifetime cap are not built, and nothing in production sets `replay_only` yet (BQ-238), so the switch would let any signed-in person spend on live models with no cap and no kill switch. Turn it on only after SEC-16 and the BQ-238 wiring exist, and only by the user. | SEC-15; SEC-16; NFR-24; BQ-238; EFF-01 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-SEC15-7 | SEC-15 security review round 1: follow-ups that are not blocking, kept so none is lost. | The gate is hardened in this ticket: websocket scopes go through the same decision and are closed (1008) before accept, only `lifespan` passes untouched, and tests pin HEAD, OPTIONS, trailing and doubled slash, `%2F`, `/docs`, `/openapi.json` and the wrong verb on `/access/declaration`. Open: (a) enforcement keys only on `K_SERVICE` or `CLOUD_RUN_JOB`, so any other host (a VM, a container run by hand) serves ungated; deploy only on Cloud Run, or add an explicit `TILO_ENFORCE_ACCESS` switch later. (b) `AccessGate._decide` and the access routes must check the same verified token once real sign-in exists (one principal resolution, never two that can disagree); today `_decide` calls the principal override with no arguments. (c) `AccessGate` is not mounted in `App` (waits for real sign-in). Update 6 Oct: resolved, `AccessGate` is mounted (`app.add_middleware(_AccessGate)` at `services/api/src/tilo_api/__init__.py:29`). (d) An in-flight live run keeps its router after `replay_only` is switched on, because the Router is built once per request or run; the kill switch stops new work, not a run already started. (e) `HEAD /health` is gated (403) and a CORS preflight (`OPTIONS`) would be refused; Cloud Run's smoke check uses GET, and the web is served same-origin, so neither bites today; allow them explicitly if that changes. (f) The 4 mypy errors at `tilo_api/__init__.py:21` (the `FastAPI(**_DOCS)` line) already existed on the base, not from this ticket (phase0-stack has since fixed them with a `dict[str, Any]` annotation). (g) The Firestore `AccessStore.declare` must be one transactional create-if-absent (documented in `access.py` and `API_SEC_15.md`). (h) **Open: the approver path.** Once deployed, share links (`/share/{token}`, FR-HIL-12) are refused for non-judges, because the gate lets in only a signed-in judge and an Approver must be signed in. Default to record (proposed in the sign-in PR, no test yet): approvers sign in with Google and pass the gate as a person with a gate assignment, never as a judge, so a share link opens for the named Approver without a judge declaration and without live models. | SEC-15; SEC-16; NFR-24 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-284 | FR-PA-07: what does `draft_reply` return for a mail that is not on the plate (another person's, unknown, unreadable, not needs_reply, flagged, no mailbox, or not the world's owner), and what if the thread moved on? | None for every refusal, with nothing drafted, stored, emitted or queued and no reason given, so a caller cannot probe another person's mail; tilo-api maps None to 404 (PE-01). The owner is checked first (`Autopilot.owns`), before any read. The reply must answer the plate mail itself: `Drafter.reply_target` (read only, before drafting) returns the reader's `message_id` (the Gmail message id, which no sender writes) of the message a reply would answer, and it must equal the tapped mail id, so a later message from a stranger or a flagged sender in the same thread gets None, and so does a later message that copies the plate mail's Message-ID header or writes it with look-alike characters (the header text is never compared for this; a mail with no Message-ID header still gets None, fail closed, because nothing proves what the draft answers). The thread is read again by the drafter: `Drafter.draft(answers=<mail id>)` raises `ReplyTargetMoved` from the second read, before the model call and before anything is stored or emitted, when the reply target is no longer that message, and `draft_reply` answers None (a retry once the thread is stable drafts normally); a draft already stored for the same tap is returned as stored, and its `in_reply_to` is still compared with the mail's own header. A read that finds nothing (`LookupError` or a Gmail 404 `status`) is a refusal; any other read failure propagates. Built: the production reader (`ConnectorMailReader` over the connectors' Gmail read, whose messages now carry a `headers` map) gives the plate reply what it needs, proved end to end over the real `GmailAdapter` and `FakeGmailApi` (a tap on an on-plate mail yields the L1 card). | PE-01, PE-10, FR-PA-17, FR-CHN-10 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-285 | FR-CHN-11, ADR-0013 (PR #423 reviews, follow-ups to BQ-278 (i)-(m), none blocking): (a) a stop mark between a space and a line break ("the . \ntrans") is not a piece cut; (b) the time pattern also swallows "am" and "pm" inside a split word ("trans 5 am fer" reads as a time); (c) the commitment family pattern for "(we or i) (am, are, 'm or 're) going to" never matches "i'm going to", because the apostrophe is not whitespace, so the phrase table `autopilot_t3._PHRASES` covers a case the regex does not. Which to fix? (d) a word can mix a time used as a cut with a time read as look-alike letters ("the\nf 5pm 3:35\nnow" is fees; plain and clean on the base too, found in the PR #423 security re-check). | Defaults, none built in PR #423: (a) treat a space next to a stop mark that touches a line break as part of the cut (small, linear); (b) accept: the cut form fails closed and a plain space still signals; (c) fix the regex so the apostrophe forms need no whitespace before them and add the plain-text test, then keep `_PHRASES` in step. Do (a) and (c) with the next `autopilot_t3` change; (b) parked. (d) in the look-alike pass also try each time as a cut; do it with (a)-(c) in one small follow-up, with a test that checks the family, not only not-clean. | FR-CHN-11, PRD 10.4 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTR10-1 | FR-MTR-10: what does "tier" mean in "cost per task by tier"? | The world tier (outpost, village, town, city) from `world.planned` `data.tier`, as PRD 14.5 and EV-07 use it; null when a run was never planned. Grouping by risk tier (T1-T3) needs a cost-attribution rule first (no cost is recorded against a risk tier, and a task spans several), written into a contract or ADR; not built. | PRD 14.5, EV-07, PRD 9.1 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTR10-2 | FR-MTR-10: how does a ledger entry map to a task type? | `by_task_type` assumes `LedgerEntry.step == plan.units[].id` and reads `unit_type` from the run's plan; a step the plan does not list is reported under null. Nothing in `contracts/` says a step is a plan unit (MT-03 says "step"), so this needs an ADR or contract rule. A task itself has no type; only units do. | `contracts/metering.rules.md` MT-03, plan.schema.json | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTR10-3 | FR-MTR-10: "per task" or "per successful run"? | "Per task": every run with spend counts (in `by_tier` also a planned run with no spend), `credits_per_task` divides by those runs, and `totals.completed_tasks` is reported beside so a dashboard can divide by completed runs. EV-07 says "per successful run". | PRD 14.5, EV-07 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTR10-4 | FR-MTR-10: where does tilo-api get the ledger? | Nowhere yet: `deps.get_ledger` fails closed (503 `not_configured`) until the Treasury and a ledger store are wired. PRD 14.4's `runs` table is not in `contracts/` or `infra/`, and the ledger has no `world_id` (runs map to worlds through `WorldRecord.run_id` and the events). Tests inject the Treasury's in-memory ledger. | PRD 14.4, FR-MTR-02 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTR10-5 | FR-MTR-10: what does the dashboard do when a `memory.hit` disagrees with its own costs (EV-09) or is malformed? | `cost_report` raises (as the meter does); the routes refuse that report with 422 `inconsistent_ledger` instead of a 500, for `GET /costs` the whole report. No figure is shown rather than a guessed one. Open: quarantine the bad hit and still show spend. | EV-09, FR-MTR-06 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-AGT15-1 | FR-AGT-15: PRD 8.2 asks for "the domain expert's approval" but PRD 10.1 has no expert role. Who reviews a proposal? | A Team Lead of the skill's domain from a current, owner-assigned `config/teams.yaml` record, resolved on every call; never the proposer or the answering Lead, and the owner as such is refused | PRD 8.2, 8.3, 10.1 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-AGT15-2 | FR-AGT-15: who drafts the skill change, and how is it versioned? | Deterministic code, no model (PRD 16.1): the answer or typed guidance is appended to SKILL.md under a code-owned heading, `skill.yaml` `version` gets a patch bump and `reviewer` the approver; the diff covers both files and approval is refused as `stale` if the skill moved on | PRD 8.2, 16.1 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-AGT15-3 | FR-AGT-15: who may promote a Team Lead's answer, and where do proposals live? | Only the Team Lead who gave it (PRD 10.5: the action is in their console); the owner promoting is left open. Proposals are in memory behind `SkillProposals` like `CardStore`; a durable store is a later ticket | PRD 10.5 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-AGT15-4 | FR-AGT-15: tilo-api has no Decision store yet and the proposal needs no new event. | `tilo_api.skill_proposals.get_decision_store` is a dependency that answers 503 `not_configured` until a store is wired; no `skill.change_*` event (a Trace event needs a contract change first) | PRD 8.2, 13.1 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11B-1 | PPL-11b, FR-PA-13: a fact intent with no rows ("Nothing is waiting for you") has no record to cite, and `assistant.replied` requires at least one source for `answered` (HR-1). What outcome is it? | `declined`, with the intent's fixed `empty` text from `config/assistant.yaml` and no sources. A separate `empty` outcome would be an additive contract change (minor bump of the `assistant.replied` data schema) if the user wants the Trace to tell the two apart. | FR-PA-13, PE-11, HR-1 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11B-2 | PPL-11b, FR-PA-19: "per-conversation caps of 8,000 input and 1,000 output tokens". Per call or summed over a conversation id? | Per call, on the `assistant` role (`max_input_tokens: 8000`, `max_output_tokens: 1000` in `config/models*.yaml`; the Router compacts the ROWS block or refuses, EFF-03). The panel rebuilds the model context from read-model rows on every question and never carries history, so one call is the whole conversation context. No cumulative per-conversation ledger: the 50-credit daily cap bounds a person's total. If a summed cap is meant, `MeteredDecider` gains a per-(person, conversation) token ledger. | FR-PA-19, EFF-03 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11B-3 | PPL-11b, FR-PA-19, FR-MTR-06: which event records the saving of a panel cache hit? | `memory.hit` (EV-09 shape: `origin_cost` = the credits the cached decision cost, `lookup_cost` 0, `saved` = `origin_cost`) in the person's Desk world, `cause` = the `assistant.replied` id, only when `origin_cost > 0`, so `credits_saved` counts it. Its registry description names the FR-MEM-02 fit check, which the panel cache does not run; the Story line ("reused an earlier result and saved N credits") stays true. No contract change. | FR-PA-19, FR-MTR-06, EV-09 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11B-4 | PPL-11b: `deps.EventWriter` says `trace` is writer-only, but the contract fixture `assistant.replied--navigated_by_model.json` carries `model_role`, `profile`, tokens and `archive_ref`, and tilo-api itself makes the panel's model call. | tilo-api's `assistant.replied` part carries the trace for an uncached model call (actor `A_assistant`, `archive_ref` = the `model_call` raw-archive record); rule answers and cache hits carry the null trace (actor `system` for rules). The dev writer already merges a caller's trace; the production writer must accept it for tilo-api's own model calls. | FR-PA-19, PRD 9.3 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11B-5 | PPL-11b, FR-PA-19: which day does the 50-credit cap count, and how is a call sized against it? | The UTC day (BQ-44). Each model call reserves its worst case first (8,000 in and 1,000 out at the dearest candidate of the role, priced from `config/prices.yaml`) and passes it to the Router as the call's budget (PL-03), then commits the actual cost; so the cap is never passed (except a provider reporting more usage than the Router counted, committed in full) and the last 1 to 2 credits of a day stay unused. Panel spend is not yet posted to the Treasury ledger, `caps.user_day_credits` (1,500) or the SEC-16 per-uid lifetime cap; it joins them when those ledgers are wired into tilo-api. | FR-PA-19, PL-03, SEC-16 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11B-6 | PPL-11b: the spend ledger and the decision cache are process-wide in-memory objects. On Cloud Run each instance has its own, so the daily cap holds per instance, and rule answers have no rate limit. | In memory until a shared store exists (a Firestore transaction for the ledger; the cache may stay per instance); before public traffic either one instance serves tilo-api or the shared ledger lands. The SEC-16 per-uid rate limit, when built, covers the panel route too. | FR-PA-19, SEC-16 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11B-7 | PPL-11b, FR-PA-13: "what changed" has no change-feed read model. | Rows of the person's own plate whose `at` lies in the 24 hours before `as_of`, calendar rows left out (their `at` is a start time). The parked lanes stay out of the panel (BQ-271). A history-based feed belongs to FR-PA-08 (Should). | FR-PA-13, ADR-0021 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11B-8 | PPL-11b, FR-PA-13, FR-PA-17: "how is task X going" and the team's board and calendar. | Tasks are the plate's run rows (running or blocked worlds the person owns); completed runs and unit counts need a run read model (later). The panel answers nothing about the team pinboard or team calendar yet and only opens S-28 and S-29; reading them through the panel comes with the pinboard and calendar routes of PPL-11b, through the same oracle. | FR-PA-13, FR-PA-17, PE-02, PE-04 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11B-9 | PPL-11b: the model may emit any `ui_action` kind; FR-PA-16 (actions from the panel) is not in this ticket. | 11b executes and offers `navigate` only; a `mark_done`, `snooze`, `tag`, `untag` or `draft_reply` reply is dropped with the fixed `dropped` text, so a tag on a non-member is refused exactly like a tag on a member or on nobody. FR-PA-16 adds offers: each kind checked with `tilo_people` `decide` as the signed-in person (`mention`, `mark_done`, `edit`), shown as a confirm chip (PE-10), with one refusal text for every reason. `draft_reply` also needs a mail-row-to-thread mapping (FR-PA-07). | FR-PA-14, FR-PA-16, FR-PA-17, PE-06, PE-10 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11B-10 | PPL-11b, FR-PA-17: how is "what is on Priya's plate" recognised, and what may the refusal say? | Word rules in `config/assistant.yaml`: a possessive of a word outside `self_possessives`, or a third-person word, declines with one fixed `other_person` text; nothing is looked up, so the reply is the same whether Priya exists, is a teammate, has items or is the asker. A miss falls to the model, which can still only pick an intent over the asker's own plate. Privacy does not rest on the rule: no input ever selects another person (PE-26). | FR-PA-17, PE-11, PE-26 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11B-11 | PPL-11b, FR-PA-05, PE-12: the Desk world ("one lazily created Desk world per user") has no owner ticket yet, and `w_desk_<user>` can pass the 48-character id limit. | `tilo_api.desk.ensure_desk`: `w_desk_` and `r_desk_` plus the first 24 hex characters of SHA-256 of `org:user`, created on first use with the person as owner and no districts; its events carry `vis: "all"` and the world is never shared until ADR-0009 (PE-12). | BQ-MEM10-1 | FR-MEM-10: the PRD names no caps for a run bundle. What are `tilo_harness.run_bundle.DEFAULT_LIMITS`? | `max_bundle_bytes` 300,000,000; `max_uncompressed_bytes` 300,000,000 (a whole world's 200,000,000 bytes, AR-04, plus events, archive and records), checked against the ZIP's declared sizes before anything is inflated; `max_entries` 30,003 (three per version plus three); `max_events` 200,000; `max_archive_records` 100,000; `max_versions` 10,000. Exactly at a cap passes; export and import apply the same caps. Raise them in code if a real run needs more. | PRD 13.4, 13.5; AR-04; services/harness/tests/API_FR_MEM_10.md | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MEM10-2 | FR-MEM-10: what does a re-import keep, and how atomic is it? | (1) The run id is kept (the archive chain hashes cover it); only `world_id` on events and `org_id`, `world_id`, `owner` on records become the importer's. User ids that are event actors or `authz` refs are history and stay. (2) Record `sources` are dropped (AR-06); the idempotency key, `created_at` and `redacted` are kept; a `clean` claim is judged again by the importer's screener. (3) Export is read-only and adds no `exported` manifest revision (AR-06 lists that stage; a read-only export cannot write it). (4) Atomicity is validate-first (bundle, hashes, chain, secrets, and a dry run of every version, manifest and event through scratch stores), then one in-process lock per world and per (org, run) is held from the checks to the last write; an outage while writing artifacts rolls back the versions this call stored; an outage while writing events leaves a prefix of the replay that a retry of the same import completes without duplicates (an append-only stream cannot be un-written). The raw archive is keyed by org and run, so an import appends only to an empty chain, shares an identical whole chain without writing, and refuses a strict prefix (it may be another world's live chain of the run) unless the bundle is authenticated by `expected_sha256`; so a retry after an archive outage must carry the digest. Two processes importing one run into two worlds of one org at the same moment are not serialised (T8's store must append with a precondition). The in-process locks are held in a weak registry, so a lock goes away once no import holds or waits for it. T8 note: a crash between an imported record's create and its blob's create leaves a record without a blob, and `import_version` never purges one (another import may still be writing it), so every retry of that import raises `ArtifactBusy` until an operator purges the record (retention `delete`); T8 should record a write time or an import lease so a stale one can be purged safely. (5) **Only `expected_sha256` authenticates a bundle.** The per-entry index, the counts and the raw-archive chain and head can all be recomputed by whoever edits the bundle, so without the digest an import proves only that a bundle is well formed, not that Tilo exported it. The S-22 route must require `expected_sha256` taken from Tilo's own export record (never from the uploader), or mark the imported world replay-only. (6) The bundle format is a library format pinned in the API doc, not a frozen contract; freezing it in `contracts/` is a separate decision. (7) Only a world whose whole stream is one run can be exported (the events must be one run from seq 1); PRD 13.4 says "any run", so a world that holds several runs needs a per-run cut of the stream first, a later ticket. | PRD 13.2, 13.4, 13.5; AR-03, AR-06, AR-07; FR-HIL-07 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MEM10-3 | FR-MEM-10: how is an imported world shown and used? | Default (supervisor, 2026-10-06): an imported world is replay-only, and its history is shown as "imported, unverified" (honesty rules, PRD 9.4) unless the import supplied an `expected_sha256` that matched; then it may be shown as verified, but it still never gets live mode and never takes authorisation from the imported records (`authz` refs in its events are history). Built now: `ImportResult.verified` (true only when `expected_sha256` was given and matched). Not built (the only replay-only seam, NFR-24 `tilo_metering.flags.replay_only`, is deployment-wide, not per world): checklist for the S-22 export/import route ticket: (a) a per-world replay-only mark set on import, checked before any run, card answer or outbox send in that world; (b) store `verified` with the world and show "imported, unverified" or "imported, verified" in Story and Trace; (c) take `expected_sha256` only from Tilo's own export record, never from the uploader or the request, ideally through an injected export-record lookup rather than a free parameter: today `verified` only says the digest matched, so a digest the forger computed gives `verified=True` (BQ-MEM10-2 (5)); (d) never derive authorisation, a policy decision or a T3 approval from an imported event. | PRD 9.4, 13.4, 13.5; NFR-24; FR-MTR-04; S-22 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-286 | FR-CHN-11, FR-PA-07 (PR #427 security review, follow-ups, none blocking): what to do about (a) the DMARC `header.from` domain against the reply recipient's domain, (b) first-occurrence trust assuming Gmail MX ingress (mail added by POP fetch, import or `messages.insert` carries only the sender's header), (c) `_replied` deciding "own" from the sender-written From, (d) a duplicate Gmail id in the thread window, (e) `CONFIG_PATH` resolving only in a repo checkout and `lru_cache` needing a restart, (f) `ConnectorMailReader` being wired to a client that can send? | Defaults: (a) before 17 Oct leave it (the pin and the verdict prove the From domain authenticated, not that the reply goes there); if the user wants it, require the `header.from` of the dmarc clause to equal the domain of the reply's `to` address. (b) accept: Tilo reads one Gmail mailbox that receives by MX; a mailbox filled by POP or import is out of scope, and a message carrying the INBOX label only through import cannot be told apart, so document it. (c) use the SENT label (and the DRAFT label excluded) instead of the From text to decide "own", as a follow-up ticket with the connector mapper (`label_ids` is already returned). (d) fail closed: if two messages in the window share a Gmail id, `reply_target` returns None (small, with (c)). (e) the channels Dockerfile already copies `config/`; add a startup check that `trusted_authserv_id()` is not None and log its absence once (type only), and treat a config change as a deploy (restart), since the value is cached per process. (f) `ConnectorMailReader` must be wired only to a read-only RPC client of tilo-connectors that exposes `get_thread` and `get_message` and nothing else (SEC-02, SEC-03); never to the adapter object, whose side-effect method exists. | FR-CHN-11, SEC-02, SEC-03 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08-1 | FR-MTG-08: which module owns the minutes email and nudges, and what are its inputs? | `tilo_channels.meeting_followup.MeetingFollowUp`; read-only ports `meetings`, `minutes`, `work_items`, `directory.address(person_id)` (tilo-api, PE-09, PE-20); tilo-api's meeting actions call `draft_minutes_email(user, meeting, recipients=, request_id=)` and `draft_nudges(user, meeting, request_id=)`. No send path (SEC-03); the card goes through `Autopilot.preview` mode "new" (thread-less, T3 by `new_recipients`, L1) and the send is `Autopilot.send` with the Owner's `approval.resolved`. | FR-MTG-08, FR-PA-09 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08-2 | FR-MTG-08: what does "after confirmation" mean? | Only the meeting Owner; the meeting is `minuted`, `minutes_confirmed_by` is set by the Owner, and the minutes artifact names this meeting. Anything else: None / [] and nothing drafted, screened, stored, emitted or queued (never a raise). | FR-MTG-06, PE-05, PE-07, PE-19 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08-3 | FR-MTG-08: "attendees the Owner picks": by what? | Person ids of attendees that have a `user`. A pick that is no attendee, an empty pick, an outsider (`user` null) or an attendee with no resolved directory address gives no card; the audience is never changed silently. The idempotency key also holds the picked addresses, so the same request id with other picks is another draft. | PE-09, PE-20, meeting.schema | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08-4 | FR-MTG-08: what is a "confirmed open item"? | A `verified` action item whose work item is `open`, `confirmed_by` the meeting Owner and whose `source.ref` is `<meeting>#<item id>`. `proposed`, `done`, `dropped` and unlinked items get no nudge and no line in the email; an `unverified` decision is not confirmed content. | FR-MTG-04, FR-MTG-06, PE-03, PE-07, PE-19 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08-5 | FR-MTG-08: who gets a nudge? | The item's assignee (`action_items[].owner`) resolved by the directory, whether or not the Owner picked them for the minutes email; one T3 card per item, each Owner-approved; an assignee with no address gets none. A nudge prompt and body carry that item alone (PE-05). | PE-05, FR-MTG-08 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08-6 | FR-MTG-08: how is "0 invented commitments" tested when a model writes the body? | The FR-CHN-10 `Checker` runs on every body with the confirmed text as its only source (instruction and facts), plus a refusal of any body repeating text the Owner did not confirm; at most one regeneration, then `needs_you` (Edit and Cancel only) or no card (model refused, flagged answer). A faithful body gives a ready card. | FR-CHN-10, PRD 11.4 Content | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08-7 | FR-MTG-08: flagged minutes or items? | T3 with `safety_flag` (already T3 here; the modifier is shown): the safety screen of the prompt, `minutes.flagged` or a flagged work item of an included item sets it; the minutes email is flagged by any included item. | PE-21, SEC-04 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08-8 | FR-MTG-08: Edit on these cards? | Not built: `Autopilot.edit` redrafts reply formats only (it raises for mode "new"), so a changed draft is a new request id. Follow-up if the Owner needs in-place edit. `Draft.thread_id` is typed `str | None` for the thread-less draft. | FR-CHN-11 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08B-1 | FR-MTG-08b: when may a body hold a commitment word (refund, discount, pay...)? | Only inside a verbatim repeat of a confirmed entry: the whole entry, at a line start or after a list marker or a colon, ending its line (a `.`, `;` or `!` may follow). The `Checker` gets no instruction, so it grants none; commitment words are read again on the body with those repeats cut out. A paraphrase of a confirmed commitment ("Please pay the supplier" for "Pay the supplier") is a finding: one regeneration, then `needs_you`. | FR-CHN-10, PRD 11.4 Content, PE-21 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08B-2 | FR-MTG-08b: whose address does a nudge use when the minutes and the work item name different owners? | An item counts as open only when the work item's `owner` equals the minutes item's `owner`; otherwise it gets no nudge and no line in the minutes email (fail closed). The nudge goes to the work item's owner. | PE-07, PE-09, PE-19 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08B-3 | FR-MTG-08b: what does a missing or non-bool `flagged` mean? | Flagged (`is not False`): the card is T3 with `safety_flag` (PE-21 fail closed), for the minutes and for a work item. | PE-21, SEC-04 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08B-4 | FR-MTG-08b: which caps does the module enforce on the minutes? | The schema's: 240 characters per text (a longer one is cut, its raw rest only screened), 8 summary points, 30 decisions, 60 action items. More entries in any list: no draft (None / []), checked before any work item is read, so a nudge run has at most 60 cards. | minutes.schema.json, EFF-03 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08B-5 | FR-MTG-08b: can a body for a maximal minutes (~23.5k characters) be checked? | Yes: `Checker` and the answer parser take a per-use `cap`, and this module uses `MINUTES_CAP` (40,000) instead of raising `CHECK_CAP` (FR-CHN-10, mirrored by `SIGNAL_CAP`). Not decided here: the model's `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` (1,200, about 4,800 characters) still bounds what a real model writes, so a large minutes fails closed on a cut-off answer; a larger output budget for the minutes template is a cost decision (architect). | FR-CHN-10, EFF-03 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08B-6 | FR-MTG-08b: is the meeting title (the subject) checked? | By the `Checker` with no instruction and the title as its own source of facts: a commitment word or an obfuscation (mixed script, direction control) in it gives the generic subject "Meeting minutes". The title is also screened with the prompt. `t3_content_signal` is not run on it (the card is T3 for new recipients anyway). | FR-CHN-10, PE-21 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08C-1 | FR-MTG-08c (e): a body that NFKD or the fold expands past the checker's cap (2300 x U+FDFA, 40,000 x ss-folding sharp s)? | A finding, never an exception: `clean` bounds its NFKD to the cap, `_audit` turns a `ValueError` into an `obfuscation` finding (card `needs_you`, stored, so a retry pays no second model call), and `draft_nudges` skips only the failed item (type logged, no card) so one bad item never aborts the other nudges. | FR-CHN-10, EFF-03 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08C-2 | FR-MTG-08c (a): can a lead-in with no commitment word turn a verbatim confirmed entry into a commitment ("Approved: <entry>", "Yes, we accept - <entry>")? | No: a repeat of an entry that holds a commitment word counts as verbatim only when the words in front of it on its line (or the heading line above, when it has none and ends in a colon) are a label: at most 6 words, all in `LABEL_WORDS` ("Action items", "A reminder about this item"). Otherwise the entry is not cut, its commitment word is a finding and the Owner sees it (`needs_you`). Chosen over rendering the entries from a template: no prompt, fixture or test body changes, and an unknown lead-in word fails closed (a false `needs_you` only for entries that hold a commitment word). | FR-CHN-10, PRD 11.4 Content | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08C-3 | FR-MTG-08c (b): are the line-end anchor and the lead-in requirement of a verbatim repeat pinned? | Yes, by test: "<entry> and we approve it", "<entry> - approved" and "So we grant what <entry>" are no repeats, so the entry's commitment word is a finding. No code change. | BQ-MTG08B-1 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08C-4 | FR-MTG-08c (c): overlay and enclosing combining marks (U+0334-0338, U+0488-0489, U+20D2-20F0, U+A670-A672) and text of dropped characters only? | In model text each overlay mark is an `obfuscation` finding (a struck-through "No refund" is no verbatim repeat), and so is a text that holds characters but nothing readable once `clean` drops them (U+0F73 only, zero-width only). Ordinary accents still pass. Sources are not flagged. | FR-CHN-10, FR-CHN-11, FR-CHN-13 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08C-5 | FR-MTG-08c (d): how is NFKD bounded for a 40,000-character body (40k x U+0F73 took 52 s)? | `drafting_check._bound` (stream-safe, as `tilo_harness.untrusted.nfkc`): a character that would make more than 30 non-starters in a row is dropped, and the text ends before the character that takes its decomposed length past the cap; either is an `obfuscation` finding on a draft. The same 40,000-character body now reads in well under a second (the timing test allows 8 s). | EFF-03, FR-CHN-10 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08C-6 | FR-MTG-08c (f): is a link, address or number in the meeting title checked against the confirmed text? | Yes: the `Checker` runs on the title with the confirmed texts as its only source of facts and no instruction; any finding but a date word ("Monday") gives the generic subject. A label such as Q3 or v2 is no number. `t3_content_signal` is not run (the card is T3 for new recipients anyway). | BQ-MTG08B-6, PE-21 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MTG08C-7 | FR-MTG-08c: the ticket said #443 (FR-MTG-08b) was merged, but it was open at the start. | This branch merges `origin/feat/FR-MTG-08b-hardening` so the residuals sit on the code they harden; merge #443 first, then this PR rebases to the residual commits. | FR-MTG-08b | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL10-1 | PPL-10: PE-19 lists checks "on every read, confirm, edit and tag" that the reference oracle (`tests/contracts/_people_lib.permits`) makes only on create or not at all. Which wins? | The rules: `tilo_people.decide` adds S1 (a set `minutes_confirmed_by` is by the meeting owner and not in the future), S2 (a person-made work item's `assigned_by` is null or its creator), S3 (`confirmed`/`accepted` history entries are by `confirmed_by.by`), S4 (`link` exactly when `url`), S5 (`end >= start`) on every request. No valid person, plate or transcript crossref case contradicts them (checked). Not added: "an item from another person's meeting needs confirmed minutes" on reads, because the valid transcript cases read a confirmed item after its meeting was deleted, and the schema nulls `minutes_confirmed_by` then. Follow-up for the contracts lane: invalid crossref cases for S1 to S5 and the same checks in the reference. | PE-19, PE-07, PE-08 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL10-2 | PPL-10: PE-19's tombstone keeps `minutes_confirmed_by`, but `meeting.schema.json` forces it null for a deleted meeting. What does the tombstone hold? | The schema wins. The tombstone is a valid meeting with status `deleted`: id, org, owner, `world_id`, attestation, source, `ended_reason`, `created_at` kept; title "Deleted meeting", no series, schedule, agenda, attendees, transcript, minutes or deletion dates (the owner asked to delete the content, FR-MTG-10). Confirmed items made from it stay readable (their checks need the owner only); no new Tilo proposal can cite it (`released_item` needs confirmed minutes), which fails closed. | PE-19, FR-MTG-10, BQ-CCP3-2 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL10-3 | PPL-10: the rules let an org owner create a person (PE-17) and a person add a meeting they attest (FR-MTG-01, PE-18), but the reference oracle refuses every create of a person or a meeting. | `decide` grants exactly these two (G1: an org owner of the record's org creates a person; G2: the actor is the meeting's `owner` and its `attestation.by`). The first person of an org is minted by tilo-api outside `decide` (PE-17). These are the only grants beyond the reference; the parity test lists them. | PE-17, PE-18, FR-MTG-01 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL10-4 | PPL-10: whom may a spoken name in minutes resolve to, and how loosely? | Only people the meeting owner may read in directory view (PE-20: the owner, their teammates, everyone in the org for an org owner), so minutes never name a stranger. Exact match after normalization (NFKC, control and format characters removed, Latin diacritics U+0300 to U+036F folded, casefold, outer punctuation stripped) on the display name or an alias; no first-name, prefix, fuzzy or phonetic match; no look-alike mapping (a name spelled with a Cyrillic look-alike letter matches nobody). Two or more matches are ambiguous even if only one attended (FR-MTG-05: never auto-assigned). | FR-MTG-05, PE-15, PE-20 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL10-5 | PPL-10: which due phrases resolve, against which date and zone? | A closed English grammar (`packages/people/tests/API.md` section 3: today/EOD, tomorrow, weekdays, "this" weekday, end of week or month, "in N days/weeks", ordinals, month dates, ISO dates). The anchor is `meeting.scheduled.start`, else `meeting.created_at` (an upload: the owner can correct `scheduled` before minutes run), read in the assignee's zone, or the meeting owner's while the assignee is unresolved. A bare weekday equal to the meeting's, "next Friday", "next week", "this week" and slash dates are ambiguous; times of day and anything else are not understood; nothing resolves before the meeting day or beyond 366 days. In each case `due` is null and `due_text` keeps the phrase. | FR-MTG-05, PE-15 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL10-6 | PPL-10: PE-07 defines a work item's `edit`; what may an edit change on pins, calendar entries and meetings, and how do statuses move? | Table in API.md section 4. Work items: title, due, snooze, plus `reopened` (done to open) and `reassign_requested` history (PE-30 plate actions). Pins: title, body, kind, url, responsible person (a member), due, removal (Tilo's personal pins: undo). Calendar: title, times, all-day, share, proposed to declined, accepted to cancelled; a Google-synced entry only its share (its times are Google's; a write is FR-CAL-04 through the outbox). Meetings: title, series, schedule, agenda, attendees. Stamps, owners, sources, `flagged` and `confirmed_by` never change by edit. | PE-07, PE-19, PE-21, FR-PB-05, FR-PB-07 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL10-7 | PPL-10: who changes a person's time zone, aliases, preferred channel or display name? No rule names a person editing their own record, and PE-17 gives directory changes to org owners only for roles and memberships. | Nobody through `decide` for now (fail closed); records are created with these fields by an org owner (G1) or tilo-api. If people must edit their own zone or aliases before 10 Oct, the rules need a line (a self-edit of exactly those four fields). | PE-17, PE-20 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL10-8 | PPL-10: what does erasing a person delete among people records? | `erase_person`: tombstones the meetings they own (so other people's confirmed items stay checkable), deletes their work items, personal pins, calendar entries, the mentions naming them and their person record; team pins they set up stay (PE-16, "a former member"). Their Desk world events and archived assistant messages go through the FR-MEM-09 registry in `packages/memory`, not here. | PE-16, FR-MEM-09 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL10-9 | PPL-10: free/busy is Should (supplement 3.2) and an accepted entry may have `end: null`. | `decide` answers `read_busy` per entry (the crossref needs it); PPL-11b assembles the `free_busy` document (at most 31 days, PE-19) when it builds the Should route. An entry with no end gives no busy interval. | FR-CAL-06, PE-04, PE-19 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL10-10 | PPL-10: which crossref suites are PPL-10's acceptance? | `fixtures/person/crossref/` (PE-01 to PE-08, PE-17 to PE-20). The plate and transcript suites (PE-22 to PE-28) belong to PPL-11a, whose oracles call `tilo_people.decide` with `read_full` as the contract tests call `permits`. | PE-23, PE-27 | 2026-10-06 |

| BQ-SEC16-1 | SEC-16 / FR-MTR-04: how is a live unit's cost counted against the INR caps while `config/prices.yaml` is unset (PL-06), the price book is in US dollar credits and the PRD gives no US$ to INR rate? And which values does `config/spend_caps.yaml` ship? | **Default (the user may override):** each live unit reserves a flat, conservative amount in INR minor units from config, `unit_reserve_minor`, through `SpendGuard.begin_unit`, and `settle_unit` settles it to the same amount, until a price table (PL-06) and a US$ to INR rate in config exist. The Director's own calls (intake, approaches, MustAsk) reserve the same flat amount against the uid. Shipped value: 1000 (10 INR, about US$0.12, above one call of a fast model), which allows 100 live units per uid lifetime and 12 per run. `unit_reserve_minor` is a required key of `spend_caps.schema.json` 1.1.0 and `config/spend_caps.yaml`, and a seventh `SpendCaps` field; the SEC-16 config test's `GOOD`, its invalid cases and `API_SEC_16.md` were extended to match (a one-off architect edit agreed with the test-writer), and the shipped file keeps `unit_reserve_minor <= run_minor`. **Shipped values (ADR-0002 decision 16):** US$ figures converted at 80 INR per US$, below the market rate, so no INR cap exceeds its US$ counterpart: `run_minor` 12000 (PRD 17.3: 300 credits per public run, US$1.50), `global_day_hard_minor` 200000 (PRD 17.3: US$25 a day, hard), `org_day_minor` null (as BQ-42), `uid_lifetime_minor` 100000 (SEC-16). The rate limit is 240 requests per uid per 60 s window: the PRD gives no number, and the web polls `GET /worlds/{id}/events` every second during a run (`apps/web/src/api/http.ts`, `pollMs` 1000), so a limit under about 60 a minute would refuse a judge's own world view. The web treats a 429 as final (a 4xx is not retryable, so the event poll stops); a follow-up for the web lane is to back off on 429 and honour `Retry-After`. | SEC-16; FR-MTR-04; PL-06; PRD 14.1, 17.3 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-SEC16-2 | SEC-16 build: the tests name the ledger seam `deps.get_spend_ledger`, which PPL-11b (merged first) already uses for FR-PA-19's cached daily allowance; and what of the SEC-15 harness, whose judges expected live with no ledger wired? | The SEC-16 seam is `deps.get_spend_cap_ledger` (tests and `API_SEC_16.md` renamed, nothing weakened). The SEC-15 harness wires a fresh in-memory ledger and its own limiter, since SEC-16 makes live need a wired ledger (unwired is replay). `_sec16_lib` DEFAULT_CAPS `run_minor` is 1,000 so the API tests' one-run prefill fits. Not built here: the per-unit `begin_unit`/`settle_unit` wiring in `runs.py` and the Director-call reservations; the production ledger must not be wired before it. The schedule routes and the tick still read NFR-24's flag store alone. | SEC-16; FR-MTR-04; FR-PA-19 | 2026-10-07 |

If lines should separate words, `question_key` maps tab, LF and CR to a space first (a one-line spec change). | FR-PA-13, PE-24 | 2026-10-06 |

Proposed: `deps.get_source_owners()` returning `NoSourceOwners()` until PPL-31 and PPL-11a, and `desk.lock(store, user)`. | PE-08, BQ-PPL11B-11, BQ-PPL11B-13 | 2026-10-06 |

| BQ-PPL11B-21 | PPL-11b tests, FR-PA-13: A13-5 (a model picks a fact intent) is listed under slice 11b-1, but `ModelAsk`, `Menu` and `build_menu` are assigned to 11b-2. | The 11b-1 builder adds the minimal `Menu`, `MenuRow` and `ModelAsk` dataclasses and `build_menu` to `core.py`; 11b-2 extends them. The loader keeps refusing a word list that holds anything but lower-case strings, so a later `no`, `off` or `yes` fails loudly instead of matching nothing. | FR-PA-13 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11B-23 | PPL-11b tests (11b-1, 11b-2): four assertions cannot pass whatever the builder writes. (1) `test_PPL_11b_1_gate_words_config.py` lines 533 and 591 and `test_PPL_11b_2_route_navigation.py` line 1318 expect store-log kinds `meetings`, `work_items`, `persons`, but `_ppl11b_lib.SpyStore` logs `inner.kind`, which is singular (`meeting`, `work_item`, `person`). (2) `Rig.unwire` pops a dependency override that `Rig.post` and `Rig.raw` re-install at once through `activate()`, so `test_FR_PA_17_a_seam_that_is_not_wired_...` never leaves a seam unwired. (3) `test_FR_PA_14_A14_3_every_fuzzed_action_...` feeds the `action` of the reply with duplicate `choice` keys straight to the core; that action is a valid `navigate` to `plate`, so vet cannot drop it (only `parse_decision`, slice 11b-3, drops the reply). | Not bent. Builder-side probes with the same assertions on the real store kinds, an unwired seam and the other 199 replies pass. Proposed test fixes: compare the log against `world.stores.<x>.kind`; make `unwire` remember the removal so `activate` skips it; take the fuzz actions from replies that survive a duplicate-key-refusing parse. | FR-PA-13, FR-PA-14, FR-PA-17 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11B-24 | PPL-11b, FR-PA-17: `RefGate.record` and `read_as` turn a failing store read into a refusal (None), like a missing record, so a store outage while the plate is answered reads as "empty" for the rows concerned. Only the persons list (the Directory) and the plate fail the whole request (`failed`). | Fail closed and say nothing: a refusal and an outage look the same, which is the privacy-safe side.
