| BQ-EFF04-1 | EFF-04 needs a cached-token count per event, and the frozen event contract has none. May the trace gain one? | **User confirmed 2026-10-06** (relayed by the supervisor): an additive, optional `trace.tokens_cached`; `event.schema.json` 1.1.0; left out means unknown, never zero; EV-16 keeps it at most `tokens_in`. ADR-0022 | PRD 6.6 EFF-04, 9.3 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF04-2 | The Gemini API leaves a zero `cachedContentTokenCount` out of `usageMetadata`. Is a missing count a miss or unknown? | A miss: when `usage_metadata` is present the Google adapter reports 0, as it already does for a missing thoughts count. A model without implicit caching then shows 0%, which is true. ADR-0022 decision 6 | EFF-04 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF04-3 | Cached input tokens are often billed below the input list price. Should credits price them lower? | No: credits price all of `tokens_in` at the input list price (conservative). A cached price would be a `providers.schema.json` and `config/prices.yaml` change and needs its own decision. ADR-0022 decision 7 | EFF-04, PL-06, PRD 14 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF04-4 | "Cache-hit rate reported where the provider supports caching": how is support decided, and what does a role with no counts show? | Support means a provider reported counts; there is no capability flag. The rate is Σ cached ÷ Σ `tokens_in` over events that carry the count, with coverage shown; a role with none shows "unknown" (null in JSON), never 0%. ADR-0022 decision 8 | EFF-04 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF05B-1 | What cron and time zone is the nightly eval smoke? | Daily `0 19 * * *` in `Etc/UTC` (00:30 IST), variable `eval_nightly_schedule` in `infra/envs/dev/eval_nightly.tf`; the test-writer's example. Scheduler retry_count 1, attempt deadline 180 s (the call only starts the job) | EFF-05b | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF05B-2 | Where does the nightly smoke run: GitHub Actions, a Cloud Run service endpoint or a Cloud Run job? | Cloud Scheduler in tilo-dev (user confirmed 2026-10-06 (relayed by supervisor)); a Cloud Run job `tilo-eval-nightly` started through the Cloud Run Admin API with an OAuth token, per the test-writer's recommendation. Scheduler account `tilo-eval-scheduler` holds only `roles/run.invoker` on that job; runtime account `tilo-eval-runner` holds no role. No `serviceAccountUser` added (if the first run shows Scheduler needs it, stop and ask) | EFF-05b | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF05B-3 | Job limits? | `parallelism` 1, `task_count` 1, `max_retries` 1 (the idempotency key makes a retry safe), task timeout 3600 s, 1 vCPU and 1 GiB. Scheduler retry plus job retry means at most two starts per failed night; if a `:run` response is lost after the execution was created the strict bound is 2 executions x 2 task attempts, 4 x 3600 s x 1 vCPU, negligible | EFF-05b | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF05B-4 | How does the job get an image without giving `tilo-cloudbuild` new grants? | `evals/Dockerfile` and `evals/cloudbuild.yaml` (`just build-evals-image`) only build and push `tilo/evals:latest`, which `tilo-cloudbuild` already may do; Terraform owns the job and points it at `:latest`, so nothing swaps images and the existing cloudbuild pins (5 roles, 17 bindings) stay. The user runs the build before the apply | EFF-05b | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF05B-5 | The root `.gcloudignore` and `.dockerignore` keep `evals/` out (pinned by tests). How does the eval image get its cases? | A separate allowlist `evals/cloudbuild.gcloudignore` (passed with `--ignore-file`) and `evals/Dockerfile.dockerignore` (BuildKit reads it instead of the root file); the root files are unchanged. The replay job carries no secret, so uploading `evals/cases` and `evals/fixtures` adds no exposure | EFF-05b | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF05B-6 | `:latest` is a mutable tag. Is that acceptable for the job image? | Yes for tilo-dev (a replay job with no roles, no secrets and no live switch); every build also keeps an immutable `:<build id>` tag. Pin by digest if the job ever gets a role or a secret | EFF-05b | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF05B-7 | Nothing sets the live switch in the job. How is a live nightly ever run? | It is not: the job's environment has no live switch and no secret, so the nightly is replay only. A live run is a later, separate decision of the user's | EFF-05b, EFF-01 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF04-5 | EFF-04b: when is a role's cache-hit rate flagged as resting on too little data? | A role is flagged "low coverage" when the events carrying a cached count hold under 50% of its input tokens (`LOW_CACHE_COVERAGE` in `evals/src/tilo_evals/results.py`). The rate is still shown, with its coverage; the threshold is a report label only and gates nothing. Scoped viewers still do not see `tokens_cached` (`redaction.py` unchanged, ADR-0022 decision 5). | EFF-04 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF05-1 | Where does the nightly eval smoke run? | **User confirmed 2026-10-06** (relayed by the supervisor): Cloud Scheduler in tilo-dev, not GitHub Actions. The scheduler and job are EFF-05b; the entrypoint is `python -m tilo_evals nightly` (EFF-05a) | PRD 6.6 EFF-05, 19.5 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF05-2 | What does a batch job do between submit and done? | `polls_until_done=N`: submit returns pending and does no work, polls 1..N-1 return running, poll N runs every item and returns the terminal state (succeeded, partial = some failed, failed = all failed). An item failure is data, never an exception | EFF-05 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF05-3 | Same idempotency key twice? | Same key and same requests join the same job and run no item twice; the same key with different requests is a `RequestError` naming idempotency. Default nightly key: `eval-nightly-smoke-<UTC date>`, so a Scheduler retry the same night joins the job | EFF-05 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF05-4 | Which recordings does the batch replay read, and what is a miss? | The inline path's: `model-recordings/<role>/<key>.json`, one format. A miss is one item error naming the key, so the job ends partial or failed and never hides the other results | EFF-01, EFF-05 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF05-5 | What does the smoke pass send through the batch path, as it is replayed recordings and not a model call? | One batch job for the smoke selection; each item's prompt is `{"case_id": ...}` and its answer is the recorded outcome as JSON (`events`, `outputs`, `credits`), validated like a recording. Checks, cap and counts then run as in `run_pass`. Default wiring replays and never builds a model client, deployed or with the live switch set | PRD 19.5 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF05-6 | Nightly exit codes and report? | As `run` (0 ok, 1 a case failed, 2 could not run, 3 credit cap) plus 1 when a selected case was not runnable (failed batch item, bad answer) and 2 for no smoke case or a batch not finished in `--max-wait-s` (default 3600; poll every 30 s). The report is the git-ignored `evals/nightly.local.md` and the summary is printed; `evals/results.md` is never touched | PRD 19.5 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF05-7 | Is the fake batch provider allowed when deployed or live? | No, the same rule as `FakeSafetyProvider` (SEC-04): refused when the live switch is on or `K_SERVICE` or `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` is present (even empty). Replay is allowed on a Cloud Run job | EFF-05, SEC-04 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF05-8 | Does the live batch path record? | Not in this ticket: `RecordReplayBatchProvider` never writes recordings. The Google adapter uses inline requests; Vertex batch may need a GCS source and destination, to decide before the first live night | EFF-05 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF05-9 | EFF-04 cache-hit section in the nightly report? | Not yet: `trace.tokens_cached` is not read by the report until EFF-04b; the nightly report must include it then | EFF-04, EFF-05 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MLINK-1 | Meeting link check (issue #464, ADR-0016 decision 14): `meeting.schema.json` 1.2.0 refuses text that 1.1.0 accepted. Is a stricter pattern a minor bump? | Yes, 1.2.0: PE-13 already said the title, agenda titles and attendee names hold no link, and none of the 115 meeting records in the repository (fixtures, streams, web copies) changes verdict. Decided by default under the user's 6 Oct delegation (supervisor, 16:45 IST "do what you think is excellent for our repo and end product"): keep 1.2.0 as a minor bump, because the pattern corrects an implementation of a rule that already existed (it does not reject data the contract allowed). The user may override: a major bump with a migration if any narrowing counts as breaking (ADR-0002 decision 2). Pre-deploy note: before deploying to tilo-dev (or any environment that already holds meeting records), scan its meeting stores with the 1.2.0 schema; the confirm and read paths refuse a record that no longer validates, so an affected meeting would become unconfirmable and drop out of reads | FR-MTG-01, PE-13 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MLINK-2 | Which link forms does the meeting schema still let through? | Not refused, to keep false positives low: a scheme split by a visible space (`https: //`), fullwidth or ideographic dots and letters that IDNA maps to ASCII, IP hosts with a path, app schemes without `//` (`msteams:/l/...`), dial-in numbers and PINs. Refused although harmless (accepted false positives): a dotted name ending in a TLD-like word followed by a slash (`ASP.NET/Blazor`) and a meeting host named by its domain. Revisit if a pilot stores a join link in one of these forms | FR-MTG-01, PE-13 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MLINK-3 | Astral default-ignorable characters (variation selectors 17 to 256, U+E0100 to U+E01EF, and unassigned U+E0000 to U+E0FFF outside the tag block) are not Cf, so `tilo_people.text.clean_line` keeps them, and no pattern that Python, ECMA-262 and Rust all read can name them: they can still split `://` invisibly. Who stops them? | `tilo-api`'s writer (people_writes `Writer.text`, PPL-11a) removes or refuses them before the schema check; follow-up for the PPL-11a lane, not a contract change. Until then the schema alone does not stop them | FR-MTG-01, PE-13, PE-24 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MLINK-4 | The review asked to refuse U+FE0F anywhere in meeting text. Emoji typed on phones carry it (U+2764 U+FE0F), in titles and in participants' display names. | Contextual: an invisible combining mark (U+034F, U+17B4, U+17B5, Mongolian free variation selectors, U+FE00 to U+FE0F) is refused at the start or right after an ASCII character, except U+FE0E or U+FE0F after a keycap base (`#`, `*`, a digit). That covers every split of `://` or of an ASCII host and keeps emoji. Refuse anywhere if the user prefers | FR-MTG-01, PE-13 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-MLINK-5 | A participant's display name that is only invisible characters (U+3164 is a common "blank name") or holds a link now makes the whole meeting record invalid. What does the writer store? | The writer (meeting bot, `tilo-api`) cleans first and, if the name is still refused or empty, stores "A participant" (the label PE-24 shows) with `user: null`; it never drops the meeting. Builder ticket, not a contract change | FR-MTG-01, FR-MTG-05, PE-24 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF05C-1 | Is this PR stacked? | Yes: branch `feat/EFF-05c-lite-hardening` is cut from `feat/EFF-05b-nightly-scheduler-v2` (PR #465, still open, the user merges it). The diff shows #465's commits until #465 merges, then reduces to the EFF-05c-lite commits; do not merge this before #465 | EFF-05 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF05C-2 | How is a failed nightly alerted? | Alert policy `eval_nightly_failed` in `infra/modules/monitoring`: metric `run.googleapis.com/job/completed_execution_count`, `result="failed"`, job name from variable `eval_nightly_job_name` (default and env value `tilo-eval-nightly`), threshold above 0 over 300 s sums, same email channel and shape as the NFR-27 cost alert, no log-based metric. Exposed as a separate module output and `output.monitoring.eval_nightly_alert_policy`, so the NFR-23 `alert_policies` pin (three keys) is unchanged and `monitoring.tftest.hcl` is untouched; the new pin is `infra/envs/dev/tests/eval_nightly_alert.tftest.hcl`. Gaps: a Scheduler that never starts the job raises nothing; the `result` label value `failed` is from the metric docs and is to be confirmed on the first failing `gcloud run jobs execute` | EFF-05, EFF-06 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF05C-3 | How is an injected grant caught? | `tests/repo/test_EFF_05c_infra_hardening.py` reads every block under `infra/` and fails if any block other than the scheduler job, the one `job_iam_member` and the job's `service_account` names an eval account by resource reference, account-id literal or `local.*` alias; the job may have exactly one IAM resource (`roles/run.invoker`, member the scheduler account, no public member after local expansion); the job may have no volumes or secret blocks, at most cpu 1 and 1Gi, and an image only from `<region>-docker.pkg.dev/<project>/tilo/evals`. 19 injected mutants (all the review survivors plus act-as, module extra_invokers and an IAM policy) are each reported. The pinned EFF-05 test file is unchanged. Limits: static text analysis, so a grant built through a module this test does not know, or `for_each` over a variable map, is only caught if it names the account | EFF-05 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-EFF05C-4 | Credential files in the Docker build context? | Both `.dockerignore` and `evals/Dockerfile.dockerignore` gain `**/client_secret*.json`, `**/token*.json`, `**/credentials.json` and `**/*.pem` (a bare `*.pem` only matches the context root). | BQ-EFF05C-5 | Artifact Registry cleanup? | The repository `tilo` had none anywhere in `infra/`. | BQ-MEM11-2 | What is `lookup_cost` for a checkpoint reuse? | 0: a checkpoint lookup reads recorded events and compares a digest, and the Verifier pass was paid for by the parent. A configurable value would need an `agents.schema.json` change | FR-MEM-11, EV-09 | 2026-10-06 |

| BQ-NFR05-1 | NFR-05 ("event emitted to rendered <= 1 s p95"): how far does the local harness measure? | To client receipt only (an event appended through the dev writer until a `GET /worlds/{id}/events` response holds it); the report's NFR-05 `note` says so. `apps/web/src/api/http.ts` polls events every 1000 ms by default (`pollMs`), so the client poll interval alone can consume the whole 1 s p95 render budget: as built, NFR-05 cannot be met (polling alone adds up to ~1000 ms, ~950 ms at p95). Proposed fix, issue #471: an SSE push stream for world events (preferred), or a shorter poll such as 250 ms, after the Park rebuild, which owns the web client files. EV-02 (time to first plan) stays `needs_live_or_pilot`. | PRD 17.1, 3.5, EFF-01 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-AGT14-1 | FR-AGT-14: the PRD says Cloud Scheduler starts schedules (6.3) and a weekly schedule reuses memory (13.1), but gives no schedule record, no scheduler topology and no way to mark a scheduled run in the event stream. What do we build? (Next free number after BQ-278 across every remote branch at 2026-10-06; the merger may renumber.) | (a) A schedule is a store-side record (Firestore `schedules/{schedule_id}`, in-memory fake in tests), not a contract: owner, title, weekly cadence (weekday, hour, minute, IANA tz), the world id of the run the owner approved, budget cap, enabled, `next_due`, last slot and last world id. (b) ONE Cloud Scheduler job `tilo-schedule-tick` (every minute, its own SA with `run.invoker` on the harness only) posts `/internal/schedules/tick`; there is no Scheduler job per user schedule. The tick claims each due slot with a compare-and-set on `next_due` before starting the run, and the slot string is the idempotency key, so it fires once per slot. Missed slots collapse into one firing plus one `alert.raised`; `replay_only` starts nothing and does not advance `next_due`; at most 20 runs per tick. (c) A scheduled run starts with `world.requested` on channel `schedule` (additive: `common` 1.4.0, `world.requested` 1.1.0; 1.3.0 is taken by the open CC-PEOPLE-3 PR #401), destination null, title the schedule's title. `schedule` is never an owner instruction (OB-01 counts only `web` and `mobile_web`) and never a person's approval; T3 still waits for a person. (d) The `actor` is the schedule's owner, not `system`: OB-01 takes the world's owner from the actor of its first `world.requested`, and a `system` owner is not a user id, so nobody could open a gate in a scheduled world. Revisit only if the user wants a separate owner field on the event. (e) Known side effect: `schedule` is now also accepted by `card.answer_channels` (needs-you and blocked cards), `world.paused.channel` and `policy.notify_channel`, which share the `channel` definition; no consumer reads it there, and narrowing them is a later minor bump. | FR-AGT-14 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-AGT14-2 | FR-AGT-14 builder decisions for the gaps the test-writer found (BQ-AGT14-1 is the design). (Next free number after BQ-286 across every remote branch at 2026-10-06; the merger may renumber.) | (a) The Cloud Tasks task name is a stable encoding of the slot key (`tilo_harness.schedule.task_name`: `sched-` plus the sha256 hex), and the raw slot key stays the idempotency key. (b) A claim held by a crashed instance is a lease that expires after 5 minutes (`CLAIM_LEASE`, enforced store side). (c) A DST gap fires at the end of the gap (03:00 for a 02:30 slot on a spring-forward day); an ambiguous time fires once, at its first occurrence. (d) The first firing's `prior_world_id` is `None`; later ones chain to the previous firing's world id. (e) A raising `store.due`, `claim` or `complete` or `flags.get` fails closed: the tick starts nothing it cannot prove safe, the route answers 500 with a fixed body (no detail), and nothing starts twice (a failed `complete` keeps the claim held until the lease lapses, and the same key dedupes a restart). A raising `release` is swallowed (the lease lapses). An alert sink failure after a successful start never undoes or repeats the start. (f) Web results go stale after 7 days (PRD 13.1), so a weekly schedule reuses file results and re-fetches web ones: documented, not a bug. (g) A schedule title over 140 characters is truncated to 140 when building `world.requested` (the contract limit); an empty one becomes "Scheduled run". (h) Catch-up to the latest missed slot is arithmetic over whole weeks and then `next_slot`, not stepping from `next_due`, so a year-old due time costs the same as a minute-old one. (i) `ScheduleStore.due` returns the oldest `next_due` first (ties by schedule id), so the 20-per-tick rate limit never starves an old schedule. Out of scope here, a follow-up ticket: the production Firestore `ScheduleStore` (with the lease and the oldest-first query) and the Cloud Tasks `RunStarter`, and wiring `TickWiring` into the production app; until then `POST /internal/schedules/tick` answers 503 and the Scheduler job fires into that 503. | FR-AGT-14; PRD 6.3, 8.6, 13.1; BQ-AGT14-1 | 2026-10-06 |
