Not asserted anywhere: whether a meeting world's events may precede `budget.granted` and `world.started` (event.rules.md); the tests check each event against its schema and the registry only | FR-MTG-09; API_FR_MTG_09.md | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-MTG09-25 | MTG09-b build defaults where the pins are silent: a meeting with no world, a paused world, a receipt that cannot be recorded, the needs-you card's events, who may name `create_meeting_event` | A meeting with no world (or a world another person owns) is 409 `not_hostable`; a paused world or any earlier run in the meeting's world is 409 `already_hosted`. After the send the run records the receipt, its manifest and the draft's `sent` revision; if that fails the unit and world fail with a plain reason (the invitation did go out). The needs-you card is written to the card store with no event of its own (a card is not an animation); the post-send `artifact.delivered` is the generic in-Tilo one. `Run.owner_started` makes a Director plan that names `create_meeting_event` fail at validation, so only `HostRun` (the owner's own request) can start it. The route builds no must-ask providers, no Director and no router. The broker-to-`on_connection_event` wiring is the composition root's, not built here | FR-MTG-09 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-PPL11C-1 | PPL-11c, issue #464: what is the largest body a people POST route reads, and when is a bigger one refused? | Defaults (the PRD names no number): 32768 bytes (`MAX_BODY_BYTES`). Every people POST route, old and new, refuses a bigger body with 413 `{"detail": "body_too_large"}` before the rest is read: a declared `Content-Length` over the cap is refused with no body read, a stream is cut after the cap plus one read-ahead of at most 64 KiB; a body of exactly the cap is read. The largest honest body (a meeting with 20 agenda items and 50 attendees) is about 7 KiB. | API_PPL_11c.md section 7; issue #464; `services/api/tests/test_PPL_11c_s4_write_limits.py` | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11C-2 | PPL-11c, SEC-10, issues #462 and #464: what rate cap protects the people writes, and what counts? | Defaults (SEC-10 gives no number): 60 writes a minute per principal (`WRITES_PER_MINUTE`), counted over every people POST route; every attempt that gets past sign-in counts, a refused one (403, 404, 409, 422) too, a read never does. The 61st inside a minute is 429 `{"detail": "rate_limited", "code": "rate_limited"}` with `Retry-After` (whole seconds, 1 to 60), decided before the body is read; a minute after the first write the person may write again; another principal is unaffected. Time is `deps.get_clock`; the counter belongs to the install's people stores, not to module state. | SEC-10; API_PPL_11c.md section 7; issues #462, #464 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11C-3 | PPL-11c, issue #464 and the #470 review comment: what is a valid `email` for `POST /v1/people` and the first person, and may two people share one? | Defaults: the contract pattern accepts NUL, DEL, U+202E and more, and a recipient is resolved from this field (PE-09), so the API accepts only an ASCII dot-atom address, never echoed when refused (422): before the `@`, letters, digits and `. _ % + - '` with no leading, trailing or doubled dot; after it, dotted labels of letters, digits and hyphens (63 at most, none starting or ending with a hyphen), at least two, the last letters only and two or more; 254 at most. An email belongs to one person of the org, compared case-insensitively over the whole address: 409 `{"detail": "email_taken", "code": "email_taken"}`, one winner when callers race; the stored value is as typed; another org's address is not a clash. The 409 is a small oracle to an org owner about their own org, accepted. An existing PPL-11a test that created the same person twice now uses a second address. | PE-09, PE-17, PE-20; issue #464; API_PPL_11c.md section 4 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11C-4 | PPL-11c, issue #464: how does a retried create not duplicate? | Defaults: an optional `Idempotency-Key` header (`^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}$`, else 422) on the four creates (meeting, person, work item, first person). The same principal, key and body again returns the first answer byte for byte (201) and creates nothing; racing retries create one record; the same key with another body is 422 `idempotency_key_reused`; another principal's identical key is independent (no oracle); a refused first attempt does not use the key up; without a key nothing is deduplicated. The mechanism is the builder's (for example an id derived from a keyed hash of principal and key with create-if-absent). Dev CORS allows the header and exposes `Retry-After`. | API_PPL_11c.md section 7; issue #464 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11C-5 | PPL-11c, PE-17, BQ-PPL10-3: who may become the first person of an org, and as what? | Defaults: `POST /v1/people/first` is allowed only when `deps.ORG_ID` has no person and the signed-in principal has no record; the record id is the principal's id, `org_id` is `deps.ORG_ID` (never from the request), role owner, no memberships, `created_by` the principal and the server clock; 201 with the membership view, otherwise 403. Two principals racing for an empty org: one 201, the rest 403. It sits outside `decide`, as BQ-PPL10-3 says ("minted by tilo-api"). A principal is bound to exactly one record. | PE-17, PE-19; BQ-PPL10-3; API_PPL_11c.md section 4 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11C-6 | PPL-11c, PE-30, HR-1: if the event of a work-item write cannot be appended, what happens to the write? | Default: commit first, then resolve the Desk world and append once; if either fails the write is undone by compare-and-set (a created record is deleted) and the answer is 503 with no record text, so no row looks changed without its event. An event first is rejected: it would leave an event for a write that lost its compare-and-set, a lie in an append-only stream (EV-10). A lost compare-and-set writes nothing. The version may move twice on an undone write; content is restored. | PE-30; EV-10; HR-1; API_PPL_11c.md section 5 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11C-7 | PPL-11c, PE-12, ADR-0009: which world, and which `vis`, do the work-item events carry? | Defaults: `work_item.created` and `work_item.changed` only (no new event type), written through the single writer in the Desk world of the person who may read the record in full, `vis: []`, `district` null, `authz` null, a summary of at most 140 characters that names no title, due phrase, quote, address or link. A confirm (a PPL-11a route) is written in the assignee's Desk world (PE-12 reads "confirmed for P"), actor the confirmer, target the assignee; the other reading, the confirmer's world, is one line in the test. The Desk world of `u_X` is `w_desk_X` (lazy, idempotent); an id whose suffix exceeds 43 characters has no Desk world (503); minted ids are 24 hex characters. Meeting, person, membership and role writes emit nothing (no registry type). Needs contract change (ADR-0009 is accepted, its architect PR is not merged): `district_list` has `minItems: 1`, so `vis: []` fails the schema until then. | PE-12, PE-30; ADR-0009, ADR-0021; API_PPL_11c.md section 5 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11C-8 | PPL-11c: PATCH and DELETE, or POST only? | Default: GET and POST only (`.../edit`, `.../delete`, `.../done` and so on), because dev CORS allows only GET, POST and OPTIONS and a PATCH or DELETE would need a CORS change for no gain; this matches the PPL-11a confirm routes. | API_PPL_11c.md section 1; `tilo_api.dev` | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11C-9 | PPL-11c, FR-MTG-10, PE-16, issue #464: what does deleting a meeting delete? | Default: the transcript and minutes artifacts (every blob generation and record, in the meeting's own scope), then the PROPOSED work items, pins and calendar entries whose `source.ref` is the meeting or one of its action items, then the PE-19 tombstone last by compare-and-set; every step is idempotent, so a failure leaves the meeting live and a retry finishes. What the owner already released stays the assignee's, readable, its quote `expired` (a confirmed item, an accepted calendar entry, an active pin), as BQ-PPL10-2 says; active personal pins Tilo added from the owner's own meeting are not dropped. The user may override: FR-MTG-10 "all derived data" may mean those too (one line in the test). Confirm also refuses (404) a proposed item of a discarded or deleted meeting, and the item quote's gone path needs `assigned_by.by` to equal the meeting owner on the record or tombstone. | FR-MTG-10; PE-16, PE-19, PE-23; BQ-PPL10-2, BQ-CCP3-2; issues #456, #464 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11C-10 | PPL-11c, PE-03, PE-07, PE-29, PE-30: the loose ends of the work-item routes. | Defaults: a work-item action returns the full stored record (the owner reads it in full); the assigner's confirm keeps the status view. A no-op edit is not pinned. Put back covers a snoozed item (the snooze is cleared) and an asked item (the ask is withdrawn), history `edited` either way; a done item is put back with reopen. A second `done` on a done item is 404 or 409 and changes nothing (the existing PPL-11a confirm does the same); not-mine on an item nobody assigned or already asked is 409 with non-empty `problems`. Authorisation comes before the record's own schema check: an item the caller may not touch is the one 404 even when the record it would produce is schema-invalid. | PE-03, PE-07, PE-29, PE-30; API_PPL_11c.md section 2 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11C-11 | PPL-11c, FR-MTG-10, PE-05, PE-15, PE-22: the loose ends of the meeting routes. | Defaults: a deleted meeting is unreadable to its owner on `GET /v1/meetings/{id}` (the content is gone; the quotes route still answers `expired`), a discarded one is still the owner's to read; a discarded or deleted meeting is not editable. An attendee edit keeps a resolved `user` for an unchanged name and sets null for a new one (otherwise an edit would erase PE-15's resolution; a request never sets identity). The minutes may be read by the owner before they confirm them, only while `now < delete_minutes_after` (compared with `>=` as the transcript sweep does), with every string cleaned and redacted. No create or edit takes `world_id`; it is written once by `POST /v1/meetings/{id}/world` from the world maker (BQ-273) and needs one `check_write` row in `packages/people`. | PE-05, PE-15, PE-22; BQ-273, BQ-CCP3-2; API_PPL_11c.md sections 3 and 6 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-PPL11C-12 | PPL-11c, issue #464: "every write lists the whole org directory": which writes may? | Default: create meeting, create work item, both confirms and every work-item, meeting and world write read the actor's person record by id (at most two person records) and never call `persons.list`. Directory writes (create person, first person, memberships, roles) may list, because uniqueness and membership need it. | API_PPL_11c.md section 7; issue #464 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-AGT14-3 | FR-AGT-14b (the schedule store, the schedule routes, the scheduled-run start and the tick, all in `tilo-api`; supervisor option B, 6 Oct): what is decided where BQ-AGT14-1, BQ-AGT14-2 and the PRD are silent? (Follows BQ-AGT14-2; the merger may renumber.) | (a) The schedule record snapshots what the owner approved: the brief, the chosen approach and the granted cap, copied by the server from the owner's task. It does not depend on an in-memory `TaskRecord` surviving a redeploy. (b) The schedule routes (`POST`, `GET`, `PATCH`, `DELETE /schedules`) are owner-scoped: the uid comes from the session, never the body; another user's schedule or task answers the same 404 as an unknown one; only a task whose budget is already granted, and only one of its approaches, can be scheduled; at most 10 schedules per owner (409). Several schedules for one task are allowed, because the cap bounds them. (c) The budget at fire time goes only through the policy engine: the owner's L3 `grant_budget` policy that covers the cap grants it, with authz by policy. With no covering policy, or any other outcome, the firing is skipped with `budget_not_granted`, no world is created, `next_due` moves to the next slot and the failure backs off. A model never decides it, and an earlier `budget.granted` is never reused. (d) World and run ids are deterministic from the slot key (sha256), so a repeated or concurrent start finds the world and starts no second run. (e) Failures: a failed start records the generic code `start_failed`, never exception text; `failures` resets on a successful `complete`; a claim is refused during backoff; `PATCH` with `enabled: true` clears `failures` and `backoff_until`; `record_failure` with a stale `due` is a no-op; after `MAX_FAILURES` = 8 the schedule is disabled; the backoff is `min(60 s * 2**failures, 24 h)`. (f) A record with no brief snapshot is skipped with `snapshot_missing` and backs off. (g) The title is an owner-typed string, 1 to 140 characters, control characters refused, no model screening. (h) The timestamps of a fired run's events and authz use the injected clock, not the wall clock. (i) A live Firestore path for the schedule store opens only by explicit config (`TILO_SCHEDULES_STORE=firestore` plus `TILO_FIRESTORE_PROJECT`, ADR-0023, an addendum to ADR-0003); the default refuses and builds no client. (j) `POST /internal/schedules/tick` on `tilo-api` is a public route of `AccessGate` with its own OIDC check (`iss`, `aud`, `email_verified`, and the caller email from config). `access.PUBLIC_ROUTES` and the SEC-15 pinned set therefore gain it: an owner decision the PR flags. Unconfigured, it answers 503. The default verifier reads the unverified JWT header before any network call to Google's certificates, so a malformed token costs no fetch. (k) A raising `store.complete` is logged per schedule and the tick goes on; this changes BQ-AGT14-2 (e). (l) No system test is added: the dev app has no verifier or clock seam, and adding one would put a dev-only bypass in an auth route; the flow is covered in-process on the real app instead. (m) PR 2 (infra, security-flagged, the user rolls it out) retargets the Scheduler job from the harness to `tilo-api`; until then the tick answers 503. No Cloud Tasks client is built in this shape because the run starts in-process; the in-process starter replaces the Cloud Tasks `RunStarter` that BQ-AGT14-2 left to a follow-up. | FR-AGT-14; PRD 6.3, 8.6; ADR-0003, ADR-0023; BQ-AGT14-1, BQ-AGT14-2 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-WEB-7b | Is there a hand-written line cap on a Park page PR? | Supervisor ruling 2026-10-06, overridable by the user; it widens BQ-WEB-7 (merged or open as #489) so a string of per-page exceptions is not needed. A Park page ships as ONE PR whatever its size. CSS transcribed 1:1 from the design `.dc.html` counts as transcription when the PR body names its source file. There is no hand-written line cap on a single-page PR, provided the code reviewer states it read the whole hand-written diff (model and logic files included) and posts PASS, and the PR body states the sizes. Shared files stay limited to the `pages.ts` line and the `REAL_PAGES` entry. Applies to Ledger (#486, about 1000 TS/TSX lines plus 750 CSS), Agenda (#494, about 1136 TS/TSX lines: AgendaPage 90, Boards 289, Detail 121, Week 213, model 314, useAgenda 109, plus the ~960-line `Agenda.css` transcribed from `Tilo App Agenda.dc.html`) and the other pages. | CLAUDE.md ticket loop (400 lines), plan 10, BQ-WEB-7 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-WIRE2-1 | WIRE-2, FR-WLD-08, S-14: what does a Park world show when `GET /worlds` serves `title: null` (a world with no recorded `world.requested`)? | Default: the neutral label "Untitled world" for both `name` and `short`. A real title is passed through unedited and untruncated (the API serves one title; the Park `short` is not a different field). No scenario name, no sample copy (HR-1). | PRD 9.4 HR-1..HR-7, `services/api/tests/API_S_14_WORLDS_LIST.md`, `apps/web/src/park/api/live/worldsFromList.ts` | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-WIRE2-2 | WIRE-2, S-14, PRD 9.4: how do the five served `GET /worlds` statuses (idle, started, paused, completed, failed) map to the four Park `WorldState` values (needs, running, finished, scheduled)? | Default: started -> running and completed -> finished are the only exact matches. idle -> scheduled and paused -> scheduled (nothing is running, no failure or finish is recorded, no `when` is shown). failed -> finished (the run ended; the Park type has no failure state, and `out`, `runs` and `meta` stay empty so it claims no result). `needs` is never produced from the list: a world needs a person only from a recorded open card, which the list does not carry, and `running` only from `world.started` or `world.resumed` (HR-1, HR-5). An unknown status in a reply is an unreadable reply (BAD_REPLY), not a guess. A failure state in `WorldState` is a follow-up for the Park lane. | PRD 9.4 HR-1, HR-5, section 8 world events (`paused`, `failed`), S-14 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-WIRE2-3 | WIRE-2, S-14: which `World` fields does `GET /worlds` not serve, and what do they hold? | Default: `tool`, `spend`, `nextL`, `nextV` and `owner` are ""; `agents` and `spendV` are 0; every optional field (`meta`, `prog`, `of`, `budget`, `when`, `isNew`, `plan`, `districts`, `activity`, `row`, `single`, `runs`, `skill`, `out`) is absent or empty; `last_seq` has no Park field and is dropped. `started` is `created_at` as served (an ISO string, the time of `world.requested`; "" when null), unformatted: showing it as a person's date is a Park UI matter. `tier` is required by the Park type and is not served, so it is "Village", the smallest tier and the weakest claim, until the API serves a tier; making `World.tier` optional is a follow-up for the Park lane. | PRD 9.4 HR-1..HR-7, `apps/web/src/park/api/types.ts` | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-WIRE2-4 | WIRE-2: how do the session-local `added` worlds and the `dec` flags combine with the live list in `getWorlds(dec, added)`? | Default: `dec` is ignored (the list carries no decision state; `needs` comes from recorded cards only, never from a local flag). `added` is kept as the fixture Api keeps it: first, in the order given, then the live worlds; if a live world has the same id as an added one the live world wins and the added copy is dropped. `added` is not mutated. | PRD 9.4 HR-1, `apps/web/src/park/api/fixtures/worlds.ts` (`worlds`), `apps/web/src/park/app/store.ts` (`addWorld`) | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-WIRE2-5 | WIRE-2, S-14: how does the live `getWorlds` walk the pages, and what is the cap? | Default: `GET /worlds?limit=100` (the API maximum), then the same request plus `cursor=<next_cursor>` (URL-encoded, never parsed) while `next_cursor` is a string; any failed or unreadable page rejects `TiloApiError` with no partial list. The walk stops after 10 pages (1,000 worlds, newest first) so a server that never ends it cannot hang a page; at the cap the worlds read so far are returned. A paged or searched Worlds page is a follow-up. | `services/api/tests/API_S_14_WORLDS_LIST.md` (BQ-S14-3), FR-WLD-08 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-S14-1 | API-WORLDS-LIST (`GET /worlds`, S-14; follows BQ-WIRE1-3): which worlds does a caller see? The PRD has no per-person world list rule. | The existing rules: a principal with role `owner` sees every world whose record `owner` is their `user_id`, whatever world their principal names (as `GET /costs` does); any other role sees only the one world `Principal.world_id` names, if it exists (as the per-world routes' membership check). An unknown or no world is an empty list, not a 404 (no membership oracle). Another person's world never appears. | S-14, FR-WLD-08, PRD 10.1, `tilo_api.costs`, `tilo_api.redaction` | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-S14-2 | API-WORLDS-LIST: what does an item carry, when `WorldRecord` stores no title, status or created time and no contract has a world summary? | No contract change: the item is `world_id`, `title` (`data.title` of the first `world.requested`, else null), `status` (the latest registered world event, the same rule as the web's `worldStatus`: `idle`, `started`, `paused`, `completed`, `failed`; never the `paused` flag alone), `created_at` (`ts` of that first `world.requested`, else null) and `last_seq` (0 with no events). Nothing is invented: a world with no events lists as null, `idle`, 0. | S-14, FR-WLD-08, PRD 9.4 HR-1, HR-5, `world.requested` data schema | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-S14-3 | API-WORLDS-LIST: order, page size and cursor. The PRD names none. | Newest first by `created_at`, ties by `world_id` descending, nulls last; `limit` default 20, 1 to 100; an opaque keyset `cursor` (no world id in it), `next_cursor` null on the last page, no empty trailing page. | S-14 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-S14-4 | API-WORLDS-LIST: what is `last_seq` for a member who is not the owner, whose events route renumbers and drops hidden events? | The last seq of their own view of `GET /worlds/{id}/events`, so the list leaks no count of hidden events (SEC-07). The owner gets the stored last seq. | S-14, FR-HIL-09, SEC-07, FR-WLD-08 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-S14-5 | API-WORLDS-LIST: which status for a bad `limit` or `cursor`? | `{"detail": ...}` like every route here: 422 for a bad `limit` (FastAPI query validation), 400 `bad_cursor` for a cursor the API did not mint for this caller. The tests accept 400 or 422 for either, and 200 only with the caller's own worlds for another person's cursor. | S-14, API error shape | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-WIRE3-1 | WIRE-3, S-06: how does a live Park `Decision` keep the card's `world_id` and `card_id`, when the shared park `DecisionKey` is "depot" or "mail" and `Decision` has no card fields? | Default: no shared park type changes. `decisionsFromCards` gives each decision an opaque, non-empty `k` that is unique per (world_id, card_id) and stable, and sets `world` to the world_id. The live Api keeps the way back from `k` to world_id, card_id and the answer option in memory, filled by `getDecisions`; `decide` of a key it never served rejects `TiloApiError` and sends nothing (so a stale or forged key never reaches the network). A follow-up may widen `DecisionKey` to string when the Park pages are rewired. | `services/api/tests/API_S_06_CARDS_FEED.md` (BQ-S06-2), FR-HIL-01, FR-HIL-02 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-WIRE3-2 | WIRE-3, S-06, FR-HIL-01: `Api.decide(key)` takes no option, but a card has two or three. Which option does a tap answer with? | Default: the affirmative one, and `Decision.yes` is its label. A gate card: `approve` (only an explicit person action opens it; the suggested option there is `hold`, the safe default). A consent card: `connect`. A needs_you or meeting card: the one `suggested` option. Edit, hold, snooze and delegate are not reachable from the Park decision button yet; a follow-up adds `decide(key, option?)` and the page controls for them. Nothing answers a card except `decide`: reading the feed only sends GET. | `services/api/tests/API_S_06_CARDS_FEED.md` (BQ-S06-2), FR-HIL-01, FR-HIL-02 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-WIRE3-3 | WIRE-3, S-06: how does the live `getDecisions` walk the pages, and what is the cap? | Default: as WIRE-2 (BQ-WIRE2-5): `GET /cards?limit=100` (the API maximum), then the same request plus `cursor=<next_cursor>` (URL-encoded, never parsed) while `next_cursor` is a string; any failed or unreadable page rejects `TiloApiError` with no partial list. The walk stops after 10 pages (1,000 cards, oldest first) so a server that never ends it cannot hang a page; at the cap the cards read so far are returned. Nothing is cached, so an answered card is gone on the next read (FR-HIL-11). A feed item the Api cannot read (unknown kind, no affirmative option, `card.world_id` or `card.id` differing from its wrapper) is an unreadable reply for the whole page. The needs-you count (`GET /cards?limit=1` -> `count`) has no Api method and is left to the shell lane. | `services/api/tests/API_S_06_CARDS_FEED.md` (BQ-S06-2), FR-HIL-01, FR-HIL-02 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-WIRE3-4 | WIRE-3, S-06, FR-HIL-02, SEC-06: the Park `Decision` has no field for what a gate or consent card holds. How does the person see what a tap does before an explicit approval? | Default: no type change. `title` is `what_happened` and `body` is `why_it_matters`, both unedited. A gate card's `body` also carries `gate.action.exact` and every `gate.recipients` entry; a consent card's `body` also carries every `consent.scopes` entry. A follow-up may add a typed detail field to `Decision`. | `services/api/tests/API_S_06_CARDS_FEED.md` (BQ-S06-2), FR-HIL-01, FR-HIL-02 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-WIRE3-5 | WIRE-3, S-06, PRD 9.4: which `Decision` fields does `GET /cards` not serve, and what do they hold? | Default: `icon`, `done`, `toast` and `approvedTitle` are "" and `facts` is absent: never sample copy, never a scenario word. Pages that read them must show nothing for an empty value (a page follow-up; this lane does not touch pages). A follow-up may make these fields optional. | `services/api/tests/API_S_06_CARDS_FEED.md` (BQ-S06-2), FR-HIL-01, FR-HIL-02 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-WIRE3-6 | WIRE-3, S-06: what does `decide` resolve with, and what is `dec`? | Default: `{ data: { ok: true, dec }, source: "live" }` only for `200 {outcome: "answered"}`, where `dec[k]` is true for this key and every key answered earlier through the same Api instance (as the fixture Api accumulates). A 4xx `{outcome: "rejected", reason}` (already_resolved, card_not_found, forbidden, busy, invalid_option, channel_not_permitted, invalid_channel) rejects `TiloApiError` (served status, retryable false, `write` true, a plain sentence from `src/api/http.ts`); a 200 body that says rejected is the same error; a 5xx (503 `event_write_failed` included), a network failure and any other 200 body reject with `write` true. Exactly one POST per call: no retry and no refresh read, so a person's answer is never sent twice by the code. | `services/api/tests/API_S_06_CARDS_FEED.md` (BQ-S06-2), FR-HIL-01, FR-HIL-02 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-WIRE3-7 | WIRE-3, S-06: what does `undo` do on the live Api? | Default: it stays `NotBuilt`. The first answer wins and no route reopens a card, so the Park undo has nothing real to call; it never fakes success. A follow-up may remove the Undo control for live decisions or add a route through a contract change. | `services/api/tests/API_S_06_CARDS_FEED.md` (BQ-S06-2), FR-HIL-01, FR-HIL-02 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-PPL11C-13 | PPL-11c S4, SEC-10: does a 413 (oversized body) count against the 60 writes a minute per principal? | Yes: every authenticated write attempt counts, a 413 included, so oversized-payload spam cannot bypass the limiter. The cap is counted before the body is read, and a 429 itself is not counted. Supervisor decision 7 Oct, safer default. | SEC-10, BQ-PPL11C-1, BQ-PPL11C-2 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-PPL11C-14 | PPL-11c S4, BQ-PPL11C-4: what scopes an `Idempotency-Key`? | The principal and the route: the same key on another create route is a separate request, never a replay of (or a 422 reuse of) the first route's result. In memory per install for now (a restart forgets keys; a Firestore-backed store is the follow-up). Keys are bounded: 16 per principal (least recently used first, only by that principal's own creates) and 512 principals, 8,192 stored answers in all; a refused, failed or still-running create holds no stored answer, takes no principal slot and evicts nobody (only a principal with a stored answer counts against the 512, and the one that is storing is never the one pushed out); a key in use is never evicted. The 512-principal and 8,192-answer bounds are approximate: requests in flight can exceed them briefly, by at most the number in flight; the per-principal pass of 16 still counts a principal's own in-flight entries (its own traffic only). Remaining bound: 512 or more principals with successful creates push out the least recently used idle principal's keys (a replay of such a key then creates again). Supervisor decision 7 Oct, safer default. | BQ-PPL11C-4, PE-19 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-S06-1 | API-CARDS-FEED (`GET /cards`, S-06; follows BQ-WIRE1-4): which worlds and which cards does a caller's feed cover? The PRD has no cross-world queue rule. | The S-14 scope (BQ-S14-1): role `owner` covers every world whose record `owner` is their `user_id`, whatever world their principal names; any other role covers only `Principal.world_id`, if it exists. Within each world the feed reads the world's own `CardStore.list_for_world(world_id, principal=...)` with the principal narrowed to that world, so visibility (owner all; Team Lead their districts, never a console-routed question; Approver assigned cards; one card per merged batch) and redaction are exactly the per-world route's and the feed can never show more. A Viewer, a person with no world, or an unknown world gets an empty list, never a 403 or 404 (no membership oracle; the per-world route's 403 is not repeated). | S-06, FR-HIL-01, FR-HIL-02, FR-HIL-09, SEC-07, PRD 10.1, BQ-S14-1 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-S06-2 | API-CARDS-FEED: what does the response and each item carry? No contract has a feed shape. | No contract change. `200 {"cards": [item, ...], "count": int, "next_cursor": str or null}`. An item is exactly `{"world_id", "card_id", "card"}`: `card` is the card byte for byte as `GET /worlds/{id}/cards` returns it (so FR-HIL-01 structure and every redaction are unchanged), and `world_id` and `card_id` repeat `card.world_id` and `card.id` so a client routes an answer to `POST /worlds/{world_id}/cards/{card_id}/answer` without reading the card. No owner id, district, assignee, outbox id or run id is added. | S-06, FR-HIL-01, card.schema.json | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-S06-3 | API-CARDS-FEED: what order is the queue in? The PRD names none. | Oldest open first: `card.created_at` ascending, ties by `world_id` then `card_id` ascending (a total, deterministic order). It is the per-world route's order (oldest first) extended across worlds, so the card that has waited longest is first and a card's place never depends on its risk tier or on a model. | S-06, FR-HIL-02 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-S06-4 | API-CARDS-FEED: page size and cursor, and which status for a bad one? | As S-14 (BQ-S14-3): `limit` default 20, 1 to 100, and `next_cursor` null on the last page with no empty trailing page. The cursor is opaque (no world or card id in it), a keyset on the order above bound by a keyed MAC to the caller's user id, so a card answered between two pages causes no duplicate and no gap. Stricter than S-14 on the status, because the ticket fixes it: a bad `limit` is 422 and a bad, forged or another person's cursor is 400 `{"detail": ...}`; the scope is applied on every page whatever the cursor says. The MAC key is the builder's choice (it may reuse `TILO_WORLDS_CURSOR_KEY`). | S-06, BQ-S14-3, BQ-S14-5 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-S06-5 | API-CARDS-FEED: what is an "open" card, and what do expiry and timeouts do to the feed? FR-HIL-11 says an answer elsewhere must vanish on the next read. | Open is the store's own status: `CardStore` marks a card resolved when a person answers it, or `tick()` applies a question's safe default after its deadline (FR-HIL-02), and a resolved card is gone from the feed on the very next read (2 s or less, FR-HIL-11, no cache). A question past its deadline but not yet ticked is still listed (as on the per-world route); a gate or consent card is only held by a timeout, never resolved, so it stays listed until a person answers. The feed is read-only: it never calls `tick()` and never writes an event. | S-06, FR-HIL-02, FR-HIL-11, PRD 10.3 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-S06-6 | API-CARDS-FEED: can the shell's needs-you count come from the same route? | Yes: the body carries `count`, the number of open cards in the caller's whole queue after the same visibility filter, independent of `limit` and `cursor` (the same on every page). The shell reads it with `GET /cards?limit=1`. It reveals nothing the per-world lists would not (a Team Lead's count excludes hidden districts, SEC-07). | S-06, FR-HIL-11, SEC-07, BQ-WIRE1-4 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-S06-7 | API-CARDS-FEED: how does a person answer from the feed, and what about T3? | The feed has no write verbs (POST, PUT and DELETE on `/cards` are 404 or 405) and no answer route of its own: an answer goes to the existing `POST /worlds/{world_id}/cards/{card_id}/answer` with the item's `world_id` and `card_id`, unchanged (identity from the session, a gate opens only in a signed-in web session, first answer wins). T3 gate cards are listed like any other card; reading the feed never resolves one, and no model or page decides authorisation. | S-06, FR-HIL-01, SEC-06, FR-HIL-04 (T3 needs a person), PRD 10.4, BQ-WIRE1-4 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-S06-8 | API-CARDS-FEED: what if one world's card store fails during a read? | The whole read is a 503 `{"detail": ...}` with a fixed message and no exception text, never a 200 that silently omits that world: a partial queue would hide a decision that is waiting for a person. A bad `limit` or `cursor` is refused (422, 400) before any world is read. | S-06, FR-HIL-11, BQ-S14-5 (API error shape) | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-AGT09-1 | FR-AGT-09 (context handoff at 70%): what is decided where PRD 8.5 and the contracts are silent? | (a) The successor is the first spare agent of the unit's rank in layout order that works no unit and was not yet a successor; none left fails the unit closed before anything is written. (b) Order: validate the note against `handoff.schema.json`, store it, then `agent.context_handoff_started`, `agent.spawned`, `agent.assigned`, the successor's verification, `agent.context_handoff_completed`, `agent.retired` for the old agent. (c) The successor verifies through an optional `runner.verify(unit, context)`; no `verify`, an exception or anything but True is a failed verification, which still emits `completed` with `verification_passed` false and fails the unit closed (both agents retire, no `agent.handoff`). (d) An unfinished outcome below `config.context.handoff_at` (or with no share) fails closed; a finished outcome at any share is not a handoff. (e) `context_pct` is `int(round(share * 100))`. (f) The note is stored as `application/json` with the AR-05 key `<run>:<unit>:<1000+n>:0`, clear of unit outputs, and is not announced as an artifact event. | FR-AGT-09; PRD 8.5, 8.6, EV-10 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-XWORLD-1 | API-CARDS-XWORLD (S-06 cross-world answering, issue #539): who may answer a card in a world their session does not name, and how is ownership checked? The PRD has no cross-world answer rule. | Role `owner` only, checked per card on every request: the card's world's `WorldRecord.owner` is the principal's `user_id` and the run's `CardStore` owner for that world is the same user. Either one naming someone else, a disagreement between them, or no such world is the same 404 (status and body) the principal gets for a world id that does not exist, so a real card and no card in another person's world look alike. Answering in one world opens no other. Approver, Team Lead and Viewer keep today's one-world binding for answers and reads. A session bound to the card's world is unchanged. | S-06, PRD 10.1, FR-HIL-09, SEC-07, BQ-S14-1, BQ-S06-1 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-XWORLD-2 | API-CARDS-XWORLD: which sessions may answer cross-world? `deps.Principal` has no "minted for one world" field. | Only the user's own signed-in web or mobile_web session: `Principal.channel` in (`web`, `mobile_web`) and not a tilo-channels caller. An email session (`channel` email, or any tilo-channels caller, which is how signed single-use email links act, PRD 10.3) answers only in the world its principal names; share links (FR-HIL-12) are Approver principals on `/share/...` and stay bound. An ineligible session gets the unknown-world 404 before any channel check, so it learns nothing. An owner web session that names no world (`world_id` None) may answer in an owned world, as the feed shows it those cards. If sign-in later adds a session kind, only a full web or mobile_web session qualifies. | S-06, PRD 10.3, SEC-06, SEC-08, FR-HIL-12 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-XWORLD-3 | API-CARDS-XWORLD: which checks apply to a cross-world answer, and in what order? | First the cross-world eligibility (BQ-XWORLD-1, BQ-XWORLD-2; 404). Then every existing check exactly as for a session bound to the card's world, same order, same status and body: Pause all of the card's world (409), a body claiming `pin_verified` or a non-web channel (403 `channel_not_permitted`; no PIN path exists, ADR-0019), `session_allows` (400 `invalid_channel`), then the store's `card_not_found`, `forbidden`, `already_resolved` (first answer wins), `busy`, `invalid_option`, `invalid_channel`. An unknown card in an owned world is what the bound session gets. Reading `GET /cards` or `GET /worlds/{id}/cards` never answers or ticks. | S-06, FR-HIL-04 (T3 never automated), FR-HIL-11, SEC-06, BQ-S06-7 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-XWORLD-4 | API-CARDS-XWORLD: do the events of a cross-world answer carry the session's world? | No. The parts handed to the card's world's writer are byte for byte a bound answer's (type, data, `authz` `{by: person, ref: user_id, at}`, `event_id`, scope). `approval.resolved` data and `common#authz` are `additionalProperties: false` with no session-world field, and the event already names the actor (`authz.ref`, for gates and consents), the target world and the card, which is what PRD 10.4 Audit and SEC-11 require. Putting the session's world into the stream would need a contract change (a new registered event type, or a field on `approval.resolved`, `question.answered` and `connection.consented`); not done. | S-06, PRD 10.4, SEC-11, FR-HIL-08, contracts/events/data/approval.resolved.schema.json | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-XWORLD-5 | API-CARDS-XWORLD: how is a cross-world answer audited? | An application log record per answered cross-world request (INFO or above, a `tilo_api.*` logger), also for a gate `edit`/`hold` or a consent `hold` that writes no event, naming the actor's `user_id`, the session's world (when there is one), the target world, the card id and the option. Ids only: no bearer token, cookie, header value or card text. It is an operations log, not an event (BQ-XWORLD-4); a later audit-log sink may ingest it. | S-06, PRD 10.4, SEC-11, root CLAUDE.md (no secrets in logs) | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-XWORLD-6 | API-CARDS-XWORLD: does `GET /worlds/{world_id}/cards` open cross-world too? | Yes, for an owner, matching the feed: role `owner` and the world's record owner is the caller (the run's `CardStore.list_for_world` still decides what is shown), whatever channel the principal names, because `GET /cards` has no channel rule. The result equals what a session bound to that world gets and the feed's items for it. Another person's world is the unknown-world 404; non-owner roles stay bound. | S-06, BQ-S06-1, SEC-07 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-XWORLD-7 | API-CARDS-XWORLD: which scope ties the routes together? | One scope: for an owner, the worlds they can answer in equal the worlds `GET /worlds` lists and `GET /cards` covers (BQ-S14-1, BQ-S06-1); for anyone else only the world their principal names. The S-06 feed and S-14 list tests stay as they are. | S-06, S-14, BQ-S14-1, BQ-S06-1 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-XWORLD-8 | API-CARDS-XWORLD: whose Pause all applies to a cross-world answer? | The card's world's: a paused target world refuses the answer (409, as for a bound session, BQ-90); a paused session world does not stop an answer in an unpaused owned world, since Pause all stops one world's run. | FR-WLD-07, BQ-90 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-AGT14-4 | FR-AGT-14d (rollout follow-ups of the scheduled-runs security and code reviews, `tilo-api` and `tilo-harness`; 6 Oct): what is decided where BQ-AGT14-3 and the PRD are silent? (Follows BQ-AGT14-3; the merger may renumber.) | (a) The tick answers 503 for an unset audience or caller before the token and before any factory is called. The schedule store and the run seams are built by two factories (`get_schedule_store_factory`, `get_seams_factory`) that the route calls only after the token passed, once per request, store first; an anonymous or refused call builds neither. A good token with no store configured is 503 `not_configured`; any other factory failure is a fixed 500. (b) `disable_for_owner(owner)` on both stores counts only records that changed from enabled to disabled (`last_error="owner_disabled"`); an invalid owner id raises ValueError and changes nothing; a held claim does not stop it; there is no endpoint or trigger yet (FR-MEM-09 owns that). (c) `ScheduleRetention` in `tilo_harness.schedule_store` (tilo-harness already depends on tilo-memory, so no new dependency) yields every document, including undecodable ones by their raw owner; a document with no owner has `user_id=None` and survives an erasure; `entity` is "schedule" and `created_at` is the document's own or the store clock's now. "schedule" is NOT in `packages/memory` `RETENTION_DAYS`: `purge_expired` over a registry holding the adapter needs an entity and a retention window decision first (open). (d) `StartSkipped(count=False)` releases the claim and records nothing (no failure, no backoff, no move; it wins over `advance`); the tick continues with the next schedule, because the seams are global and the rest skip alike; the code is logged once per tick. `run_seams_unwired` uses it. This changes BQ-AGT14-3 (e) for that code: two 14b tests that pinned `failures == 1` are changed deliberately. (e) `PATCH {"enabled": true}` moves `next_due` to the first slot strictly after the store's now only when the schedule was disabled before and its `next_due` is at or before now: an already enabled due schedule hit by `enabled: true` keeps its slot, so no legitimate fire is skipped; an explicit `next_due` (a cadence change) wins. (f) The default Google verifier fetches certificates with a 3 s timeout (`CERT_FETCH_TIMEOUT_S`), reuses them for 900 s from the fetch (`CERT_TTL_S`), through an injectable transport and clock, as one shared instance. An unknown `kid` inside the TTL refetches at most once per 30 s; a failed fetch is never cached; a failed refresh past the TTL is refused, never served from stale certificates. Security review of 14d: single-flight plus a cooldown replaced the unbounded flood risk (junk tokens that pass the unverified pre-checks used to queue behind the lock, each with its own fetch, and starved the thread pool). The fetch lock is taken with a non-blocking acquire, so a caller that finds a fetch in flight is refused at once and at most one thread is ever inside a fetch; certificates are read from an immutable snapshot with no lock, so a known `kid` on a fresh snapshot never waits; every attempt (cold, expired, unknown `kid`, failed) starts a `CERT_RETRY_COOLDOWN_S` of 10 s, so a dead endpoint costs at most one fetch per cooldown. The pinned retry-at-once test now advances its clock past the cooldown before the retry. An empty `kid` fails the unverified pre-checks. (g) When the verifier raises, the tick logs one warning with `type(exc).__name__` only (never the token, the text or a traceback) and still answers 401. | FR-AGT-14; FR-MEM-09; PRD 6.3, 8.6, 15.1; ADR-0023; BQ-AGT14-3 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-SEC10-1 | SEC-10 (PRD 16 "App Check", PRD 6.2), `tilo-api`: what turns App Check enforcement on, and what is the default? | The environment flag `TILO_APPCHECK_ENFORCE`, read on every request; only exactly `"1"` enables it (the convention of `TILO_API_DEV` and `TILO_JUDGE_LIVE`); unset, empty or anything else is off. The default is OFF (infra/ACCESS.md section 7) and the owner alone flips it, in the Cloud Run service's environment. Off, a missing, empty, refused or unverifiable `X-Firebase-AppCheck` token is logged once as a WARNING on `tilo_api.appcheck` (the error type only, never the token) and the status and body are unchanged. Test-writer default, supervisor-approved. | SEC-10; infra/ACCESS.md s7 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-SEC10-2 | SEC-10, `tilo-api`: which routes are never checked for an App Check token? | `GET /health`, `GET /healthz` (Cloud Run probes carry no token) and `POST /internal/schedules/tick` (Cloud Scheduler; its own Google OIDC check, FR-AGT-14b, BQ-AGT14-4). Every other route, the public access routes included, is checked. A browser CORS preflight (`OPTIONS`) carries no custom header, so it can never pass the check; it must not be answered by the gate and `OPTIONS` is never exempted for handlers. CORS therefore has to sit OUTSIDE `AppCheckGate` (dev mode adds `CORSMiddleware` last, so it does; its `allow_headers` lists `X-Firebase-AppCheck`). The deployed web app is NOT same-origin through Hosting: `web.app` and the Cloud Run API are different origins (Hosting has no rewrite to the API), so before enforcement is switched on the deployed API needs CORS middleware outside the gate that allows the web origin and the `X-Firebase-AppCheck` header (open; the deployed API has none today, so the browser cannot call it cross-origin at all yet). Test-writer default, supervisor-approved; corrected by the PR-2 builder. | SEC-10; FR-AGT-14b | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-SEC10-3 | SEC-10, `tilo-api`: what does enforcement answer, and how is a token verified? | Enforcement on, a missing, empty, refused or failing-verifier token is 401 `{"detail": "A valid App Check token is required.", "code": "app_check_required"}` before the route runs (a verifier that raises closes the call, never a 500); a valid token passes. The middleware `tilo_api.appcheck.AppCheckGate` sits outside `AccessGate` and inside `NoStore`. `deps.get_app_check_verifier()` returns `GoogleAppCheckVerifier`: RS256 signature against `https://firebaseappcheck.googleapis.com/v1/jwks` (3 s timeout, keys cached 900 s, single-flight fetch, 10 s cooldown after any attempt), `exp`, `nbf`, issuer prefix `https://firebaseappcheck.googleapis.com/`, and `aud` containing `projects/<project>`. The project is `TILO_FIRESTORE_PROJECT`, else `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT`; with neither, or in dev mode (`TILO_API_DEV=1`), the verifier refuses without opening a socket. Built on `cryptography`, already a `google-auth` dependency (no new package). Test-writer default for the 401, supervisor-approved. | SEC-10; PRD 6.2 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-XWORLD-9 | API-CARDS-XWORLD-2 (PR #561 security flag 1; supervisor decision 7 Oct): what does an owner session that is not a full web sign-in read of another owned world's cards? `reading_principal` opened full cards, gate text and recipients included, to an owner session on any channel. | Decided by the supervisor: only a redacted summary. An owner session without the `full_session` marker (BQ-XWORLD-10; an email session, a signed-link session) reads a world other than its bound one, on `GET /worlds/{world_id}/cards` and in `GET /cards` alike, as the same cards in the same order, each reduced to exactly `{id, world_id, kind, created_at, timeout, risk_tier, redacted: true}` (`risk_tier` is the gate's tier, else null). Default chosen for the shape: an allowlist with no free text, so no gate action text, destination or recipients, no consent scopes, no options, and no `what_happened` or `why_it_matters`, which can name a recipient. The feed's item `card` stays what the per-world route shows the same principal (BQ-S06-2); count, order and paging are unchanged. Bound-world reads are unchanged; a full-session owner gets full cards across worlds as before; another person's world is still the unknown-world 404. This narrows BQ-XWORLD-6. | S-06, PRD 10.1, 10.3, SEC-07, SEC-08, BQ-XWORLD-6, BQ-S06-2, `services/api/tests/API_CARDS_XWORLD.md` | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-XWORLD-10 | API-CARDS-XWORLD-2 (PR #561 security flag 2; supervisor decision 7 Oct): how does the server tell a full web sign-in from a one-world session, so a signed-link landing session minted as "web" cannot answer across worlds? | Decided by the supervisor: an explicit marker on the server-side `deps.Principal`, `full_session: bool = False`, set only by the server for a full web sign-in session (the dev-mode principal is one, `full_session=True`); nothing in a request sets it. `deps.Principal` is not a contract, so no contract change. Cross-world answers need the marker on top of every BQ-XWORLD-1/2 rule (role owner, channel `web` or `mobile_web`, not a tilo-channels caller, both owner checks). Without it, a `web` or `mobile_web` principal and every email session stay bound: any other world is the unknown-world 404 for every kind and option, T3 gates included, and the Pause-all guard does not run for them (a paused other world is the 404, not the 409). Bound-world answers are unchanged. Test fixtures that build a full web session set the marker. This narrows BQ-XWORLD-2. | S-06, PRD 10.3, FR-HIL-04, SEC-06, SEC-08, BQ-XWORLD-1, BQ-XWORLD-2, BQ-XWORLD-8 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-PL02-1 | PL-02 / PL-LIVE-E2E: what `max_output_tokens` does a thinking role get, and how is a reply cut off at its cap treated? | A reply that stops at its output cap (Gemini `MAX_TOKENS`, OpenAI-compatible `length`) raises `OutputTruncatedError` (never a reply, never retried or schema-repaired, a fallback profile is still tried); `vertex_smoke.classify` names it `truncated`. Roles with thinking `high` or `medium` (director, verifier, lead) get `max_output_tokens: 16384` in `config/models.yaml`, `models.gemini.yaml` and `models.nim.yaml`: thinking tokens count against the cap, the repo states no model output limit, 4096 was used up by thinking alone (first live B-007 run), and 16384 is 4x that; the tests require 8192 to the profile context, and 32768 was tried first but a director quote of ~65 credits broke `test_PL_LIVE_06_a_403_echoing_a_token_never_shows_in_the_router_error` (50-credit budget), so a larger cap needs that budget raised. The B-007 dry-run worst case (every Director call using its whole cap) rose from 33.2 to 88.5 credits against the default 40 cap (hard maximum 200); the meter, not the estimate, bounds a live run, so a person may pass `--max-credits`. Open: `live_e2e._OUT` still caps the Lead at 400 and the Verifier at 300, and nothing reads a role's `thinking` value. | PRD 6.4 PL-02, 6.6 EFF-03; issue #340; BQ-LE2E-9 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-CHNGATEE-1 | CHN-GATE-E (ADR-0025): where does `Participant` live, does the mailbox always appear in `read_participants`, and does the name filter's `_numberish` length rule also cap two neighbouring words joined? | The tests assume `Participant` and `read_participants` are in `tilo_channels.participants`; the mailbox owner is always one participant (its base local part only; display names vouch nothing, 8 Oct amendment, BQ-CHNGATEE-2), so no thread or a failed read gives `{mailbox: {local part}}`; the 100-participant cap is tested to allow either counting of that participant; a display name or local part is dropped whole when any word is not 2 to 24 ASCII letters (so `j.smith@` names nobody), and `_numberish` moves unchanged, so two neighbouring words that join to more than 24 letters are dropped too (a single 24-letter word is kept; the tests do not pin two-word names near the limit). Names are matched to the draft's To and Cc by base address (plus tag dropped). | ADR-0025 decisions 1, 3, 4, 7; issue #298 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-SEC10-4 | SEC-10, `tilo-api` (PR-1 review follow-up): what happens to the key set when a refresh fails or the process is cold? | Today a snapshot older than `JWKS_TTL_S` (900 s) is never served and a failed fetch stores nothing, so a cold start or an outage of the key endpoint refuses every token (a logged warning while enforcement is off, a 401 when it is on). BUILT in SEC-SWEEP-1 PR-1: the last good key set serves a key id it holds for up to `JWKS_STALE_GRACE_S` (1 h) past the TTL, only while a refresh is in flight or has just failed (still single-flight), so a rotated-out key is accepted for at most 75 min and only while refreshes are failing or running; the missing-token warning is sampled to one line a minute. Still open before enforcement is switched on: a true cold start with nothing held refuses every concurrent caller until the first fetch lands. Limited-use tokens (replay protection) are also not built. | SEC-10; BQ-SEC10-3 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-SEC10-5 | SEC-10, web client: where do the Firebase ids come from, when does App Check start, and what does the CSP allow? | (a) `FIREBASE_APP_ID` and the reCAPTCHA Enterprise site key are public literals in `apps/web/src/appcheck/config.ts`, recorded in infra/ACCESS.md s7 (supervisor-approved); the Firebase `apiKey` and `projectId` come only from `VITE_FIREBASE_API_KEY` and `VITE_FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID`, which `just deploy-web` fills at build time from `firebase apps:sdkconfig WEB <app id>` (`scripts/web_build_env.py`; nothing written to disk) ONLY when the owner sets `TILO_BAKE_FIREBASE_KEY=1`, which they do after restricting the browser key (API restriction to the Firebase App Check API, referrers `https://essential-wares-510320-f0.web.app/*` and `https://essential-wares-510320-f0.firebaseapp.com/*`, checked that it cannot call `generativelanguage` or another paid API: this project also uses Gemini Developer API keys, so "public by design" holds only for a restricted key; steps in infra/ACCESS.md s7 and docs/deploy-dev.md). The script also checks the key shape (`AIza` + 35 characters) and that the project id is the dev project's, and drops any `VITE_FIREBASE_*` left in the shell. With either missing, App Check is skipped and one console line (info) says so. (b) App Check starts on the first API request, not at page load, so the deployed fixture world never loads reCAPTCHA, and the Firebase SDK is a dynamic import off the landing bundle (NFR-01); local hosts (localhost, 127.0.0.1, ::1, `*.localhost`) never start it (EFF-01). (c) A start or token request that takes over 5 s (`TOKEN_TIMEOUT_MS`) gives up, and the call goes out without the header. (d) CSP additions, exactly: `script-src` https://www.google.com/recaptcha/ and https://www.gstatic.com/recaptcha/; `frame-src` https://www.google.com/recaptcha/; `connect-src` https://content-firebaseappcheck.googleapis.com. The connect host is what `@firebase/app-check` 0.13.1 calls (`BASE_ENDPOINT` in its `index.esm.js`), found by the security review: the first pin, `firebaseappcheck.googleapis.com`, is the server-side host `tilo-api` reads keys from, and is NOT in the CSP. The SDK's own code names no Firebase Installations or Identity Toolkit host, so none is added. The script origins come from the SDK's `RECAPTCHA_ENTERPRISE_URL`; the reCAPTCHA challenge frame host is not named by the SDK, and whether `https://recaptcha.google.com/recaptcha/` is also needed in `frame-src` is a pre-enforcement check (below), not added. `style-src` stays `'self'` (P0-07e): reCAPTCHA's own badge styling may be restricted by it, which is cosmetic; revisit only if the badge breaks the layout. (e) Until the deployed API has CORS for the web origin (BQ-SEC10-2), a deployed web app cannot call it, so App Check has nothing to protect there yet. (f) The Park pages' own `HttpClient` (`park/api/liveApi.ts`) gets the same getter as the main app. (g) After a deadline (one 5 s deadline covers the start and the token request) the getter answers null for 60 s (`BACKOFF_MS`), so a hung SDK costs one wait, not one per request and event poll; a dynamic import that failed is retried after that pause, a start refused by the SDK or a missing config is not; a token with characters a header cannot carry is sent as no header. (h) The SDK caches its token in the browser's IndexedDB (refreshed automatically). (i) BEFORE enforcement is switched on, a real-browser check on the deployed host is required: no CSP violations in the console for reCAPTCHA (frame-src `recaptcha.google.com`, the badge under `style-src 'self'`), a token is exchanged and the API log shows none refused. Follow-up text only, not built: `fetch` with `redirect: "error"` for API calls, and hardening the CSP test against scheme-less host sources. | SEC-10; PRD 6.2, 16; NFR-01; EFF-01 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-AGT14-5 | FR-AGT-14e (Cloud Tasks hand-off of scheduled runs, `tilo-api` and `tilo-harness`; 6 Oct): a scheduled run must not live on the Scheduler's request (Cloud Run bills request-based CPU, `cpu_idle = true`, so a run thread started by the tick is throttled the moment the tick returns and lost on scale-to-zero); what is decided where BQ-AGT14-3 and the PRD are silent? (Follows BQ-AGT14-4; supervisor decision: stay scale-to-zero under the EFF rules, no always-on CPU or minimum instances, which the user may still choose; the merger may renumber.) | (a) ONE route, two authenticated callers, no new public route: `POST /internal/schedules/tick` keeps its path, `access.PUBLIC_ROUTES` and the SEC-15 pin are unchanged. The verified caller picks the mode and neither identity ever gets the other's: the Scheduler (`TILO_SCHEDULE_TICK_CALLER`, no body) FANS OUT; Cloud Tasks (`TILO_TASKS_CALLER`, a task body) EXECUTES ONE RUN. The Scheduler with a body, the Tasks identity without one, any other identity, and one account configured for both are refused (422, 403 or 503, a fixed body, no side effect); the 14d token machinery (503 before the token for an unset audience or caller, `iss`, `aud`, exact `email`, `email_verified`, factories only after the token) is reused for both. `TILO_RUNS_QUEUE` (`projects/<p>/locations/asia-southeast1/queues/runs`) or `TILO_TASKS_CALLER` unset or malformed: 503 after the token and before anything is claimed (nothing runs inline any more); both are read per request and are the user's to set. (b) FAN-OUT: the Ticker claims each due slot and `TaskEnqueuer` (the tick's `RunStarter`) creates one Cloud Task named `task_name(slot_key)` in the queue (dispatch deadline 1800 s, retry policy left to the queue, OIDC token for `TILO_TASKS_CALLER` with the api audience, JSON body of five keys, no brief, owner, cap or secret), then the slot completes as before. It builds no run seams (`get_seams_factory` is not called), writes nothing to any world and needs only the schedule store and a Tasks client built after the token (`get_tasks_client_factory`; the real one, `CloudTasksRestClient`, is the Cloud Tasks REST API through google-auth's `AuthorizedSession`, 10 s timeout, no new dependency, never logging the body or the token). ALREADY_EXISTS (an HTTP 409 whose body says `error.status` is `ALREADY_EXISTS`; any other 409, such as ABORTED, is a failure) is a success; a 401, 403 or 404 from Cloud Tasks is the service's (wrong `TILO_TASKS_CALLER`, missing actAs, no queue), so it is an uncounted `StartSkipped(count=False)` (`tasks_unavailable`: claim released, nothing recorded, logged once per tick) and never disables schedules; any other failure takes the existing path (claim released, one counted `start_failed`, backoff, same slot retried). The 20-per-tick limit stays. (c) EXECUTE ONE RUN: the body is closed and strict (422 otherwise, nothing echoed; 415 for another content type): `schedule_id` fullmatches `^sched_[0-9A-Za-z_]{1,64}$`, `slot` is exactly `slot_key(schedule_id, due)` and `due` is the UTC-aware instant spelled the way `slot_key` spells it. The route re-reads the schedule record, refuses (2xx `{"ran": 0, "skipped": code}`, no retry) a missing record, a `due` more than 60 s ahead of the clock and a `due` that is not an instant of the record's cadence, then calls `ScheduleStarter.start` (owner, snapshot and cap from the record only; the budget only from the owner's own L3 `grant_budget` policy; ids deterministic from the slot, so a duplicate or concurrent delivery finds one world and one run) and WAITS in the request until the run is done or parked at a person, or `IN_REQUEST_DEADLINE_S` (25 minutes, under the 1800 s dispatch deadline), then answers 200 `{"ran": 1, "state": "done" | "parked" | "timeout"}`. The run thread keeps its normal behaviour; T3 still needs a person. The starter's permanent refusals (`schedule_missing`, `schedule_disabled`, `owner_mismatch`, `budget_not_granted`, `snapshot_missing`) are 2xx so Cloud Tasks does not retry; a start that raises, and `run_seams_unwired` (counted against nothing), are a fixed 5xx so it does. (d) R1: the task body carries two server-written fields beyond the slot, `skipped` (integer 0 to 100000, default 0) and `prior_world_id` (null or a world id): fan-out writes `RunStart.skipped` and the schedule's `last_world_id` as it was BEFORE `complete`; `TaskEnqueuer` returns the slot's deterministic world id (`slot_ids`, the starter's own derivation) so `complete` records the world this slot makes. Both are untrusted data, bounds-checked and never authority: the delivery offers `prior_world_id` to the new world only if that world exists and belongs to the record's owner and is not the new world itself. (e) R2: the collapsed-missed-slots info `alert.raised` is raised in Tasks mode, after `world.requested`, only when the body's `skipped` is above 0, once per world (a duplicate delivery finds it), exactly as the Ticker built it (actor `system`, info, `card_id` null, visible to all); the fan-out raises none. (f) R3: both stores gain `record_refusal(schedule_id, code)` through the shared transition code: it sets only `last_error` to a fixed code (checked like the other fixed codes), changes no failure count, backoff, slot or claim, and is a no-op for an unknown id; Tasks mode uses it for `budget_not_granted`, `snapshot_missing` and `owner_mismatch`, not for a missing or disabled schedule; the next `complete` clears it. A refused slot has been completed at fan-out, so `last_slot` and `last_world_id` name a slot and a world that never ran (an earlier 14b assertion that they stayed null no longer holds); the next firing then offers no prior world because the delivery drops one the owner does not have. (g) R4: replay-only blocks both modes: fan-out answers 200 `{"fired": 0}` and moves nothing; a delivery answers 503 and is retried once the flag clears. (h) R5: no extra window rule for older slots: deterministic world ids make a delivery for an already run slot find its world; only a forged `tilo-tasks-invoker` token could replay, and both `tilo-api` and `tilo-harness` hold `serviceAccountUser` on it and `cloudtasks.enqueuer` (`infra/envs/dev/main.tf`), so either can create a task with a body of its choosing; the trust set of the tick is {Scheduler SA, api SA, harness SA}, and a forged slot can only run an on-cadence slot of an existing schedule with that record's owner, brief, cap and the owner's L3 policy (open follow-up: refuse a slot older than `last_slot`). (i) R6: `Run.wait_until_parked_or_done(timeout_s, clock, poll_s)` says `done` when the run's thread ended, `parked` only when the whole run is quiet at a person (a unit is waiting on a gate card or a question, every live unit is that waiting, and no unit is about to start), `timeout` otherwise at the deadline; a paused world is winding down, not parked. It is conservative in one case: a unit that could start but waits for a free specialist slot behind a gate counts as about to start, so the answer is `timeout`, not `parked`. (j) R7: a good Scheduler token builds the store once and no seams; a good Tasks token with a good body builds the store and the seams once each; the 14d assertion `(1, 1)` is changed deliberately. (k) No IAM or Terraform change is needed: `tilo-api` already holds `cloudtasks.enqueuer` and `serviceAccountUser` on `tilo-tasks-invoker`, the queue `runs` exists, api is public so a task's delivery needs no `run.invoker`. One setting is not in Terraform and is the user's: Cloud Run's request timeout on `tilo-api` is the 300 s default and must be at least the 1500 s in-request wait (set 1800 s, `infra/README.md`); until then a delivery of a run that is still working after 300 s is cut, Cloud Tasks retries it and the retry meets the same world. Open follow-ups: an owner-visible refusal status beyond `last_error`; the unused harness tick route of #446 stays unused; the queue keeps Cloud Tasks' default retry policy; a delivery holds one worker thread while it waits (no more than 40 per instance); a Terraform ticket for the request timeout. | FR-AGT-14e; FR-AGT-14; PRD 6.3, 8.6; EFF rules; TILO_RUNS_QUEUE; TILO_TASKS_CALLER; ADR-0023; BQ-AGT14-3, BQ-AGT14-4 | 2026-10-06 |
| BQ-XWORLD-9 | API-CARDS-XWORLD-2 (PR #561 security flag 1; supervisor decision 7 Oct): what does an owner session that is not a full web sign-in read of another owned world's cards? `reading_principal` opened full cards, gate text and recipients included, to an owner session on any channel. | Decided by the supervisor: only a redacted summary. An owner session without the `full_session` marker (BQ-XWORLD-10; an email session, a signed-link session) reads a world other than its bound one, on `GET /worlds/{world_id}/cards` and in `GET /cards` alike, as the same cards in the same order, each reduced to exactly `{id, world_id, kind, created_at, timeout, risk_tier, redacted: true}` (`risk_tier` is the gate's tier, else null). Default chosen for the shape: an allowlist with no free text, so no gate action text, destination or recipients, no consent scopes, no options, and no `what_happened` or `why_it_matters`, which can name a recipient. The feed's item `card` stays what the per-world route shows the same principal (BQ-S06-2); count, order and paging are unchanged. Bound-world reads are unchanged; a full-session owner gets full cards across worlds as before; another person's world is still the unknown-world 404. This narrows BQ-XWORLD-6. | S-06, PRD 10.1, 10.3, SEC-07, SEC-08, BQ-XWORLD-6, BQ-S06-2, `services/api/tests/API_CARDS_XWORLD.md` | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-XWORLD-10 | API-CARDS-XWORLD-2 (PR #561 security flag 2; supervisor decision 7 Oct): how does the server tell a full web sign-in from a one-world session, so a signed-link landing session minted as "web" cannot answer across worlds? | Decided by the supervisor: an explicit marker on the server-side `deps.Principal`, `full_session: bool = False`, set only by the server for a full web sign-in session (the dev-mode principal is one, `full_session=True`); nothing in a request sets it. `deps.Principal` is not a contract, so no contract change. Cross-world answers need the marker on top of every BQ-XWORLD-1/2 rule (role owner, channel `web` or `mobile_web`, not a tilo-channels caller, both owner checks). Without it, a `web` or `mobile_web` principal and every email session stay bound: any other world is the unknown-world 404 for every kind and option, T3 gates included, and the Pause-all guard does not run for them (a paused other world is the 404, not the 409). Bound-world answers are unchanged. Test fixtures that build a full web session set the marker. | BQ-PL02-1 | PL-02 / PL-LIVE-E2E: what `max_output_tokens` does a thinking role get, and how is a reply cut off at its cap treated? | A reply that stops at its output cap (Gemini `MAX_TOKENS`, OpenAI-compatible `length`) raises `OutputTruncatedError` (never a reply, never retried or schema-repaired, a fallback profile is still tried); `vertex_smoke.classify` names it `truncated`. Roles with thinking `high` or `medium` (director, verifier, lead) get `max_output_tokens: 16384` in `config/models.yaml`, `models.gemini.yaml` and `models.nim.yaml`: thinking tokens count against the cap, the repo states no model output limit, 4096 was used up by thinking alone (first live B-007 run), and 16384 is 4x that; the tests require 8192 to the profile context, and 32768 was tried first but a director quote of ~65 credits broke `test_PL_LIVE_06_a_403_echoing_a_token_never_shows_in_the_router_error` (50-credit budget), so a larger cap needs that budget raised. | BQ-AGT14-6 | FR-AGT-14f (issue #502, 7 Oct delta-review follow-up and item 8): a service-wide Cloud Tasks refusal (401/403/404, `tasks_unavailable`: a revoked actAs or enqueuer role, a deleted queue) was one WARNING per tick and a 200 `{"fired": 0}`, so every scheduled run stopped silently while each tick still tried every due slot. What does the tick do where the PRD is silent? (Follows BQ-AGT14-5; the merger may renumber.) | (a) Any `tasks_unavailable` in a Scheduler tick makes the tick answer 503, also when slots were handed over before it (the issue's default: the queue is broken, and NFR-23's error-rate alert and Cloud Scheduler's failed-attempt record should see every such tick); the slots handed over stay completed and a retried tick does not hand them over again. (b) The 503 body is fixed, `{"detail": "tasks_unavailable"}`, the same for 401, 403 and 404, with no exception or provider text. (c) The first refusal stops the tick: no further enqueue call that tick; the refused slot and every later one stay due (no failure, no backoff, `next_due` unmoved, claim released), so they fire on the first tick after Tasks works again. (d) It is logged once per tick at ERROR on a `tilo_api.*` logger with the code, the HTTP status when known and counts only (NFR-26): no token, task name, queue, account email, schedule or owner id, exception text or traceback. No log-based alert or Terraform change is added here (NFR-23 error-rate alerting covers the 503; a dedicated alert is the user's infra call). (e) Unchanged: per-schedule failures are counted and do not stop the tick (200), ALREADY_EXISTS is a success, replay-only and a tick with nothing due answer 200 `{"fired": 0}`, Tasks-mode deliveries are as in BQ-AGT14-5. (f) One 14e test (`test_FR_AGT_14e_a_service_wide_tasks_failure_counts_against_no_schedule`) pins the old 200 `{"fired": 0}` body for a refused tick and conflicts with (a)/(b); the test-writer left it unedited, and the builder PR changes that one line deliberately and lists it. Open from #502: transient service-wide failures (429, 5xx, transport) still count per schedule; a per-tick wall-clock deadline. | FR-AGT-14f; FR-AGT-14; PRD 6.3, 8.6, NFR-23, NFR-26; issue #502; BQ-AGT14-4, BQ-AGT14-5 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-AGT14-7 | FR-AGT-14g (issue #502, security reviewer's follow-up on PR #579): a revoked or broken metadata credential surfaces as `google.auth.exceptions.RefreshError` (not a 401) and a network failure as `google.auth.exceptions.TransportError` or `requests` `Timeout` / `ConnectionError`; each was counted against every due schedule, so a credential or network outage disabled every schedule after `MAX_FAILURES` = 8 ticks, each a 200. Which enqueue failures are service-wide, and with what code? (Follows BQ-AGT14-6; the merger may renumber.) | (a) Those four, and their subclasses, raised during an enqueue (by the session or by the credential refresh it makes first, whichever `TasksClient` raised them), are service-wide and behave exactly as `tasks_unavailable` in BQ-AGT14-6 (a)-(d): the first one stops the tick, it and every later slot stay due (no failure, no backoff, `next_due` unmoved, claim released), the tick answers 503, one ERROR line per tick on a `tilo_api.*` logger. Two new codes, same 503 shape, so the person fixing it knows which half is broken: `RefreshError` -> `{"detail": "tasks_credential"}` (fix the service account or its metadata credential); `TransportError`, `Timeout`, `ConnectionError` -> `{"detail": "tasks_transport"}` (the network to Google). The raised type decides (a `ConnectTimeout` is transport; a `RefreshError` wrapping a transport failure is credential). The body never carries exception text; the log line names the code and at most the exception's type name. Stopping costs at most a one-minute delay (the slot stays due), while counting cost up to 20 x 10 s calls a tick and disabled schedules. (b) 429 RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED, 5xx (500, 502, 503, 504), 400, 409 ABORTED and any other exception stay counted per schedule as today (200, the tick goes on), now pinned by a test: Cloud Tasks answered, so the credential and the network work; such an answer can be one call's, and the per-schedule backoff (2 min doubling, at most 24 h) already spaces the retries, while a stop would hold back every later slot for one transient answer. (c) After recovery each due slot is handed over once: deterministic task names, and ALREADY_EXISTS (also for a task whose create reply was lost to a read timeout) is a success. (d) No 14e or 14f test conflicts. Open: NFR-23 asks for alerts on quota errors and token-refresh failures; the 503 feeds the error-rate alert, but a sustained 429/5xx stream (about 4 h of backoff to 8 failures) still disables schedules quietly, and a dedicated log-based alert is the user's infra call; a Tasks client that cannot be built (`google.auth.default()` raising) stays 14e's fixed 5xx. | FR-AGT-14g; FR-AGT-14; PRD 6.3, 8.6, NFR-23, NFR-26; issue #502; BQ-AGT14-5, BQ-AGT14-6; `services/api/tests/API_FR_AGT_14g.md` | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-AGT14-8 | FR-AGT-14h (issue #502, BQ-AGT14-7 Open; NFR-23 alerts on quota errors, NFR-26 structured logs): the per-schedule WARNING for a failed enqueue names only the type `TasksError`, so a log-based quota alert cannot see a 429, and a quota or server outage that every due slot meets is counted against every schedule and disables them all after `MAX_FAILURES`. What is logged, and when is a 429/5xx the service's? (Follows BQ-AGT14-7; the merger may renumber.) | (a) A per-schedule enqueue failure that carries an HTTP status (400, 409 ABORTED, 429, 5xx) is logged once at WARNING on `tilo_api.schedules` or `tilo_harness.schedule` with `extra` fields `event="schedule_enqueue_failed"`, `status` (int) and `code` (the code recorded against the schedule, its `last_error`), ids only: no task name, queue, URL, email, token, exception text or traceback. The names are constants in one place of the test (`test_FR_AGT_14h_quota_signal.py`) so the cloud lane's alert and the code can rename them together. (b) Service-wide when the FIRST TWO enqueue calls of a tick both fail with the same status and it is 429 or a 5xx (exact status: 502 then 503 is mixed); the minimum is two calls. Then no further call, 503 `{"detail": "tasks_quota"}` (429) or `{"detail": "tasks_server"}` (5xx), one ERROR line per stopped tick on a `tilo_api.*` logger naming the code and carrying the same `status` and `code` fields, so the quota alert sees a stop too. (c) Which slots are counted, changed from the proposed default "the first is counted as today": NEITHER of the two paired slots is counted; both and every later slot stay due (no failure, no backoff, `next_due` unmoved, claim released). Why: counting the first leaves a sustained outage almost as harmful as today, because the counted slot goes into backoff and the next tick's oldest due slot is a different schedule, so every schedule is still counted at about its backoff rate and all are disabled after about 4 h; with neither counted, schedules due together never gain a failure and all fire on the first tick after recovery. A lone due slot (one call cannot tell one schedule's answer from the service's), a success or ALREADY_EXISTS before the failures (only the first two calls can pair), different statuses, 400 and 409 ABORTED (a request's own answers, never service-wide) and unclassified exceptions keep today's per-schedule counting, so a one-off 429 still counts against that schedule only. Cost of a false pairing (two one-off blips in a row): one minute's delay for that tick's slots and one 503. (d) Conflicts, reported and not edited: `test_FR_AGT_14f_a_per_schedule_tasks_error_is_counted_and_does_not_stop_the_tick` (status 429, 500, 503) and `test_FR_AGT_14g_quota_and_server_errors_still_count_per_schedule_and_the_tick_goes_on` (all statuses) pin three slots all answering the same 429/5xx as counted with 200; (b) makes those ticks 503 with nothing counted, so the builder PR changes them deliberately and lists them (a minimum of four calls would avoid the conflict but leave two or three schedules due together unprotected, so it was not chosen). (e) The FR-AGT-14 notes move verbatim from `services/api/CLAUDE.md` to `docs/schedules.md` (written in the tests commit; the builder adds the 14h line and the link). Open: no log-based alert or Terraform here (the cloud lane's NFR-23 alert filters on the fields in (a)); a lone schedule in a long quota outage is still counted and can be disabled. | FR-AGT-14h; FR-AGT-14; PRD 6.3, 8.6, NFR-23, NFR-26; issue #502; BQ-AGT14-6, BQ-AGT14-7; `services/api/tests/API_FR_AGT_14h.md` | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-PPL11C-15 | PPL-11c-FS, issue #541 item 1: who may found an empty org when the stores are production Firestore, and what if no one is configured? | Default (the PRD names none): `POST /v1/people/first` on an empty org is refused 403 `forbidden`, the same body as every other refusal of `/first`, unless the principal's uid equals the configured `TILO_PEOPLE_BOOTSTRAP_UID` (a `common#user_id` as written: no trimming, list or wildcard; anything else counts as unset). Unset means refuse for everyone: the production default is closed, a declared visitor or judge cannot claim an empty org, and a typo closes the door rather than opening it. The check runs before any store read and before the schema check. The tilo-dev rollout sets the variable to the user's uid (the user's step, with the Firestore switch). | PE-17, BQ-PPL10-3, BQ-PPL11C-5; issue #541; API_PPL_11c_FS.md sections 3 and 5 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-PPL11C-16 | PPL-11c-FS, issue #541 item 2: when may the last person of an org be erased? | Default: only when the org is founded, i.e. has a founder marker (`orgs/{org}/people_directory/founder`, written in the same transaction as the founder and never deleted). `erase_person` of an org's only person with no marker raises `LastPerson`; the check is atomic with the final person delete (FS-17), so an erase that loses a race raises `LastPerson` after it may already have erased the subject's own meetings, work items, pins, mentions and calendar entries (the other records may already be erased; the erase is idempotent), and the future erase route must report a partial erase honestly. A founded org may be emptied person by person and `/first` stays 403 forever, so an erased org cannot be re-founded to join the old team's pins; the cost is an org with no owner, recovered by an operator (deferred). Reading of the brief: "refused unless a founder marker says otherwise" taken as "unless the marker exists". Two concurrent erases of the last two people can empty a founded org (still closed). | PE-16, PE-17; issue #541; API_PPL_11c_FS.md section 3 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-PPL11C-18 | PPL-11c-FS-1c, issue #577 items 2 and 5: what does the people store do when it finds a person's address reservation held by another person (on `delete` or an address move), and what does `get_people_stores` answer inside the backoff window after a failed build? | Default (the PRD names neither): the holder is checked before a reservation is deleted; a reservation held by someone else is never deleted and the whole transaction fails loudly with a `PeopleError` that is none of the specific refusals (no address, no text), nothing written; an absent reservation is not an error. A commit-time `AlreadyExists` on a reservation is `EmailTaken`, on the founder marker `OrgAlreadyFounded`. A founded org may be emptied person by person and `/first` stays 403 forever, so an erased org cannot be re-founded to join the old team's pins; the cost is an org with no owner, recovered by an operator (deferred).
