| BQ-NFR22-1 | NFR-22 (graceful degradation): what does a model or quota failure answer, and when? | (a) Only when the router ran out of candidates and every attempt failed on quota or availability (`QuotaError`, `RetryableError`): intake and the approaches route answer the NFR-24 shape, 503 `{"detail": ..., "replay_only": true, "code": "model_unavailable"}`, a detail that offers finished runs as replays and says nothing new was started. A rejected request, a reply the model sent but got wrong, a budget or token-cap stop stay the existing 502. (b) The offer is for that request only: it never sets the process-wide `replay_only` flag, which stays the owner's and the spend cap's (NFR-24, BQ-238). (c) Not in this change: the web banner that reads `replay_only` (AG-WEB) and inbound triage retry, which would revise BQ-127; the run path makes no model call yet. | PRD 17.2 NFR-22, NFR-24; PL-02, PL-03 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-PPL11C-26 | FR-PA-10: what does "move it to Friday" do to a work item? | A snooze until that day. FR-PA-16 and `ui_action` offer only `mark_done` and `snooze` for work items, so the only typed action that carries a day is `snooze`; a due-date edit by phrase is deferred. | FR-PA-10, FR-PA-16, PE-10 | 2026-10-07 |

No new response key. | FR-PA-10, PE-10, FR-PA-16 | 2026-10-07 |

| BQ-NFR26-1 | NFR-26 (structured logs, `tilo_logging`): what is decided where the tests' docstrings and the PRD are silent? | (a) Keys beyond the pinned ones: `logger` (the logger name) and `exception` (the formatted traceback, only when a record has `exc_info`, one JSON string, never extra lines); nothing from `extra`, headers, cookies or tokens is copied. (b) Trace value: `projects/<GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT>/traces/<id>` when that variable is set, else the bare 32-hex trace id; a header whose trace id is not 32 hex characters adds no trace key. (c) `world_id` is bound from the path only if it matches the contract's `w_[0-9A-Za-z_]{1,48}`, so caller input never becomes an arbitrary log value; `run_id` is the stored world's, absent for an unknown world or when no store is wired. (d) The api binds `world_id` and the trace in the outermost middleware (no I/O, so the access gate's own lines carry them) and `run_id` in a second middleware inside the gate, off the event loop, so no world is looked up for a request the gate refuses (API_SEC_15.md); the trace id comes from a header the caller can set, so it is a correlation hint and never an audit key; the harness binds in `run_plan` from the writer's `run_id` and `world_id`, and the binding is gone when the run returns or raises. (e) `configure_logging` sets the root level (default INFO) and pins `httpx` and `httpcore` to WARNING (they log outbound URLs at INFO), adds one handler of its own and removes only a previous one of its own (idempotent; pytest's handlers stay); with no stream it writes to whatever `sys.stdout` is at emit time. (f) uvicorn's own `uvicorn` and `uvicorn.access` loggers keep their plain-text format (they do not propagate to the root); aligning them is a follow-up. | NFR-26; PRD 6, 16, 17.2 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-NFR22-2 | NFR-22 web (tests on `tests/NFR-22-web`): how does `src/api/http.ts` label a model failure, and where does the label live? The PRD fixes only the API shape (NFR-24: 503 `replay_only`). | `TiloApiError` gets a boolean `replayOnly`, true only for a 503 whose JSON has `replay_only === true` (the string "true", 1 or a missing flag do not count; no other status counts), false on every other error. `code` stays what the server sent (`model_unavailable` for NFR-22; null for NFR-24's refusal, which sends none). Its message is one fixed web sentence on both routes and both causes, saying live models are unavailable, nothing new was started and finished runs can be replayed; the server's `detail` is never echoed (it is free text). `retryable` is false (the server started nothing, so retrying at once cannot help; the replay is the next step); `write` stays as the call's own. Every other 5xx keeps "Tilo had a problem on its side." and `retryable` true; 4xx and network copy are unchanged. | PRD 17.2 NFR-22, NFR-24; BQ-NFR22-1; SEC-15 (`code`) | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-NFR22-3 | NFR-22 web: where is the banner mounted and how does a flow screen know to show it, given the Park cutover is moving routes? | A shared component `apps/web/src/components/ReplayBanner.tsx` (named export `ReplayBanner`, optional `replaysHref`, default `/worlds`, S-14 "Worlds list and replays", which is how finished runs are replayed today; `data-testid="replay-banner"`, role status or alert with the label in its accessible name, one real link, fixed copy, no motion, no focus steal). So S-02 and S-03 (loads and actions) and any screen using `Primary` (S-01 Start if kept) show it with no per-screen code. Tests pin the seam (`useRemote`, `useAction`, `Primary`, a real `HttpClient` intake failure) and S-03 whole at `/task/t1/approaches`; none depends on what `/` serves. | PRD 17.2 NFR-22, 18.4, 9.4 (HR-1..HR-7), UX-1; AG-RENDER cutover | 2026-10-07 |

When Park Home's composer calls the live intake (or approaches) and `source` is live, it must catch a `TiloApiError` with `replayOnly` and mount `<ReplayBanner replaysHref=...>` (pointing at Park's own worlds or replays page if it has one). That wiring belongs to the ticket that makes the composer live; it then adds its own acceptance test next to this one. | NFR-22; apps/web/src/park/pages/Home | 2026-10-07 |

The banner names no run and shows no counts, so nothing is made up when the list is empty (S-14's own empty state speaks then). | NFR-22, NFR-24, HR-4, S-14 | 2026-10-07 |

| BQ-PPL11C-20 | FR-PA-08 (tests/FR-PA-08, `services/api/tests/API_FR_PA_08.md`): where does `STATUS` live, and how does it differ from the FR-PA-13 intents `tasks` ("what is the status of my tasks", pinned by PPL-11b A13-1) and `changed` (last 24 hours)? | Default (supplement 3.1: a panel question answered from the same read models): two new fact intents, `status` (running tasks and items changed since yesterday) and `weekly` (counts), in `config/assistant.yaml` and `POST /v1/assistant/ask`; no new route, event or contract. `status` answers the bare command and "status update" and "give me my status"; "what is the status of my tasks" and "how are my tasks going" stay `tasks`, "what changed since yesterday" and "what's new" stay `changed`, so the 20 FR-PA-13 questions are unchanged (the old intents are the narrower asks). A model may pick either intent by id like any other. | PRD addendum 3.2 FR-PA-08, FR-PA-13; supplement 3.1; PPL-11b A13-1 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-PPL11C-21 | FR-PA-08: what is "changed since yesterday", and in whose time? The PRD names no window, and the plate (ADR-0021) has no per-change time. | Default: yesterday is the start of the previous day in the person's own `time_zone` (UTC if unset or unknown), an inclusive window from that instant to the request's clock (24 to 48 hours, moving at local midnight); a row counts when its plate `at` is in it; `parked` rows of group `done` always count (PE-27: finished within 24 hours); snoozed and asked rows are not changes. `at` is the creation, start, receipt or last pin change (PE-29), so an edit to an old work item or a card is not seen: a true `changed_at` needs a plate contract change (ADR first), left out. World rows are listed once, as tasks. | PRD addendum FR-PA-08; PE-27, PE-28, PE-29; ADR-0021 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-PPL11C-22 | FR-PA-08: what is a "running task", and how many lines? | Default: a plate `rows` row of kind `world` with reason `run` or `blocked` (the plate holds no finished or failed runs and no completion time, so those are deferred). One line each in plate order, then one per change; at most 10 claim lines per section (`answer.max_lines`), then the existing "More on My plate." line with a `navigate` to `plate`; one line per ref (the `rows` copy beats the `parked` copy); meetings are only seen as `meeting_card` rows. Nothing at all gives `declined`/`empty` (HR-1). | PRD addendum FR-PA-08, FR-PA-13; PE-27 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-PPL11C-23 | FR-PA-08: what is in "weekly summary from a template", and what source does a count cite? | Default: five fixed-word count lines (done in the last 7 days, open work items, overdue, tasks in progress, waiting for you), no titles; the week is seven local days including today; "done" is read from the person's own work items' history (latest `done` stamp, current status `done`, oracle-gated), the rest from the same gated plate rows. A `Line` has one source, so each count cites the first row of its category in plate order (Done: the latest finished item); zero counts cite nothing; a truncated plate (200 rows) adds `+` to its counts. No scheduled push: the sweeper and digest (FR-PA-05/06) are separate tickets. | PRD addendum FR-PA-08, FR-PA-06; PE-26, PE-27 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-PPL11C-24 | FR-PA-08: a failing source: 503 (as `GET /v1/plate`) or a failed answer? | Default: the panel's own contract (API_PPL_11b.md section 1 steps 5 and 7): a plate source or item store that raises is a 200 answer with outcome `failed` and `declines.failed`, no sources and no partial lines; an unwired seam is 503 `not_configured`. Never a partial STATUS or weekly (HR-1). | PRD addendum FR-PA-08; API_PPL_11b.md | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-PPL11C-25 | FR-PA-08 weekly summary (`assistant/status.py`, `weekly_lines`): the plate's `truncated` flag is true when either lane is full (200 `rows` or 100 `parked`, plate schema), but the `+` ("at least") is added to the `rows`-based counts (open, overdue, tasks in progress, waiting) whenever it is set. So a person with a full `parked` lane (100 snoozed, asked or done items) and fewer than 200 rows sees `5+` where the count is exact. Known question for the plate schema owner: should the plate say which lane was cut? | Default taken (spec section 4: when `plate["truncated"]` is true every plate count, not Done, gets a `+`): the `+` follows `plate["truncated"]` as written. It can overstate the uncertainty, never understate it, so it is the safe side. Not changed in this ticket. A per-lane flag (for example `rows_truncated`) needs a plate contract change, ADR first, if exact counts are wanted; until then the `+` reads as "at least". | PRD addendum FR-PA-08; plate.schema.json; PE-27; BQ-PPL11C-23 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-PPL11C-34 | FR-PA-10b: `ui_action.schema.json` has only `navigate`, `mark_done`, `snooze`, `draft_reply`, `tag` and `untag`, so reopen, not mine and put back cannot be proposed as actions. | FR-PA-10b needs a `ui_action` minor version with the kinds `reopen`, `not_mine` and `put_back`; it needs the architect and the user's approval, and is not queued as an ADR. Until then the rules recognise those words, never ask the model, write nothing and answer a `navigate` to My plate (12 tests pin this). The routes `POST /v1/work-items/{id}/reopen`, `/not-mine` and `/put-back` already exist. | FR-PA-10, FR-PA-10b, PE-10, PE-29, BQ-PPL11C-30 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-PPL11C-35 | FR-PA-10 builder defaults the spec leaves open: where do the grammar and the confirm line live, and is an `update` model decision cached? | The grammar is code (`assistant/updates.py`, a parser like `resolve_due`, no scenario text); the confirm lines are `answer.update.done` and `answer.update.snooze` in `config/assistant.yaml` and the four new `declines` keys sit beside the others. An `update` reply is cached like any paid decision (FR-PA-19): the cache holds the kind, the item and the day phrase, never a date, so a hit is vetted again and its day resolved again against the new request's clock. The `working_window.start` of `config/personal.yaml` is read on each snooze (not cached) and a broken file fails the answer (`failed`), never a default. | FR-PA-10, FR-PA-19, PE-15 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-PPL11C-36 | FR-PA-10 security round 1: how many prepositions may a snooze ask hold? | Three. A real ask has one or two ("move it to Friday", "snooze the budget review until Monday"); a key with more is not read by the rules and takes the not-understood path (the role may still read it), so a hostile 500-character ask cannot fan out into hundreds of title splits. Each row title is normalised once per request, not once per split (NFKC amplification, same class as #278). | FR-PA-10 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-SIGNIN-1 | SEC-15 / SIGNIN, OAuth consent screen: Testing or In production? The same Google Cloud project's consent screen carries the Gmail connector's RESTRICTED scopes (`gmail.readonly`, `gmail.compose`, `gmail.send`) AND the app sign-in scopes (`openid`, `email`, `profile`). | DECIDED by the user (via the supervisor, 7 Oct): **In production**. App sign-in (`openid`, `email`, `profile`, non-sensitive) then works for anyone, judges included, with no test-user list and no 7-day expiry. Gmail-scope consent (restricted `gmail.*`) shows Google's unverified-app warning and is capped at 100 new users until Google verifies the restricted scopes: the Gmail consent sheet (S-08) says so in plain words before the redirect (exact sentence in `services/api/tests/API_SIGNIN.md`), and "start Google verification for the restricted scopes" is a later owner step (infra/ACCESS.md section 11). Testing (listed test users only, grants expire after about 7 days, PRD R-06) stays the one-line fallback. Nothing in the code reads the publishing status, so either works. | PRD R-06, 6, 16.1; SEC-15; FR-CON-03; infra/ACCESS.md sections 6 and 11 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-SIGNIN-2 | SEC-15 / SIGNIN, `tilo_api.firebase_auth`: which Firebase ID tokens are accepted, beyond RS256, signature, `aud`, `iss`, `exp`? | RS256 against the securetoken JWKS only (`alg` none, HS256 and the rest refused before any key is fetched); `aud` exactly the project id as a string (a list is refused); `iss` exactly `https://securetoken.google.com/<project>`; `sub` a non-empty string of at most 128 characters; `iat` and `auth_time` required numbers not in the future beyond a few seconds; `firebase.sign_in_provider` must be `google.com` or `anonymous` (Tilo never handles passwords, so `password`, `custom`, `phone` and the rest are refused); a Google sign-in needs `email_verified` true (the PRD does not require it; every real Google token has it, so this costs nothing and fails closed); keys cached 900 s with single-flight, a cooldown after a failed fetch and a bounded refetch for an unknown `kid`, as `tilo_api.appcheck`; a token over `MAX_TOKEN_CHARS` (4096, flat) is refused unparsed. Any other failure is a 401 with a fixed body, never the token or the error text. | PRD 6, 16.1; SEC-15; `services/api/tests/test_SEC_15_SIGNIN_firebase_verifier.py` | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-SIGNIN-3 | SEC-15 / SIGNIN: how does the API choose `Principal.channel` (`web` or `mobile_web`), the user id, the role and `full_session` for a verified token, and how does a request say it is on a phone? | `user_id` = a stable, collision-free mapping of the Firebase uid onto the contract pattern `^u_[0-9A-Za-z_]{1,48}$` (`firebase_auth.user_id_for`; never the dev owner); `role` `owner` (each person owns their own worlds, as the dev principal does); `full_session` True ONLY for a verified Google sign-in, False for an anonymous demo session (BQ-XWORLD-10: a demo session stays bound to its own world), set by the server, never read from a request; `channel`: the header `X-Tilo-Channel: mobile_web` selects `mobile_web`, absent or any other value (including `email`) is `web` (both are web sign-in channels with identical authority; an email principal only comes from a signed link). The web client sends that header on a phone-width window (the same `max-width: 767px` test it already uses for card answers). The principal never carries the email or name. `world_id` binding for the production principal is left to the cloud lane's PROD-WIRE seam (issue #522). | PRD 10.1, 10.3; BQ-XWORLD-1/2/9/10; SEC-15 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-SIGNIN-4 | SEC-15 / SIGNIN, web: where does the Firebase Auth config come from and what does the client send? | From the build, as App Check does: `VITE_FIREBASE_API_KEY` and `VITE_FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID` plus the public app id in `appcheck/config.ts`; `authDomain` is `<projectId>.firebaseapp.com` unless the build says otherwise (the CSP `frame-src` is pinned to that host). A build without the config, or a local host (localhost, 127.0.0.1, ::1, `*.localhost`), makes no SDK or network call (EFF-01) and the controller state is `unavailable`, so dev, tests and the fixture deploy are unchanged. The Firebase SDK is a dynamic import started by the first subscribe or token request (NFR-01). Sign-in is a Google popup, with a redirect only when the popup is blocked or unsupported; the person closing the popup is a cancel, not an error. Google sign-in asks for the basic profile scopes only. The ID token goes in `Authorization: Bearer` beside `X-Firebase-AppCheck`, only when it is a header-safe string of base64url characters, never in a URL, a body or a log, and Tilo's own code writes nothing to web storage. The Firebase SDK keeps the refresh token itself: for a Google sign-in in IndexedDB (falling back to localStorage or sessionStorage), so that session survives a browser restart and a person on a shared machine stays signed in until they choose Sign out; an anonymous demo session is set to session persistence (it ends with the tab). After a successful sign-out the page does a full reload, so no state of the previous person (the module-level HTTP client) survives. A failed token read sends no header and the API answers 401. The sign-in gate shows the app only when signed in or when Firebase is unavailable. | PRD 6, 16.1; SEC-15, SEC-10; EFF-01; NFR-01; FR-UX-10 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-SIGNIN-5 | SEC-15 / SIGNIN, Hosting CSP: what is the minimum for Google sign-in? | From `@firebase/auth` 12.19.0 `dist/esm`: `script-src` + `https://apis.google.com` (the gapi loader); `frame-src` + `https://essential-wares-510320-f0.firebaseapp.com/__/auth/` (the sign-in iframe and handler on the project's auth domain); `connect-src` + `https://identitytoolkit.googleapis.com` and `https://securetoken.googleapis.com` (API_HOST and TOKEN_API_HOST). `accounts.google.com` is NOT added: the account chooser opens in the popup window or as a top-level navigation, which these directives do not govern (it may appear in `frame-src` only, if the live check shows it is needed). No wildcard, no `unsafe-eval`, no inline script. `test_SEC_10_hosting_csp_stays_tight` needs a documented edit to allow exactly these origins. The web key's API restriction must grow from App Check only to App Check, Identity Toolkit and Token Service, or sign-in is refused by Google (infra/ACCESS.md section 11, docs/deploy-dev.md). A real-browser check on the deployed host is required before the user tells judges to sign in (no CSP violations in the console). | PRD 6.5, 16; SEC-10, SEC-02; BQ-SEC10-5 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-SIGNIN-6 | SEC-15 / SIGNIN, API seam: where does the verified principal plug in? | Until the cloud lane's `resolve_principal(request)` (PROD-WIRE, issue #522) is merged, `deps.get_current_principal` itself takes the request and verifies the Bearer token through `deps.get_firebase_verifier()` (a dependency like `get_app_check_verifier`: Google's keys for `TILO_FIRESTORE_PROJECT` else `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT`; no project or dev mode refuses with no call). `access.AccessGate._decide` must pass the request to it (it calls it with no argument today). Dev mode keeps its own principal override and never reads or verifies a token; production never reads a dev header, and a dev flag in a deployed process (`K_SERVICE`) changes nothing. An anonymous demo session declares visitor or judge like anyone and is gated by the same SEC-15 rules; no public route is added. | SEC-15, SEC-10; services/api/CLAUDE.md; issue #522 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-SIGNIN-7 | SEC-15 / SIGNIN, found while pinning: can the deployed web app call `tilo-api` at all? | Not yet, and not decided here: the Hosting CSP `connect-src` has no API origin and `tilo-api` has CORS only in dev mode (`dev.install`), so a browser on `web.app` cannot reach a `run.app` API with an `Authorization` header (a preflight must allow `Authorization`, `X-Firebase-AppCheck`, `X-Tilo-Channel`, `Content-Type`, `Idempotency-Key` for the web origin). Same gap as BQ-SEC10-2. Left to the PROD-WIRE lane; the sign-in tests do not depend on it. | BQ-SEC10-2; PRD 6.2; issue #522 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-SIGNIN-8 | SEC-15 / SIGNIN, build choices the tests leave open: (a) which Firebase app does Auth use, (b) when is `X-Tilo-Channel` sent, (c) is the SEC-15 sign-up question mounted? | (a) A second, named Firebase app `tilo-auth` (the web config plus `authDomain`), never the default app: App Check (`appcheck/appCheck.ts`) owns the default app with a different config, and the SDK throws on a second `initializeApp` of the same name with other options, which would switch App Check off. (b) Only on a request that also carries `Authorization` (the channel is meaningful only with a verified token), on a phone-width window, so a signed-out or local call adds no custom header (fewer CORS preflight headers, BQ-SIGNIN-7). (c) No: `AccessGate`/sign-up stay unmounted; the PROD-WIRE lane mounts them after `SignInGate` (a 403 `declaration_required` from the API needs the world loaded first). `exp` is checked with no skew (a token is valid until its `exp`); `iat`, `auth_time` and `nbf` allow 10 s. | SEC-15; SEC-10; BQ-SIGNIN-4, BQ-SIGNIN-7 | 2026-10-07 |

The memory figure is a measured credits-saved number in PRD 14.3; it is labelled an estimate because the design shows it in money (BQ-DSN-02). | BQ-CLLIVE-1 | CL-LIVE-1 (real Google connectors in a local live run, user wish 7 Oct 2026 "agents should be allowed to work with connectors"): where does the user's OAuth token live, and when is consent asked? | Default: in process memory only (the broker's in-memory store under `FakeKms`, as `tilo_connectors.live` does), consent once per run through the loopback callback, never on disk, never in a `.env`, never in a recording, event, log or report. The grant stays at Google until the user removes it at myaccount.google.com/permissions. A run that cannot get a token refuses (fail closed); there is no fallback to the mocks. | BQ-SIGNIN-9 | SEC-15 / SIGNIN, security review follow-ups: what does sign-in NOT protect against, and what must the user decide before `TILO_JUDGE_LIVE=1`? | (a) Anonymous uids are free: anyone can mint a fresh anonymous uid, which starts a new set-once judge declaration and a new per-uid SEC-16 limit. So before `TILO_JUDGE_LIVE=1` the user must decide that a principal without a Google sign-in gets replay models only (done in code, BQ-SIGNIN-10), and consider enforcing App Check on Firebase Authentication (Firebase console, App Check, Authentication) so a bare script cannot mint uids. (b) There is no revocation on sign-out: Sign out ends the session in the browser, but a leaked ID token stays valid at the API for up to one hour (the API checks signature and expiry, not a revocation list). (c) `TILO_PEOPLE_BOOTSTRAP_UID` must hold `user_id_for(firebase uid)`, not the Firebase uid: after the founder signs in once with Google, copy the uid from the Firebase console (Authentication, Users, User UID) and run `uv run python scripts/signin_user_id.py <uid>`; put the printed `u_...` value in the variable. (d) A token carrying `firebase.tenant` is refused (Tilo has no tenants); an integer too large for a float in a time claim is a refusal. (e) The principal is resolved once per gated request (the gate stores it in the request state, the route reuses it), so a cold-start second verification cannot answer 401 with `key_refresh_in_flight`. | PRD 16.1; SEC-15, SEC-16, SEC-10; BQ-SIGNIN-2, BQ-PPL11C-15; infra/ACCESS.md section 11 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-SIGNIN-10 | SEC-15 / SIGNIN, supervisor decision: may an anonymous demo session use live models? | No, never: `Principal.demo_session` is True for any verified sign-in that is not a Google one (today: anonymous), set by the server from the token, and `deps.spend_mode` returns replay for it whatever `TILO_JUDGE_LIVE`, the user switch `LIVE` or the spend guard say (its cap is still read, so new work is refused the same way). `GET /access` reports the same `models` label. Two paths the first cut missed (SIGNIN-FIX): (1) the public schedule tick has no signed-in person, so a schedule records its owner's flag when it is created (`Schedule.demo`, from `Principal.demo_session`, never from a body; a stored record without the field reads as a demo's, so it only replays) and the tick builds that schedule's run seams in replay mode; (2) a process that is not deployed has no access gate, so `get_mode` and `get_work_mode` also take the flag from the principal the request itself resolves, verified once per request (the gate's flag still decides when deployed; no `Authorization` header keeps the user's own switch; a token that is present but cannot be verified is replay). How replay holds under `LIVE=1` (SIGNIN-FIX r2): any mode other than "live" reaches the provider layer as an injected `force_replay` (`deps.router_for_mode` -> `Router` -> `build_provider` -> `RecordReplayProvider`, and `deps.get_safety` -> `build_safety_provider`), which neither `LIVE=1` nor `live_permitted` overrides and which never records; this also covers NFR-24 `replay_only` and the SEC-16 cap for the assistant decider and the tick's seams. A direct, request-less call (no mode) still follows `LIVE`. A Google principal (full_session) behaves as before. A separate field rather than `not full_session`, because the tests' principal doubles leave `full_session` False. Also (B): the Firebase SDK keeps an anonymous demo session in session storage (it ends with the tab) and a Google sign-in in its default (IndexedDB, falling back to local storage); after a successful Sign out the page does a full reload (only when the SDK really ended the session; if its sign-out failed the controller says `signOutFailed`, nothing reloads, and the sign-in panel tells the person in plain words that the session may still be open on this device). Supervisor-approved secure default (7 Oct). | SEC-15, SEC-16; EFF-01; BQ-SIGNIN-4, BQ-SIGNIN-9 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-WEB-UX10 | FR-UX-10: what does Sign out do before the sign-in layer (`useAuth()`, CL-REACH) exists, and what shows while it is signed out? (Answered by SIGNIN-FIX: `ParkApp` now supplies the seam from the page's sign-in, `src/api/sessionAuth.ts` `useSessionAuth()`; a Google person shows by name and email, an anonymous session as "Demo session", Sign out signs out for real and the toast says "Could not sign out. Try again." when the SDK refuses; with no sign-in on the page ("unavailable") the seam is null and everything below holds as before.) | Default: the shell reads an optional `ParkAuth` seam (apps/web/src/park/shell/auth.tsx, same shape as the planned `useAuth()`). No seam: Sign out keeps the prototype toast "This is a prototype, so you stay signed in" (existing WEB_3b test, not edited) and the fixture user shows. Seam signed in: the header and avatar use its name and email, Sign out calls `signOut()` and toasts "You are signed out" only after it resolves ("Could not sign out. Try again." on failure). Seam loading or signed out: no user name, avatar "?", no Sign out row. Mobile app opens a small "Concept" dialog (copy is ours, not in the design). Names are not final; rename when `useAuth()` lands. | apps/web/src/park/shell/AppShell.tsx | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-HIL-REJECT-1 | FR-HIL-04 / cards: may a person reject a gate, or is hold the only way to say no? | A gate card's option set is the closed set approve | edit | hold (`contracts/card.rules.md` CR-07, `card.schema.json` `safe_default` hold, the `approval.resolved` option enum; BQ-17 labels). The PRD names no reject or decline for gates; FR-HIL-04 is met because nothing sends without approve. Hold is a no-op snooze today: it writes no event, the card stays open, and a nudge fires after 24 h (BQ-90), so a held gate never closes or leaves the queue. Default: hold is the reject. Product options for the user (decision in the morning): (a) a "Not now / dismiss" that closes the card as held; (b) a real `reject` option id, which is a contract change needing an ADR (`card.schema.json`, the `approval.resolved` enum, `card.rules` CR-07/01/02, `registry.yaml` text, TS/Py mirrors, apps/web reducers, durable-stop semantics for the outbox entry). Found by the AG-RENDER E2E harness (400 `invalid_option` on reject, decline, deny and cancel). Supervisor-approved. | contracts/card.rules.md; contracts/card.schema.json; FR-HIL-04; BQ-17, BQ-90 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-SIGNIN-10 | SEC-15 / SIGNIN, supervisor decision: may an anonymous demo session use live models? | No, never: `Principal.demo_session` is True for any verified sign-in that is not a Google one (today: anonymous), set by the server from the token, and `deps.spend_mode` returns replay for it whatever `TILO_JUDGE_LIVE`, the user switch `LIVE` or the spend guard say (its cap is still read, so new work is refused the same way). `GET /access` reports the same `models` label. A Google principal (full_session) behaves as before. A separate field rather than `not full_session`, because the tests' principal doubles leave `full_session` False. Also (B): the Firebase SDK keeps an anonymous demo session in session storage (it ends with the tab) and a Google sign-in in its default (IndexedDB, falling back to local storage); after a successful Sign out the page does a full reload. | BQ-UX17-1 | FR-UX-17 (Share to Slack, web side): how does "Share to Slack" on World and Worlds queue a Slack post when there is no server route or Slack connector write door yet? | Default applied: "Share to Slack" opens a focus-trapped dialog (channel from the Slack connector via `api.getConnectors()`, optional note length-capped to 240 characters and rendered as plain text, "Ask for approval") and adds one pending approval card in the page's existing gate/card UI (`WaitingCard` on World, `Spotlight` on Worlds). Nothing is sent over the network, and the card is labelled honestly ("Waiting for approval · sample" in sample mode, "Waiting for approval" in live mode, never "Shared"); when the Slack connector is not connected, the trigger is disabled and explains why. Wiring the server approval route (`POST /v1/worlds/{id}/share/slack` -> gate card + outbox entry) and the Slack MCP write door (`post_message`) is deferred to the Slack connector write slice. | docs/prd-supplement-design-2026-10-06.md 1.6 row 75 (FR-UX-17); PRD 10.3, 12.1; HR-1 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-SET-1 | FR-SET-01 "saved values persist per user": the Park `User` has no id field, so what keys the saved profile? | Default applied: the signed-in email from `getUser()` (not the editable form field), so editing the email field and saving does not orphan the save. Stored in `localStorage` under `tilo.profile.<email>` (try/catch) with an in-memory copy as the fallback. No server call: there is no profile endpoint, so Save is "kept on this device only" and says so. Swap the key for the user id when the live `User` carries one. | FR-SET-01 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-SET-2 | FR-SET-01/02 honest copy vs the pinned Profile copy: `Profile.render.test.tsx` pins every visible string and aria-label of the Profile and Notifications tabs (no extra string allowed) and `Profile saved` is pinned as the only toast. DECIDED 8 Oct (user approved): both lines are now also visible text, one `<p class="park-profile-note">` each (12.5px, weight 600, `--ink3`, the field-help style; no layout shift), the Save line above the Cancel/Save footer and the quiet-hours line directly under the Quiet hours row; `aria-description` and `title` are kept. Pinned in `profileExpect.ts` (`PROFILE_COPY.saveNote`, `NOTIF_COPY.quietNote`, added to `STRINGS.profile` and `STRINGS.notifications`) and in `Profile.BQ_SET_2.test.tsx`. | FR-SET-01, FR-SET-02 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-SET-3 | FR-SET-02: does the channel matrix or quiet hours persist? | Default applied: no. Existing tests pin them as page state that calls nothing, and the Done-when asks only for the rule (a decision keeps at least one channel; quiet hours never hide a gate). The rule lives in `pages/Profile/notifyRules.ts`; persistence waits for a notification-preferences endpoint. | FR-SET-02 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-SET-4 | FR-SET-02 "quiet hours never hide a gate": the visible copy says quiet hours keep the phone silent except decisions over the threshold, so what happens to a gate under the threshold? | Default applied: quiet hours silence the phone only. | BQ-SPD-3 | FR-SPD-08: which rows are "the ledger rows for that month", and what do the CSV columns hold? | Default: the rows of the Activity card on the page (every type, decisions already taken included), in page order, because `Ledger.tx` is not shown anywhere on the page and the live Ledger holds neither `tx` nor `activity` yet. Columns, in this stable order: Date, Time, Type, Entry, Where, Amount, Currency, Details. Date is the day heading as shown ("Thu 17 Sep"), not an ISO date; Amount is a plain decimal with no symbol or thousands separator, blank when the row shows no money (a saving's "+" is dropped; the Type column says it is a saving); Currency is GBP unless the Ledger says USD. No world id or decision key is exported. RFC 4180 with CRLF; text cells starting with = + - @ tab or CR get a leading single quote; the Blob carries a UTF-8 byte-order mark so Excel reads "£" correctly. | FR-SPD-08; apps/web/src/park/pages/Ledger/ledgerCsv.ts | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-SPD-4 | FR-SPD-08: the month picker is a toast and the page's month is the fixed text "September 2026"; which month does Export take? | Default: the page's own month label (today "September 2026"), passed to `ledgerCsvFileName`; the file name is `tilo-ledger-<month>-<year>.csv`, with `-sample` added whenever the Ledger's source is not `live`. The page's Export button still toasts "Exported september-2026.csv · also synced to Xero" and downloads nothing; wiring the real download needs that pinned toast (Ledger.page.test.tsx, WEB_7_ledger_waiting_kpi_opens_k1_and_header_buttons_toast) changed to an honest message, because there is no Xero sync ("accounting sync later") and the file name differs. DECIDED 8 Oct: the user approved the honest toast "Exported tilo-ledger-september-2026-sample.csv · sample data" (live: no "sample"), no Xero claim; the assertion and the page hook are changed. | FR-SPD-08; HR-1..HR-7; apps/web/src/park/pages/Ledger/LedgerPage.tsx | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-UX12-1 | FR-UX-12 / issue #588: "vehicles must not drive through walking agents (vehicles stop or slow)" with no new keyframes and compositor-only movers. | Default applied: lanes, not braking. Each car and walker is a linear loop (`mvXY`), so two movers whose footprints overlap across the road meet sooner or later; a stop or a slowdown needs a second keyframe set or script. Instead `scene({ avoid: true })` (set by `backdropScene` only; the engine default stays the prototype's drawing, so the parity tests are untouched) gives each mover its own lateral lane across the road, clear of the others and of the agents standing at the kerb (`engines/lanes.ts`). The rng draws are the same, so a mover keeps its direction, speed and phase; a mover with no free lane (about 1 in 9) is not drawn. Fixed numbers (car half-width 5.5, walker 7.15, standing agent 7.475, gap 0.5) come from the art. If a real stop is wanted, it needs a keyframe-allowlist decision. | apps/web/src/park/engines/lanes.ts; park.avoid.test.ts | 2026-10-08 |

(b) `navigatePark` counts followed links in `app/followed.ts` (`useFollowed`); Profile re-reads `?tab=` when the count changes. The count is deliberately NOT part of the route: a first attempt that gave pages a new route for every followed link hung ParkApp.test (a page that redirects in an effect keyed on its route would loop). | BQ-WEB-MOTION-3a | WEB-MOTION-3: which of the handoff's micro-interactions can be built under the pinned honesty and stylesheet tests? | Default applied: only transitions on transform and opacity, and tiloSpin on a ring, written in `park.shell.css` under a `.park-shell__` selector (page controls through `.park-shell__main`, so page and component stylesheets stay free of motion). Built: card/tile/pin lifts, the pinboard spring, press, the sliding thumb, the switch knobs, the nav's hover wash as an opacity overlay, an enter fade for toast, notifications popover and account menu (the shell's `Enter` wrapper flips `data-enter` pre to in after one style read; no timer or frame loop). NOT built, BLOCKED by the existing pins and listed for a supervisor ruling: (1) every `background-color`, `color`, `box-shadow`, `filter` and `width` fade of the reference (hover washes on buttons, rows and chips, the primary button's `brightness(1.08)`, progress and spend bars): `motion.transitions.css.test.ts` allows transform and opacity only; widening it to the paint-only properties `background-color` and `color` would unblock the most common reference fade; (2) new keyframes (`tiloFade` as the reference's rise-in entrance, `tiloGrow` for the memory bars, `tiloPulse` and `tiloGhost`) and any `@starting-style`: `shell.css.test.ts` pins the exact `@keyframes` list and which selectors may use `tiloFade`; (3) mount-time entrances of page content (Skills, Ledger, Agenda, Team reveals), same reason; (4) the Home glass chip's 2px lift, because it carries a `backdrop-filter` (a moving layer with a blur); (5) the Ledger tooltip's .15s fade, a mount-time opacity change on a page element; (6) the Skills try-run and World village spinners and the Skills REC pulse, declined on honesty grounds: they stand for a simulated or fixture run, not an action in flight. Exit animations of popovers, menus and toasts are not in the handoff (the reference unmounts them) and need a deferred unmount; not built. The honesty allowlist is unchanged, but the `css-transition` entry's reason (`test-support/honestyScan.ts`) was reworded (security review r1) to cover pointer-only hover and press feedback on page cards, tiles, pins and events, the slide of control thumbs and knobs, and the enter fade. The Home drafting ring and the Connectors ring were NOT animated: the draft is a 900 ms timer and connect/reconnect a page-local microtask with no call behind them, so a turning ring would imply a run (HR-1); they stay still until a real call exists. | design_handoff_tilo_web_app/README.md Interactions and motion; PRD 9.4; BQ-WEB-3; BQ-WEB-MOTION-2 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-WEB-MOTION-3b | WEB-MOTION-3: how does the stylesheet know the person chose 'still'? | Default: `ParkApp` writes `data-motion` (lively, calm or still, from the Appearance preference) on the `[data-park]` root, and `park.shell.css` ends with a `[data-park][data-motion="still"]` block that mirrors the `prefers-reduced-motion` block, rule for rule (page descendants, nav overlays, enter wrappers, plus the older chrome). The shell's JS already skipped its waits for 'still' (`useMotionOff`); this makes the CSS agree. A press of 1px on the shared `.park-btn` extends the handoff's Home start press (2px, .15s) to every button; the handoff names no press state for the others. | BQ-DEVH-1 | DEV-HARNESS-1 (e2e testability in dev mode): what are the defaults for the dev ledger, the forced replay and the dev tick? | (c) `dev.install` wires `deps.get_ledger` to an in-memory `DevLedger`, the seam production will use. It gets one `commit` entry per newly committed dev event that carries credits (the credits the dev unit runner priced from `config/prices.dev.yaml`, profile `dev-unit`), owner = the world's owner, org `org_tilo`; a repeated event id adds nothing. Owner-only routes are unchanged (in dev the one principal names any world, so a world owned by someone else answers 403 and an unknown one 404); a process that is not in dev mode still answers 503. Dev is NOT proof of the non-member 404: there the one principal is a member of any world named, so an unknown or foreign world answers 404 or 403 depending on the world, while production answers the same bare 404 for both. Dev Desk worlds get their own run id (as `desk.ensure_desk`), so one Desk's credits never count for another. (e) `TILO_DEV_FORCE_REPLAY` with exactly `1` is read only by `dev.install` and sets the process-wide `replay_only` flag (the real switch's), so deployed mode (which `install` refuses) and a process without `TILO_API_DEV` ignore it; no shipped config, script or workflow may set it (a test scans `infra`, `.github`, `justfile`, `config` and Dockerfiles). (d) `POST /dev/schedules/tick` is registered by `dev.install` only. It runs `schedules._fan_out` and `schedules._run_task` (the real tick's code after its token check) with an in-process Tasks client; `schedules.py` and the OIDC verifier are untouched. An optional `as_of` (ISO 8601 with a zone) stands in for now, because a weekly slot is days away. The L3 `grant_budget` policy is still required (`budget_not_granted` without it); the dev store has none, so a test or the owner adds one. Security round: `dev.install` gives each install its own in-memory schedule store and never reads `TILO_SCHEDULES_STORE`; the tick also refuses (503 `dev_tick_needs_memory_store`) when the environment names Firestore with a project not starting `demo-`, requires `application/json` (415), and runs every task even if one fails. `TILO_DEV_FORCE_REPLAY` is refused unless the process flag store is the in-process one. | DEV-HARNESS-1; FR-MTR-10; NFR-22, NFR-24; FR-AGT-14; BQ-MTR10-4, BQ-AGT14-3 | 2026-10-08 |

Seen only because the dev tick drives a routine end to end. | FR-AGT-14; BQ-AGT14-3 | 2026-10-08 |

| BQ-TEAMACC-2 | FR-TEAM-02: who may change roles? | Default applied (supervisor, after the security review of PR #619): only the Owner changes roles (PRD 10.1 gives the Approver no admin rights; design matrix: Invite people and Change tool access are Owner-only); an Owner still cannot change their own role. The user may widen it later; if so an Approver may never grant or take Approver/Owner, never touch another Approver, never raise a role. | FR-TEAM-02, PRD 10.1, BQ-DSN-01 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-ACC02-1 | FR-ACC-02: the Skills page library (WEB-8c) is not built, and no endpoint stores a per-skill tool level. Where does the picker live and does it persist? | Default applied: a "Tool access per skill" section on the Skills page, built from the sample skills (`Skill.tools`) and the team levels in `Learn.tools` (matched on the tool name before " · "). Options above the team level are disabled; a stale or tampered value above it is shown as the team level. The choice is page-local, not saved, and nothing enforces it until a save endpoint exists (needs contract change: a skill tool level field). Same rule as `tilo_policy.access_levels.effective_level`; the web test and the Python test share `packages/policy/tests/fixtures/effective_level_pairs.json`. | FR-ACC-02, BQ-DSN-04 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-TEAMSH-1 | FR-TEAM-01/05: the design keeps Invite always enabled (it explains itself with a toast); FR-TEAM-01 says disabled until the email is valid. The role matrix is a fixture bit table. | Default applied: the Invite button is disabled (still 60% opacity) until `isValidEmail`; Enter on an invalid email keeps the design toast. The matrix cells live in `config/team_rules.yaml` `capabilities`, read by `tilo_api.team_rules.role_can` (`set_switch` for tool access uses it); a web test compares the fixture and the displayed cells with that file. Only `change_tool_access` has an enforcement point today; other rows (invite, approve) are enforced where those routes arrive, and `invite_people` is pinned in config only. | FR-TEAM-01, FR-TEAM-05, BQ-TEAMACC-2 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-TEAMRT-1 | FR-TEAM-02: what may each role spend? The design's role limits are pound sample copy (BQ-DSN-02). | Default applied: `config/team_rules.yaml` `role_limits_minor` sets only the Viewer to 0 (a Viewer spends nothing, PRD 10.1); Owner, Approver and Member have no role limit. A person's own limit (set by the Owner on `PUT /v1/team/members/{id}/limit`, INR minor units) and the role limit only ever LOWER the SEC-16 lifetime cap (`team_access.user_cap_minor`, read in `deps.spend_mode`); an unreadable configured team store denies live. | FR-TEAM-02, SEC-16, BQ-DSN-02 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-TEAMRT-2 | TEAM-ROUTES: where does the team live, who seeds it, and how does it relate to the people directory's own role route (`/v1/people/{id}/role`, contract roles)? | Default applied: one team per install in `tilo_api.team_store` (in memory in dev, seeded with the dev owner; Firestore `orgs/org_tilo/team/settings` only for `TILO_TEAM_STORE=firestore`); unconfigured is 503 on the routes and changes nothing on the engine. Nobody can found a team over the API: seeding a deployed team is an owner step (follow-up). The Park roles map stays separate from the directory's contract roles until BQ-DSN-01 maps them; then one should feed the other. Removing a person does NOT move their worlds: it only rewrites the team store's own world-assignment map to the Owner in the same write; the worlds themselves (`deps.Store.worlds[...].owner`, their runs and cards) keep their owner (follow-up). Every team route, the read included, needs a full web or mobile_web session (PR #642 B3); at most 100 pending invites, one per address (case-insensitive); a personal limit is at most 10^12 minor units. | FR-TEAM-01, FR-TEAM-02, BQ-DSN-01, BQ-TEAMACC-2 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-TEAMRT-3 | FR-TEAM-04: what do the switches do on the engine path? | Default applied: every switch is Owner-only (PRD 10.1: the Approver has no policy rights). spend/share/undo are always on: a stored False is read as on, and on the run path every send already waits at a person's gate, so they never loosen anything. `tools`: tool access is Owner-only whatever the switch says. `weekend`: on, a scheduled start on a Saturday or Sunday (the schedule's time zone) is held (`StartSkipped("weekend_hold", count=False)`, nothing recorded against the schedule): the Cloud Tasks delivery answers 503 and is retried under the queue's retry policy, so a hold that outlasts the retries loses the slot (follow-up: hold at the fan-out). | FR-TEAM-04, PRD 8.3, PRD 10.1, BQ-TEAMACC-1 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-TEAMRT-4 | FR-ACC-01/02: where is the tool level checked, and where does a per-skill level live? | Default applied: `runs.Run.gate` checks `permits(effective_level(team_level, None), tier, read_only)` before the policy engine (tier = the risk table's for the action and facts, never below the plan's gate tier; `read_only` from config/connectors.yaml or config/mcp.yaml only). It only refuses (the step fails before any card or outbox entry); an unset tool is Act, as before. The level is read again when an approved send is dispatched (after `open_gate`, before the outbox), so a tool turned Off while its card waits sends nothing; the meeting-host run (FR-MTG-09) takes the same check (`Seams.team` has no default). A connector the tables do not declare is blocked. A configured team store that cannot be opened fails closed (the step fails, the per-user cap is 0, no scheduled start). Not wired: tilo-channels' Email Autopilot (`autopilot_policy.decide`) and tilo-connectors' `calendar_sync` (other services, no team store). Per-skill levels (FR-ACC-02): needs contract change: a `tool_access` map (connector id -> off/read/draft/act) on `contracts/skill.schema.json`; until then `skill_level` is None and the Skills page's per-skill control stays NotBuilt in live mode. | FR-ACC-01, FR-ACC-02, BQ-DSN-04 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-PA20-1 | FR-PA-20 (effort tracker, API half): which route, who reads it, and what is a "task" and a "meeting"? Work items carry no world or run, so "per task" cannot mean a work item. | Default: one read, `GET /v1/effort` (`/v1/` so it is `NoStore`, behind the SEC-15 gate, one org), person-scoped like the plate: the signed-in person's own worlds only (`WorldRecord.owner`), the world role on the session changes nothing (PE-26). A task is a run (as FR-MTR-10: the world's run id plus the run ids on its events); a meeting row is the owner's non-deleted meeting and the numbers of the world named by its `world_id` (the Outpost world, FR-MTG-07) when that world is the person's own, else a zero row with `world_id` null (no oracle on a foreign world). A deleted meeting is gone for everyone (FR-MTG-10) but its world's spend stays as an unlinked task, so totals equal the ledger. Totals are the sum of the task rows; meeting rows are a view. A person with no record is the people API's one 404 `not_found`; a person who owns no world gets zeros. No new contract: the time-saved object is `meter.schema.json` `$defs/time_saved` unchanged. | services/api/tests/API_FR_PA_20.md; prd-addendum-people-agents.md D-4, FR-PA-20 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-PA20-2 | FR-PA-20 / FR-MTR-05: no Python time-saved computation exists yet (only the meter contract) and the EV-G2 baselines are not measured. Where do the baselines come from, and what counts as a finished, verified unit? | Default: a new seam `deps.get_time_baselines()` returning `{method_url, units: {unit_type: {p25, median, p75}}}` (minutes per unit, EV-G2); unwired or malformed is 503 `not_configured`, never a guess. Time saved (PRD 14.2) = sum over the world's `building.completed` events (each event id once) of `unit_count` x median (`low` x p25, `high` x p75); a unit type without a baseline counts zero; `building.completed` is taken as a finished and verified unit (no other marker exists; Email Autopilot's `reply_handled`/`mail_triaged` are plain unit types). The pure function belongs in `packages/metering` (`tilo_metering`), the route only reads. Needs the EV-G2 study or an architect-owned `config/` shape before production wiring. | PRD 14.2, 14.4, FR-MTR-05; contracts/meter.schema.json; contracts/metering.rules.md MT-05 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-PA20-3 | FR-PA-20 / FR-MTR-05: PRD 14.2 subtracts steering and review minutes (attention spans, MT-07, MT-08), but tilo-api stores and reads no attention span yet. | Default: this ticket reads none, so every `time_saved` has `steering_measured` false and nothing is subtracted (the meter's own rule for a run with no span, MT-05). When spans are written, the same route subtracts them per run (union of one person's overlapping spans, MT-08) and sets the flag; no per-person figure is ever returned. | PRD 14.2; contracts/attention.schema.json; MT-05, MT-08 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-PA20-4 | FR-PA-20 acceptance "no per-person human-time field exists in any contract" vs `contracts/attention.schema.json` (a `user` with `started_at`/`ended_at`, PRD 14.2 steering and review spans). | Default: read the row as "no field for tracked human time" (hours worked, time spent, timesheet, billable, person-minutes): no schema names one, and `attention.schema.json` is the single schema that pairs a person with a measured span, predating FR-PA-20 and used only for the time-saved subtraction. The effort tracker neither reads nor exposes it; the response has no key that names a person or human time. A new such schema needs the architect and an ADR. Guarded by `test_FR_PA_20_no_human_time.py`. | prd-addendum-people-agents.md D-4; PRD 14.2, 16.5 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-PA20-5 | FR-PA-20: status bodies of `GET /v1/effort` when a source fails or is not wired. | Default: as the plate and the cost routes: an unwired seam (people stores, `deps.get_ledger`, `deps.get_time_baselines`) or a malformed baseline sheet is 503 `{"detail": "not_configured"}`; a source that is wired but fails to read (ledger, baselines, people stores) is 503 `{"detail": "unavailable"}`; never a partial body (HR-1), no figure and no trace in either. A 405 for any write method; no model call, no event, no `LIVE`. | people.rules.md PE-22, PE-27; API_FR_MTR_10.md; PRD HR-1 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-PW-1 | PROD-WIRE (issue #522): what durable store does the deployed `tilo-api` use, and how do its events reach it? Today `get_store` and `get_event_writer` answer 503 `not_configured` and only dev mode serves them, in memory, so a redeploy loses every world, task, policy and event. | Proposed in ADR-0026, awaiting the supervisor's / user's OK. (a) One door: `TILO_API_STORE=firestore` plus `TILO_FIRESTORE_PROJECT` (the schedule store's project regex) opens a separate `FirestoreApiStore` with its own guard on the runtime identity's ADC; outside Cloud Run it opens only the emulator with a `demo-` project; anything else is 503 `not_configured`, never cached, never memory, never implied by `K_SERVICE`; the harness `FirestoreStore` guard is untouched. (b) Under `orgs/{deps.ORG_ID}`: `worlds/{world}` (the WorldRecord fields plus `layout`, `created_at`, `rev`), `tasks/{task}` (the durable TaskRecord fields, never its lock), `policies/{policy}` (`{record, world_id, rev}`), the harness events, views and `_head` layout written only by `WorldWriter`, and `worlds/{world}/_pending/queue`: `pending_events` is durable because kill switches apply before their event commits; `dispatch_state`, runs, grant locks and memory stay in the process. (c) Write-through cache: a request commits its records (`rev` checked) and its event parts (into the world's queue) in one transaction, then changes the cache, drains the queue through the writer and answers; a failed commit leaves the cache unchanged, evicts the touched entries and answers 503 `store_unavailable` (409 `stale` on a `rev` mismatch); a failed drain leaves the parts queued for the next load; loads are lazy by path id, plus `load_owner` before any per-owner iteration; Pause all, Stop all and revoke stay applied in the process if their commit fails (503 "not saved"). (d) Every event goes through `EventWriter.append`, which assigns `seq`; `tasks._append` and the `_create_world` extend are removed; the adapter keeps one harness `WorldWriter` per world, uses a pre-plan layout holding only `A_director` (scope `shared`) until the run commits its layout to the world record, reopens a writer on a layout or run change, retries once after `WriterBusy`, and its `reopen()` closes all its writers. (e) Runs stay in `tilo-api`: api `max_instance_count = 1`, `cpu_idle = false`; a world whose run was lost gets one `world.failed` on its next load; the Cloud Tasks hand-off to a harness run route (PRD 6.1) is a later ticket. (f) No composite index; `roles/datastore.user` is enough. (g) Retention 90 days (tasks, worlds, runs) and 30 days (live events) is listed; TTL policies and the event purge are infra, not applied. Open for the user: min instances 0 (default, PRD 6.6; a run waiting more than about 15 minutes with no request is lost) or 1 (about US$50 to US$70 a month); the in-process kill-switch exception; the emulator-only guard outside Cloud Run. Follow-ups: retire `DevEventWriter` for the adapter over `MemoryStore`; serve the stored layout after a restart; backfill district views if a client must read one alone; the event retention mechanism. Slice 1a defaults (tests and ADR left open): `get_store` and `get_event_writer` stay 503 `not_configured` for every config (the door has no caller until 1b returns a Firestore `Store`); Cloud Run means a non-empty `K_SERVICE` (an empty one is not Cloud Run, so it opens only the emulator); a client that cannot be built after a valid config raises `ApiStoreUnavailable`, an `HTTPException` 503 `store_unavailable`; the adapter requires a part's `id` and `vis` (as `DevEventWriter` does), caches a committed event by `seq` order, and takes the world record from `store.worlds`; the answers route takes no writer (it appends no event); a writer failure in intake, approaches or approve is 503 `store_unavailable`; intake commits in the thread pool; the scheduled start writes through `seams.writer` and stores the world record first, removing it if the events fail; `Run._prepare` stores the built layout on the world record in 1a (one line). Review follow-ups: a refused event (`deps.EventRejected`) is a fixed 409 "the event was refused" (scheduled start: `StartSkipped("event_refused")`), logged by type only; a part's `trace` is kept (planning events carry the model profile and tokens the Trace lens needs) only after the event validator accepts it, and a part without one gets a null trace, so the writer stamps `seq`, `ts`, `world_id`, `run_id` and `v`; the door refuses an emulator host on Cloud Run (env or os.environ), an explicit `env` whose emulator host differs from os.environ's, and a `TILO_FIRESTORE_PROJECT` different from `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT` when that is present (the user sets both on tilo-api); `reopen()` skips a world whose lock is held more than 1 s and the retry path reopens only the world that raised; the per-world lock table and SIGTERM are slice 1b's. Slice 1b defaults (decisions left open by the ADR and the tests): (1) the grant, its policy events, the scenario's policies and the task commit in one transaction, the events joining the world's durable queue, so a failed or ambiguous send resends by id and the stream never claims a policy that was never stored; a retried scheduled start finds `w_s{digest}` stored and only drains its queue (tests `PROD_WIRE_1b` for both). (2) A route's records change by `Store.save` (events through the writer first on a plain store, one commit then the drain on `DurableStore`); a task is replaced by a copy sharing its lock (`dataclasses.replace`), never mutated before the commit, so dev mode now swaps the cached `TaskRecord` object on answers, approaches and approve. `next_seq` is the old hint plus the parts. (3) A kill switch lowers every CACHED policy of the owner and pauses the cached world first (no backend read, no wait on the store's lock), then loads the rest of the owner's records and lowers what is new, each within 1 s; it marks every changed policy and world `unsaved` on the store, then commits with `evict=False` every change still unsaved (this call's and any earlier failed one's) with the queued parts of the worlds they belong to, so a retry that finds nothing new to lower still saves what is pending. A failed commit, a failed load or a lock not free within 1 s is 503 `not saved` (never 200 or 202 while anything is unsaved) and sends nothing; the change stays applied here and marked until a commit lands. (4) The layout is committed in place by `Run._prepare` (reverted if the commit fails, the record kept for the run's `world.failed`). A policy loads its world and a task loads its world, so events are always cached with the record. (5) Firestore refuses arrays of arrays: every queued part, the world's `layout` and the task's free-form `draft`, `brief`, `edits` and `approaches` are stored as JSON strings and decode in `read` (the emulator tests store and reload nested arrays in each; ADR-0026 decision 2 says so). (6) The adapter's lock table is a fixed 256 stripes keyed by `crc32(world_id)` rather than pruned: bounded with no race between a pruner and a waiter; two worlds on one stripe only take turns. (7) A queued part the writer refuses (`EventRejected`) stays queued and answers 409 `the event was refused`; it blocks that world's later parts until a person looks (a poison-part policy is a follow-up, so nothing is ever dropped silently). (8) `WriterBusy` reaching any route is 503 `store_unavailable` through an app-level handler. (9) The loads are read-only where the route has not yet checked who is asking: `load_path` (the request seam) and the unverified reads never drain a queue or write a `world.failed`; `store.settle(world_id)` does, once per world, and a route calls it after its ownership check (events route for the owner, the kill switches, `start_run`); `load_world`, `load_task` and `load_owner` keep `settle=True` as their default because the pinned acceptance tests call them directly and expect the drain and the restart rule, and the callers that have not checked pass `settle=False`. The restart's `world.failed` has an id derived from the world and its run. (10) The door refuses any emulator host on Cloud Run, whatever the project (slice 1a's rule); the emulator route test builds its clients with the emulator env and removes it before the request, and also needed an access-store override, since `K_SERVICE` enforces SEC-15's gate (assertions unchanged). (11) The store's process-wide lock covers dict changes and a commit (so `rev`s stay in order), not a load's reads; `deadline(s)` bounds one thread's wait for it, which is how a kill switch never waits behind another commit. Marks of unsaved changes use their own lock. (12) A tick resolves the store only after its token is accepted. (13) Shutdown is bounded at 5 s in all, a busy world is skipped. Open: an approve whose send failed after its commit answers 503 and the retry is 409 `already approved`, so the run waits for the next load or restart to drain the queue and is not started by the retry; the poison-part policy; the `DevEventWriter` retirement and the stored layout after a restart (ADR follow-ups). Review round 2: `load_world`, `load_task` and `load_owner` are read-only by default (`settle=False`); a route passes `settle=True` only after its own ownership or membership check, so a new caller cannot write by forgetting. A kill switch's mark carries a generation: a commit clears only marks it saw before reading its records, never replaces a cached record that is still marked, and an eviction never drops a marked world. | PROD-WIRE (issue #522); PRD 6.1, 6.2, 6.6, 15.1, 15.3; FR-HIL-07; FR-WLD-07; HR-1; ADR-0003; ADR-0018; ADR-0023; ADR-0026 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-UX16-1 | FR-UX-16 "the switches decide whether Tilo joins, takes notes and briefs": no Api route stores a per-event preference, so what does a switch change do? | Page state only, said so in the panel ("Not connected yet. These switches are kept on this page only, so Tilo does not act on them yet."), no Api call, no fetch. The join switch also decides the notes (the design merges them in one switch) and, with no notes, the pins switch reads "No notes, so nothing to pin". The brief switch off hides the brief and its arrival time and shows "Ask Tilo for a brief", which turns the switch on. The existing toasts "Tilo will join ..." and "Tilo will brief you ..." are pinned by WEB_9 tests, so they stay; the panel sentence is the honest line. Follow-up when an events-preference route exists: add an Api method and replace the sentence with the real result. | FR-UX-16; PRD 9.4 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-UX16-2 | FR-UX-16 day load and the "N events from Google Calendar" count do not follow the calendar chips (the reference's behaviour): should they? | Default: keep the reference. The load is the day's meetings (Tilo-only runs and the Tilo calendar excluded) and the chips only filter what the grid draws. Revisit if the user wants a chip to change the load. | FR-UX-16; design_handoff Agenda | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-CHNGATEE-2 | CHN-GATE-E (ADR-0025, amendment 8 Oct): may a display name ever vouch a name word, and a plus tag? | No, user may widen. Only BASE address local parts (plus tag dropped, split on `. _ - +`, filtered, capped 100 x 8) of the draft's own To / Cc and of the mailbox vouch a word, because Gmail copies an inbound From display name into the user's SENT To and a header-only message can write a plus tag for someone else. The bench mailbox is `sarah.ops@` and `scripted_thread(names=...)` adds recipients with those local parts. Header text parsed per read is capped at 60,000 characters (an attacker-shaped thread costs about 0.1 s). | ADR-0025 decisions 3 and 4, amendment 8 Oct; BQ-CHNGATEE-1; security review r1 of CHN-GATE-E | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-SET-1 | FR-SET-04, needs contract change: sessions list and revoke. Security shows "Signed in on" with sign-out per session and "everywhere else", but the API has only Firebase ID tokens (firebase_auth.py, access.py), no session record. | Default: the web shows a labelled stand-in list and a pure model (profileSessions.ts); nothing is sent. Needs: (a) a sessions list endpoint with a "this one" marker; (b) revoke-one; (c) revoke-all-but-current. Firebase revokeRefreshTokens revokes ALL of a user's tokens, so per-session revoke needs Tilo's own session record (opaque id, device label, last active, created). Not invented here; architect to add to contracts/ via ADR. | Open |
| BQ-MTG17-1 | FR-MTG-17 "then follows FR-MTG-02": the FR-MTG-02 ingest (VTT, SRT, text, meet-bot package, versioned Transcript artifact, origin record, Trace event) is not in the repo or an open PR on 8 Oct. What does the recording path hand it? | The smallest self-contained seam, `tilo_planning.recording.transcribe_recording` (pure): returns a schema-valid Transcript content dict (`contracts/transcript.schema.json`, ids `u0001` upward, one-line text), an ingest origin record `{"kind": "recording-upload", media_type, size_bytes, sha256, utterances}` (never the audio) and the safety verdict (`safety_flagged`, `safety_reason`; chunks of at most 20,000 characters through the injected `SafetyProvider`). A flag is kept and marked, never dropped; what a flagged transcript may reach, the artifact write and the Trace event are FR-MTG-02's. The artifact schema's own `origin` is `{kind, unit, agent}` with kind `upload`; mapping the ingest origin record onto it is FR-MTG-02's call (no contract change needed for the seam). | FR-MTG-17, FR-MTG-02; docs/speech-recording-upload.md | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MTG17-2 | FR-MTG-17: what are the upload route, the size cap and the duration check? | No route is built (tilo-api wiring waits for FR-MTG-02). `config/speech.yaml` caps one upload at 15 MiB (`max_bytes`, checked before any request, fails closed) and 2 hours (`max_duration_ms`). Duration cannot be read from raw bytes without a media parser, so it is enforced on the last utterance offset after transcription plus an optional `declared_duration_ms` from the client. Unverified, not fetched: the Gemini inline-data request limit is believed to be about 20 MB, so 15 MiB stays under it; a longer recording needs a Cloud Storage handoff, a later ticket. Media types: `audio/*`, `video/mp4`, `video/webm`; anything else is refused before any speech call. | FR-MTG-17, EFF-03 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MTG17-3 | FR-MTG-17 / FR-MTG-11: where do the speech vendor and model live, and are they routed and metered like a model role? | `config/speech.yaml` (vendor `google` or dev `fake`, profile, model, caps; the PL-07 denylist of `config/models.yaml` applies), loaded by `tilo_providers.speech.load_speech_config`. Not in `config/models.yaml`: `contracts/providers.schema.json` (read-only) has no speech role or provider kind, so the Router (cascade, fallback, budget) does not serve it and `config/prices.yaml` has no speech price; a failed Gemini audio call raises a provider error and the caller reports it. Needs contract change if speech should be a router role with a price: add a `speech` role and a speech profile kind to `providers.schema.json`. | FR-MTG-17, FR-MTG-11, PL-06 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-SEC16-3 | SEC-16: what are the beta spend caps? | user approved 8 Oct: per run INR 500 (`run_minor` 50000), per uid lifetime INR 3,000 (`uid_lifetime_minor` 300000), per UTC day for the deployment INR 5,000 (`global_day_hard_minor` 500000), all in `config/spend_caps.yaml` only (no number in code or in the web). It supersedes the shipped values stated in `BQ-SEC16-1` (INR 120, INR 1,000, INR 2,000). The daily cap of INR 5,000 is above PRD 17.3's US$25 hard cap at the 80 INR per US$ default (US$62.5); the user chose it knowingly. The replay-only fallback at the daily cap is unchanged (NFR-24). `contracts/` (the shipped_defaults fixture, schema text, ADR-0002 decision 16) carry the old numbers until an architect pass. | SEC-16, FR-MTR-04, PRD 17.3 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-FEEDBACK-1 | Beta feedback: what does a person see at the lifetime cap, and where does their feedback go? | user request 8 Oct, not in the PRD: at the lifetime cap (existing 403 `spend_cap_reached`, no new code) the web shows a dialog ("Beta allowance used") with a feedback box; `POST /v1/feedback` takes `{text, context: "lifetime_cap"}`, uid only from the verified principal, plain text cleaned to 1 to 2000 characters (8000 raw bound), 5 an hour and 20 in a lifetime per uid, answer 201 `{received: true}`; stored under `orgs/{org}/feedback` (Firestore) and never logged. Only a full (Google) sign-in may send it (an anonymous demo session is 403 `full_session_required`); the body is read like the people writes (415, 413 over 64 KiB before the read, a 422 with field locations only, never the input); invisible-only text is 422; a busy store is 503 `busy`; slots `00`..`19` bound both limits by construction. The pop-up says Thanks only after the server confirmed. There is no read route for ordinary users; the owner or admin read path is deferred (no admin convention exists in tilo-api), until then the owner reads the collection in the Firebase console. Follow-ups, not done: erase the per-uid subtree in `tilo_memory.erasure` (its required-store registry has no feedback entry and needs a packages/memory change); a per-IP or global daily cap on feedback writes (a refused call reads up to 20 documents); an app-wide `RequestValidationError` handler that drops `input` (every pydantic-body route still 500s and logs on a lone surrogate or NaN); check the INR 150,000 a month budget alert before `deps.get_spend_cap_ledger` is wired. | beta testing, SEC-16 | 2026-10-08 |

Registry priority `should` (not a PRD 9.8 row); the user may raise it. | BQ-MROOM-6 | ADR-0028: who pays for the room's model calls and which profiles does it use? | The convener: each reply reserves against the room's `credit_cap` (20 credits) and the convener's per-user cap (SEC-16, lowered by their team role); the run's budget pays nothing. Example roles: `meeting` on `lite-31` (Quick, default) and `meeting_thinking` on `flash-37` thinking low (Thinking); the user picks the final models. | ADR-0028 decisions 4, 9; FR-MTR-04; SEC-16; PL-01 | 2026-10-08 |

Which consumers assume every run starts with `world.requested` (the world's owner is the actor of its FIRST one, OB-01)? | The owner stays the actor of the world's first `world.requested`; a revision run writes none, so no L2 instruction can arise from a room. MR-H2 and MR-W1 check the run-start assumptions in tilo-api (`runs.py`, the Worlds list) and the web store before relying on them. | ADR-0028 decision 8; OB-01; ADR-0026 | 2026-10-08 |

| BQ-LE2E-10 | EFF-02 / PL-03: the Router never applied a role's `thinking`, so planning calls sent no thinking level and the model's default (dynamic, high on Gemini 3.x) ran, billed as output (live run 2: 13.5 credits). Which level wins, and what do the Director's planning calls ask for? | An explicit `GenerateRequest.thinking_level` wins; when the caller left it unset the Router fills the role's configured `thinking` (like `max_output_tokens`, BQ-63), per candidate and only for a profile that declares the `thinking` capability (the others would answer a 400; a role with no `thinking` sends none). The planning code sets its own: brief extraction `low`, approaches `medium`, plan compile `medium` (as the supervisor and user asked), so the Director role's `thinking: high` is only a default for callers that set none (`config/models*.yaml` comment). The planning requests changed, so the B-007 fake-model recordings were regenerated with `evals/tests/regen_live_e2e_fixture.py` (no network). Whether `medium` is cheap enough for the 5-credit target is for the user's live run to show | PRD 6.6 EFF-02; live run 2, 8 Oct 2026 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-LE2E-11 | EFF-02 / FR-MTR-01: a truncated reply (`OutputTruncatedError`) fell back to a sibling profile sent the same cap (a second paid call burning the same tokens), and the meter billed it at a word count and the full cap. What is the rule? | A truncated reply is terminal for its cap: the Router raises `RouterExhaustedError` (every attempt, the error as `__cause__`) and makes no second call. Every candidate of a call is sent the same cap (BQ-63), so no sibling has a larger one; the live runner's own retry builds a NEW request with twice the cap and still works. The call was billed: `OutputTruncatedError.input_tokens` (Gemini `prompt_token_count`, OpenAI-compatible `prompt_tokens`; None with no usage) is new and optional, the Router charges a truncated call from input plus output plus thinking tokens (else its pre-call quote) and writes them on the attempt and `RouterError.budget_left`, and the live meter bills it the same way, else the provider's own count of the input (`RecordReplayProvider.provider_count_tokens`: a count call, no generation) and the cap it burned as output. A response with no usage is counted by the provider's own count, not words. Other raised calls (quota, retryable) stay uncharged. Narrator and Lead have no larger-cap retry of their own: a cut Narrator leaves no Story, as for any Narrator failure | `packages/providers/src/tilo_providers/errors.py` docstring; PRD 6.6 EFF-02; FR-MTR-01 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-LE2E-12 | EFF-02: which profile is the specialist's primary? | The cheapest first: `lite-31` then `flash-37` in `config/models.yaml` and `config/models.gemini.yaml` (was `flash-37` then `lite-31`); escalation on provider or schema failure as for every role, the Verifier rule unchanged. `config/models.nim.yaml` already puts the smaller `nim-gemma-3-27b` first. Caps and thinking kept (8192, `low`; `lite-31` declares no thinking capability, so none is sent to it) | PRD 6.6 EFF-02 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-EXPECTED-OUT-1 | PL-03 / EFF-03: the Router reserved input plus the FULL `max_output_tokens` before a call, so a writer call (cap 16384, ~12.5 credits quoted, really ~1.6) was refused (`stopped_at_cap`) while the budget could pay for it. What is reserved, and when is a call refused? | A call is reserved at its input plus the role's EXPECTED output, `min(expected, the call's own cap)`; `max_output_tokens` stays the hard cap sent. Refused before sending only when (a) that reserve is over the budget left, or (b) input plus the full cap is over 2x the budget left (`_CAP_HEADROOM`). Anything past both is sent and settles from the real usage (else the reserve; a cut reply with no usage, the whole cap); the budget is clamped at 0 (`_spend`, `_charge_truncated`), never negative. Expected sizes: `contracts/providers.schema.json` closes the role object (`additionalProperties: false`), so a `expected_output` role field would need a contract change; instead a separate `config/expected_output.yaml` (`roles: {role: tokens}`), loaded by the provider config loader next to the models file (optional; one file serves every models file, an unknown role is ignored, a size must be an integer 1..max_output_tokens). Default for a role without one: `min(max_output_tokens, 1024)` (1024 when it has no cap), which is what it was reserved for before for those roles. Values (director 3000, lead 1500, specialist 2000, verifier 1500, writer 3000, narrator 1000) are estimates, not measurements: the repo has no recorded real usage (the B-007 fake recordings carry a scripted 500), so they come from the docs/live-e2e.md run-2 table (Director ~600 out at low thinking, ~6000 at high; a writer note ~1500) and the estimate floor in `live_e2e.py`; the user's live run should replace them with a p90. The live-e2e `credits_high` stays an explicit worst-case ceiling (every call at its whole cap), not this reserve. Consequence at flash-38 prices: a call at the 16384 cap (~12.5 credits) needs about 6.3 credits left, so `--max-credits 5` still cannot send the Director or writer; a factor of 3 would allow about 4.2 | PRD 6.6 EFF-03, PL-03; `config/expected_output.yaml`; `docs/live-e2e.md` | 2026-10-08 |

Until then the #641 seam stays as it is and dev uses a fake `speech` profile priced 0. Per-minute pricing (Google Cloud Speech-to-Text) is not covered; D-1 chose Gemini audio. | FR-MTG-17, FR-MTG-11, PL-06, FR-MTR-01; ADR-0027 | 2026-10-08 |

No EV rule checks them yet: an event-level rule fixture would break the harness parity test (D-11) until `tilo_harness.validator` learns it, and a stream rule needs a Desk-world layout fixture. Proposed for the TEAM-ROUTES wiring ticket: an EV rule in `contracts/event.rules.md`, `scripts/contracts_events.py` and the harness validator in one change. The log is owner-only (`vis: []`); showing it to Approvers would be a new decision. | FR-TEAM-04, PE-12, ADR-0009, ADR-0027 | 2026-10-08 |

If the user wants them in the Trace, a later `team_member.changed` (additive, its own ADR) would carry them. | FR-TEAM-01, FR-TEAM-02, FR-TEAM-04, ADR-0027 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-ADR27-4 | ADR-0027 decision 3 (FR-ACC-02): the per-skill level is part of the signed-off `skill.yaml`. What does the Skills page's per-skill control do in live mode? | Default: it shows the skill's declared level, read-only, beside the team's level, and a change goes through a new skill version (expert review, PRD 8.2; FR-AGT-15 for proposals). A per-team override edited at run time would need a team-store field and a `team_rule.changed` rule kind, which is a later decision. Any value can only lower the team's level, so who sets it is not a security question. | FR-ACC-02, PRD 8.2, BQ-ACC02-1, BQ-TEAMRT-4, ADR-0027 | 2026-10-08 |
