| BQ-CAL07-6 | FR-CAL-07 (a) build defaults where the test file is silent: (1) `calendar_today` with more than 10 listed entries keeps the existing `More on My plate.` line and its navigate to My plate (the one-entry offer is the only change); (2) with no known time zone `calendar_prepare` shows `HH:MM UTC` like `calendar_today`; (3) any invalid time of day in the question (`13pm`) is `which_entry` even beside a valid one; (4) a title that is empty or missing reads as the untitled text. | Defaults applied; implementation in `tilo_api/assistant/calendar.py` and `config/assistant.yaml` (`report: calendar`, `report: prepare`). | API_FR_CAL_07a.md 2 and 3 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-CLLIVE-6 | CL-LIVE-2: how does an item the run made become an email or a Drive file for a real write? | Default: an email's subject is the first non-empty line of the stored text (leading `#` dropped, 120 characters, else "Draft from Tilo") and its body is the whole text; a file is named `<artifact_id>` plus an extension from its media type, with that media type. Strict UTF-8 for email. The run's dev destination `thread-dev-1` means "a new message, no thread" (Gmail) and "the Tilo folder" (Drive); the card shows that. Recipients are the run's stand-in until a real resolver exists. | evals/src/tilo_evals/live_writes.py; services/connectors/tests/API_CL_LIVE_2.md | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-CLLIVE-7 | CL-LIVE-2: what counts as the person's approval of a real write, and how long does it last? | Default: the exact word `yes` typed at an interactive terminal on the gate card for that write; `y`, Enter, `--auto-approve-t1t2`, piped input and the tier never count, even for a T1 draft. The approval is bound to a digest of connector, action, recipients, destination, item and content, is spent by the first entry that uses it and survives that entry's retries (the adapter's own earlier-attempt lookup stops a second draft or send). Attachments are not supported in v1 writes (refused). `--allow-writes` is refused where there is no person (`live-e2e`); `--dry-run` with the flags only prints the plan. | services/connectors/src/tilo_connectors/real_writes.py; services/connectors/tests/API_CL_LIVE_2.md | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-CLLIVE-8 | CL-LIVE-2 (security review, for CL-LIVE-3): do the drive/thread digests cover a Gmail thread subject or a Drive folder name the item is saved under? | Default: no. A reply thread or an existing folder is not resolvable yet (the run's stand-in destination means no thread / the Tilo folder), so the digest covers connector, action, recipients, destination, item and content only. CL-LIVE-3 must add the thread subject (or the folder name) to the card and the digest when a real thread or folder can be named. | services/connectors/src/tilo_connectors/real_writes.py | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-CLLIVE-9 | CL-LIVE-2 (security review, for CL-LIVE-3): `gmail.compose` can send mail at the token level, so is the draft-only rule enough? | Default: the rule is enforced by the wrapper (the send door exists only behind `--allow-send` and a typed yes), not by the scope; Google offers no draft-only scope. CL-LIVE-3 should keep the send scope out of the consent screen unless `--allow-send`, and treat a token that carries `gmail.send` without `--allow-send` as a refusal. | services/connectors/src/tilo_connectors/real.py; docs/live-e2e.md | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-CAL07B-1 | FR-CAL-07 (b): where does the free/busy for a reschedule proposal come from (the addendum says "from the free/busy read (FR-CON-14 acceptance)")? | Default applied (supervisor ruling): the cached `calendar_entry` records (FR-CAL-08 cache), through the pure `tilo_people.free_slots.find_free_slots`. No `read_free_busy` action, no new scope, no config change, no new Google call. Busy = the person's own `accepted` entries and `free_busy` intervals of the person; proposed, declined, cancelled and other people's entries do not block. An all-day entry blocks its local day (or start to end when it has an end); an entry without an end blocks 60 minutes (`default_entry_min`). The contract has no transparent flag, so every accepted entry is busy. The working window is an argument (the caller reads `working_window` of config/personal.yaml); personal.yaml has no working-days key, so the function takes `working_days` (default Monday to Friday). Search horizon capped at 14 days, 2000 entries, 50 results. | FR-CAL-07, FR-CAL-08, FR-CON-14 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-CAL07B-2 | FR-CAL-07 (b): how does a person connect their calendar and consent to the read? | Default: out of scope for FR-CAL-07 (b): no calendar connect/consent route exists today, so the cache is only as full as what FR-CAL-08 has synced; the free-slot search treats an empty cache as a free calendar, and the caller (ticket 3) must say so in the proposal when the person has no synced calendar. | FR-CAL-07, FR-CAL-08 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-ADR32T1-1 | ADR-0032 T1: the ADR caps the send body at 64 KiB, which JSON Schema cannot express. What does `meeting_host_rpc.schema.json` cap, and how is an oversize body refused? | Default (architect): the route refuses a body over 65536 bytes before parsing it; the schema caps `draft.content_b64` at 43692 characters (the base64 of 32 KiB), so a send request with 50 recipients of 254 ASCII characters still fits (pinned by a test). An oversize, non-JSON or non-contract body answers 422 `invalid_request`, not 413, so ADR-0032 decision 6's stub table (401/403/422 = refused before anything acted) covers it. Error bodies are `{error}` with four codes: `unauthenticated` 401, `forbidden` 403, `invalid_request` 422, `not_configured` 503. | contracts/meeting_host_rpc.schema.json; ADR-0032 decisions 2, 3, 6 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-ADR32T1-2 | ADR-0032 T1: how closed are the send request and the `parts` list? | Default (architect): `send_request` narrows `entry` to MH-10 (state `queued`, `create_meeting_event` on `meeting_host`, T3 with `new_recipients`, 1 to 50 recipients, no attachments, an item, destination `primary`, undo 0, `authz.by` person) and `evidence` to the T3 gate path (`approval.requested` and `approval.resolved` with their data schemas, policy, Team Lead and instruction null, `earlier_applied` empty), so a schema-valid request is already a meeting invitation; HB-03/05/07 and OB-01/05 stay validator checks. `parts` holds at most 16 items: `{type, connector, user_id}` for `connection.expired`, `connection.rate_limited` and `connection.revoked` of `meeting_host` only (`connection.established` is not emitted on this surface, since consent is not on it), and `{type: alert.raised, alert_id}`; a connection part names the call's own user (the send's `entry.authz.ref`, else the request's `user_id`), and tilo-api's client (T4) drops any other. `receipt` and `may_have_sent` are always present and null when they do not apply (ADR-0011 is only proposed); a `status` of `none` has `scope_ok` false. | contracts/meeting_host_rpc.schema.json; ADR-0032 decisions 2, 5, 7 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-LW5-1 | LW-05 (ADR-0029 decision 3): where does the region check run, and what does it break? | `ProvidersConfig.check_live` runs in `load_providers_config` when `LIVE=1` and in `Router(live_permitted=True)`; replay, dev and tests are never checked. It refuses a `google` profile unless `GOOGLE_LOCATION` equals the sheet's `region` (`model_armor`: `TILO_MODEL_ARMOR_LOCATION`, default asia-southeast1) and any profile past its `valid_until`. A deployed service that builds a live router therefore needs `GOOGLE_LOCATION=asia-southeast1` (BQ-LW-11) or it refuses to start; no deploy file was changed. | PL-06, EFF-01; ADR-0029; BQ-LW-11 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-LW5-2 | LW-05: the SearchProvider has no `count_tokens`, so how does EFF-03 cap a search query? | The router estimates two bytes a token (an upper bound) and refuses a query over the role's `max_input_tokens`; a query is never compacted. The provider itself refuses a query over 2,000 characters. | EFF-03, PL-03; ADR-0029 decision 7 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-LW5-3 | LW-05: what is a "cleaned" source URL, and what happens to a grounding source that has none? | `clean_source_url` keeps `https://host[:port]/path?query` and removes control characters, userinfo and the fragment; a source that is not an https URL with a host, or is over 2,048 characters, is dropped, and duplicates collapse. Titles and domains are kept as untrusted text. The redirect URL is stored as `redirect_url`; `resolved_url` stays None until LW-08 resolves it through tilo-browser. | PRD 12.2; SEC-09; ADR-0029 decision 7 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-LW5-4 | LW-05 (ADR-0029 decision 7): the search role's fallback is the same profile "retried once"; does the router retry? | No. `Router._candidates` already lists a profile once when primary and fallback are equal, so a failed search is one attempt and `RouterExhaustedError`; the caller decides whether to ask again. Changing this would change every role, so it is left to a router ticket. | NFR-25, PL-03; ADR-0029 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-LW5-5 | LW-05: the dev `search-fake` profile needs a price in `config/prices.yaml` as well as `config/prices.dev.yaml`? | Yes, at 0 with a 0 query fee: the PL-06 start-up check and the search-role rule run `config/models.dev.yaml` against `config/prices.yaml`, as for `director-dev` and `safety-fake`. No real price was added. | PL-06; ADR-0029 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-PW-3 | PROD-WIRE 3 (issue #522): how does the deployed `tilo-api` hold its Firestore client, where does SEC-15's declaration live, and what of the production wiring is still open? | Built: (1) one client per project per process, `tilo_api.prod.firestore_client` (single-flight, a failed build never cached, `FirestoreUnavailable` never echoes the error), the one guard run on every call before the cache is read: the project matches the schedule store's id shape; with `K_SERVICE` or `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` present (even empty) any present `FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST` (even empty) is refused, because the library enters emulator mode whenever the variable exists; a live project opens only when `K_SERVICE` is non-empty, anywhere else only the emulator and a `demo-` project open; `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT`, when set, equals the project; the cached client is keyed by project and emulator host. `store_door.client_factory` and the people door's default factory delegate to it, so the api store, the people stores and the access store share one client object (`people_firestore.emulator_people_stores` keeps its own guarded constructor, FS-06). (2) The access store: `orgs/{org_id}/access/{uid}`, field `declaration`; `declare` is one `create` and, on `AlreadyExists`, a read-back (never `set`); a malformed stored value reads as none (fail closed) and a `declare` that meets one answers 503 rather than overwrite it; a contended create is retried a few times, then 503; a uid Firestore refuses as a document id (empty, `/`, `.`, `..`, `__.*__`, over 1500 bytes) is a `ValueError` before any client call, and the access routes answer it 503 `not_configured`, never 500; a Google API error is `FirestoreUnavailable` (503 `store_unavailable`, only the type logged). `deps.get_access_store` returns it only when `TILO_API_STORE=firestore` and the api store door opens, else 503 `not_configured`, never cached; after a failed client build it backs off for `PEOPLE_STORES_BACKOFF_S` like the people door (FS-18). `firestore.rules` names `orgs/{org}/access/{uid}` server-only. (3) Terraform (code only, nothing applied): the service module takes `cpu_idle` per service, on both Cloud Run resources (the managed one and the `ignore_env` one tilo-api lives in); the dev `api` entry gets `max_instance_count = 1` always (ADR-0026: one writer; `deploy-dev.yml` and `cloudbuild.yaml` pass `--max-instances 1` for api too, since a gcloud update overrides Terraform's revision maximum) and, only with the new variable `api_durable_stores = true` (default false), `cpu_idle = false` (CPU allocated for the in-process kill-switch state). The store switches are NOT Terraform's: tilo-api has `ignore_env = true`, so `TILO_API_STORE` and `TILO_PEOPLE_STORE` = `firestore` (with the `TILO_FIRESTORE_PROJECT` it already has) are runtime env set with `gcloud run services update` (docs/deploy-dev.md "Durable stores"). Setting them is the user's security-flagged rollout (ADR-0023; ADR-0026 is still Proposed), so no apply switches anything on by itself. Still 503 `not_configured`: `deps.get_ledger` (FR-MTR-10): needs contract change, because no ledger or runs store exists in `contracts/` or `infra/` (BQ-MTR10-4). The user decides and applies: when they accept ADR-0026 (and ADR-0023's people store), they set `api_durable_stores = true` for the dev env and apply the Terraform plan (CPU always allocated on the one instance; this raises the api's idle cost, PRD 6.6), then run the two-variable gcloud step in docs/deploy-dev.md, then check the api instance count and `/health`. With the default false the plan still sets the one-instance maximum and nothing else changes. This change applies nothing and deploys nothing. OPEN (security round 2 follow-up): the api store door (`durable_store.deployed`) still retries a failing client build on every request under its lock, with no FS-18 backoff (the people and access doors have one); add it with PR4's smoke. OPEN (base merge, 9 Oct): `team_store._build` (TILO_TEAM_STORE=firestore, from another lane) builds its own `firestore.Client` without the laptop guard, so a laptop with ADC and a live project reaches it; it should delegate to `prod.firestore_client` (one guarded client per process). Exempt in the PROD-WIRE 3 sources guard until then. | PROD-WIRE (issue #522); PRD 6.2, 6.6, SEC-15; ADR-0023; ADR-0026; BQ-MTR10-4 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-WEBAPI-1 | WEB-TO-API: how does the deployed web app reach the deployed tilo-api without weakening `deploy-web`? | Default: a separate recipe `deploy-web-live` (fixed API origin constant in scripts/web_build_env.py, no parameter, Firebase config required) so `deploy-web` stays the fixture build; one origin added to CSP connect-src; tilo-api adds CORS only when `TILO_API_CORS_ORIGINS` is set (exact https origins, any bad entry = none, no credentials, outermost so a preflight needs no token while real requests still pass every gate). The origin is hard-coded to the current tilo-dev service URL; if the service is recreated the constant and CSP change together. | services/api/src/tilo_api/cors.py; scripts/web_build_env.py; docs/deploy-dev.md | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-COSTFAST-1 | COST-FAST-1 (user decision 8 Oct): what do Quick and Thinking mean, and what is the default? | Questions go to the assistant (Quick), work still gets approaches (Thinking). `POST /intake` takes an optional form field `mode` (`quick` or `thinking`); absent means `thinking`, so nothing existing changes; anything else is a 422 like a bad scenario. Quick is one call on the `assistant` role (lite tier, 8000 in / 1000 out, low thinking, 0.5-credit router budget), no Director, no approaches, no task or world. Thinking is today's flow (FR-INT-06). The classifier only suggests: Thinking chosen is always Thinking; Quick plus work is an explicit `switch_to_thinking` offer (HTTP 200, nothing stored, no charge), never a silent Thinking run. Shapes and reasons: `services/api/tests/API_COST_FAST_1.md`. | COST-FAST-1, FR-INT-06, EFF-03 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-COSTFAST-2 | COST-FAST-1: how does the classifier decide, and how long may a Quick question be? | Rules first, in code, zero cost; reasons are a closed set (`attachments`, `side_effect`, `deliverable`, `needs_tools`, `multi_step`, `too_long`, `scenario`, `unclear`). Any attachment or a scenario with Quick is an offer. A question longer than `config/assistant.yaml` `question.max_chars` (500, FR-PA-19), judged on raw length, is `too_long` (an offer, not a 422). The model is a fallback only for a text no rule reads: one JSON-mode call on `assistant`, reply exactly `{"kind":"simple"}` or `{"kind":"work","reason":<set>}`; anything else is no verdict and the offer with `unclear`. The builder lists which texts reach the model. | COST-FAST-1, FR-PA-19 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-COSTFAST-3 | COST-FAST-1 / PRD 9.4: does a Quick answer need an event or a world, and where is its cost recorded? | No event, no world: nothing animates, so HR-1 has nothing to cause. `assistant.replied` cannot carry it (`answered` needs at least one cited record and a raw-archive `message_ref`). Credits are recorded in the person's FR-PA-19 daily allowance (`deps.get_spend_ledger()`: reserve, commit actual, release on failure) and, deployed and live, one `unit_reserve_minor` on the SEC-16 uid ledger (BQ-SEC16-1). If the web must tick a meter for Quick it needs an event: needs contract change, smallest option: allow `assistant.replied` outcome `answered` with empty `sources` for a new `kind: general` and a nullable `message_ref`. | COST-FAST-1, HR-1, FR-MTR-06 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-COSTFAST-4 | COST-FAST-1: what does the Quick model see, and is a Quick answer cached or archived? | The question alone, cleaned (control and bidi characters removed): no plate, context, store, memory, tools or ids; the `assistant` role elsewhere also gets the person's own gated plate rows. No cross-person cache (a repeat is a new call unless an `Idempotency-Key` repeats it). The question and answer are not logged and not archived; the reply is bounded to 8000 characters and is never parsed. The reply is not safety-screened on the way out (as the panel's templates were not); a follow-up if the user wants it. | COST-FAST-1, SEC-07 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-COSTFAST-5 | COST-FAST-1: Quick under replay-only, outages, the spend cap and the day allowance. | `replay_only` is 503 `replay_only: true` for Quick, answer or offer (as new work). Quota or availability failure is 503 `model_unavailable`; a rejected request or empty reply is 502; no `assistant` role is 503 `not_configured` and never a Director fallback. Deployed and live, Quick reserves and settles one unit on the SEC-16 ledger (released on failure) and is refused 403 `spend_cap_reached` at the cap or when one more unit would pass it. A day whose FR-PA-19 allowance cannot cover the 0.5-credit budget is 429 `{"error": ...}`. | COST-FAST-1, NFR-22, NFR-24, SEC-16 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-COSTFAST-6 | COST-FAST-1: retries. | Optional `Idempotency-Key` header on a Quick ask, with people_limits' rule: same principal, key and body again is the first answer byte for byte, one model call, one charge; another body is 422; another principal's key is its own; a bad key (over 64 characters or outside `[A-Za-z0-9._-]`) is 422 and never echoed; a failed first attempt leaves the key free; racing retries make one call. Thinking ignores the header, as today. | COST-FAST-1 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-COSTFAST-7 | COST-FAST-1: where is a Quick call metered, given "nothing stored" and FR-MTR-01? | Every Quick call that reaches a model (the answer and the classifier fallback; a rules offer costs zero) is held, committed and released on the person's FR-PA-19 daily allowance (`deps.get_spend_ledger()`, the per-user spend limit, the same ledger the panel's model fallback uses) and, deployed and live, one `unit_reserve_minor` is reserved with a fresh `quick_<id>` run and `deps.ORG_ID` on the SEC-16 ledger, so the uid, run, org-day and global-day caps all count it. The P8 Treasury is run-based and has no writable seam in tilo-api (`deps.get_ledger` is read-only and fails closed, BQ-MTR10-4), and a Quick call has no run or world, so it is not on `GET /costs`. Open, needs the user (a new ledger seam, not a contract field): once the Treasury is wired, Quick opens a per-user pseudo run and commits there. | PRD 14.1, 14.5; FR-MTR-01, FR-MTR-10, FR-PA-19, SEC-16; test_COST_FAST_1_metering.py | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-QT-1 | COST-FAST-1 web: where does the Quick / Thinking control sit, and what is the default? | The Park has no design for it (design/source has no composer depth control). Default: a two-item `SegmentedControl` named "Depth" (Quick, Thinking) in the composer panel's bottom row between "Size of job" and the hint, styled like the size control; Thinking is the default (today's flow), so nothing changes for a person who never chooses. The size of job (village, town, city) is a different thing and stays; Quick ignores it (the size is sent only as the Api's `mode` argument and never reaches the server). The hint line says in words what each depth does and no number. The design needs a look: the row is crowded at 1040 px. | COST-FAST-1; docs/park-designed-not-in-prd/Home.md | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-QT-2 | COST-FAST-1 web: how is the choice remembered, and under what key? | `localStorage` key `tilo.park.effort.<signed-in email, lower case>` (`tilo.park.effort.anonymous` until the user read lands), read and written in try/catch with an in-memory fallback only for what storage refused to keep. `profileStore.ts` is not on this base, so the same approach was written beside the composer (`pages/Home/effortStore.ts`); fold the two key helpers together when the profile store lands. A choice made before the email is known is not overridden when it arrives. | COST-FAST-1 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-QT-3 | COST-FAST-1 web: Quick on the fixture path and the live path before the server supports `mode`. | Fixture: Quick returns a fixed sample answer labelled "Sample answer", no cost, never claiming a model was asked. Live: the Park Api sends `mode=quick` and reads the 200 body strictly (answer, switch offer, or an unreadable reply); there is no disabled control. An old server that ignores `mode` answers a Quick ask with a task-shaped 201: the page says it cannot read the reply (and that server did make a task), which is why the server change must ship first. `startWorld` takes "quick" as a third argument at run time and the page reaches it through one helper, `askQuick(api, mode, query)` in `park/api/index.ts`; the `Api` type itself is unchanged because `types.test.ts` pins `startWorld`'s parameters and `keyof Api`, and `liveApi.test.ts` pins the method list at 23. Promote it to a typed Api method when the owner of those pins agrees. Thinking calls are unchanged: two arguments, no `mode` field on the wire. | COST-FAST-1; src/park/api/index.ts | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-QT-4 | COST-FAST-1 web: 403 `spend_cap_reached` and the other refusals of a Quick ask. | One honest sentence each and the typed text stays: 429 "limited right now (today's allowance or too many asks)"; 403 with code (or text) `spend_cap_reached` "The spending limit for this account has been reached, so nothing new can run." with a code comment marking the seam for CL-REACH's lifetime-cap pop-up (not built here); 503 "Live models are unavailable right now"; other 5xx "a problem on its side"; 422 shows the server's own short sentence. The server's field for `spend_cap_reached` (`code` or `error`) is not in the spec; both are read. | COST-FAST-1 spec "Limits"; PRD 9.4 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-QT-5 | COST-FAST-1 web: where else does work start, and the 500-character cap. | The only Park start flow is the Home composer; Worlds "New world" goes to Home (`app.go("home")`), so it gets the control for free. The legacy `StartScreen` (/start, "Start a real task") is out of scope. Quick input over 500 characters (counted in characters, as the server's `question.max_chars`) is refused in the page with a plain sentence, never cut; the number 500 is the server's cap, the only number shown before an answer. A Quick answer is shown as plain text inside the composer (no markdown), with `Cost ... credits` / `Saved ... credits` only when the server served them. | COST-FAST-1 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MTG09UI-1 | MEETINGS-1 / FR-MTG-09: the ticket expected `POST /v1/meetings/{id}/host` to carry the T3 gate card. It does not: the 200 is `{"world_id"}` only (people.py host_meeting; API_MTG09_b.md), and the card is written to the world's card store by the host run on its own thread. How does the web get the card? | After a 2xx the page reads `GET /worlds/{world_id}/cards` (the existing `listCards`) up to 10 times, 1 s apart, and shows the newest T3 gate card whose action is `create_meeting_event` (the card carries no meeting id or run to bind on, so an older held card in the same world cannot be told apart except by `created_at`), through the existing card pieces (`CardHead`, `CardOptions`, `useAnswer`) with no option preselected. If none appears the page says the approval card has not appeared and links to Needs you; the button does not return (a second request is a 409; a 409 `already_hosted` also removes the button and links to Needs you). The client never answers the card | FR-MTG-09; FR-HIL-01 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MTG09UI-2 | MEETINGS-1: the fixture client (and any client without `hostMeeting`) cannot host. Sample gate card or honest "not connected"? | Honest "not connected": the button still shows for the owner of a scheduled bot meeting, and a click says "Hosting isn't connected yet. Nothing was started." with no call and no card. `hostMeeting` is on `HttpClient` only, not on the frozen `TiloClient` interface (like `pauseWorld`) | FR-MTG-09; HR-4 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MTG09UI-3 | MEETINGS-1: S-26 lists fixture meetings and none is a scheduled bot meeting, so the button would never show. Which meeting carries it? | S-26 opens the one upcoming fixture meeting `mt_q4_plan_next` (already in `ALL_MEETINGS`, owner `u_owner`, status `scheduled`, source `bot`) when its address names it (`/meetings/mt_q4_plan_next`); the list and its counts do not change. The owner is `SIGNED_IN`, as on S-27 and the calendar. The fixture meeting has no `world_id`, so against a real API it would answer 409 `not_hostable`, shown in words. Wiring S-26 to the API list is a later ticket | FR-MTG-09; PE-05 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MTG09UI-4 | MEETINGS-1: the API's refusals carry a status and a short word (`rule` MH-0n on 422, `detail` `not_hostable` / `already_hosted` / `not_found` / `not_configured`). `TiloApiError` kept neither. | `TiloApiError` gets an optional trailing `refusal` (a short token only, never free text), read in `request()` for 4xx and 5xx; every existing constructor call is unchanged. The page words each refusal itself and never shows the server's text (it can name ids) | FR-MTG-09 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-COSTFAST-1 | COST-FAST-2: which skips go when an approach lists more than the room FR-INT-15 gap lines leave (5 minus the gaps)? | Keep the first `room` skips in the model's order and drop the tail, in code (`approaches.trim_skips`), with no repair call; PG-07/08/09 breaks still get their one repair. The repair prompt no longer carries a skips problem. | `packages/planning/src/tilo_planning/approaches.py` | 8 Oct |
| BQ-CHN18-1 | FR-CHN-18 (daily digest, PRD 11.4, 11.6): what action type, policy level, link format, and sending domain does the daily digest use? | Default: (a) `DailyDigestBuilder` requires explicit `base_url`, `keys` (`KeyRing`), and `nonces` (`NonceStore`), keeps `build()` state local, scopes `policy.applied` (L2/L3) events and outbox entries to the owner's `world_ids` and FYI mail to `user_id`, validates every feed field against its contract schema before rendering (reporting skipped lines as `"N actions not listed"`), and issues/redeems 24 h single-use HMAC-SHA256 Undo/Report links via `tilo_channels.links.LinkService` against an injected real clock. (b) Because a scheduled digest has no per-run instruction (`instruction=None`), `DigestService` checks `quiet_hours.should_notify("digest", ...)` (FR-HIL-13) before storing/emitting/enqueuing, stores the rendered text as an immutable artifact version first (OB-05), asks `DigestPolicyPort` (`decide` + required `policy_for`), builds full OB-01 evidence and checks `autopilot_gate.opens` before emitting `policy.applied` and enqueuing via `autopilot_state.enqueue_digest` (SEC-03), and deduplicates per UTC day (`digest:<user_id>:<YYYY-MM-DD>`). A dedicated transactional notification sender domain outside the user's Gmail connector is deferred until a system mail transport is configured. | FR-CHN-18, FR-CHN-07, FR-HIL-13, SEC-03, SEC-08, OB-01, OB-05 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-JOIN-1 | ADR-0024 amendment 4: the join was sketched as `GET /v1/calendar/{entry}/join`. Which id does the route take? | The meeting id: `GET /v1/meetings/{meeting_id}/join` (`meeting_join.schema.json`). A Tilo meeting is never a `calendar_entry` (the agenda cites it as `{kind: meeting, id}`), and the owner, world and host receipt all hang off the meeting. A synced Google copy of the hosted event carries no Join (MJ-10). | ADR-0024 am. 4, FR-CAL-01 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-JOIN-2 | ADR-0024 amendment 4: may attendees other than the owner (Tilo persons who were invited) join from Tilo's calendar too? | No: the owner only (per-owner scope, MJ-01, PE-05). Invitees join from Google's own invitation; their synced copy keeps no link (PE-13). Widening it needs a per-invitee check against the host event's attendee list and is the user's call. | ADR-0024 am. 4, PE-05 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-JOIN-3 | ADR-0024 amendment 4: how long may the browser hold the link, and how often may one person ask? | `expires_at` = server clock + 60 s (MJ-06); at most 10 join requests a minute per principal, counted before Google is asked (MJ-08). Constants in tilo-api, not config; the user may change either. | ADR-0024 am. 4, SEC-10 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-JOIN-4 | ADR-0024 amendment 4: which host receipt does the join read when a meeting's world holds more than one (a re-host after a restart)? | The latest by event `seq` whose bytes parse as a `meeting_event_receipt` of this meeting; Google's own `status` then decides (MH-20). Re-hosting is not offered today (409 `already_hosted`), so this is defensive. | ADR-0024 am. 4, MTG09-b | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-JOIN-5 | ADR-0024 amendment 4: should a join be recorded as an event or Trace row? | No: a join changes no world (HR-1), so it writes no event; the access log line has route, status and principal only, never the link (MJ-09). An audit event would need a registry entry and a data schema without the link; add it only if the user wants joins in the Trace. | ADR-0024 am. 4, HR-1 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-JOIN-6 | ADR-0024 amendment 4: the owner is an invitee of the hosted event, so their own FR-CAL-08 sync also caches a `google` copy of it next to the meeting row. Which one does the agenda show? | Both until the FR-CAL-01 agenda route merges them; default for that route: hide a `google` entry whose `source.ref` equals the `event_ref` of a host receipt of a meeting the same person owns, and keep the meeting row (the one that can be `joinable`). | ADR-0024 am. 4, FR-CAL-01, FR-CAL-08 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MTG11-1 | FR-MTG-11: which roles does the meeting bot ask for, and with what caps? | Two new roles in `config/models.yaml`, `models.nim.yaml`, `models.gemini.yaml` and `models.dev.yaml` (config, not a contract; `test_NFR_25` pins the role set and lists both): `meeting` (agenda analysis, JSON in and out, `max_input_tokens` 16000, `max_output_tokens` 2048, primary `flash-37`, fallback `lite-31`) and `meeting_fast` (one-sentence direct answers, 8000 in, 256 out, primary `lite-31`, fallback `flash-37`). The caps are defaults: the brain sends at most 12000 characters of transcript, and a reply is a sentence or two. Existing roles did not fit: `assistant` caps at 8000 in and 1000 out and is the assistant panel's. Prompts stay Python strings inside `meet-bot/bot/brain.py` for now (the prototype's own); moving them to `prompts/` is a follow-up, not part of FR-MTG-11. | FR-MTG-11, PL-01, NFR-25, EFF-03 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MTG11-2 | FR-MTG-11: which model id serves the bot's voice (speech output)? | Not verified (no network in the build). `config/voice.yaml` reuses the id `config/speech.yaml` already names for audio input, as a placeholder; whether it serves speech OUTPUT is the user's to confirm or replace in that one file before the first live meeting. The loader applies the PL-07 denylist to it. The Google voice is built only with the live switch, so nothing calls it until the user sets that and the project. | FR-MTG-11, FR-MTG-16, PL-07 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MTG11-3 | FR-MTG-11: how is audio OUTPUT (and a per-minute speech price) priced and metered? **Resolved by ADR-0029 (audio-output price shape, with the user-supplied Flash TTS prices: audio out $9/1M tokens, text in $0.50/1M).** Until that ADR's contract change merges and the price is wired, a live voice call is still not metered; the per-meeting caps and the live switch bound it. | It is not. `contracts/providers.schema.json` (ADR-0027 adds `audio_input` only) has no field for audio output tokens or a per-minute price, and `config/prices.yaml` has none, so a live voice call is not metered and `SpeechProvider.synthesize` returns no usage. needs contract change: an additive `audio_output` price (per million audio output tokens) beside `audio_input`, and a decision for per-minute vendors. Until then the cost of the voice is bounded only by the caps in `config/voice.yaml` (`max_chars` 400, `max_audio_bytes` 2400000) and by the user's own live switch. | FR-MTG-11, PL-06, EFF-03, ADR-0027 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MTG11-4 | FR-MTG-11: how do Google credentials reach the bot for a live meeting? | **Default: only a dedicated identity that holds no project role (ADR-0024 amendment 3(a)), or no live run. Never mount the Owner's gcloud ADC into the container, and do not run on the host while the browser has no sandbox.** The compose file passes only the user's own `LIVE`, `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT` and `GOOGLE_LOCATION` from the host shell; there is no key variable and no mounted credentials file (the provider layer uses application default credentials and refuses a service-account key). Risk, recorded: on the user's laptop the gcloud ADC is the project Owner's account, and the container runs Chromium with `--no-sandbox` behind a password-less VNC while it renders Meet pages other people control, so credentials inside it are one browser exploit from the whole project. An identity for the bot with only the Vertex AI user role on the one project, and its own ADC, is the user's to create before the first live meeting; until then no live run is made. A host run is not a fallback: `bot/browser.py` passes `--no-sandbox` to Chromium every time, so it would sit unsandboxed next to the Owner's gcloud ADC (worse than the container) until MTG09-c drops the flag outside the container. The image cannot be built or run in this build, so nothing here was exercised against Docker. | FR-MTG-11, SEC-15, EFF-01, ADR-0024 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MTG11-5 | FR-MTG-11: what does the bot use when the live switch is not set, and what does a developer override? | Offline fakes. `bot/providers.py` reads `config/models.dev.yaml` (profile `meeting-fake`, a deterministic `FakeModelProvider`, price 0), `config/speech.dev.yaml` and `config/voice.dev.yaml` (vendor `fake`) unless `LIVE` is `1` (then `models.yaml`, `speech.yaml`, `voice.yaml`). `TILO_MODELS_FILE`, `TILO_SPEECH_FILE`, `TILO_VOICE_FILE` and `TILO_REPO_ROOT` override each; `meet-bot/tests/conftest.py` clears all of them and the live switch for every test. A bot started by hand without the switch therefore answers with canned text and speaks a tone: honest, and free. The Google voice is not recorded for replay (audio is large and not a transcript): without the switch it raises `NotConfigured`. | FR-MTG-11, EFF-01 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MTG11-6 | FR-MTG-11: what was removed from meet-bot, and what was kept as history? | Removed: the third-party streaming speech client, `stt-probe`, keyterms, `AudioGate` and `SpeakerNames` (streaming-only helpers, rebuilt with FR-MTG-16 if needed), the third-party TTS client, `GeminiLLM` and the model-list lookup, the Live probe (`live_probe.py`, it spoke a vendor protocol) and the genai smoke test, the vendor key plumbing and the log redactor that existed only for those keys (the bot now holds no secret). Replaced by short notes: `docs/ARCHITECT_BRIEF.md` and `docs/HANDOFF-2026-10-02.md` (they compared and named removed vendors; originals on `chore/meet-bot-baseline`, c2991182). `docs/VOICE_ARCHITECTURE.md` lost its third-party option rows and vendor facts and says so at the top. One test timing bound in `test_llm_fast.py` was loosened from +0.05 s to +0.5 s (it flaked on a starved laptop; the assertion that the retry is immediate stays). | FR-MTG-11, FR-MTG-16 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MTG11-7 | FR-MTG-11: how do the meet-bot tests run, and what changed for Docker? | Root `pyproject.toml` gets `pythonpath = ["meet-bot"]`, so `just test` (root pytest) collects `meet-bot/tests` offline with the repo venv, about 220 tests; `just meet-bot-test` runs only them. The image build context moves from `meet-bot/` to the repository root (compose `context: ..`, `docker/Dockerfile.dockerignore` whitelists exactly what is copied) so the image carries `packages/providers`, `packages/contracts-py`, `config/` and `prompts/` under `/opt/tilo` (`TILO_REPO_ROOT`, `PYTHONPATH`). A static test checks the Dockerfile copies what the adapter imports and the requirements list no vendor package. `meet-bot` is not added to the `just typecheck` mypy line: `mypy meet-bot/bot` still reports 25 errors, almost all in prototype code that predates this ticket (`brain.py`, `session.py`, `captions.py`); `providers.py` and `llm_fast.py` are clean. | FR-MTG-11, PL-01 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MTG11-8 | FR-MTG-11: how does the bot's speech call relate to the `speech` role of ADR-0027? | ADR-0027 (Proposed) adds a `speech` role; it is not in `config/models*.yaml` on this branch, so the bot (like the FR-MTG-17 upload) reads `config/speech.yaml`. When the role lands, `bot/providers.py` is the one place to switch. ADR-0027 explicitly leaves Cloud TTS and Gemini Live out of that role: the voice therefore has its own config (`config/voice.yaml`) and the additive `SpeechProvider.synthesize`, an interface method (not a contract). | FR-MTG-11, ADR-0027, BQ-MTG17-3 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MTG11-9 | FR-MTG-11: are SEC-16 spend caps wired for the meeting bot, and what bounds one meeting? | No. The bot's model, speech and voice calls run through `bot/providers.py` with the Router's `budget=None`, outside the SEC-16 per-user and per-day spend caps (those are the services' own, keyed by a signed-in user the bot does not have), and anyone in a call can make the bot ask a question (one fast model call per question). Default: a per-meeting count cap in config, `config/meeting.yaml` (`max_model_calls` 200, `max_speech_calls` 400; `tilo_providers.meeting`), counted per bot process (one process is one meeting) and checked before every request; at the cap the bot logs one warning and degrades (no more answers or analysis, no cloud speech, the local voice speaks, the transcript keeps being written). A count is not money: the voice has no price (BQ-MTG11-3). Wiring the bot into the SEC-16 meter, with the bot's account as the principal, is a follow-up. The image requirements pin the provider layer's direct dependencies to `uv.lock` (a test compares them); transitive dependencies are not pinned. | FR-MTG-11, SEC-16, EFF-03 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MTG11-10 | FR-MTG-11: known test and logging defects left in meet-bot (not fixed here). | (1) `bot.main.main()` calls `setup_logging()`, which replaces the root log handlers; when a test calls `main()` inside the root pytest process, later tests log "I/O operation on closed file". The new tests patch `setup_logging` out; `main()` should not rewire the root logger when imported (move it under `__main__`). (2) Four timing tests fail on a laptop at 100% CPU: the hanging-attempt and instant-retry bounds in `test_llm_fast.py` and the `test_VA_03` wait bounds in `test_ui.py`; they only guard against hangs, so their bounds were loosened to about 3x (the assertion that the retry is immediate stays). (3) The base image `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright/python:v1.52.0-noble` is pinned by tag, not by digest; pin the digest at the first build. | FR-MTG-11, NFR-26 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MTG11-11 | FR-MTG-11: baseline meeting-bot behaviour that MTG09-c (ADR-0024 amendment 3) still has to change. | Left as the prototype has them, listed so none is forgotten: `x11vnc -nopw` (VNC without a password, 3(b)), Chromium `--no-sandbox` (3(d)), the `--auto-admit` option and `ADMIT_ALLOWED_NAMES` (3(c); the bot-hosted meeting never uses them), `start` printing `MEETING_URL` to stdout and being enabled for the persistent profile (3(d)), and no `.claude/hooks/guard.py` deny for `docker run`/`exec`/`volume` or for `bot.main` run by an agent (3(e); guard.py is protected, so this is reported, not changed here), and `meet-bot/README.md` still tells the user to open http://localhost:6080 (noVNC) for debugging, which ADR-0024 3(b) restricts to the login (MTG09-c rewrites those README passages). `start`/`join` now refuse the dev fakes without `LIVE=1`, and the bot refuses `TILO_RECORD`; neither replaces those items. | FR-MTG-11, FR-MTG-09, ADR-0024 | 2026-10-08 |

| BQ-ADR32-3 | ADR-0032 T3 (connectors routes): (1) nothing schedules `retry_pending_revokes` and the contract has no route for it. (2) Connectors keeps no outbox state between requests, so "disconnect abandons pending sends" is tilo-api's job (decision 7); in connectors a disconnect only marks the user as closing while it runs, so a send that has not fetched its token yet is `refused`, not retried. (3) A record whose sealed `connected_at` cannot be authenticated (tampered, or sealed before T2) fails closed as a retryable TokenError, not `refused`; HB-06 itself (`not_before` in `access_token_for_adapter`) is `refused`. (4) `may_have_sent` after `retry` is true when any request left for Google during the call, else false. (5) A malformed Firestore token document reads as `expired` (present but unreadable), so a disconnect clears it and a new consent replaces it. | Default: (1) `HostRuntime.retry_pending_revokes(user_id)` is the hook, documented in `services/connectors/CLAUDE.md`; a Cloud Scheduler route needs a contract addition (a fourth route) in T6. (2)-(5) as stated. `uv.lock` now lists `google-auth` for connectors (the T2 kms/firestore lock entries were already there). | ADR-0032 T3, HB-01..HB-06, FR-MTG-09 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-ADR32-4 | ADR-0032 T3 security round 1 notes, not fixed: (1) no clock-skew margin on the HB-06 comparison between tilo-api's `authz.at` and connectors' `connected_at` (two clocks); (2) `status` does not authenticate the record (a store read only, by design); (3) the retry-revokes trigger is still only the hook (BQ-ADR32-3). A certificate outage answers 502 with the fixed `not_configured` body, which tilo-api (T4) must treat as retryable, unlike 503. | Default: leave as is; revisit with T4/T6. | ADR-0032 T3, HB-06 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-ADR32-5 | ADR-0032 T4 (tilo-api client): (1) A 502 or any 5xx, a timeout, a cut connection, a 200 that breaks its contract, and a 503 without the fixed `not_configured` body (a platform 503) are all ambiguous retries, not final: the send may have reached Google, and MH-13 stops a second invitation. Only 401, 403, 422 and connectors' own `not_configured` 503 are `SideEffectRefused`. (2) A token that cannot be minted is a clean retry (nothing was sent). (3) A `sent` receipt that fails HB-07, or that the run's receipt store refuses, is `PossiblySent` (failed with an alert): the invitation is at Google. (4) The stub sends the entry in its enqueued state `queued` (the outbox hands over `sending`; the contract takes `queued`). (5) A clean retry raises a private `NotSent(AdapterError)` with status 409, because the outbox reads a 4xx status as nothing sent and `tilo_connectors.tokens` errors may not be imported. (6) The host route's preflight `status` fails only on a call that gives no answer (503 `not_configured`); a `none` or `expired` owner still reaches the gate (BQ-ADR32-1). (7) `tilo-contracts` reaches tilo-api through tilo-connectors and tilo-harness; not added to its pyproject (that needs `uv lock`). (8) The client has no `disconnect` yet: T6 adds it with the owner's routes. Security round 1: (9) the host run never abandons an entry whose retry state is ambiguous or that was alerted: it stays unfailed with exactly one `alert.raised` (ids only, `HostRun._record_alert`), and the world then ends `world.failed` with the reason "the invitation may have gone out; check the host calendar" (the world stops, and says it cannot tell). (10) `on_connection_event` checks ambiguity and abandons in two steps: the connectors store has no public atomic form, and this ADR does not touch its internals; the window is one lease. (11) An alert part in a send answer is recorded by the run too. (12) The client's send is `send_invitation`, NOT in `SIDE_EFFECT_METHOD_NAMES` (that scan allows registered names only in outbox.py, so registering it failed test_SEC_03); a local AST test pins that only the stub calls it and the stub verifies the outbox Grant first. Follow-up (Opus): a per-name allowlist in the SEC-03 scan across the repo, or make `send_invitation` take and verify the Grant, would also catch `getattr` and `_post("send")` forms. (13) T3 residual left: connectors does not ULID-check an `alert_id` it emits (tilo-api's contract validation rejects a malformed one). | Default: as stated. | ADR-0032 T4, HB-07, FR-MTG-09 | 2026-10-09 |
| BQ-ADR32a-1 | ADR-0032 addendum 1: does a person's connection view (`POST /internal/connections/views`, feeding `GET /v1/connectors`) list the bot account's `meeting_host` grant? | Default (architect, the user may override): yes, but only to the person who connected it. The record is keyed by that person's user id (ADR-0024: only they host with it), and HB-08 reads only the request's user, so nobody else sees it, not even the org's Owner. It shows its one scope, its state and `connected_at`, never the bot account's address. Hiding it would show a connected host account as `available` and hide an expiry the person must fix before hosting. | docs/adr/0032a-connection-views.md decision 5; ADR-0024 decision 5; FR-ACC-04, FR-MTG-09 | 2026-10-09 |
| BQ-ADR32a-2 | ADR-0032 addendum 1: where does FR-ACC-04's "warning 3 days before a sign-in expires" get its expiry, now that the connection view carries no `expires_at`? | Default (architect): the stored `expires_at` is the access token's (about an hour, refreshed silently), so it is not sent. tilo-api derives the sign-in expiry as `connected_at` plus 7 days, the testing-mode sign-in lifetime in PRD 11.5, as one constant, and warns 3 days ahead. A re-consent keeps `connected_at`, so the warning can come early, never late. A null `connected_at` gives no warning. `GET /v1/connectors`' row `expires_at` becomes that derived instant or null, and #740's tests re-pin it when T4b moves their override to `deps.get_connection_views`. The constant goes once the OAuth app is verified and its sign-ins no longer expire. | docs/adr/0032a-connection-views.md decision 7; PRD 11.5; FR-ACC-04; BQ-LIVEGAPS-1 | 2026-10-09 |
| BQ-ADR32a-3 | ADR-0032a T4b: how #740 (`feat/LIVE-GAPS-1b`, `GET /v1/connectors`) reads the new route, and what it must drop. tilo-api now has `deps.get_connection_views_rpc()`: 503 `not_configured` unless `TILO_CONNECTORS_URL` builds the client, else a callable `views(user_id: str) -> list[MeetingHostRpcConnectionView] | None` (`from tilo_contracts import MeetingHostRpcConnectionView`; fields `connector`, `state` connected|expired, `scopes`, `connected_at`; HB-09 order and uniqueness already checked; None = unknown for any failure, never raises, 10 s). | Default: not wireable with one line without touching #740, because its seam is the per-connector `.view(user_id, connector) -> (state, scopes, expires_at)` and `deps.get_connection_views` has no argument-free callable shape. The EVALS lane makes `connectors_list._optional_views` depend on `deps.get_connection_views_rpc` (one provider swap) and builds each row from one `views(principal.user_id)` call: `None` = every row `available`, `state_known` false; a connector with no view = `available`, known; `connected` = `connected`; `expired` = `attention`. It also (1) re-pins the row `expires_at` to the derived sign-in expiry or null (BQ-ADR32a-2) and (2) drops `expires_at` from `TokenBroker.connection_view` (or the method), and `via TokenBroker.connection_view` from `services/api/CLAUDE.md`. Connectors side: `TokenBroker.display_records(user_id)` is new in this branch (a store read), so #740's `connection_view` edit in `tokens.py` is a separate hunk. | FR-CON-02, FR-ACC-03, FR-ACC-04, FR-MTG-09, SEC-02 | 2026-10-09 |
| BQ-ADR32-T6-1 | ADR-0032 T6: the ADR names no route paths, and the status contract has no `connected_at`. Default: `GET /v1/meeting-host/connection` answers `{state, scope_ok}` only (the UI reads `connected_at` from `GET /v1/connectors` views), and `POST /v1/meeting-host/disconnect` answers `{disconnected, revoked_at_provider}`. A disconnect answer's `alert.raised` parts make the answer carry `revoke_pending: true` (nothing stored: `revoked_at_provider` false and `revoke_pending` false; a clean revoke: `revoked_at_provider` true) and are logged by alert id (there is no world to record them in). Nothing schedules a retry of a kept revoke, here or in connectors: the person must remove Tilo under Google's Third-party access (ADR-0024 runbook step 2); UI copy should read "not confirmed", not "failed". Abandon-first is per entry, not one atomic step (the outbox store has no batch abandon); a leased or ambiguous entry stays, and connectors' HB-06 refuses its stale approval. A failed outbox read refuses the disconnect (503) before the call. Abandon-first covers only the tilo-api instance that served the disconnect (the outbox store is per process); other instances rely on HB-06. The store's \`_abandon\` now refuses an ambiguous entry under its own lock. Google revokes the whole (account, OAuth client) grant, so two users on one bot account, or a person connecting meeting_host with their own Google account, lose more than one connection (ADR-0024 decision 5, not T6 code). The disconnect POST needs the people stores for its write limit (\`TILO_PEOPLE_STORE=firestore\`, else 503 \`not_configured\`); the status GET has no per-principal limit (no limiter exists that avoids the people stores). | FR-MTG-09 | 2026-10-09 |

I think it's fair" (both reported by the supervisor from the user's messages) -> `uid_lifetime_minor` 50000 (INR 500); `run_minor` 15000 (INR 150, user-approved: INR 500 per run would let one run spend a person's whole allowance, INR 150 still covers the deep GATE-job estimate of INR 60-140); `global_day_hard_minor` stays 500000 (INR 5,000); supersedes BQ-SEC16-3's figures for run and lifetime | SEC-16; FR-MTR-04 | 2026-10-09 |
| BQ-LW6-1 | LW-06: what are the hard caps on media parts, and where are they set? ADR-0029 names none. | Constants in `tilo_providers.media`: 10 parts a request (FR-INT-01), 10 MiB a part, 14 MiB a request in total (inline data rides base64, under Gemini's 20 MB request limit), 100 PDF pages. A config field would need a contract change. Over any cap the request is refused with a `RequestError` (a `RouterRequestError` from the router), never trimmed. | LW-06; ADR-0029 decision 6 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-LW6-2 | LW-06: how does the router bound the tokens of a PDF or of audio and video before sending, when the page count and duration are not what the caller says? | PDF: the pages are measured by code in a child process (see BQ-LW6-11) that walks the page tree with pypdf (every node without /Kids is a page, so object streams, escaped names and a false /Count change nothing; a node budget cuts off cyclic or fanned-out trees); an unreadable, encrypted, slow or oversize PDF, or one over 100 pages, is refused, and a stated `pages` must equal the measured count. Audio and video: duration is never taken from a caller or a header. A plain PCM WAV is measured exactly (every byte after its data header over its byte rate) once its header agrees with the file (PCM, 1 to 8 channels, 8 to 192 kHz, block align and byte rate consistent, data chunk not larger than the file) and has no chunk ambiguity: more than one fmt or data chunk, a fmt after the data, an odd-sized chunk before the data, a chunk past the end of the file or trailing bytes that are no chunk make it unmeasured, because decoders resolve those differently (ffmpeg reads the first fmt). Every other audio or video part (mp3, ogg, flac, mp4, webm, an ambiguous or lying WAV) is assumed to last the provider's documented maximum, config-free constants: `UNMEASURED_AUDIO_SECONDS` 34,200 (9.5 hours, Gemini audio understanding) and `UNMEASURED_VIDEO_SECONDS` 3,600 (one hour, the length Gemini documents for video with audio; an assumption to confirm on the first live run). That is 855,000 audio tokens at 25 a second and 929,000 video tokens at 258, so any capped role (the Director's 100,000 included) refuses such a part with `TokenCapExceededError`, and an uncapped role is priced at the same figure before the call (PL-03). Size says nothing about the length of DTX Opus, FLAC silence or a 1 fps static video, which is why no size-based bound is used. Unmeasured audio and video are therefore usable only by roles without a token cap, until LW-18 adds a measuring decoder. The estimate is never billed; the reported usage is. | LW-06; ADR-0029 decision 6 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-LW6-3 | LW-06: which media types are accepted? FR-INT-02 names PDF, PNG and JPEG, which are the only types `SafetyProvider.check` can screen. | The exact allow-list is `application/pdf`, `image/png`, `image/jpeg`, `image/webp`, `audio/wav`, `audio/mpeg`, `audio/flac`, `audio/ogg`, `video/mp4` and `video/webm`, each matched to its leading bytes; no aliases, case folding or parameters. Screening is the caller's step, not the provider's: LW-12 (documents and images) and LW-18 (audio, video) must screen before building a part, and audio, webp and video have no native screen yet. | LW-06; FR-INT-02 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-LW6-4 | LW-06: when a profile cannot take a media part (no `media_tokens`, an image-only adapter given a PDF, an audio part with no `audio_input` price), does the router refuse the call or fall back? | Fall back, per the ticket: the candidate is recorded as an attempt with `MissingCapabilityError` and the reason on the next attempt reads "fallback after MissingCapabilityError", and nothing is sent to it. If no candidate can take the part the run ends in `RouterExhaustedError`. A bad part (type, bytes, sha256, size, URI) is a `RouterRequestError` with no attempt. | LW-06; ADR-0029 decision 6 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-LW6-5 | LW-06 (ADR-0029 decision 1.2): how is the audio share of the input tokens reported and charged? The Trace event has no audio field and the contracts are frozen. | The google adapter reads `usage_metadata.prompt_tokens_details` and reports `usage["audio_tokens"]` (a part of `input_tokens`), absent when the breakdown is missing or unusable. The router charges it at `audio_input`, and charges the pre-flight audio estimate when a request has audio parts and the reply reports none (never understated); a request with no audio part has none. `RouterAttempt.tokens_audio` carries it; EV-16 does not, so the Trace is unchanged. `PriceBook` prices `Usage.tokens_audio` the same way and refuses audio on a profile with no `audio_input`. | LW-06; ADR-0029 decision 1 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-LW6-6 | LW-06: may a batch request (EFF-05) carry media parts? The batch wire holds the prompt only. | No: `BatchRequest` refuses a request with parts, since dropping a file silently would answer a different question. Media goes through the live path. | LW-06; EFF-05 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-LW6-7 | LW-06 (security review): a video part also carries an audio track billed at the audio rate; is it priced? | Not yet: a video part is estimated at `video_per_second` only and `audio_tokens` of a video reply count only if the reply reports them. LW-18 (audio and video intake) decides, with a decoder. | LW-06; ADR-0029 decision 1 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-LW6-8 | LW-06 (security review): which GCS buckets may a URI part name once ADR-0018 T8 enables them? | None: a URI part is refused today. T8 must add a bucket allow-list (Tilo's own buckets only, owner-scoped objects) and a reader that re-hashes the object before the part is sent. | LW-06; ADR-0018 T8 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-LW6-9 | LW-06 (security review): media is untrusted, so should a unit that sees a media part get side-effect tools (SEC-05)? | The provider layer does not decide that. LW-12 and LW-18 must screen every part (`SafetyProvider.check`) before building it and the tool runner (LW-07) must treat a unit that read media like one that read fetched content: no side-effect tool. | LW-06; SEC-05 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-LW6-10 | LW-06 (security review): are 25 audio and 258 video tokens a second really upper bounds for every profile? | Taken from ADR-0029 decision 6 as stated. The first live run (the user's `LIVE=1`) must compare the reported usage with the estimate, and the numbers in `config/models.yaml` change by a pull request if any reply exceeds them. | LW-06; ADR-0029 decision 6 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-LW6-11 | LW-06 (security review): how is counting the pages of an untrusted PDF kept from burning the service's CPU or memory? | `pdf_pages` runs `tilo_providers._pdf_pages_child` as a child process (`python -I -m`, almost no environment, the file on stdin) under a 5 second wall clock that includes start-up, and kills it on overrun, refusing the part; at most 2 children run at once and the rest wait. The child also ends itself after 10 seconds (a watchdog, so an orphan cannot run for hours), limits its address space to 1 GiB where the platform has RLIMIT_AS (POSIX only, not exercised on the Windows development machine), sets pypdf's decode limits to 1,000,000 bytes (any object stream, xref stream or filter that inflates past it is refused), logs nothing and prints one line. The parent never imports pypdf. A verdict on the file (counted, too many pages, unreadable) is cached by its sha256 (32 entries); a timeout, a crash or a failure to start is not a verdict and is tried again next time. On Windows memory has no OS limit: it is bounded by the decode limits and the 10 MiB part cap, and the worst case measured was about 100 MB per concurrent child, so about 200 MB for the two. A PDF that pypdf can only read by rebuilding its xref is still accepted (refusing it would refuse many real downloaded papers); the time and decode limits bound that path. Each uncached PDF costs one process start (about 0.3 s). | LW-06; SEC-09 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MEM30-1 | ADR-0030 (Proposed): what is the embedding profile, and what does it cost? | Decided 8 Oct 2026: user approved ADR-0030 with this default. Needs a user-confirmed price from Google's page (PL-06): nothing is invented. Until the user gives it, only the dev fake (price 0, no paid call) ships, and the live profile stays out of `config/models.yaml`, because the loader refuses an unpriced profile while `prices.yaml` is `status: set`. If the price is per 1,000 characters rather than per 1M tokens, it needs ADR-0029's per-unit price shape. | ADR-0030 d5, PL-06 | 2026-10-08 |

MEM30-d checks the API's delete behaviour (synchronous or a long-running operation) against the live service with the user's `LIVE=1`. | ADR-0030 d2, FR-MEM-09 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MEM30-4 | ADR-0030: does the Firestore emulator answer vector (`find_nearest`) queries? | Unconfirmed on 8 Oct (the docs do not say). MEM30-b checks it. If it does not, the store's tests run the in-memory exact-KNN fake behind the same interface, plus one emulator test of the pre-filtered query's shape, and the real index is checked once live by the user. | ADR-0030 d3, ADR-0003 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MEM30-5 | ADR-0030: the numbers. `k` 5, `offer_max` 3, dimensions 768, `max_distance` none, lookup cap 0.5 credits, one lookup per run, batch 64, query-vector cache 7 days, recall@5 at least 0.8. | These are the defaults in `config/memory.yaml` (MEM30-b) and the MEM30-e eval. `max_distance` stays unset until the recall eval on recorded vectors suggests a value. | BQ-MEM30-6 | ADR-0030: does the "Start from one of these?" offer count against the run's question cap? | Yes: it is one question card with `safe_default` "Start fresh". When the cap is already used up, no offer is made and the run plans as today. | ADR-0030 d4, FR-INT-04 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MEM30-7 | ADR-0030: does starting from an earlier deliverable count as credits saved? | No, unless the item replaces a unit's work as it is and the fit check and the Verifier accept it (PRD 13.1). Only then is a `memory.hit` emitted. A revised starting point saves nothing on the meter, so savings are never overstated. | ADR-0030 d4, PRD 14.3 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MEM30-8 | ADR-0030: may a teammate's past deliverables be offered (for example from a world shared with the team)? | No: the owner pre-filter is per person. Team-shared memory needs a team scope on index entries and its own review. | ADR-0030 d3, PE-05 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MEM30-9 | ADR-0030: which model runs the fit check? | A new config role `memory_fit` on the cheapest profile, with a dearer fallback (NFR-25), at most 200 output tokens and a closed `{"fits": [ids]}` answer checked in code. MEM30-c adds the role and updates the role-set pin test. | ADR-0030 d4, PRD 13.1, EFF-03 | 2026-10-08 |

| BQ-ADR32-1 | MTG-WIRE-1 (0bde0544) built the meeting-host `TokenBroker` (Cloud KMS, Firestore token store, OAuth client secret) inside tilo-api, but PRD 6.5, 12.1, 16.1 rule 2 and SEC-02 let only tilo-connectors decrypt OAuth tokens, and BQ-MTG09-21 never said where the broker runs. Where does it live, and how does tilo-api reach it? | **Proposed in ADR-0032, needs the user's approval; until then nothing is wired and MTG-WIRE-1 is not merged as it is.** The broker, token store, Google transport and the adapter's token use stay in tilo-connectors. tilo-api calls three internal routes (`send`, `status`, `disconnect`) with its own Google ID token, and each route accepts the exact tilo-api account only (HB-01). No token and no Meet link crosses. Supersedes BQ-MTG09-21's two seams. Architect's defaults inside the ADR, overridable: `connection.expired` abandons unsent `meeting_host` entries like `connection.revoked` (amends BQ-MTG09-23); a grant made after the approval never sends it (HB-06); connectors runs one instance until CAS lands (CON-D9's own default); a failed seam build answers 503 for 30 s. Open and not decided here: (1) connecting the bot account on a deployed stack (the consent code exchange) has no route yet and is a separate ticket; (2) ADR-0024 amendment 4's join read needs a fourth route whose answer is the Meet link, reserved here for that ticket; (3) a not-connected owner still learns it only after the gate; (4) channels' mail reader has no path to connectors (no VPC egress, no invoker), so its RPC client is a separate decision | FR-MTG-09; SEC-02; PRD 6.5, 12.1, 16.1; CON-D9 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-FREETEXT-1 | FR-INT-04 (live run 8 Oct): a free-text deliverables answer with no format ("A detailed end to end curriculum, plan, topics, old papers, mcqs") was refused and the person's wishes were lost. | Default applied (supervisor ruling, PRD silent): an answer of 3+ words naming no format becomes the deliverable's title (clipped to 120) in the default format markdown, and the full text (clipped to 300) is kept as a constraint "Deliverable wishes: ..."; a short or junk answer ("banana", "1 and 2") and a mixed one ("a plan as a slide deck") still ask again for the format. | Stated in the issue; PRD 7.1 has no default format. |
| BQ-DECLINE-1 | FR-INT-06/08: the PRD does not say what happens when a person declines every proposed approach with a reason. | Default applied: the live terminal run revises ONCE: the reason joins the brief's constraints ("Must address: ..."), the old approaches are dropped and the Director proposes a new set; a second decline, or a decline with no reason, ends the run as declined. | Stated in the issue; no PRD text. |

Root cause of the hang is unconfirmed (the 8 no-usage calls are exactly the failed ones, so no usage-parsing bug); the next live run's class names will say. | Stated in the issue; no PRD text. |
| BQ-CAL07B-3 | FR-CAL-07 (b) ticket 2: what does a mail need to count as a "move this meeting" request? | Default applied: rules only (`tilo_channels.reschedule_intent.detect_move_request`, word lists in `config/move_request.yaml`, English): a move, cancel-and-rebook or availability phrase in the subject (not a Re:/Fwd: subject) or the unquoted body (quoted `>` lines, attribution lines and forwarded or original-message blocks are cut; only the first 4,096 characters after one NFKC pass are scanned), with no negating or conditional word (not, never, no need to, if, unless, whether, no longer, ignore, disregard, can't ...) in the six words before it in the same clause. An availability phrase alone (a new meeting) is not a request. Undecided mail is None: there is no model fallback in this ticket (the cost and injection surface are not worth it before the draft ticket needs it). The sender is never read: who may reschedule is ticket 3/4 and T3. The marker is a label, never authority: ticket 3 must check that the sender is a participant of the resolved meeting and keep every send behind T3. Known false positives (a negation more than six words back, booking footers) are left to that gate. | FR-CAL-07 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-CAL07B-4 | FR-CAL-07 (b) ticket 2: which fields does the detector return, and does triage store them? | Default applied: `{"intent": "move_meeting", "kind", "constraint", "ref_hints"}`; constraint holds only code-computed `day` (via `tilo_people.dates.resolve_due` against the mail Date and the caller's `time_zone`, default UTC; ambiguous phrases such as "next week" give no day), `earliest_time` / `latest_time` (HH:MM after or before markers) and `duration_min` (5 to 480, not a shift like "by 30 minutes"); ref_hints holds the cleaned subject (max 120 characters), and `ics_uid` / `thread_id` only when they match a strict id alphabet. Triage stores only `{"kind", "constraint"}` as an internal `move_request` marker on a `needs_reply` result (no subject, no mail text, no contract or event change); ticket 3 re-runs the detector on the mail it reads for `ref_hints`. Triage has no person time zone, so it uses UTC; ticket 3 should call the detector with the person's zone. | FR-CAL-07 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-COSTFAST-8 | COST-FAST-1b (answers the open part of BQ-COSTFAST-7): how does Quick spend reach `GET /costs`? | New write seam `deps.get_ledger_writer()` (`append(LedgerEntry)`, `tilo_api/quick_ledger.py`), unwired and failing closed (503) like `get_ledger`, so deployed behaviour is unchanged (replay-only) until the Treasury is wired; `MemoryLedger` is the dev and test implementation, wired by dependency override, and #650 DevLedger can implement `append` the same way. Each person has one pseudo run `r_quick_<24 hex of sha256(uid)>` (no world, event or task); each billed Quick ask (including truncated, classifier-then-failed answer and whitespace-only reply) appends ONE `commit` entry (step `quick`, org `deps.ORG_ID`, profile from the last answering call) for exactly the credits the FR-PA-19 allowance commit took; rules offers and unsent refusals write nothing; an Idempotency-Key replay writes nothing. `GET /costs` adds the caller own run only (label: task_type `quick_answer`, run_id prefix `r_quick_`, tier null); `/worlds/{id}/costs` never has it. No contract field added. Decision (the answer to BQ-COSTFAST-7 for the user): best effort, not fail-closed: the allowance and SEC-16 holds stay authoritative, a ledger write failure is logged by exception type only and the served answer is unchanged; there is no retry queue yet, so an unwired or failing Treasury under-reports until it is wired (follow-up: queue/retry once the Treasury store exists). Erasure (FR-MEM-09): `MemoryLedger.erase_quick(uid)` deletes the run; the Treasury has no erase path, so a follow-up must add one for the real store. | PRD 14.1, 14.5; FR-MTR-01, FR-MTR-10, FR-MEM-09, FR-PA-19; test_COST_FAST_1b_ledger.py | 2026-10-08 |

Tokens are sealed by the broker, stored at `token_grants/{user}__{connector}`; the bot grant is its own `meeting_host` record. `google-cloud-kms` is imported lazily and is not yet a declared dependency (add it with the deploy lockfile change). | FR-MTG-09, ADR-0024 5 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-NOTIF-1 | NOTIF-1: what does `GET /v1/notifications` return? | Default: `{"notifications": [{id, kind "needs"\|"done", created_at, world_id, title, card_id, artifact_id}], "count"}`, newest first, `limit` 1 to 100 (default 20), owner only (anyone else gets an empty bell). "needs" is an open card (`id` `c:<card id>`), "done" an `artifact.created` or `data.transfer` event in the owner's worlds (`id` `e:<event id>`). Titles are fixed text per source: no address, link, card text or event summary. Read state, a "warn" kind (connection expiry) and a world title are not included; the Park app maps `go` from `world_id` and `card_id`. | FR-HIL-01, FR-MTG-09, PE-05 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-ADR27-5 | ADR27-WIRING (FR-ACC-02): is there a route to save a per-skill tool level? | Default: no. A skill's `tool_access` is part of the version-pinned skill (ADR-0027 decision 3), so changing it is a new skill version and there is no `PUT` for it. The run gate reads it from the skill registry (`LoadedSkill.tool_access`, the lowest declared value across the unit's skills; unknown skill: the team level applies, logged by id; a value that is not a level counts as off). The Skills page picker stays a sample until the skill-proposal flow (FR-AGT-15) can propose a new version. With no team store wired, nothing changes (a skill alone never raises or lowers). | FR-ACC-02, ADR-0027, BQ-ADR27-4 | 2026-10-08 |

Dev mode wires it. Wired with the Desk. | FR-TEAM-04, FR-ACC-02, ADR-0027 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-ADR27-7 | ADR27-WIRING: what order do `team_rule.changed` events show in when two Owners write at once? | Default: append order per Owner, not state order (B's event may show before A's). Each event carries its own from and to. | FR-TEAM-04, FR-ACC-02, ADR-0027 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-ADR27-8 | ADR27-WIRING: skill `tool_access` restrictions are dormant while no team store is configured (`tool_check` returns allow with no team state). | Default: accepted; every deployed and dev install has a team store, and a skill alone never raises a level. | FR-TEAM-04, FR-ACC-02, ADR-0027 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-ADR27-9 | ADR27-WIRING: can a team rule change still end up unlogged? | Default: yes, in two narrow cases: the event store and the undo both fail, or a crash falls between the compare-and-set and the append. Not built (kept simple): one same-part-id retry before the undo, plus a pending-event marker reconciled by the next write. The undo itself only runs while the store version is still the one the request wrote. | FR-TEAM-04, FR-ACC-02, ADR-0027 | 2026-10-08 |

| BQ-ONBOARD-1 | ONBOARD-1 (user-requested, no PRD requirement): what does the first-run welcome and walkthrough of the Park app do, and when does it show? | Scope as the user asked: (a) a welcome dialog on a person's first visit (what Tilo does in three steps: describe, the Director plans, agent teams run it and you approve); (b) a skippable coach-mark tour over the real nav (Home intake box, Worlds, Skills, Ledger, Agenda, Team, notifications bell, account menu) with Back/Next/Skip, Esc to skip, arrow keys, Tab kept inside, focus moved in and returned, a polite step announcement and the target's aria-describedby; (c) shown once per person (`localStorage` key `tilo.onboarding.v1.<google:email, anonymous or demo>`, every access in try/catch; a blocked store means it may show again, nothing breaks) and replayable from the account menu ("Take the tour"). Defaults chosen: the welcome is NOT shown to unit tests (MODE=test) or automated browsers (`navigator.webdriver`) unless the address has `?onboarding=1`, so no other spec meets an overlay it did not ask for (replay always works); the tour has no filled primary button (Next/Back/Skip are secondary) so each page keeps its one UX-1 primary, the welcome holds its own; nothing moves (no transition, animation or smooth scroll; reduced motion has nothing to turn off, HR-1) and no copy says a run happened. Optional last step "A sample project" (`Open the sample project` to /world/<id>) only when `GET /worlds` returns an item with `sample: true` (STARTER-1); no such item, no API (fixture world) or a failed call leaves it out and the tour is otherwise identical. Wiring: Root loads `src/onboarding/register.ts` (own chunk) on Park addresses; it registers with the seam `park/shell/tour.ts`, which ParkApp (draws the layer) and AppShell (account menu row) read. | ONBOARD-1, UX-1, UX-7, HR-1..HR-7 | 2026-10-08 |

| BQ-DEMO-EPH-1 | DEMO-EPHEMERAL-1 (user decision 9 Oct: the sign-in card says "Nothing you do is saved"). An anonymous demo session (`Principal.demo_session`, the verified token's uid is the only bucket) writes nothing durable: `deps.get_store`, `get_event_writer`, `get_artifact_store`, `get_redactor`, `get_run_archive`, `get_people_stores`, `intake.get_store` (uploads) and, in `runs.run_seams`, the outbox and (empty) connector adapters give it a per-session in-memory bundle (`tilo_api.ephemeral`) with its own memory, policies, cards and run; no Firestore, no raw archive or artifact backend, no process-wide outbox, no connector send (T3 still needs a person). Dropped after 30 min idle (swept on every access, no thread) or on `Registry.drop`. Caps, chosen so a normal demo (about 200 items, one or two runs) fits with room and 100 sessions cannot exhaust a small instance: 100 sessions (503 `demo_capacity`), 1000 items and 8 MB per session (429), 20000 items and 100 MB in total (503); fail closed, never evict another live session, never spill to Firestore. A request whose principal cannot be resolved is the fixed sign-in 401, never a durable store. Schedules: a demo's create is 403 `demo_schedules_unavailable` (a schedule outlives the session); a record already stored with `demo` still ticks in replay. NOT covered: Firebase Auth keeps the anonymous account itself; the access/declaration store and App Check state are not user data and stay; a session lives on one Cloud Run instance: ACCEPTED (supervisor ruling) - a demo landing on another instance starts empty and nothing is stored, so the promise holds; CL-REACH adds `--session-affinity` to tilo-api at the next deploy; process-wide in-memory things keyed by uid (spend allowance, decision cache, rate limiter) are not per-session and die with the process; structured logs carry ids (world, run) but no demo text; the artifact store has no screener and the redactor is the dev stand-in (FR-MEM-05 not built); the Privacy page (`apps/web/src/legal/content.ts`, "Where it is stored and how long") says tasks, worlds, runs, cards and files are kept 90 days and a model-call record 30 days, which is untrue for a demo: AG-WEB should say so. Follow-ups: a session takes a slot only on its first write, an empty session idle over 60 s may be evicted for a new one (never one with data), a demo may have 2 runs at once (429 `demo_runs_busy`); the scheduler tick/Tasks route is marked no-person and keeps the durable store; old stored demo schedules still tick in replay (PATCH of a schedule by a demo is refused); the redactor is a stand-in; durable READS remain in `log_context.py` and `__init__._world_owner` (a demo's world id looks up the durable store, read only, never returned). | Default: as built. | SEC-15, SEC-16, BQ-SIGNIN-10 | 2026-10-09 |
| BQ-LIVEGAPS-1 | `GET /v1/connectors` reads connection state only through the read-only seam `deps.get_connection_views`, which is unwired in production, so every row is `available` with `state_known` false. tilo-api must never hold a TokenBroker, token records, a Kms or the OAuth client secret (PRD 6.5/12.1, SEC-02, ADR-0032). | Default: unwired answers 200 all `available`. | BQ-IQ1-1 | INTAKE-QUALITY-1: the PRD is silent on how a prompt learns today's date. The Director's brief, approaches and plan prompts (and the repair prompts) and the Specialist, Writer, Verifier and Lead prompts state `$today`, `$deadline` and `$days_to_deadline`. Production gets the real date; a test, or `deps.get_today`, passes a fixed one. A live-e2e replay states the date in the recording's `TODAY` marker (written when it records), so recording keys do not move daily; B-007 is regenerated with 2026-10-09. | FR-INT-03, FR-INT-06, FR-INT-09, FR-AGT-12 | 2026-10-09 |
| BQ-IQ1-2 | INTAKE-QUALITY-1: what does the Verifier's deterministic date check read, where, and what does a hit do? The PRD gives FR-AGT-12 only claim checking. | Default: before any model call a verifier-role unit (default runner and live-e2e runner) reads the dates in the work it checks (`tilo_harness.schedule_dates`): ISO `2026-11-03`, `3 Nov 2026`, `Nov 3, 2026`, `March 2025` (by month; `May` only with a capital M). A date after the deadline fails wherever it stands. A date before today fails only in schedule-like context: its line (a table: any line of it, so a header cue covers the rows) holds a generic timing word (start, begin, due, deliver, milestone, phase, week, day, month, session, revise, mock, exam, deadline, schedule, sprint, finish, until, from..to, or `by` right before the date) and holds no citation cue (published, accessed, retrieved, `source:`, et al, doi, vol., pp., reference, cited, copyright, isbn, `(Author 2019)`); a line that is both is not read for past dates, so a past publication date passes. Other forms (numeric 01/03/2025, bare years, quarters, seasons, weeks, relative spans) are not checked. A hit makes the unit FAIL: `success=False`, the summary quotes the dates, no model call. The executor then skips the unit's dependents and the run ends with the existing `world.failed` (no new state): the person sees `Stopped: The work has dates outside today to the deadline: "March 2025"` against that unit, nothing is delivered, and the unit's Trace summary has the full verdict. A clean schedule goes on to the model verifier. Not applied to the claim-level `verifier.Verifier` (artifact manifests), which has no schedule text. | FR-AGT-12 | 2026-10-09 |

The check runs before the gate card and again before the enqueue. A fake or mock adapter (every other test rig) is not bound by LC-10. The hash is memory of this run: another instance would refuse until a follow-up persists it with the entry. The send carries the pinned bytes (the stub's hash-checked read), never the Gmail draft | services/api/src/tilo_api/runs.py | 2026-10-09 |
| BQ-ADR35L6-4 | ADR-0035 L6 abandon-first: which unsent entries does a person's disconnect, or a `connection.revoked` or `connection.expired` part, abandon? An entry has no owner field, and `authz.ref` is the approver (a Team Lead, or a policy id). | **Default (builder):** for gmail, google_drive and google_calendar, the entries in a world the person owns (`store.load_world(world_id).owner`), because the owner's one shared grant (CON-D10) is the one they use, so any of the three revoked abandons all three; for `meeting_host` unchanged (`authz.ref`). An ambiguous entry or one a live lease holds is never touched. The disconnect route and the callback route now read the worlds through `deps.get_store`; a world store that cannot be read is 503 `unknown` before the call (nothing disconnected). `on_connection_event` for a person connector with no `owner_of` abandons nothing | services/api/src/tilo_api/meeting_host.py, connect_routes.py | 2026-10-09 |
