| BQ-ADR35L6-6 | ADR-0035 L2+L6 Opus round 1, left open on purpose. (a) A person connector's run raises a gate card with no connectors-side preflight (no `status`, no consent card, LC-06 / CR-13): an unconnected person gets a card that retries and then fails with a plain reason. (b) `all_entries()` streams the whole `outbox` collection per call (disconnect, tick, LC-10, abandon). (c) The L7 index on `outbox` (`transfer_pending`, `enqueued_at`) must be applied before `TILO_OUTBOX_STORE=firestore` is set. (d) The Firestore store ran only against the faithful fake; the emulator run is pending (BQ-ADR35-L2-1). (e) The tick cannot flag an entry whose run died: a person-connector stub is skipped by `sweep_due`, so such an entry stays queued with no alert until its owner disconnects; a follow-up adds a "stuck entry" alert (entries unsent past the lease with no live run) once the evidence is persisted with the entry (BQ-ADR35L6-2) | **Default:** as listed; each is a follow-up, none changes an outcome of an executed send | services/api | 2026-10-09 |
| BQ-ADR35-L2-5 | ADR-0035 L2 (Opus round 1): whose clock decides a lease and a not-before? | **Default:** the store's `clock` (the process's own UTC time in production, `store_door`'s `now`), not Firestore server time; two instances' clocks may differ by their skew, and a lease is 30 s, so a skew above a few seconds could let a second instance take a live lease. The take-over marks the entry ambiguous, so it is never failed and a person is alerted, never a duplicate send (Google-side markers, OB-03). A follow-up may use `firestore.SERVER_TIMESTAMP`; Cloud Run's NTP skew is well under a second. An expired unreleased lease is ambiguous for `abandon(keep_ambiguous=True)`; `all_entries()` skips (and logs the id of) a malformed document | services/api/src/tilo_api/outbox_firestore.py, services/connectors/src/tilo_connectors/store.py | 2026-10-09 |

Only the allowlisted fields of the L5 formats are returned (email: subject, body_text, in_reply_to, references + the card's recipients; file: name, mime_type, content_b64, origin render; calendar_event: title, start, end, time_zone, attendees). Its recording is made by `tilo_evals.web_replay`, never by hand. | Decided. | ADR-0029 (LW-16), PRD 19.2, BQ-67, BQ-INT15-7 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-PW-2B | PROD-WIRE 2b (issue #522): which choices did the acceptance tests settle for the durable artifact store and raw archive, and what must infra grant? | Defaults built (tests pin them): (1) artifact record paths keep the world segment, `orgs/{org}/worlds/{world}/artifacts/{art}/versions/{n:06d}` (+ `/manifests/{n:04d}`), as ADR-0018 section 2 and the store's `_record_path` have it; the ticket text's `orgs/{org}/artifacts` loses. (2) `FirestoreMetaBackend(client, collection_root="orgs")`: the Firestore document path IS the store path; every segment is `[0-9A-Za-z_]+` (not `__x__`), a path outside the root or with an odd segment count is a `ValueError` before any client call; the record is one JSON string field because Firestore refuses arrays of arrays; `paths(prefix)` walks `list_documents` and sub-collections (no collection-group query, no new index). (3) Blobs: the GCS object name is the blob key; create-only with `if_generation_match=0`; `delete` removes every generation of the exact key. Security round 1: google-cloud-storage and the Firestore client retry a precondition-guarded create, so a refused create (412, `AlreadyExists`, on the first attempt too) over identical bytes or an identical document body is our own write whose answer was lost (success); anything else is `FileExistsError`. (4) Raw archive (changed in security round 1): one create-only object per line, `{org}/{run}/{n:08d}.jsonl`. A record line (a JSON object with `seq` or `prev_hash`; every `RunArchive` line) goes to the index of its own `seq` (1 to 99,999,999, else `ValueError`, nothing written), so a second writer of that seq gets 412: identical bytes are our own lost-reply write (success), other bytes are `FileExistsError` and the caller reopens at the head (the assistant route already reopens per append and answers 503 `write_failed`; the harness only reads the archive). A record never lands with a stale prev_hash, so two live writers of one run (up to 3 tilo-api instances) keep one chain that `verify_chain` accepts (BQ-MEM10-2, FR-MEM-04). Only a line that is not a record moves to the next index after a lost race (bounded at 100, then an error, never a silent loss); a non-JSON line is refused. (5) Switches are explicit, never implied by `K_SERVICE`: `TILO_ARTIFACTS_STORE=gcs` + `TILO_ARTIFACTS_BUCKET`, `TILO_ARCHIVE_STORE=gcs` + `TILO_ARCHIVE_BUCKET`, and `TILO_FIRESTORE_PROJECT` for both (the archive needs the project only for its storage client). Bucket names are checked against the GCS naming rules before any client exists. ADC only. (6) Outside Cloud Run a door opens only the emulators it needs on a `demo-` project (`FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST` and `STORAGE_EMULATOR_HOST` for the artifacts, the storage host for the archive; there is no official GCS emulator, so the real-GCS check is the deployed smoke, PR4), an injected factory included (security round 1). Before any client is built, both doors also refuse (PROD-WIRE 1a's rules): an emulator host in `env` or the process while `K_SERVICE` is present; `API_ENDPOINT_OVERRIDE` anywhere; a `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT` other than `TILO_FIRESTORE_PROJECT`; a passed `env` whose emulator hosts differ from the process's when a default factory (which reads `os.environ`) builds the client; and `TILO_ARCHIVE_BUCKET` equal to `TILO_ARTIFACTS_BUCKET`. A client or bucket handle that cannot be built is `RunStoreUnavailable` (a `RunStoreNotConfigured`, so still a `NotImplementedError`): only its type is logged, the run seams start no run, the assistant answers 503 `store_unavailable`. `deps` keys a kept store on these variables too. (7) `redact_leaves(str_redactor)` masks every string leaf and dict key of an archive payload and fails closed (two keys that redact to one key are a `ValueError`, never a dropped value); `deps.get_run_archive` wraps `deps.get_redactor()` in it and `deps.get_artifact_store` passes it as is; both read it at call time and check it before building any client, so while FR-MEM-05 (BQ-109) is unbuilt they still refuse. (8) `archive_lookup` defaults to "no record", so a put citing archive sources is refused (AR-03) until a lookup over the archive is wired; the default alert sink logs the alert summary (ids only). (9) `deps` keeps a built store per config (env values), never a refusal, and `cache_clear()` forgets them. INFRA GAPS for the user (infra/ untouched): (a) tilo-api needs `roles/storage.objectCreator` and `roles/storage.objectViewer` on the `archive` bucket (today only harness has viewer + creator there), no delete; (b) DECIDE BEFORE `TILO_ARTIFACTS_STORE=gcs` IS SET: tilo-api itself erases artifacts today (`POST /v1/meetings/{id}/delete` -> `people_writes._purge` -> `ArtifactStore.retention().delete` -> `GcsBlobBackend.delete`, every generation), and no retention service account exists. Either (i) grant tilo-api `storage.objects.delete` (plus list of noncurrent generations) on `artifacts` only, a user decision against BQ-261 / ADR-0018's "only the retention and erasure identity deletes", or (ii) move that erasure path to a separate erasure identity (its own service and service account) and keep tilo-api at creator and viewer; until one is chosen and applied, a meeting delete over the GCS store fails at the blob step; (c) tilo-api env: `TILO_ARTIFACTS_STORE=gcs`, `TILO_ARTIFACTS_BUCKET`, `TILO_ARCHIVE_STORE=gcs`, `TILO_ARCHIVE_BUCKET`, `TILO_FIRESTORE_PROJECT` (already set for the people and schedules stores), and Firestore access for the artifact records; (d) the `artifacts` bucket still has a 365-day lifecycle Delete in `infra/envs/dev/main.tf` while AR-08 and BQ-261 (accepted 2026-10-05) say 90 days plus noncurrent generations, and the `archive` bucket deletes at 30 days: confirm both against PRD 15.1 before the deployed smoke; (e) downgrade tilo-render's `roles/storage.objectAdmin` on `artifacts` (`infra/envs/dev/main.tf`, `render_artifacts_writer`) to `objectCreator` + `objectViewer` (BQ-261); (f) the versioned `artifacts` bucket needs a lifecycle Delete for noncurrent generations (an age Delete only makes a versioned object noncurrent, so the bytes stay). OPEN ITEMS (security round 1 follow-ups, not built): `GcsArchiveStore._next` keeps one entry per (org, run) for the life of the process (bound or evict it); `FirestoreMetaBackend` is rooted at `orgs` (root it at the artifact layout, `orgs/{org}/worlds`, later); `GcsBlobBackend` accepts keys outside `orgs/` and `keys()` with no prefix lists the whole bucket (restrict both to the artifact layout); the `archive` bucket's 30-day age Delete would cut a long-lived Desk run's chain, and every assistant append reopens the run (lists and reads every line), so a long run costs more per append. Round 2 (security PASS) residuals: two writers that produce byte-identical records (same seq, payload, time and prev_hash) both succeed with one ref and one object, so one record stands for two identical calls (no fork; give a record a unique per-call field if per-call counts matter); the same identical-means-success rule in `FirestoreMetaBackend.create` and `GcsBlobBackend.create` lets two identical concurrent `import_version` calls both report written, so a rollback of one can remove a version the other reported (an edge of BQ-MEM10-2); lines with no `seq` or `prev_hash` still take the next free index and two identical ones merge (no production writer sends them; refuse them later); a Desk append that loses a race is 503 `write_failed` with no retry (one reopen-and-retry would hide it), and a 503 after `assistant.asked` leaves that event written (pre-existing). | PROD-WIRE 2b | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-STARTER-1 | STARTER-1 (user-requested, not in the PRD): what is a new account's default sample project, and how does it stay honest? | A read-only replay of the recorded stream the web fixture mode already plays (`village-basic.jsonl`, 44 events, plus `village_basic.json`), shipped as package data in `tilo_api` (`sample_data/`, copies pinned byte-identical to `contracts/` by `tests/repo/test_STARTER_1_sample_ships_in_api_image.py`, so the image carries them whatever the build context's contracts/ holds), under the reserved id `w_sample_village_basic` (only the world id differs from the recording; nothing new is recorded, no model call). `GET /worlds` adds an item (`sample: true`, title `Sample: <its world.requested title>`, `completed`, `last_seq` 44) for an owner with no worlds of their own, or on `?include_sample=1` ("Show sample project"); the list response is not a contract schema, so no contract change. Its GET events, layout and cards (`[]`) answer any signed-in principal through routes registered ahead of the owner-scoped ones; every POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE under its path is 409 `sample_is_a_replay` (nothing reaches the outbox, store, writer or meter); both stores refuse a real world with that id. Hiding it is client-side (localStorage); the server stores nothing. Fail closed: files missing or damaged = the sample is absent and one WARNING line says why (a deployed image without the package data would show an empty list again; the repo test guards that). The browser never sends a write for the sample (`HttpClient.post` refuses it: "your answer was not sent"); nothing is simulated, and the recording’s one approval is already resolved. `GET /worlds/{id}/delivery` for it is read-only and empty (no artifacts, the recorded credits) so the delivery, library and digest screens show no error. A stored doc under the reserved id is never adopted by the deployed store and is dropped from the list; the reserved id is also refused as a parts key, a policy’s world and a task’s world_id. Open: the 13-credit figure in the recording is a recorded value, not a charge. | Default: as described; revisit when a real onboarding flow exists. | STARTER-1, HR-1..HR-7 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MROOM-A1-17 | MROOM-776b, SEC-16: how are room model calls charged? | Default: like Quick. Live only (`get_work_mode`), one unit (`caps.unit_reserve_minor`, BQ-SEC16-1) reserved on the uid ledger under the room's pseudo run `r_room_<room id>` before the call (so `run_minor` also bounds a room) and settled after it, released when no model was reached; the uid cap is lowered by `team_access.user_cap_minor`, unreadable limits are 0, a capped uid is 403 `spend_cap_reached` before anything is written. One `LedgerEntry` commit row per call (run `r_room_<id>`, user, step `turn_<n>`, credits, usage) via `deps.get_ledger_writer` (best effort, like Quick). A Router error after billed attempts records their token cost (an output-capped one the whole budget), never 0. The per-room MR-08 credit cap stays. | MROOM-776b, ADR-0028 | 2026-10-09 |
| BQ-MROOM-A1-18 | MROOM-776b: one 404 message for every room route. | Default: unknown world, a sign-in with no world_id, another world, a missing room and a room that is not yours all answer 404 with the same body (`meeting_room.NOT_FOUND`), so there is no oracle (MR-12). The owner routes' SEC-WORLD-1 fix is AG-WEB's. | MROOM-776b, ADR-0028 | 2026-10-09 |
| BQ-MROOM-A1-19 | MROOM-776b, MR-05: stable closing id and non-JSON bodies. | Default: each `meeting.adjourned` has the id `stable_part_id(room_id, 'adjourned')`, and a closing waiting in the dispatcher queue counts as closed, so double Pause all or a queued closing plus a housekeeping adjourn write exactly one. The room POSTs answer 415 for a non-JSON content type when they carry a body (the people rule); adjourn needs none. | MROOM-776b, ADR-0028 | 2026-10-09 |
| BQ-MROOM-1b-1 | MROOM-1b: `POST .../rooms/{id}/changes`, and what waits for MR-H2. | Default: the convener confirms a proposal by id with their wording; the event copies the proposal's own units, artifacts and agent, the wording goes through the safety screen (flagged 422 `declined`, a screen error 503) and the redactor into the archive, each proposal once (409 `already_confirmed`, `not_a_proposal`, `max_changes`), all under the room's write lock. It is not a side effect: no `world.requested`, no `plan.revised`. `deps.get_change_planner` is the seam for the revision planner and the owner budget grant (MR-09, MR-10, MR-H2, the next ticket): with none, the answer is 202 `planning: pending` and the request waits recorded; a planner failure also leaves it pending. #781 follow-ups folded in: a live room whose pseudo run is full answers 409 `room_limit` (not 503 replay-only), and a queued closing stops a late reply from being written. | MROOM-1b, ADR-0028 | 2026-10-10 |
| BQ-MROOM-H2a-1 | MROOM-H2a: what does a revision unit carry, and what is PG-13's `changes` argument? | Default: the Director's reply names only `id`, `revises`, `unit_type`, `title`, `skills` and `depends_on` (any other field, including `change`, `actions`, `connectors`, a tier or a policy, fails the shape check and is repaired or raised, never dropped). Code sets `district` and `role` from the revised unit, `change` from the confirmed proposal id, and empty `connectors` and `actions`, so a revision unit has no side effect of its own yet and no gate is added (MR-11); a revision that must send waits for a later ticket. `validate_plan(changes=...)` takes proposal id -> unit ids of every confirmed change of the world; `compile_revision` builds it from the plan's earlier revision units plus this change, and H2b may pass `changes=` from the world's `meeting.change_requested` events to be exact. | MROOM-H2a, ADR-0028, PG-13, MR-09 to MR-11 | 2026-10-10 |
| BQ-MROOM-H2a-2 | MROOM-H2b: what the API wiring must do (the `get_change_planner` implementation, `deps.py`). | Default: on the planner callback for a stored `meeting.change_requested`: screen the wording (SafetyProvider; flagged is not planned), call `compile_revision(plan, change, wording, router, inputs)`, then write `plan.revised` with `cause` the change request, `run` `same_run` when the discussed run had not finished and `revision_run` when it had (a `revision_run` uses a new `run_id` no earlier event of the world uses), `units` from `RevisionResult.revision_units`, `credits` from `RevisionResult.credits` (None: take the estimate range the budget code gives, MR-10). For `revision_run` or any change not confirmed by the owner: write `budget.requested` to the owner and start nothing until the owner's in-person `budget.granted` (`authz.by` person, `authz.ref` the owner, never a policy), then `world.started` with that cap. A `same_run` change confirmed by the owner starts only if the estimate fits the run's remaining budget (MT-03). The planning call is reserved against the confirmer's per-user cap (`revision_plan_max_output_tokens`, ADR-0028 decision 6). | MROOM-H2a, ADR-0028, PG-13, MR-09 to MR-11 | 2026-10-10 |
| BQ-MROOM-H2b-1 | MROOM-H2b: who starts a revision, and what does H2b not do? | Default: `meeting_room_planner.plan_change` only plans. It writes `plan.revised` (actor the Director, `cause` the change request, vis the room's) and then `budget.requested` to the owner (vis `[]`, `needs_you`) for every change, `same_run` or `revision_run`, owner or not: MR-10 (a) (a `same_run` change the owner confirmed whose estimate fits the run's remaining budget) is not implemented, because no code in the API reads a run's remaining budget (MT-03 lives in the Treasury, which the API does not hold), so the owner's in-person `budget.granted` is required for all. It never writes `budget.granted` or `world.started` and starts no unit (a policy can never grant it). A `revision_run` switches the world record's `run_id` to a new `r_rev_...` id before `plan.revised`, which is then that run's first event. Starting the revision units after the owner's grant (`world.started` with that cap, running the units on the revised plan, MR-10 (a)) is MROOM-H2c. A failed `budget.requested` write after `plan.revised` raises an alert and leaves the plan recorded; H2c finds a `plan.revised` with no `budget.requested` and asks again. | MROOM-H2b, ADR-0028 decisions 6 and 8, MR-09, MR-10 | 2026-10-10 |
| BQ-MROOM-H2b-2 | MROOM-H2b: where does the current plan live, and what is the estimate with no cost history? | Default: the planner reads the plan from the in-process run (`store.runs[world].plan`, or `.revised_plan` after an earlier revision) because no recorded plan exists in the events or the store; with no run object (another process, a restart) or an earlier `plan.revised` whose revised plan is not held, it plans nothing and raises the alert (a new unit id could collide). A durable plan record is needed before a deployed revision survives a restart (H2c). `unit_cost_seed.json` is empty, so `compile_revision` returns no estimate for any unit type: the planner then records `{min: 0, max: ceiling}` with the ceiling `deps.budget_cap_ceiling()` (the most a grant may be, 300 credits), so the owner sees the real upper bound and nothing is hidden; `budget.requested.remaining_units` lists the revision units only (`building.completed` carries no unit ids), and `spent` and `cap` are the current run's event credits and `world.started` cap (0 and 0 for a `revision_run`). The planning call is metered on the confirmer's uid ledger under the pseudo run `r_plan_{room_id}` (live only; replay holds nothing) and its credits are on `plan.revised`; a confirm with the uid at its cap answers 403 `spend_cap_reached` before anything is written. | MROOM-H2b, ADR-0028 decisions 6 and 10, MR-10 | 2026-10-10 |
| BQ-IQ1-3 | INTAKE-QUALITY-1: where do the questions come from, and what do they replace? FR-INT-04 says "at most 3, ranked by expected change, each with suggested answers" and the generic questions (format, rules) ignored the topic (a live run asked "format?" and "rules?" for an exam plan). | Default: the Director's brief output gains an optional `questions` list (only the derived Director-output schema and `prompts/director/brief_system.md` change; `brief.schema.json` does not). Each item is a `label`, `text`, one to three `suggested_answers` (the first is the Director's guess, used on a skip) and an `expected_change` (0..1). Code vets them (`brief.topic_questions`): plain text, tags and control characters removed, label 30, question 200 and answer 120 characters, duplicates and repeated labels dropped, an unusable item dropped (never an error), ranked by `expected_change`, at most 3. They get the ids `topic_1`..`topic_3` and are asked first; they REPLACE the generic deliverables and rules questions (those fields are then recorded as assumptions, not asked); the other ambiguous fields fill only the slots left under the cap of 3; with no usable Director question the generic questions are asked as before. Memory (FR-INT-05) still runs first on the generic fields. | FR-INT-03, FR-INT-04, FR-INT-05 | 2026-10-09 |
| BQ-IQ1-4 | INTAKE-QUALITY-1: how is a Director question's answer stored with no contract change, and how are its model-written strings screened? | Default: an answer, or the guess on a skip, becomes the constraint `Label: value` (for example "Branch: CS"; it is only ever appended: a stated constraint is never removed or edited. A Director label that matches a stated constraint (`Budget: ...` already in the brief, case-insensitive) or a reserved label (Assumed, Deliverable wishes, a chip's name) is dropped in code and the generic question fills the slot); a skip also adds the assumption `{question, assumed: "Label: value"}` (shown as "Assumed: ..."). The answer is cleaned (NFKC-folded, one plain line, 250 characters), and the route keeps its 300-character limit, the safety screen and the 422 re-ask. The question text, and the guess on a skip, are model text that enters the brief, so `POST /tasks/{id}/answers` screens them with the typed strings (a flagged suggestion cannot enter by a skip; another suggestion or a typed answer still works). They are not screened at intake (no extra safety call per task). The suggested answers stay at 3 (the contracts pin nothing here). | FR-INT-04, SEC-* | 2026-10-09 |
| BQ-IQ1-5 | INTAKE-QUALITY-1: which kinds of work does the deliverables question offer, and how is a multi-pick answered? The PRD is silent on the list (a live run asked only "which format?"). | Default: a config file, `config/deliverable_kinds.yaml`, lists the kinds (id, label, format, `available`, and `needs` when unavailable). The question offers the available ones, in file order, as choices after the Director's guess (the guess stays the first answer, so a skip still keeps it); the cap of 3 suggested answers is lifted for this question only (up to 6 kinds). Shipped: Study plan, Notes and Question bank are available (text the Writer produces today, as markdown); Mock-test quiz (needs LW-12) and Past-paper links (needs LW-14) are `available: false` and never offered until the flag is set. No kind is offered when none is available: the old format question stays. Several kinds are picked in one answer ("Study plan; Notes", "study plan and notes"; the terminal turns "2 and 3" into the first form): each becomes a deliverable titled with the kind's label in the kind's format; the Director's guess among the choices keeps the brief's own deliverables and adds the kinds. An answer that names a kind marked unavailable (whole words, case-blind, alone or in a mix) is refused with the 422 re-ask, saying it is not available yet and what it needs, and is never read as free text; one that names nothing offered reads as before (formats, free text BQ-FREETEXT-1, or the 422 re-ask). The Director's guess among the choices keeps the brief's deliverables only when it stands as a whole segment of the answer. The parsed file is cached until its modification time or size changes. The file is validated at load (bad file: 503 at intake before anything is stored, a 503 on an answer); the server does not read bare numbers ("1,3"), the client does. | FR-INT-04, FR-INT-06 | 2026-10-10 |
| BQ-PEOPLESEAMS1-1 | PEOPLE-SEAMS-1 (PRD 11.3, 11.6; ADR-0021 decision 2): the plate's mail rows and the owner of a mail message need a triage store tilo-api can read, but the only `TriageStore` is tilo-channels' (an in-memory test double; channels holds no Firestore role, SEC-12). Where does it live and what does a record hold? | `orgs/{org}/mail_triage/{doc}` holds `{owner, message_id, category, flagged, label?, received_at, answered, dismissed}` (no subject, body or sender, BQ-269), written by tilo-channels through an API route not built yet; tilo-api only reads: by `owner` for a person's plate (at most 500), by `message_id` (two documents at most; two holders = nobody) for who owns a message. A row needs `needs_reply` (the person's label beats the model's), not flagged, a well-formed id and time, boolean `answered`/`dismissed`. Until channels writes it the collection is empty and so are mail rows; a store with no Firestore client has none. The reader takes 501 documents so it knows when the 500 cap was hit (`StorePlateSources.truncated`, which the plate builder reads: `truncated` is then true when the rows lane is full). The PR that adds the triage writer must filter server side (`needs_reply`, not answered, not dismissed) and order by `received_at`, so a heavy mailbox never hides a reply behind the cap. | PRD 11.3, 11.6; ADR-0021; PE-27; PEOPLE-SEAMS-1 | 2026-10-10 |
| BQ-PEOPLESEAMS1-2 | PEOPLE-SEAMS-1 (PE-27, PE-29): what makes a run `running` or `blocked` on the plate, and where do its goal and date come from? | PE-27 says runs of worlds the person owns: the runs source starts from the person's own worlds (stored records, loaded by owner), never from tasks, because a scheduled routine and a meeting host run create a task that is never stored. Running = the world's recorded events hold `world.started` and no `world.completed` or `world.failed`, and the world is not paused; blocked = an `agent.blocked` that no later `agent.recovered` or `agent.retired` of the same agent has followed. Goal and deadline (when a calendar date) come from the world's stored task (one read by `world_id`), else the process's own `Run.brief`, only when that task or Run names the same owner; else the row has a null title and no date. Desk and meeting worlds have no `world.started`, so they never appear. Honest limit: the events of a world are read when this instance first loads it and are not refreshed while it stays cached, so a run another Cloud Run instance advanced shows its new state only after this instance restarts or evicts the world (the same limit as `GET /worlds`). | PE-27, PE-29; ADR-0021; PEOPLE-SEAMS-1 | 2026-10-10 |
| BQ-PEOPLESEAMS1-3 | PEOPLE-SEAMS-1 (PE-27): cards are held in each run's in-memory `CardStore` (ADR-0026 stores no cards), so which cards can a plate carry in production? | Those of runs this process holds: every open card of a world the person owns (the stored world record must name them too), and an open gate whose live Approver assignment names them. After a restart or on another instance a card is not on the plate until a durable card store exists (not in the PRD's current tickets); the plate is then complete for what the process knows, as `GET /cards` is. | PE-27; ADR-0006; PEOPLE-SEAMS-1 | 2026-10-10 |
| BQ-PEOPLESEAMS1-4 | PEOPLE-SEAMS-1 (PE-12, BQ-273): which world ids do production Desk and meeting worlds use? API_PPL_11c.md section 5 names `w_desk_<user id without u_>` for the dev Desk. | Production Desk = the person's one Desk world of the assistant panel (`desk.ensure_desk`, `w_desk_<24 hex of org and person>`, never adopted from another owner, its record made again on first use; its events are what is durable). A meeting world is `w_mtg_<24 hex of org and meeting id>`, one persisted world per meeting, owner = the meeting's owner, refused if held by another owner. Both are untitled worlds with no `world.requested`, so `GET /worlds` lists them for their owner as idle and untitled; hiding them there is a follow-up. | PE-12; BQ-273; PEOPLE-SEAMS-1 | 2026-10-10 |
| BQ-PEOPLESEAMS1-5 | PEOPLE-SEAMS-1: wiring `get_desk_worlds` in production also turns on every write that was waiting on it: work-item events (`work_item.created|changed`, PE-12) and `team_rule.changed` (ADR-0027) now append to the person's Desk world instead of answering 503. Is that intended now? | Yes: the ticket's order is every feature live on tilo-dev by 17 Oct; each write is owner-scoped (`desk_owner` is checked before an append, a failed append undoes the write). | PE-12; ADR-0027; FR-TEAM-04; PEOPLE-SEAMS-1 | 2026-10-10 |
| BQ-PEOPLESEAMS1-6 | PEOPLE-SEAMS-1 security review r1: `sharing.py` `create_share` accepted any well-formed `u_` id as the Approver (it checked only the pattern and != the owner), so a world owner could put a gate and its text on the plate of a person who is not on their team or org. Should `create_share` require the Approver to be in the owner's people directory or org? | Decided (supervisor): org members only. `create_share` reads the people directory (`persons.get(deps.ORG_ID, approver)`) and answers one fixed 403 `{outcome: rejected, reason: not_allowed}` for an unknown id, a person of another org, and a directory that is not wired or cannot be read (deny by default); the owner naming themself stays 422. Nothing is created on a refusal. | ADR-0006; FR-HIL-12; PE-27; PEOPLE-SEAMS-1 | 2026-10-10 |
| BQ-PEOPLESEAMS1-7 | PEOPLE-SEAMS-1 security review r1 notes, left as they are: (1) `StoreMeetingWorlds` reads the world without settling it and then creates it with a create precondition; two instances racing end with one world (the loser rereads and checks the owner), but nothing settles a half-made world. (2) Desk and meeting worlds are untitled, event-less worlds, so `GET /worlds` lists them as idle for their owner until the list hides worlds with no `world.requested` (follow-up, see BQ-PEOPLESEAMS1-4). | As noted; no change in this ticket. | BQ-273; PE-12; PEOPLE-SEAMS-1 | 2026-10-10 |
| BQ-SKILLS-1 | SKILLS-2 (FR-SKL-04): the taught-skill store is in memory only (`tilo_api.skill_store`, wired in dev mode); there is no Firestore store (`orgs/{org}/taught_skills/{id}`), so a deployed install answers 503 `not_configured`. The per-step prior in `config/skills.yaml` (3 credits, 20 seconds) is a placeholder, and TS-06 checks the action against `config/connectors.yaml` only (not `config/mcp.yaml`). | Default: the Firestore store is a named follow-up ticket copying `people_firestore.py` (CAS by version); the prior is replaced with numbers measured from replay; MCP tools join the check when a taught step may use one. | FR-SKL-04 | 2026-10-10 |
| BQ-SKILLS-1 | SKILLS-2 (FR-SKL-04): the taught-skill store is in memory only (`tilo_api.skill_store`, wired in dev mode); there is no Firestore store (`orgs/{org}/taught_skills/{id}`), so a deployed install answers 503 `not_configured`. RESOLVED (SKILLS-2b, 2026-10-10, store half): `tilo_api.skill_firestore.FirestoreSkillStore` keeps the record verbatim at `orgs/{org}/taught_skills/{id}` (create for a new id, an id held by another owner is a store error, `scope.team in` queries chunked by 30); `deps.get_skill_store` reads `TILO_SKILLS_STORE` = `firestore` (+ `TILO_FIRESTORE_PROJECT`; outside Cloud Run only the emulator with a `demo-` project) or `memory` (refused when deployed), unset in dev mode = memory, else 503 `not_configured`, a failed client build backs off like the people stores. A deployed tilo-api needs `TILO_SKILLS_STORE=firestore` in its Terraform env (CL-REACH). The prior and MCP points below stay open. The per-step prior in `config/skills.yaml` (3 credits, 20 seconds) is a placeholder, and TS-06 checks the action against `config/connectors.yaml` only (not `config/mcp.yaml`). | Default: the Firestore store is a named follow-up ticket copying `people_firestore.py` (CAS by version); the prior is replaced with numbers measured from replay; MCP tools join the check when a taught step may use one. | FR-SKL-04 | 2026-10-10 |
| BQ-SKILLS4-1 | SKILLS-4 (FR-SKL-01/02, text path): ADR-0034 names a `skill_capture` role that config does not have yet, a `cap_` capture id, and 'one' captured event; the contracts need a `tsk_` skill id and write one `skill.captured` per step. | Default: the role is `specialist` (one constant, `tilo_api.skills_capture.CAPTURE_ROLE`); the dev models file has no `specialist`, so capture answers 503 `not_configured` in dev until the user adds it. A capture is a DRAFT taught-skill record (status `draft`, empty plan) in the skill store with a `tsk_` id, so no second store and the events name a real skill; `capture_id` is that id. One `skill.captured` per step (HR-1, the contract), credits and trace on step 1. The capture call yields step text only; Tilo's version is a second call, and its estimates are always source `prior` (a draft has no runs, so no ledger rows; `ledger` arrives with runs). | FR-SKL-01, FR-SKL-02 | 2026-10-10 |
| BQ-SKILLS4-2 | SKILLS-4: how is a steps proposal kept for a person who closes the page before saving, and when does a draft expire? | Default: the version is stored on the draft (plan, question, estimates), so it survives; asking again answers the same version (no new call). Expiry is ADR-0034's 90 days for a draft (Task retention), not yet enforced by a sweeper; the save-from-draft route and the upload, guided and record methods are later tickets. | FR-SKL-01, FR-SKL-02 | 2026-10-10 |
| BQ-MTG11-13 | FR-MTG-11: BQ-MTG11-2 and -3 are DECIDED. Which voice, and how is it metered? | **Decided by the user (relayed 2026-10-08).** The bot's voice is Google Cloud Text-to-Speech with a Chirp 3 HD voice, by STREAMING synthesis (`StreamingSynthesize`), so the bot starts speaking about 1 s after the first reply text. `config/voice.yaml`: vendor `google-cloud-tts`, profile `chirp3-hd`, model `chirp-3-hd` (the voice family; Cloud TTS has no separate model id), voice `en-US-Chirp3-HD-Leda` (the user may change the Name to any Chirp 3 HD voice). No preview model id is used and the PL-07 denylist is unchanged. The adapter is `tilo_providers.cloud_tts_adapter` (`SpeechProvider.synthesize_stream`, additive, default one piece): the first PCM piece is forwarded as it arrives, the char and audio caps and the PCM contract (16-bit mono little-endian at `sample_rate`) are kept, a real client needs the live switch and the shared Google settings guard, the SDK call has a deadline (`timeout_s`), dev and CI use the fake. Metering is by characters: `config/prices.yaml` profile `chirp3-hd` has `unit_price {unit: character, per_1k: 0.03}` (US$30 per 1M, no promotion; the 1M free characters a month stay a comment, the meter charges from the first character, ADR-0029 decisions 1 and 5). `VoiceTiming.characters` is the unit count to charge. Latency: each reply logs `voice turn: characters, chunks, audio_bytes, first_audio_ms, total_ms` (counts only, no text) and the bot logs `voice latency: characters, first_audio_ms` (text in to first audio byte). | Default: as described. The `google` (Gemini speech output) vendor stays selectable in config but is no longer the shipped voice. STT end to first audio byte is not logged: the bot has no STT-end timestamp at the speak call (`Speaker.say` times from its own start); add it with MTG09-c if wanted. | FR-MTG-11, PL-07, ADR-0029 | 2026-10-08 |

Whether Cloud TTS serves Chirp 3 HD streaming there is from memory of the docs, not verified: if the first call is refused or slow, set `region: global` (the default endpoint) or `us`/`eu`. (2) The request asks for audio encoding `PCM` at `sample_rate`; the streamed reply may open with a WAV header, which the adapter drops if present (so either shape works), and an odd byte at a chunk edge is carried to the next chunk. If the service rejects `PCM`, the alternative is `LINEAR16`. (3) The voice `en-US-Chirp3-HD-Leda` exists in the project's locale list; the service rejects an unknown voice with a 400 (a `RequestError`, so the fallback voice takes over). (4) The measured `first_audio_ms` in the log is the number to tune against the ~1 s goal. | Default: as described; the user checks these once with the live switch and edits `config/voice.yaml` only. | FR-MTG-11 | 2026-10-08 |
