| BQ-FRMEM05-1 | FR-MEM-05 / SEC-14 (PRD 13.2, 16.1 rule 4, 16.2): what exactly does the shared redactor remove and keep, and how is a card number told from a timestamp? The PRD names tokens, passwords, credentials and card numbers, and the artifact store, connectors and run bundle REFUSE what the redactor would change (MF-06, AR-07), so a false positive breaks real work. | `tilo_redact` (`packages/redact`, standard library only) removes private-key blocks (PEM and PGP; one with no END line loses only its header and the base64 lines after it), the `scripts/secret_scan.py` token shapes plus `ya29.c.` service-account tokens, `4/0A` auth codes, Firebase `AMf-vB` refresh tokens, GitHub fine-grained, Slack (`xox[abeprs]-`, so "xoxo-mum-and-dad" is kept; `e` is Slack's refresh-token prefix) and AWS keys, JWTs and a `Bearer` token in any case (20 token characters, or 8 with a digit, one of `_~+/=` or an inner `.`, so "Bearer securities", "Bearer authentication" and "Bearer instruments" are kept); the userinfo of any `scheme://user:pass@host`; labelled values; and card numbers. **One key rule, both modes** (second security review): a key is split into words at every non-alphanumeric character, `%XX` and camelCase; it is secure when its last words spell password, passwd, passphrase, secret, secret_key, client_secret, token, access_token, refresh_token, id_token, auth_token, api_key, private_key, authorization, cookie, aws_secret_access_key or aws_session_token, joined or not (`apikey`, `accessToken`, `X-Api-Key`, `db_password`, `push_token`, `payment_authorization`), when its last word ends in password, passwd, pwd, secret or token (third review: `PGPASSWORD`, `MYSQL_PWD`, `dbpassword`, `csrftoken`), or when the whole key is `pass`. Paging cursors are exempt in both modes: a key ending in page_token, sync_token or continuation_token, joined or not (page_token, next_page_token, start_page_token, new_start_page_token, sync_token, next_sync_token, continuation_token and the camelCase forms such as `nextPageToken`, and `nextpagetoken`). `{"pass": true}` is kept in both modes: `pass` is secure, but a bool or null cannot hold a secret, so a tree keeps it too (a tree still replaces a string, number, object or list whole). `{"payment_authorization": ...}` is secure in both modes: in structured data a key ending in `authorization` fails closed; only the prose line "Payment Authorization: approved" is kept (see below). A test checks the tree and five text forms on 72 keys. **Text values:** after a quoted key (JSON, JSON-in-JSON `\"`, `&quot;`) or after `=` the value always goes. After an unquoted `key:` it goes when it is quoted, credential-shaped (16 characters, or 8 with a digit or symbol) or a lone word (then the line end or punctuation); a shorter word followed by more words is prose and stays ("The secret: ship small", "Token: of appreciation"). This tightens the earlier choice that a label with any value fails closed, but only for secret, token, authorization and cookie labels: after an unquoted password, passwd or passphrase label the first word always goes (third review), so `password: hunter2 please`, "Username: ann  Password: changeme (temporary)" and "Wifi password: coffeeshop for guests" lose the password, and a passphrase goes to the line end or punctuation. `pass` and `pwd` need a credential shape or quotes ("Boarding pass: gate 12", `pass=200` stay). An `Authorization`, `Proxy-Authorization`, `Cookie` or `Set-Cookie` value (any key ending in those words) goes only when it starts with an auth scheme (Basic, Bearer, Digest, Token, Negotiate, NTLM, AWS4-HMAC-SHA256, Bot, ApiKey, any case), `name=value` (so `key=` and a cookie pair) or a credential shape; it then goes to the end of its line, or, inside a JSON string, to the quote that closes that string. "Payment Authorization: approved by finance on 3 Oct", "Prior Authorization: required for the MRI referral" and "Cookie: oatmeal raisin, 2 dozen" are kept, in text and inside JSON strings. `code`, `key` and `signature` are secrets only as `?x=` or `&x=` parameters, and `code` only in a URL that has `state=`, `scope=`, `redirect_uri=`, `grant_type=`, `client_id=` or `code_verifier=`, or whose host or path holds oauth, callback, auth, login, signin, sso or a `/cb` segment (`?code=SUMMER20` on a shop URL is kept). `state` is not on the list, so it is never a secret. **JSON stays JSON** (AR-07 stores what this returns): no value crosses the quote that closes a JSON string or splits a backslash escape, at any JSON-in-JSON level; a quoted value ends only at a quote of its own level or a shallower one, so a deeper, escaped quote is text and a secure field that holds JSON, a JSON array or a repr loses all of it (third review; in a `'`-quoted value a `"` is text unless the label sits inside a JSON string); a value that starts with `{` or `[` (or `[REDACTED]`) is left to the rules inside it, so a JSON schema `{"properties": {"password": {"type": "string", "minLength": 8}}}` is unchanged; a JSON number under a secure key becomes `"[REDACTED]"` (a numeric PIN is a secret, the result is valid JSON and the same string a tree gives), and true, false and null stay; a card typed as a JSON number becomes the string `"[REDACTED] (ends 1234)"`; a service-account key file's type marker keeps its quotes (`"type": "[REDACTED]"`). Hypothesis properties check that random JSON documents (and JSON-in-JSON) stay valid and are fixed points. **Fixed points:** a `#2` collision key is unchanged by a second pass (`password=[REDACTED]#2`), and a URL run stops at `[REDACTED]` so a second pass never sees new OAuth context. `RedactingFilter` applies the key rule to `extra=` field names. Every pattern is linear: 87 one-megabyte hostile inputs each finish in under a second. **Kept as before:** emails and phone numbers (recipients and message bodies are the data the archive holds; the live-e2e recording scrub stays its own). A card is 13 to 19 digits (plain, or in groups under a space, no-break space or dash), Luhn-valid, not touching a letter, digit, `_`, `.` or `-`, and leaves `[REDACTED] (ends 1234)`; a run starting with 0 is never a card, and a plain 13-digit run from 1500000000000 to 1999999999999 is an epoch-millisecond timestamp (about 10% of those pass Luhn); the sample generator draws leading digits 2 to 9. In a tree two keys that redact to one string both stay (`#2` suffix); tuple keys, sets and bytes are redacted inside; any other type, a tree over 200 levels or a cycle is refused (TypeError, ValueError). **Accepted false positives:** an auth scheme word or a credential-shaped word after an Authorization or Cookie label in prose ("Prior Authorization: Basic plan only", "Payment Authorization: 2026-10-03" lose the rest of the line); a lone word after a secret label (`token: 5`, `Password: none, ask Ann`); the first word after a password label even in prose ("Password: reset link sent to Ann", "Password: minimum 8 characters"), which a user may override; a number under a secure JSON key becomes a string; a card in prose after a quoted word and a colon, or inside `[...]`, gets quotes (`see [1, "[REDACTED] (ends 1111)"]`), as a card standing in a JSON number's place must; a line starting with 16 base64 characters right after an unterminated key header; any `auth` in a URL's host or path makes its `code=` a secret (`https://authors.example/x?code=1`). **Accepted misses:** a value under 8 characters followed by more words after `secret:` or `token:` (`secret: opensesame to enter`); an unquoted text value that starts with `[` or `{`, so an array under a secure key keeps its strings in text (`{"token": ["a", "b"]}`; a tree replaces it whole); a value after an unquoted key whose opening quote is followed by `,`, `:`, `}` or `]`; a repr whose `'`-quoted values hold an odd number of `"` can end a later `'`-quoted value early; a one-word key longer than 68 characters; a card that is a whole JSON document, or a JSON number two JSON-in-JSON levels down, is not re-quoted, so that document stops being JSON. **Open, not fixed now:** IMEI and WhatsApp `wa_id` numbers that are Luhn-valid look like cards; Unicode look-alikes and zero-width characters inside a token (no NFKC normalisation); a truncated one-line PEM (header and body on one line) keeps its body; Tilo's own `/l/<kid>.<jwt>.<mac>` answer links are not recognised; dotted, glued or double-space card groups; 16-digit and 19-digit timestamps are not exempt; `{"session": ...}` is not a secure key; base64-wrapped secrets; `embed` requests and model responses are not redacted by the Router; the safety screen sees raw text. The archive is append-only, so a miss is permanent. FR-MEM-05 stays open until NFR-26's `configure_logging` attaches `RedactingFilter`. The Router redacts `prompt`, `system`, `tool_outputs` and a repair prompt; a rule change moves the replay key of any recording whose prompt holds newly matched text. Overridable default. | PRD 13.2, 16.1, 16.2; FR-MEM-05, SEC-14; MF-06, AR-07; BQ-109, NFR-26 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-PW-3 | PROD-WIRE 3 (issue #522): how does the deployed `tilo-api` hold its Firestore client, where does SEC-15's declaration live, and what of the production wiring is still open? | Built: (1) one client per project per process, `tilo_api.prod.firestore_client` (single-flight, a failed build never cached, `FirestoreUnavailable` never echoes the error), the one guard run on every call before the cache is read: the project matches the schedule store's id shape; with `K_SERVICE` or `CLOUD_RUN_JOB` present (even empty) any present `FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST` (even empty) is refused, because the library enters emulator mode whenever the variable exists; a live project opens only when `K_SERVICE` is non-empty, anywhere else only the emulator and a `demo-` project open; `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT`, when set, equals the project; the cached client is keyed by project and emulator host. `store_door.client_factory` and the people door's default factory delegate to it, so the api store, the people stores and the access store share one client object (`people_firestore.emulator_people_stores` keeps its own guarded constructor, FS-06). (2) The access store: `orgs/{org_id}/access/{uid}`, field `declaration`; `declare` is one `create` and, on `AlreadyExists`, a read-back (never `set`); a malformed stored value reads as none (fail closed) and a `declare` that meets one answers 503 rather than overwrite it; a contended create is retried a few times, then 503; a uid Firestore refuses as a document id (empty, `/`, `.`, `..`, `__.*__`, over 1500 bytes) is a `ValueError` before any client call, and the access routes answer it 503 `not_configured`, never 500; a Google API error is `FirestoreUnavailable` (503 `store_unavailable`, only the type logged). `deps.get_access_store` returns it only when `TILO_API_STORE=firestore` and the api store door opens, else 503 `not_configured`, never cached; after a failed client build it backs off for `PEOPLE_STORES_BACKOFF_S` like the people door (FS-18). `firestore.rules` names `orgs/{org}/access/{uid}` server-only. (3) Terraform (code only, nothing applied): the service module takes `cpu_idle` per service, on both Cloud Run resources (the managed one and the `ignore_env` one tilo-api lives in); the dev `api` entry gets `max_instance_count = 1` always (ADR-0026: one writer; `deploy-dev.yml` and `cloudbuild.yaml` pass `--max-instances 1` for api too, since a gcloud update overrides Terraform's revision maximum) and, only with the new variable `api_durable_stores = true` (default false), `cpu_idle = false` (CPU allocated for the in-process kill-switch state). The store switches are NOT Terraform's: tilo-api has `ignore_env = true`, so `TILO_API_STORE` and `TILO_PEOPLE_STORE` = `firestore` (with the `TILO_FIRESTORE_PROJECT` it already has) are runtime env set with `gcloud run services update` (docs/deploy-dev.md "Durable stores"). Setting them is the user's security-flagged rollout (ADR-0023; ADR-0026 is still Proposed), so no apply switches anything on by itself. Still 503 `not_configured`: `deps.get_ledger` (FR-MTR-10): needs contract change, because no ledger or runs store exists in `contracts/` or `infra/` (BQ-MTR10-4). The user decides and applies: when they accept ADR-0026 (and ADR-0023's people store), they set `api_durable_stores = true` for the dev env and apply the Terraform plan (CPU always allocated on the one instance; this raises the api's idle cost, PRD 6.6), then run the two-variable gcloud step in docs/deploy-dev.md, then check the api instance count and `/health`. With the default false the plan still sets the one-instance maximum and nothing else changes. This change applies nothing and deploys nothing. OPEN (security round 2 follow-up): the api store door (`durable_store.deployed`) still retries a failing client build on every request under its lock, with no FS-18 backoff (the people and access doors have one); add it with PR4's smoke. | PROD-WIRE (issue #522); PRD 6.2, 6.6, SEC-15; ADR-0023; ADR-0026; BQ-MTR10-4 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-PW-4 | PROD-WIRE 4 (issue #522): which origins, headers and methods may a browser use against the deployed `tilo-api`, and what of the browser path is still open? | Built: `tilo_api.PRODUCTION_ORIGINS` and `_ProductionCors` (Starlette `CORSMiddleware`, read per request, on only while `access.enforced()`, the outermost layer so a preflight is answered before the SEC-15 gate and the gate's 401/403/503 carry the headers). Allowlist, exact strings: `https://essential-wares-510320-f0.web.app` and `https://essential-wares-510320-f0.firebaseapp.com`; no wildcard, no regex. Request headers: `Authorization`, `X-Tilo-Channel`, `X-Firebase-AppCheck`, `Content-Type` (plus the three safelisted names Starlette always lists). Credentials off. Methods GET, POST, PATCH, DELETE, OPTIONS (no PUT: no router serves one). Dev mode's own CORS is unchanged. Open: (1) the live web client sends no `Authorization`, `X-Tilo-Channel` or `X-Firebase-AppCheck` yet (only `content-type: application/json`); a later web slice adds them, until then a deployed API answers it 401. (2) `Retry-After` is not exposed, because the web client reads no response header; add `expose_headers` when it does. (3) The CSP `connect-src` change in `firebase.json` is NOT in this slice: it waits on CL-REACH's host list (coordinated through the supervisor on #522), and until then a browser on the hosting origin cannot reach the API even with CORS open. (4) `just deploy-web <api-url>` and `just smoke-dev <api-url>` (and `scripts/smoke_dev.py`) accept only tilo-api's own Cloud Run service, named `tilo-api` by `infra/modules/service` (`tilo-${key}`): `https://tilo-api-<hash>-as.a.run.app` (hash `[a-z0-9]+`) and `https://tilo-api-<project-number>.asia-southeast1.run.app`, lower case, no path, port or query; a bare label, another service name, host, region or form is refused, so a typo or pasted link never sends a person's ID token to a stranger's service. (5) The signed-in smoke steps cannot pass on tilo-dev until the Firebase ID-token verifier (`deps.get_current_principal`, CL-REACH) is wired. (6) Smoke hygiene: `TILO_SMOKE_ID_TOKEN` must be a JWT (checked before any request; a line-wrapped paste is refused with a fixed message); a failure reason holds the HTTP status plus at most 120 characters of a JSON `detail` string, never a raw body, withheld when it holds any 4-character run of the token however spelled; an exception reports its type name only. (7) The smoke answers a gate card only when it is in-app: tier T1/T2, no recipients, and an action with no connector, item or destination (`gate.risk_tier`, `gate.recipients`, `gate.action` of `card.schema.json`); otherwise `FAIL gate-card: not an in-app card; the smoke approves nothing that leaves Tilo` and no answer is sent. This guard exists because `deps.get_connector_adapters()` returns `{}` today, so nothing is sent for real; it MUST STAY when adapters are wired. It fails closed: a gate card with no `gate` object (missing, null or not an object) or no `action` is refused with the same line. Because CR-10 makes every real gate name a connector, the signed-in smoke on tilo-dev ends at `gate-card` BY DESIGN until an in-app connector exists. (8) `PRODUCTION_ORIGINS` is the dev project's Hosting origins: it must become per-environment config before any tilo-demo deploy (a user-approved step). (9) Production CORS does not allow `Idempotency-Key` (dev does, and the PPL-11a creates accept it): add it when the web client sends it. OPEN (security round 2): the url is pinned to the `tilo-api` service name and asia-southeast1 but not to tilo-dev's project number, which is not a literal in the repo (infra reads it from a data source); pin it once it is recorded in config or a Terraform output. | PROD-WIRE (issue #522); BQ-SIGNIN-7; CL-REACH; PRD 6.2, 6.6, SEC-15; BQ-PW-3 | 2026-10-07 |
| BQ-MTG02-1 | FR-MTG-02: the caps and the formats the spec leaves open (upload size, line cap, meet-bot offsets, plain-text speakers, timing in text files) | One upload is at most 4,000,000 bytes (twice the 2,000,000-byte stored text cap of ADR-0018, since timing lines and tags are overhead), at most 100,000 lines and 10,000 utterances (the contract); `state.json` at most 1,000,000 bytes. The meet-bot `t` is seconds from the session start (`bot/captions.py` `Utterance.t`), kept as `t_ms` (rounded, clamped to 0..100 hours; an unusable `t` keeps the previous offset). Plain text has no timing, so every `t_ms` is 0 and file order is the order; "Name: text" (a colon followed by a space, speaker part at most 60 characters) sets the speaker, so an unlabelled line that starts "Decision: ..." reads as speaker "Decision"; a line with no label is "Speaker 1". VTT `<v Name>` wins over a "Name:" prefix. A malformed meet-bot line is skipped and counted (`origin.skipped_lines`), a malformed `state.json` is ignored; only `agenda_title` is read from it. | FR-MTG-02 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MTG02-2 | FR-MTG-02 "injection flagged in the Trace": which event carries it? | No new type or field: the registered `artifact.created` (`artifact.versioned` for a next version) with severity `fyi` and `trace.tool` `safety_provider`, the same signal a flagged intake file gives (FR-INT-02); a clean ingest is severity `info` with the same tool. Transcript text never enters the event. A dedicated meeting-level safety event, or a `flagged` field on `artifact.created`, needs contract change. | FR-MTG-02, FR-INT-02 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-MTG02-3 | FR-MTG-02: where does the per-source origin record live, and whose verdict is `screening`? | The store's artifact record (`origin {kind, unit, agent}`, AR-02) has no field for the upload kind, size or utterance count, and a unit origin needs an agent and an idempotency key, so every transcript is stored with origin kind `upload`; the richer origin `{kind: vtt, srt, text or meet-bot, size_bytes, sha256, utterances}` is returned by `ingest_transcript` for the caller to keep on the meeting record, never the upload itself. Needs contract change if the origin must live in the artifact record (still open: `artifact.schema.json` has no field for it). Screening is CLOSED for the transcript path: ingest screens line by line in chunks of 20,000 characters and the store screens the whole payload once, so the two could differ; ingest now passes `NewVersion.screening_hint="flagged"` when its own screen flagged, and the store takes `flagged` if either verdict says so. The hint can only raise a verdict (never makes a version clean; any other value is refused), so a stored transcript never reads `clean` when ingest flagged it and ADR-0020 decision 4 can read the version's `screening` alone. | FR-MTG-02, AR-07, ADR-0020 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-PPL11C-37 | PPL-11c-FS-2 (a): how long does a durable `Idempotency-Key` record and a rate-limit counter live, and what does an expired but unswept document do? | Default applied: a key is a replay for 24 h of the request clock (`deps.get_clock`), a counter lives the clock plus 120 s (between 60 s and 24 h plus 1 min, so it outlives the minute it counts), an unfinished claim lapses after 5 min. A document past its `expires_at` that the Firestore TTL sweeper has not yet deleted is ignored (the key is free, the counter empty). `expires_at` is an aware datetime (a Timestamp), so a TTL policy on it works; the policy itself is infra, not this ticket. | services/api/tests/API_PPL_11c_FS_2.md section 6 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-PPL11C-38 | PPL-11c-FS-2: do the in-memory bounds of BQ-PPL11C-14 (16 keys a principal, 512 principals) apply to the durable store? | Default applied: no, they bound the in-memory `Limits` only. The durable store is bounded by the TTL and the 60 a minute cap. | API_PPL_11c_FS_2.md section 6 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-PPL11C-39 | PPL-11c-FS-2: what does a durable key record hold, and when is the key free again? | Default applied: a reference (record id, record kind, body fingerprint, a claim token), never answer or request text; the id is left out when it is the principal's own uid. A replay re-reads the record and rebuilds the answer (so a changed record answers its current state, and on Firestore the field order of the replay is the store's, not the first answer's). A record that is gone (erased, undone, a deleted meeting) frees the key. | API_PPL_11c_FS_2.md section 6 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-PPL11C-40 | PPL-11c-FS-2: is the durable minute a sliding or a fixed window? | Default applied: sliding, as the in-memory limiter: the accepted attempts of the last minute are kept (at most 60 floats a principal); a refused attempt is not counted; `Retry-After` is whole seconds to the oldest counted attempt. | API_PPL_11c_FS_2.md section 6 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-PPL11C-41 | PPL-11c-FS-2 (b): how often is the work-item event append retried? | Default applied: at most 3 `append` calls per write with one part id, resent unchanged (the writer is idempotent by part id, EV-10), so one lost or failed attempt is survived and the event is recorded once. Every attempt failing is the fixed 503 `unavailable` with the record undone and no error echoed. No backoff between attempts. | API_PPL_11c_FS_2.md section 6 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-PPL11C-42 | PPL-11c-FS-2: NOT PINNED, needs a decision: should `Limits.erase_person` be registered as a `tilo_memory` `RetentionStore` for FR-MEM-09? | Not built. The limiter documents hold no uid (they are hashed), so `erase_user`'s `record.user_id == user_id` match cannot find them, and the entity has no window in `RETENTION_DAYS`. `limits_of(stores).erase_person(org_id, user_id)` exists and is tested; wiring it into the erase route is open. | API_PPL_11c_FS_2.md section 6 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-PPL11C-43 | PPL-11c-FS-2 (b): GHOST EVENT, open: if an append lands on an ambiguous attempt and every later retry then fails, the 503 undoes the record but the event stays. Is there a compensating event type? | Not built. The event registry has no compensating type, so this is a contract or ADR question (HR-1, PE-30). The same ghost can follow when a created record is undone after its event landed. Until decided, the window is the three-attempt retry plus a store that dies in between. | API_PPL_11c_FS_2.md section 4 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-RUNBAR-1 | RUN-INDICATOR (user request 8 Oct 2026): what does the Park app know about "a run in progress", and where does the label come from? | Default applied: the only recorded run state the Park Api serves is the worlds list (`getWorlds`; live: `world.started`/`resumed` is `running`, `completed`/`failed` is `finished`, `paused` is `scheduled`), so the bar reads that, not an event stream (the Park Api has no events method; `src/world/` is off limits to `park/`). The label is "N agents working" when the Api serves agents, else "Working"; the detail is the newest activity line of a single running world, else its title, else "N worlds". No per-event label ("Planning", the current step) until a Park Api method serves a world's latest event. A REAL started world gets the bar through the server list: a listed `started` world shows "Working" with its title. | RunBar, runStatus | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-RUNBAR-2 | RUN-INDICATOR: refresh and failure. When is the state read? | Default applied: on mount, on page change, and when a decision or a started world changes; and every 15 s through the injected scheduler only while a live run shows or is held. The terminal state (finished, failed, paused) removes the bar. A failed re-read hides the bar and stops the polling; it is retried on the next page or decision change (no retry timer). | RunBar | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-RUNBAR-3 | RUN-INDICATOR: which worlds count? | Default applied: LIVE counts only worlds the server list served; the session's own started worlds that `liveApi.getWorlds` prepends (canned `makeWorld` objects: `running`, a template agent count) when the list lacks them are ignored (not recorded runs, never end). FIXTURE counts only worlds the person started in this session, shown as "Working" plus the world's name with no agent count or activity line (the template's are not served data); the canned sample worlds (K1, Hires, ...) never show the bar (HR-2). The nav's Sample data chip says why. | RunBar | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-RUNBAR-4 | RUN-INDICATOR: held runs and the moving part. | Default applied: a `needs` world with nothing running shows the static label "Waiting for your approval" with a pause mark and no ring. Live `needs` is never emitted by `worldsFromList` today (BQ-WIRE2-2), so held is reachable only on the fixture path or once the list serves an open card. The ring is the loader's existing `park-shell__ring` (tiloSpin .7s, already covered by the shell's honesty entries and reduced-motion rule); no keyframe, allowlist entry or pinned test was added. Reduced motion and the 'still' preference draw a still dot (decided in JS by `useMotionOff`; the Park root has no `data-motion`). A pulse of its own would need a new keyframe, which the pinned lists forbid. | RunBar, park.shell.css | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-RUNBAR-5 | RUN-INDICATOR: why is the bar an `<output>` and not `role="status"`? | Default applied: `<output aria-live="polite">` is the native status live region (implicit role `status`); `ParkApp.app.test.tsx` (WEB_3a_shell_addWorld_toast_Open_goes_to_the_new_world) finds the toast through the first `[role="status"]` and expects none after the toast goes, so an explicit role on the bar broke it. That test was not edited. | RunBar | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-UX11-1 | FR-UX-11 live wiring: tilo-api has no notifications route, so what does the live bell show? | The popover says "Not connected yet" (a failed read says "Notifications could not load"), never the sample list. Items are not derived from the `/cards` feed because a notification needs a time and a subtitle the card does not carry (inventing them would be a guess, HR-1..HR-7); the live notifications feed is the follow-up when a route exists. The bell dot counts the decisions the Api served that are still open (not the fixture-based `needsLeft`). RESOLVED 2026-10-08: on the live path the store's `needsLeft` is the same served-open count (`app/servedDecisions.ts`), so the Worlds nav badge, the Home and Worlds counts and the World chip read it too; with nothing served (loading, a failed read, an empty list) it is 0 and no count or badge shows ("Needs you" instead of "0 decisions"). The fixture path still counts the sample decisions. | docs/prd-supplement-design-2026-10-06.md FR-UX-11; PRD 9.4; BQ-WIRE3-1 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-VEVAL-1 | VERTEX-EVAL-1 (ADR-0033): `config/prices.yaml` has no price for the Gen AI evaluation service. What does it charge, in what unit, and is it offered in `asia-southeast1`? | No live run until the user adds two things in one change. (1) `config/prices.yaml`: a `vertex-eval` row with its source and date, in US$ per 1M judge input and output tokens (thinking included), plus ADR-0029's `unit_price` `{unit: character, per_1k}` only if the price page bills a fee per character. (2) `config/models.yaml`: the `vertex-eval` profile (`provider: vertex_eval`, `model` = the judge, chosen by the user) and the `eval_judge` role (primary and fallback both `vertex-eval`). If the page bills only the judge model's tokens, the row copies that model's rates. A profile without a price stops start-up while prices are `status: set`. Until then the command refuses live (`config_invalid`) and prints the lines; dev and CI use the fake. Location: `TILO_VERTEX_EVAL_LOCATION`, default `asia-southeast1` (PRD 6.5, the ADR-0029 price region). A location failure stops the run (exit 1, code `location`), and another region needs that region's price row. | PRD 6.3, 6.5, 14.1, PL-06 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-VEVAL-1 | VERTEX-EVAL-1 (ADR-0033): `config/prices.yaml` has no price for the Gen AI evaluation service. What does it charge, in what unit, and is it offered in `asia-southeast1`? | No live run until the user adds two things in one change. (1) `config/prices.yaml`: a `vertex-eval` row with its source and date, in US$ per 1M judge input and output tokens (thinking included), plus ADR-0029's `unit_price` `{unit: character, per_1k}` only if the price page bills a fee per character. (2) `config/models.yaml`: the `vertex-eval` profile (`provider: vertex_eval`, `model` = the judge, chosen by the user) and the `eval_judge` role (primary and fallback both `vertex-eval`). If the page bills only the judge model's tokens, the row copies that model's rates. 8 Oct 2026 lookup: no published price for the evaluation service itself was found (the pricing page text did not load; the docs say judge calls are Gemini inference through a Google-managed service account), so the working assumption is the lite judge's own token rates from ADR-0029; the user confirms before any row lands. A profile without a price stops start-up while prices are `status: set`. Until then the command refuses live (`config_invalid`) and prints the lines; dev and CI use the fake. Location: `TILO_VERTEX_EVAL_LOCATION`, default `asia-southeast1` (PRD 6.5, the ADR-0029 price region). A location failure stops the run (exit 1, code `location`), and another region needs that region's price row. | PRD 6.3, 6.5, 14.1, PL-06 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-VEVAL-2 | VERTEX-EVAL-1: which ~20 recorded pairs are compared, and what does the groundedness score claim? No pre-cost-fix and post-cost-fix recordings of the same cases exist on disk: only the scripted bank (40 cases) and B-007's synthetic recordings. Model recordings store responses only, keyed by a request hash. | Any two bank-format folders over the same briefs (paired by id and an identical input digest). First live comparison: baseline = the committed scripted bank (reference plans), candidate = a folder captured from live Director runs of the same cases. The capture is follow-up VERTEX-EVAL-1c, its own live spend, outside the 20-credit cap. Until it exists, live judges plan groundedness on whatever single set the user has. "Plan groundedness" (a judge's 0 or 1: are the Director's factual statements supported by the brief, answers and files) is not EV-05 (claims supported by a cited source, E-RES). It is reported apart and never in an EV row, `evals/results.md` or the metrics report. | PRD 3.5, 19.3 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-VEVAL-3 | VERTEX-EVAL-1: the user's cap (< 20 credits a run), PRD 19.3 ("the judge runs on the strongest profile") and ~20 cases cannot all hold at ADR-0029 rates: 1.38 credits a typical case on the flash judge (~13 fit in 18 credits), 0.52 on the lite judge (all 20). Also, `evaluateInstances` returns no token usage, the service's wrapper around a rubric is undocumented, and it is unclear whether `autoraterConfig` can cap the judge's thinking or output. | RESOLVED 8 Oct 2026 (user: "accept ADR-0033 with the cheaper judge"): the judge is the cheaper (lite) profile, so all 20 cases fit under the 18-credit cap; PRD 19.3's strongest-profile wording is waived for this run and the report says which judge ran. Keep the cap (default 18, hard maximum 19 in code). Earlier text, kept for the record: the PRD's strongest-profile judge. The run judges as many whole cases as fit and says so with counts first ("13 of 20 judged, 7 not run: credit cap"); the user may pick a cheaper judge in config to judge all 20. Each call is charged its quote: ceil(bytes/3) + 400 overhead input tokens, plus 512 assumed output tokens (thinking included), labelled "estimated" and reconciled against the billing export (PRD 14.1). Send `samplingCount: 1` and `flipEnabled: false`, and no output cap. The builder confirms the nested v1 REST field names and the custom-metric output format at build time. | PRD 19.3, EFF-07, 14.1, FR-MTR-08 | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-AGENDA-MEET-1 | Agenda: what does a click on an upcoming Tilo-joined meeting open? | Default (supervisor ruling 8 Oct): the Tilo meeting page, which fetches the transient join link (ADR-0024 am4, #678); no Meet URL sits in agenda data. The Park shell has no meeting page yet, so the button toasts "not connected yet" and opens nothing until the route exists. | ADR-0024 am4; apps/web/src/park/pages/Agenda | 2026-10-08 |
| BQ-SCHEDPLAN-1 | FR-AGT-14 SCHED_PLAN (issue #656, BQ-DEVH-2): a scheduled run snapshotted only the chosen approach, so `compile_plan` got one approach where `plan.schema.json` wants 2 to 3 and failed after the world and the budget grant existed. How is the offered list kept, and what of records made before? | (a) No contract change: `Schedule` is a store-side dataclass. `Schedule.approaches` (optional, default None) holds a deep copy of the task's full offered list, written by `POST /schedules`, persisted by both stores, absent from the schedule view; an old document without it loads as None. (b) `POST /schedules` answers 409 unless the offered list has 2 to 3 dicts with unique string ids, each within 16 KiB of UTF-8 JSON bytes, and the entry for the chosen id equals the chosen approach; the chosen approach stays `Schedule.approach` and is what `start_run` gets. (c) `ScheduleStarter.start` re-checks the same rule on the stored record BEFORE the grant lock, so a legacy or tampered record raises `StartSkipped(snapshot_incomplete)` (counted and recorded like `snapshot_missing`, 2xx so Cloud Tasks does not retry) and creates no world and no grant; the owner re-creates the schedule to repair it (no migration: dev schedules only). In Cloud Tasks mode such a record is refused and recorded every week, never disabled; disabling it on the first refusal is a possible follow-up. (d) The `/dev/schedules/tick` part of the ticket is not on this base; its test is left to the DEV-HARNESS branch. | FR-AGT-14; PRD 6.3, 8.6; issue #656; BQ-DEVH-2; BQ-AGT14-5 | 2026-10-08 |


| BQ-INTAKE-SCREEN-1 | Office upload screening verdict versus exact stored bytes | DOCX/PPTX/XLSX intake records the verdict on converted text; artifact.schema.json and ADR-0018 AR-07 require exact-byte wording. Architect must prepare an ADR amendment and contract change before any consumer treats Office uploads as outbound-safe; attachments remain T3. | SEC-REVIEW-1b; FR-INT-02 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-PROV-1 | ADR-0037 (FR-SKL-05, FR-SKL-06): what ends a real run for provenance, and may a person rate a run that Pause all stopped? | Default applied: the terminal enum is `completed` and `failed` only, written once from the run's own committed `world.completed` or `world.failed` (`finished_at` is that event's `ts`). Pause all and the kill switch finish nothing, because BQ-90 makes Pause all final for the run, so that run stays unfinished and is never rateable. `stopped` would be an additive enum value, and its writer must then never reopen a finished record. | FR-SKL-05, FR-SKL-06, ADR-0037 decisions 2 and 3, BQ-90, TS-14 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-PROV-2 | ADR-0037 (FR-SKL-07): where does the link from a question-card answer to a taught-skill run live? | Default applied: a separate `run_answer` record (`taught_skill.schema.json` 1.1.0) at `real_runs/{run_id}/answers/{card_id}`, not a field on the card, because the card record is a closed channel-facing contract shared by tilo-api and channels. `by` and `at` are copied once from the card's recorded answerer; only a question card a person answered makes one (a gate, consent, timeout default and repeat answer from a Decision make none); no text is copied; nothing is rebuilt from events. | FR-SKL-07, BQ-DSN-05, ADR-0037 decision 7, TS-16 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-PROV-3 | ADR-0037 (FR-SKL-05, FR-SKL-06): who may rate which run, how often, and how long are provenance records kept? | Default applied: a rating needs a finished `real_run` of the same org, skill, version and run id with `by` equal to the rater; a thumbs up needs `completed`, a thumbs down is accepted on `completed` or `failed`; a run takes one rating, never replaced, and `good_runs` counts completed runs rated up once. Records are kept one year like ratings and fixes and go with the skill and the person; the owner reads all of a skill's records and anyone else only their own. Changing a rating later is not supported (it would be additive). | FR-SKL-05, FR-SKL-06, FR-MEM-09, ADR-0037 decisions 3 and 6, TS-15, TS-17 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-USET-1 | ADR-0039 (FR-SET-02): the old quiet-hours threshold was a string ("£1,000"); what is stored, and what does it do? | Default applied: typed money `{amount_minor, currency}` or null (an integer in minor units and an ISO 4217 code); the string form is refused, never parsed. No delivery code reads it in this release: a gate card has no structured amount and FR-HIL-13 lets only `gate` and `blocked` notify in quiet hours, so it can never withhold a gate, a card or a Needs you row. Narrowing a phone push by it needs its own ADR and an amount on the gate. | FR-SET-02, FR-HIL-13, ADR-0039 decisions 5 and 6, USET-05 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-USET-2 | ADR-0039 (FR-SET-01): where do the unsaved defaults, the language labels and the currency minor-unit table live? | Default applied: not in the contract (it stays value-free, as BQ-SEC16-1 learned). The route's code or config supplies the defaults (`version` 0, the decisions row with phone on) and the client supplies the language labels; tilo-api refuses a currency code it has no minor unit for (USET-03). A closed currency or language list would be an additive minor bump. | FR-SET-01, FR-SET-02, ADR-0039, USET-02, USET-03 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-USET-3 | ADR-0039 (FR-SET-02): the Profile copy says the phone stays silent in quiet hours "except decisions over the threshold", but every gate and blocked notice notifies (FR-HIL-13) and the threshold changes nothing. | Default applied: the contract keeps the server's bypass. The web lane rewords the copy and aria text (a pinned-string edit, BQ-SET-2); the stored threshold stays display data until its own ADR. | FR-SET-02, FR-HIL-13, BQ-SET-2, BQ-SET-4, USET-05 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-PLACE-1 | ADR-0038 (FR-PB): which boards and columns may a pin sit in, and how many? | Default applied: any two ids of the shape `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,31}$` and an `order` from 0 to 9999; the contract holds no catalogue, because board and column are presentation inside the pin's own scope and never an access grant. A per-scope cap or a closed list would be an additive minor bump the web lane may ask for. | FR-PB-01, FR-PB-08, ADR-0038, PE-36 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-PLACE-2 | ADR-0038 (FR-PB-05): does a move touch `updated_at` and `history`, or write an event? | Default applied: no. A move changes `placement` only, so the plate's "last change" (PE-29) and teammates' history stay as they were, and no event type is added (the ADR authorises none). The cost is that moves are unaudited; a `moved` history change and an `updated_at` bump would be an additive minor if the user wants an audit. | FR-PB-05, ADR-0038, PE-29, PE-36 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-PLACE-3 | ADR-0038 and issue #893: "pinboard titles can only be typed by the person, content inside is editable" next to placement. | Default applied: only the guard is in this change: a placement write may not change `title` (PE-36 and the crossref cases). The titles-typed-only-by-the-person rule itself, and which fields count as "content inside", are not implemented and wait for the user's reading (the ADR says not to implement it yet). | FR-PB-05, ADR-0038, issue #893, PE-36 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-PA15-1 | FR-PA-15: nudges come "from the FR-PA-05 sweeper", which is not built yet. What does FR-PA-15 deliver without it? | `tilo_channels.nudges.NudgeGate` is the sweeper's port: `offer(Nudge(user, item_id, rule))` decides window, cap and once per (item, rule, day) in code; `snooze` and `release` handle the snooze. Rules are a closed list with fixed template lines (`due_24h`, `overdue`); no model, no title in the text. The clock job, the plate read and the routing of the part to the person's Desk world (`emit(user, part)`) are the sweeper's and tilo-api's. | FR-PA-15, FR-PA-05, PE-12 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-PA15-2 | FR-PA-15: `alert.raised` data holds only `alert_id` and `card_id`, so how does the panel know which item a nudge is about, and where is snooze state kept? | The part carries the one-line template in `summary`, severity `nudge`, `target` the person, `vis: []` (owner-only, Desk world), `card_id` null. The alert id is derived from (person, item, rule, local day); `NudgeGate.item_of(user, alert_id)` maps it back server-side, from the same per-person state that holds the day count and the snoozes (`NudgeStore.update`, atomic per person; in-memory store here, a Firestore adapter later). No contract change. | FR-PA-15, PE-12 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-PA15-3 | FR-PA-15: how is the window judged, when does a snoozed nudge return, and what counts toward the cap? | The window is `working_window` of `config/personal.yaml` (start inclusive, end exclusive) in the person's zone (default Asia/Kolkata), and the cap is `nudges.daily_cap` (default 5) per local day; a refused offer (outside the window or over the cap) records nothing, so it may be offered again. Snooze is `hour` (now + 1 h) or `tomorrow` (the window start of the next local day). A snoozed nudge returns once, as a new event, at the first `release` inside the window with the cap not spent; a returning nudge counts toward the cap. | FR-PA-15, FR-PA-05 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-PA15B-1 | FR-PA-15b: no FR-PA-05 sweeper exists in the repo under any name (only the `reminded` change in the work-item contract); what does the first one do? | `tilo_api.nudge_sweep.sweep`, called by the Scheduler's existing tick (off unless `TILO_NUDGES=1`): per person inside their window it returns ended snoozes, builds the plate with the one PE-26 builder and offers a nudge for each unflagged work item row: `due_24h` when `due` is today (a date is due at the end of its day, so a date of tomorrow is not within 24 h in the 09:00 to 18:00 window) and `overdue` when `due` is past, oldest first. The overdue merged `needs_you` card of FR-PA-05 is not built: its default here is the same capped daily nudge. `work_item.changed` `reminded` is not written (the nudge is an event of its own; no item record is touched). | FR-PA-05, FR-PA-15, PE-21, PE-26 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-PA15B-2 | FR-PA-15b: where is the nudge state (day count, once-keys, snoozes) kept so it holds across instances and restarts? | One document per person at `orgs/{org}/nudge_state/{user}` holding a JSON string, read and written with the document's last update time as precondition and retried (`FirestoreNudgeStore`); deployed it is Firestore or 503 `not_configured`, never memory. The nudge event ids are derived from (person, item, rule, day), so a retried write is the same event. Not run against the emulator here. Alerts are kept for the last 200 raised. | FR-PA-15, EV-10 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-PA15B-3 | FR-PA-15b: the panel's snooze chips. | The routes are built (`GET /v1/nudges`, `POST /v1/nudges/{alert_id}/snooze` with `{"choice": "hour"|"tomorrow"}`, the signed-in person's own only, 404 for any other). The web panel (`apps/web/src/assistant`) is still a fixture adapter that answers "not connected" and does not call `/v1/assistant/ask`, so there is no live panel to put chips on: named follow-up (connect the panel adapter, then render the nudge list with two chips). | FR-PA-15, FR-PA-13 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-WLD15-1 | FR-WLD-15: what does a theme change, and is it read at run time? The exports have no theme file format, `park.css` values are pinned verbatim, the CSP is `style-src 'self'` (no inline style), and the PixiJS/SVG city art keeps its own palette. | Default applied: a theme is `config/themes/<id>.json` (id equals file name, name, default, day, night; colour values only, role names as in `park.css`). Metropolis is the default and overrides nothing (it is the base `park.css`). `just tokens` generates `tokens/themes.css` and `themes.generated.ts` from the folder (a drift test pins them), so a new theme file needs no code edit, only the generator run. Themes recolour the UI role tokens, not the city art, and status colours (ok, amber, red) are not themeable. | FR-WLD-15 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-WLD15-2 | FR-WLD-15: how does a person pick a theme? Profile and Appearance copy is pinned (`profileExpect.ts`), so a picker needs approved strings. | Default applied: no picker. The choice is the `theme` pref (`setPref("theme", id)`, stored in `localStorage` as `tilo.theme`); an unknown id is Metropolis. A picker waits for approved Appearance copy. | FR-WLD-15 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-CON21-1 | FR-CON-21 (Could, labelled an interpretation in the supplement): what is a "vendor site brief", what does "track status" read, and which route? No event type exists for a partner status read, and FR-CON-18 (Lovable MCP route) is not on this base. | (a) The brief is the FR-CON-16 accepted requirements plus fixed sentences chosen from closed lists (what vendors need, what the team tracks, invite or link access): no free text, no credential field, so it carries only approved fields and goes through `prepare_handoff` and the Lovable link route (T3, a person). (b) Status is a pure view over the run's raw archive: the latest recorded `tool_call` read of each page or connector the person listed, else `not_read`; nothing is parsed or inferred from page text, so quotes, orders and deliveries are the person's labels on connected sources. No event is emitted, so no contract change. (c) The MCP status read-back and the FR-CON-22 pier belong to FR-CON-18 and FR-CON-22. The user may refine the vocabulary lists (`vendor_site.py`). | FR-CON-21; supplement s6; HR-1 | 2026-10-12 |

| BQ-UX18-MCP-OAUTH | FR-UX-18 catalogue can display nine OAuth services, but config/mcp.yaml is empty and McpClient accepts only an injected transport, without the deployed per-person OAuth seam. | Catalogue cards show owner setup required, with no grants, fabricated scopes, endpoints or connected status. Execution remains refused before transport. Supervisor 12 Oct 17:00: the catalogue/cards are ready to ship separately. Explicit reserved mapping is documented beside each config/connectors.yaml entry: each catalogue ID maps to mcp_<catalogue ID> (including mcp_microsoft365). It is a naming reservation, not a runtime binding or allowlist grant; comments preserve the frozen connector schema. A later OAuth/transport ticket needs the owner's OAuth app registrations, broker, durable outbox/egress and security review before allowlisting these servers. Do not offer passwords or bypass the allowlist. | FR-UX-18, FR-CON-12, SEC-02 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-CHN17-1 | FR-CHN-17: which policy lets `send_push` run without a card? A push is a side effect, so it goes through the outbox and the policy engine; unlisted actions are T3. | Default applied: `send_push` is T1 in `config/autonomy.yaml` (own device, card title only, no recipient beyond the owner's own address) and runs only under the owner's standing L3 policy for it with `undo_window_s` 0 (any undo window refuses: a push is never delayed). With no such policy nothing is queued and the card still shows in the app. tilo-api creates that policy when the person turns push on (not built; needs the Profile settings route of ADR-0037). | FR-CHN-17 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-CHN17-2 | FR-CHN-17: the real sender (VAPID JWT signing plus payload encryption) needs `pywebpush` or `cryptography` calls and the Secret Manager read. | Default applied: no new dependency in this ticket; `PushSender` is a port and the module holds the secret NAME only. The production adapter, the `/v1/me/push` subscribe route in tilo-api and the web service worker are separate tickets; the real device check is the user's. | FR-CHN-17 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-CHN17-3 | FR-CHN-17: ADR-0037 (notification matrix, quiet hours) is Proposed, so what does push read? | Default applied: the existing `PreferenceReader` shape (`quiet_hours`, FR-HIL-13) plus, when present, `notifications.<row>.phone` with ADR-0037's row names (decisions, spend, finished, digest, weekly); only an explicit `false` switches a row off, and a gate that is not pushed stays in Needs you. No stored settings are written. | FR-CHN-17, FR-SET-02 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-CHN17-4 | FR-CHN-17 (SEC-REVIEW-7): what does 'Stop all autopilot' do to push, and which card kinds may be pushed? | Default applied: kinds are the closed set gate, blocked, spend, finished, digest, weekly (PRD 10.2: consent is in-app only, a nudge is a toast); the standing push policy is an ordinary policy, so Stop all autopilot (policies to L1) silences every push including gates, and the in-app card is untouched. Whether push needs its own switch is for ADR-0037. Quiet hours need `tzdata` (declared by tilo-people); an unknown zone name makes the quiet-hours reader return None (pre-existing, FR-HIL-13). | FR-CHN-17 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-CHN17b-1 | FR-CHN-17b: who creates the owner's standing L3 `send_push` policy, and what does the subscribe route say meanwhile? It is the user's decision (BQ-CHN17-1). | Default applied: nobody creates it. The subscribe route stores the device and answers `delivery: off_until_owner_policy` until a policy of the principal with `action_type` `send_push` at `L3` is in the store (then `ready`); the Profile control says the same in plain words. Storing a device grants nothing. tilo-channels reading the subscriptions (an internal read route or a shared store), and calling `PushNotifier.notify` from the notification feed, are not wired yet. | FR-CHN-17 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-CHN17d-1 | FR-CHN-17d: where does the browser get the VAPID public key it needs to subscribe, and where does the worker live? The web build may read only three VITE variables (repo test P0-07c). | Default applied: tilo-api answers the key in `GET /v1/push` (`public_key`, from its env `TILO_WEBPUSH_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`, shape-checked, null when unset), so no new build variable; the control then says push is not set up. The worker is `apps/web/public/push-sw.js` at `/push-sw.js` (CSP `worker-src 'self'` already allows it). The control renders only for a live signed-in Google session (never in the fixture world or a demo), is off by default and the person turns it on; it says plainly that nothing is sent until the owner's push policy exists. User steps: make a VAPID key pair, put the private key in Secret Manager as `webpush-vapid-private-key`, set the public key on tilo-api and the secret-to-env mount and `TILO_WEBPUSH_VAPID_SUBJECT` on tilo-channels. | FR-CHN-17 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-CHN17e-1 | FR-CHN-17e: the user said yes to web push going live (12 Oct), so how is the owner's standing L3 `send_push` policy made? Closes BQ-CHN17-1 and BQ-CHN17b-1. | Default applied: only by the signed-in owner's own confirm in the Profile control (consent line: Tilo may send notifications to this device for your own approvals and updates; turn off any time). `POST /v1/push/policy` {on: true, confirm: true} stores one policy record per owner with `Store.save` and a `policy.changed` part (authz person, owner) in the owner's Desk world; scope is connector `web_push`, no undo window, `limits.per_day` from `config/web_push.yaml` (`policy_per_day`, 50). Off (`on: false`) ends it the way the kill switch does and removes every stored device. `delivery` is `ready` only while it is L3 and the principal's role is owner; a member's role keeps it off. 'Stop all autopilot' also ends it. | FR-CHN-17 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-CHN17b-2 | FR-CHN-17c: how does the production sender get the VAPID private key, and what contact subject does it sign with? Secret Manager reads need a client library tilo-channels does not have; the secret is granted to channels already (`infra/envs/dev/main.tf`). | Default applied: the sender reads the key from a mounted environment variable named from the secret NAME (`webpush-vapid-private-key` becomes `WEBPUSH_VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY`, Cloud Run secret-to-env) and Tilo's contact from `TILO_WEBPUSH_VAPID_SUBJECT` (`mailto:` or https; never a person's address). It is built only on Cloud Run (`K_SERVICE`) with both set; otherwise the fake stays. The Terraform secret-to-env mount, the contact value and an egress allowlist for the four push hosts are the user's apply, not done here. pywebpush (MPL-2.0) and its tree are listed in `docs/licences.md`. | FR-CHN-17 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-CHN17f-1 | FR-CHN-17f: where does the push feed run, what does it read, and what about the injection flag and the settings? tilo-channels holds no Firestore role (SEC-12) and the VAPID secret is channels-only, but the policies, cards, outbox and devices are tilo-api's. | Default applied: the outbox, policy engine and `PushNotifier` run in tilo-api (the channels package is a library there); only the send crosses to tilo-channels over `POST /internal/push/send` (OIDC; `TILO_CHANNELS_URL` on tilo-api, `TILO_PUSH_AUDIENCE` and `TILO_PUSH_CALLER` on tilo-channels). The trigger is the Scheduler's existing tick (`_sweep_push`), reading the same open gate cards the bell reads; a gate card <= 15 min old is one notice with fixed text and a path built from the card id, so no card, mail or model content reaches a lock screen and the card is passed as known clean on that ground (cards carry no injection flag in the contract). No durable notification settings exist (ADR-0037 Proposed), so no quiet hours or matrix are read by the feed; the notifier honours both when a reader is wired. The outbox entry is `send_push` on adapter `web_push`, the policy event and the transfer go to the owner's Desk world. | FR-CHN-17 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-CHN17-5 | FR-CHN-17: the user-only steps to make push live (nothing applied by Claude). | One checklist: (1) make a VAPID key pair locally (never paste it in chat); (2) store the private key in Secret Manager as `webpush-vapid-private-key` (granted to tilo-channels in `infra/envs/dev/main.tf`); (3) tilo-channels: mount that secret as env `WEBPUSH_VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY`, set `TILO_WEBPUSH_VAPID_SUBJECT` (`mailto:` Tilo's contact), `TILO_PUSH_AUDIENCE` (its own URL) and `TILO_PUSH_CALLER` (tilo-api's service account email), allow ingress from tilo-api only and egress to the four push hosts in `config/web_push.yaml`; (4) tilo-api: set `TILO_WEBPUSH_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` (the public half) and `TILO_CHANNELS_URL`, with `TILO_OUTBOX_STORE=firestore`; (5) merge the stack #877, #887, #888, #891, #904 and the feed PR after review; (6) on an Android Chrome device sign in, Profile > Notifications > Browser notifications, accept the consent line, then open a gate card from another device and time the notice (the real device check, <= 10 s). | FR-CHN-17 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-PA06-1 | FR-PA-06: the sweeper (FR-PA-05) does not exist yet and nothing in the repo reads the plate on a clock. Who builds the plate for the mail and when does it run? | `PlateDigestBuilder.build(user_id, plate, kind)` takes the plate dict a caller (tilo-api, later the FR-PA-05 sweeper) built for that person with the one PE-26 builder; it validates it against `plate.schema.json`, re-computes the PE-30 fingerprint and refuses a plate of another user. The clock job and the plate fetch are the caller's. `kind="reminder"` is the same mail limited to overdue and due-soon work items. | FR-PA-06, FR-PA-05, PE-26 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-PA06-2 | FR-PA-06: what does an emailed Done or Snooze link do, and where is it applied? | `LinkService` answer links bound to the work item id with options `done` and `snooze` (24 h, single use, FR-CHN-07). `PlateDigestBuilder.redeem` verifies and consumes; `redeem_and_apply` then calls the caller's `PlateActionPort.apply(user, item, action, authn="link")`, which must re-check owner and `open` status at the write (PE-10) and resolve Snooze to tomorrow 09:00 local (BQ-PPL11C-28). The link is a path token (`{base}/l/<token>`), not a fragment: the page that serves it (FR-PA-06b) must answer `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, mask the path in its access log, and treat a GET as a non-consuming inspect that renders a confirm page; only a POST consumes. `base_url` is https with no user info, query or fragment. | FR-PA-06, FR-CHN-07, PE-10 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-PA06-3 | FR-PA-06: does the plate mail share the daily send limit of the FR-CHN-18 digest? | Same L3 policy and outbox path, a separate idempotency key per kind (`plate_digest:<user>:<UTC day>`, `plate_reminder:<user>:<UTC day>`), so each goes once a day. The person's digest policy `per_day` limit is shared by all three; the default policy needs `per_day` 3 (BQ-CHN18-1 default is 1) for all to go, and its `scope.recipients` is pinned to the owner address (the send re-checks owner and mailbox itself, so no caller can name another address). A plate row the safety screen flagged (PE-21) is never rendered, titled or linked: only a count line is mailed, and a plate with nothing but flagged rows sends nothing. A title's links and addresses become `[removed]` (PE-24). | FR-PA-06, FR-CHN-18 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-PA06B-1 | FR-PA-06b: the emailed link opens with no session, so its path must be public to the SEC-15 gate. How is that added without a wildcard? | `access.public_link(method, path)`: `GET` or `POST` of exactly `/l/<one segment of A-Za-z0-9_.- characters>`, no query, no extra segment. `PUBLIC_ROUTES` and its three pins are unchanged. The route verifies the signature, expiry and single use itself, so the gate lets nothing else through; a new public path stays the owner's decision. | FR-PA-06b, SEC-15, SEC-08 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-PA06B-2 | FR-PA-06b: what must be set for the links to work live, and where is single use kept? | Runtime env on tilo-api (gcloud, like the store switches): `TILO_LINK_SIGNING_KEY` (a dedicated 32+ byte secret from Secret Manager, never reused), `TILO_LINK_KEY_ID` (default `key_a`), `TILO_LINK_BASE_URL` (the API's https origin, no user info, query or fragment). Deployed, a nonce is one `create` at `orgs/{org}/link_nonces/{sha256(nonce)}` through the API store door (shared by every instance); it needs a Firestore TTL policy on `expires_at` (`gcloud firestore fields ttls update expires_at --collection-group=link_nonces --enable-ttl`). Unset or unusable: 503 `not_configured`, nothing spent. The access log masks `/l/<redacted>`; Cloud Run's own request log still records the path, so the response sends `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` and the link lives 24 h. | FR-PA-06b, SEC-08, BQ-82 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-PA06B-3 | FR-PA-06b: who sends the plate mail on a clock? | The Scheduler's tick calls `plate_links.sweep` (off unless `TILO_PLATE_DIGEST=1`): per person inside their working window it builds the plate and hands it to a `PlateMailer`. No production `PlateMailer` exists, because FR-CHN-18's digest has no production adapters either (the L3 policy source, the artifact store port, the mailbox lookup, the Desk-world emit); `get_plate_mailer` is 503 until they are wired, so nothing is sent and the tick's answer is unchanged. The digest policy it needs pins `scope.recipients` to the owner's address and `per_day` 3 (BQ-PA06-3). | FR-PA-06b, FR-CHN-18, FR-PA-05 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-CHN18B-1 | FR-CHN-18b, FR-PA-06c: which standing rule lets the plate mail send, and where is it read? | The person's own `send_email` policy in the API store (`deps.get_store().policies`, owner = the person), and only one that is L3, `undo_window_s` 0, `scope.connectors == ["gmail"]`, `scope.domains == []` and `scope.recipients == [<their own address>]` exactly (a rule with any other recipient, an undo window or another level is not used, so the engine gates). Scope districts must include `D1` (the digest's district), `limits.per_day` 3 (BQ-PA06-3). The address is the person's own record `email`, read server side; a changed address means the rule no longer matches and nothing is sent. No route creates this rule yet: it is the owner's own standing rule and creating it is a product decision (`needs product decision: who creates it and how`). | FR-CHN-18b, FR-PA-06c, FR-CHN-18, PE-09, PE-20 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-CHN18B-2 | FR-CHN-18b: why must the rule have no undo window, and what happens to an entry that was not sent in the tick? | The live Gmail stub (`ConnectorRpcStub`) holds the entry's evidence in memory and `needs_run`, so the Scheduler's outbox sweep skips it; the mail is therefore enqueued and dispatched in the same call, which needs `undo_window_s` 0 (an undo window on a mail to oneself has nothing to undo). An entry still unsent after that one dispatch is abandoned (so it never goes out later without evidence) and the day's key stays used: no second try the same UTC day. One that may have gone out (a lost answer) is never failed or repeated and raises one `alert.raised` in the Desk world (FR-AGT-13). | FR-CHN-18b, FR-PA-06c, SEC-03 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-CHN18B-3 | FR-CHN-18b: what is not built? | The FR-CHN-18 daily digest of automated L2/L3 actions has no trigger and its Undo and Report links have no route (`/l/<token>` serves `done` and `snooze` only; what Report does is not specified). The overdue/soon reminder (`kind="reminder"`) is not triggered either (no FR-PA-05 sweeper decides when). Quiet-hours preferences have no API store, so the person's working window (config/personal.yaml) is the only gate. The plate mail uses the person's own Desk world for its artifact, `policy.applied` and `data.transfer` (`vis: []`, EV-12: target is the world id, never a gate id). | FR-CHN-18b, FR-PA-06c, FR-CHN-18, FR-PA-05 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-PA06B-4 | FR-PA-06b security review 6b: a mail click has no sign-in. How does the public POST reach the durable people store and event writer, and what about App Check? | The owner accepted the public mail-link route on 13 Oct 2026 ("You have my YES"): `GET` and `POST /l/<token>` are a second named public set in `AccessGate` (`_sec15_lib.PINNED_PUBLIC_LINK_ROUTES`; the exact-route pin is unchanged) and the `/l/` prefix is exempt from App Check (`AppCheckGate`, via `access.public_link`; `EXEMPT_ROUTES` is unchanged), because a mail client sends no token and the signed single-use token is the credential. The POST resolves its seams in the handler only after `peek` verified the token (`ephemeral.mark_no_person`, the tick's pattern, then the app's own `get_people_stores`, `get_event_writer`, `get_desk_worlds`): the durable store, never a demo session's, with the person taken from the token alone; a forged or malformed token reaches no store, and a seam that is not wired is 503 before the link is spent. `peek` accepts only a `wi_` work-item id, so a question card's link is not spent here. | FR-PA-06b, SEC-15, SEC-10, SEC-08 | 2026-10-13 |
| BQ-CTX-TEAMWORLD | Team-shared worlds (a world-to-team link or share grant) have no stored record; team visibility of world runs and artifacts in /v1/context is not built. | Needs an ADR plus a WorldRecord or grant contract change (architect) AND a user-approved amendment of PE-03 for team work-item visibility. Default meanwhile: own personal pins; current-member team pins with immediate revocation; own work items and assigner status only through the unchanged oracle (teammates have no work-item view); existing server-bound/owner world access; calendar omitted; nothing else (supervisor 12 Oct 14:40 ruling). | CONTEXT-P2, SEC-02 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-CON19-1 | FR-CON-19: Canva's Connect API gives no idempotency key, so a retry after a crash between "design made" and "export stored" could make a second design. Where does the at-most-once record live? | The adapter keeps an in-memory progress record per outbox entry (design id, then export ref): a retry after a failed export only repeats the export, and a retry after an unknown create failure raises `PossiblySent` (a person checks Canva). Same as the MCP call ledger (BQ-CON12-2): it moves into the durable outbox store with it. | FR-CON-19; PRD 12; OB-03 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-CON19-2 | FR-CON-19: where is Canva declared, and what is the tier of `create_design`? The PRD names only "sharing outside the company is T3". | (a) `config/canva.yaml` (one `connector.schema.json` entry), NOT `config/connectors.yaml` yet: that file is the Director's catalogue and the registry-wide invariants (a mock and a real adapter per id, connect routes, the recorded plan prompt) would all change for a connector that cannot connect until the workspace owner registers a Canva app. Activation is moving the entry. (b) Both actions are T3, unlisted in `config/autonomy.yaml` (unlisted is T3, BQ-44; listing them would break the SEC-11 catalogue pin until Canva is in `connectors.yaml`): creating a design sends approved content and granted assets to a third party, like `build_handoff`; granted assets are `attachments` (T3) anyway. Tiers are configuration (PRD 10.4) and people set autonomy levels, not tiers: the adapter takes the tier from the risk table, never from the declaration or the entry, and the tests pin both actions at T3. (c) Scope names and the share call follow Canva's Connect API as understood; the person registering the Canva app confirms them (Canva has no documented share-to-email endpoint known to us, so the fake API pins one `share` call). | FR-CON-19; supplement s6; BQ-44 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-PROV-4 | FR-SKL-05, FR-SKL-06 routes (ADR-0037, skills_feedback.py): what do the rating, fix and state routes do when the event write fails after the record committed, and what does Learn list? | Default applied: the record commits first, the event second (TS-11), a failed event is a fixed 503. A retry of the same rating or fix (same run and answer, or same version, step and text) is a 200 that only repeats its event under a stable part id; another answer for a rated run is 409 `already_rated`, a stale fix 409 `version_conflict`. A state change that committed and lost its event is not repeated by a retry (the change is no longer a change), so the Trace can lack that one event until a sweep exists. `GET /v1/learn` `needs_rating` is the caller's own finished, unrated, recorded runs, newest first, at most 50; no recorded run is an empty list. Fix route and body are the ones of #860 so its web half works unchanged. | FR-SKL-05, FR-SKL-06; ADR-0037; HR-1 | 2026-10-13 |
| BQ-INTAKE-SCREEN-1 | Office upload screening records a verdict on converted text, while artifact.schema.json and ADR-0018 AR-07 describe exact stored bytes. | Architect must propose the ADR amendment before contract wording changes. Until accepted, an Office upload verdict must not be treated as outbound-safe bytes; attachments remain T3 with person approval. with_upload_text stays intake-only. No contract or policy change in this follow-up. | FR-INT-02, AR-07, SEC-05; SEC-REVIEW-1b and supervisor16:20 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-USET-4 | FR-SET-01 route: what does `GET /v1/me/settings` answer for a person who never saved, given the contract requires a name and an email? | Default applied: code defaults (`user_settings.defaults`): version 0, `updated_at` null, a placeholder name and a reserved `.invalid` email, UTC, 09:00-17:00 weekdays, and the page's existing matrix (BQ-SET-3). A client treats version 0 as unsaved and keeps its own sign-in name and email for display. Nothing is stored by a read. | FR-SET-01, USET-02, BQ-USET-2 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-USET-5 | FR-SET-02: where is a stored quiet window read by delivery? | Default applied: `user_settings.StoredPreferences` is the `.get(user_id)` view `CardStore(preferences=)` and the channels `PreferenceReader` take (window and rows, never the threshold; None for no saved settings or an unreadable store, so nothing is hidden). It is NOT yet passed to the run's `CardStore` (`runs.Run` builds it with no preferences): that is a `Seams` change for a follow-up ticket; until then gates and blocked notices behave exactly as before. | FR-SET-02, FR-HIL-13, USET-05 | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-USET-6 | FR-SET-01: may an anonymous demo session save settings? | Default applied: no. A demo session stores nothing durable (DEMO-EPHEMERAL, BQ-SIGNIN-10): `GET` answers the defaults and `PUT` is 403 `full_session_required`. The client says so and keeps its device-only copy, labelled. FR-MEM-09 erase is not yet wired to `SettingsStore.erase` (the store has the call; `erase_person` is in `tilo_people`). | FR-SET-01, USET-08, DEMO-EPHEMERAL | 2026-10-12 |
| BQ-SETWEB-1 | FR-SET-01/02 web: Profile and Notifications now read and save through the settings API, so which design details change? | Default applied: (a) Notifications gets its own "Save changes" (the matrix and quiet hours were instant page state; a whole-document PUT needs an explicit save, and a failure keeps the toggles); this is the tab's one primary button (UX-1), the design had none there. (b) The Time zone field shows the IANA name (`Europe/London`, not `Europe/London (GMT+1)`) and Language is a tag with a label for four common ones; Working hours is text `08:30 – 17:30, Monday to Friday`, read back strictly, never guessed. (c) The email help is "Display only · does not change how you sign in" (USET-06, was "Verified · used for sign-in"). (d) The threshold line is always "Decision threshold: Not available yet" (BQ-USET-3). (e) The old device-only copy (`tilo.profile.<email>` in localStorage) is no longer read or written, never silently imported (ADR-0039). (f) A demo or sample session reads the sample document and a save says nothing was saved (BQ-USET-6: no device-only copy is kept). User to approve (a) to (c) as design. | FR-SET-01, FR-SET-02, ADR-0039, BQ-SET-2, BQ-USET-3 | 2026-10-13 |
| BQ-M2-1 | FR-MTG-11 (M2): what gates a LIVE Gemini Live voice call, and what does it cost? | Default applied: three gates, all before any client is built: the user's `LIVE=1`, `voice.live.price_approved: true` in `config/voice.yaml` (the user sets it once a current regional Live quote is approved, PL-06), and `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT`. No price is invented in `config/prices.yaml`; `last_usage` carries the whole-number token counts the meter will need. The flag `voice.live.enabled` ships false: Chirp stays the voice. | FR-MTG-11, PL-06 | 2026-10-13 |
| BQ-M2-2 | FR-MTG-11 (M2): does the Live voice record audio for replay? | Default applied: no. Replay serves hand-written, synthetic recordings (`voice-live-recordings/<key>.json`, keyed by profile, model, voice and the SHA-256 of the text; the digest is stored, never the words). A miss is `NotConfigured` (the bot falls back to its local voice for the meeting), never a live call. Audio of a meeting reply is not kept, as for Chirp. | FR-MTG-11, EFF-01 | 2026-10-13 |
